# VT protocol coverage

What baud implements, what it does not, and what it has decided not to. Every
row traces to a primary source in `docs/research/sources/`, cited as
`[[source: <key>]]` and checked by `scripts/check-sources.sh`.

**And it is checked in both directions.** The specification side covers plain
CSI finals, CSI forms with markers and intermediates, the `ESC Fe` controls,
every `OSC Ps` and every `DCS` form — the whole escape surface an application
can reach, rather than the part baud happened to list.

 Every **done** row is fed to a real
`Term` and must reach a handler (`enumerated.rs`); and every plain CSI final
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` defines must be either handled or counted in
`Term::unsupported` (`spec_inventory.rs`). The second matters because this file
is *baud's own* enumeration: an operation the specification defines and baud
never listed is invisible here — not `todo`, not `out`, absent — and checking
the table against the code cannot see a row nobody wrote.

The scope subset is deliberate and the check accommodates it rather than
demanding parity: ctlseqs documents far more than baud commits to, so the
requirement is not that every operation be implemented. It is that none be
*silently consumed*.

**This file is the scope definition.** "Implement the VT protocol" is not a
statement with a truth value until the surface is enumerated, because the
surface is not finite in any useful sense: `[[source: ctlseqs]]` documents 117
CSI sequences alone, many of them xterm extensions with no other implementation.
Tiers below say what baud is committing to and what it is not.

Status values are deliberately blunt:

| | meaning |
|---|---|
| **done** | implemented, dispatched, and covered by a test that would fail if it regressed |
| **partial** | implemented but known-incomplete; the gap is named in the row. Twenty-two rows carry it: BDSM, DCSM, DECRLM, `OSC 22`, kitty's `OSC 99`, `OSC 1337 ; Notification` and `File`, DECRC, DECNCSM, DECSCPP, DECSWBV, DECSMBV, DECAUPSS, the DEC Special Graphics glyph table, the national sets' translation, JIS-Roman, DEC Technical, the DEC Hebrew, Greek and Turkish Supplementals, DEC Cyrillic, SCS and Russian NRCS, kitty graphics' `a=p`, and ReGIS |
| **parsed** | recognised by the parser and then **dropped** — worse than missing, because it looks supported |
| **todo** | in scope, not implemented |
| **out** | deliberately not in scope; the reason is in the row |

A `parsed` row is a bug. It is the state where a terminal accepts a sequence,
does nothing, and gives the application no way to find out.

**A `done` row cannot quietly become a `parsed` one.**
`every_done_sequence_reaches_a_handler` synthesises each done row's sequence
*from this table* and feeds it to a real `Term`; `Term::unsupported` must not
move. Marking a row done for an operation the parser drops is therefore a test
failure, not something a reader has to notice. Verified against a planted
regression rather than assumed.

Be exact about what that does and does not prove. It proves the parser reaches
a handler for every operation this table claims. It does **not** prove the
handler is correct — that is what the per-protocol suites are for, and no
mechanism can prove it in general.

**No row carries `parsed` or `todo`. Twenty-two carry `**partial**`, and
each names its gap.** `no_row_is_unfinished` in `baud-core/tests/coverage.rs`
holds all three, in this file, `input-coverage.md` and every file under
`docs/protocols`, and reads a status in any spelling, bold or bare and in any
case: a `parsed` or `todo` row fails it, so does a `partial` row whose cell
names its gap in fewer than twelve characters after the status, the
length `every_out_row_states_a_reason` holds an `out` reason to, and the rows at `partial` must equal the count
the legend and this paragraph state
(`the_vt_coverage_partial_counts_equal_its_partial_rows`), so a row that gains
or loses the status moves both or the test fails. The legend is the first
table in this file and is passed over by its place, not its words; it must
hold the five statuses and nothing else. Until 2026-10-30 the test matched
only a cell that began with the bare word, so every bold row passed it unseen,
and it did not read `input-coverage.md`. Until 2026-10-23 the legend read
twenty-eight, and named kitty graphics' file mediums, done since 2026-10-16.
The rows that carry it are DECSC, DECRC and the DEC Special Graphics glyph
table, and
since 2026-10-12 five DEC character set rows: JIS-Roman, DEC
Technical, the three VT500 supplementals, DEC Cyrillic, and SCS and Russian
NRCS, which read `done` while every one of them drew its byte, and the
national sets' translation, which read `done` beside a gate DEC's texts
disagree on, and since 2026-10-14 DECNCSM and DECSCPP, whose width change
the embedder's resize reflows, and since 2026-10-16 kitty graphics' `a=p`,
whose resampling filter and default-background readings are interim, and
since 2026-10-19 DECAUPSS, whose ISO Latin-1 assignment is not
acted on, and `OSC 22`, whose shapes macOS sets as the nearest
system cursor and the Linux driver as the nearest `cursor-shape-v1` shape, and
ReGIS, built a part at a time, and since 2026-10-21 kitty's `OSC 99`,
whose close macOS
reports untracked before permission is known, and since 2026-10-22
iTerm2's `Notification`, whose picture shows only as a PNG, JPEG or GIF, and iTerm2's `File`, which draws no format but PNG, JPEG and GIF, no hierarchical JPEG, none whose height follows its first scan, none of two components or more than four and none whose sampling ratios are not whole, and no GIF's text, routes no header past 512 bytes, and keeps a picture's pixel size when the cell size changes, and since
2026-10-20 DECSWBV and DECSMBV, since 2026-10-24 BDSM and since 2026-10-26
DECRLM and DCSM, each with its gap in its row. Three SGR rows, 10-19, 20,
26 and 50, carried it from 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-23, recorded and drawn by
no path until each was drawn. `OSC 1337 ; RequestAttention` carried it from
2026-10-21 to 2026-11-01, until its fireworks were drawn, and `OSC 1337 ;
SetBadgeFormat` from 2026-10-22 to 2026-11-01, until its expressions were
evaluated and its outline drawn. `?30` carried it from 2026-10-20 to
2026-10-21, until the Linux driver drew its scrollbar. Kitty's `OSC 21`
carried it from 2026-10-22 to 2026-11-01, until its visual bell took a
colour. The `dcs_passthrough`
state carried it from 2026-10-09 to 2026-10-23, until a `SUB` there drew the
error character under its setting. DECSCL carried it from
2026-10-19 to 2026-10-22, until each level executed only its own functions. KAM carried it from 2026-10-19 to
2026-10-20, until the indicator status line drew its Wait. SGR 52 carried it on 2026-10-18
until its curve was drawn. Kitty graphics' file mediums carried it on
2026-10-16 until the embedder's reader landed
the same day. Unicode placeholders carried it on 2026-10-16 until its two forks were settled the same day. DECALN carried it from 2026-10-13 to
2026-10-14, while it kept DECOM, the rendition and the side margins.
The `dcs_param` state carried it from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-19, while
what `3A` does there was open. DECCOLM carried it from 2026-10-13
to 2026-10-14, while it kept the
side margins and asked for no width. DECOM carried it on 2026-10-13, while
its origin ignored the left margin and then while its columns were not
confined. SGR 6's rapid blink carried it from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-14,
while it blinked at 5's slow rate. GR bytes under `ESC % @`, the `escape` and
`escape_intermediate` states and `DEL` in `csi_entry` or `csi_intermediate`
carried it from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-11. Each row at `partial` names
work that is not finished. `out` is the only status that
means "not implemented", and `every_out_row_states_a_reason` requires each one
to say why **in the row** — because otherwise "no row is `parsed` or `todo`"
would be a word game, satisfiable by relabelling anything inconvenient as out of scope.
The check cannot judge whether a reason is *good*; that is review. It can insist
one exists where a reader will find it. Do not
relabel an unfinished operation as `out` to satisfy the check: reaching for
`partial` is the signal that the operation is not done, and the fix is the
operation.

---

## Tier 0 — the parser state machine

Everything below depends on this being right, so it is not a tier of features
but the substrate. `[[source: williams]]` specifies the DEC ANSI parser as a
13-state machine. baud now has all of them; the `Utf8` state below is an
addition, not a substitution.

| state | in baud | note |
|---|---|---|
| `ground` | done | |
| `escape` | done | **A `DEL` is ignored and the sequence goes on**, as `[[source: williams]]`'s `escape` ignores `7F` and stays (286), and so after the `ESC` that may begin a string's `ST`, which is williams' `escape` reached from a string: `ESC DEL \` there is the `ST`. Until 2026-10-11 the `Escape` node took `7F` to `Consume` into ground, so `ab ESC DEL 7 cd ESC 8 X` drew `ab7cdX`, the DECSC lost and the `7` drawn, where `ab ESC 7 cd ESC 8 X` draws `abXd`, and the string's `ESC` cancelled the string (`a_del_in_any_escape_or_control_sequence_is_ignored_and_the_sequence_goes_on`, from fifteen states under both encodings at every read size and every split into two reads; `a_del_in_an_escape_or_control_sequence_is_ignored_and_the_node_stays`, the table). **Under `ESC % @` a GR byte is read as its 7-bit byte**, here and in every state of an escape or control sequence, the ISO 8859-1 row (below). **Under UTF-8 a byte `80-FF` cancels the sequence and is the ground's**, here and in `escape_intermediate`, so a character after `ESC` is written, the UTF-8 row "a byte `80-FF` after `ESC`" (below) |
| `escape_intermediate` | done | **collects** intermediates, plural — it kept only the last, which made every two-byte charset final unreachable. **A node of its own, `EscInter`, so `P X [ ] ^ _` are finals here**: williams' `escape intermediate` dispatches `30-7E` (224) and only `escape` opens a `CSI` or a string on them (222, 226, 232, 233). Until 2026-10-11 one `Escape` node was both states and opened them here too, so `ESC ( ]` read whole designated a set through `fast_esc` and split before the `]` opened an `OSC` that took what followed, and `ESC ( % P` opened a DCS in any read (`after_an_intermediate_the_introducer_bytes_are_finals_at_every_read_size`, five intermediate spellings and the six bytes under both encodings at every read size and every split; `escape_intermediate_dispatches_every_final_and_opens_nothing`, the table; the property atom `esc-inter-introducer`). **A `DEL` is ignored and the sequence goes on**, as williams' `escape intermediate` ignores `7F` and stays (293). Until 2026-10-11 it ended the sequence, so `ESC ( DEL 0 q` designated nothing and drew `0q`, where `ESC ( 0 q` draws a DEC Special Graphics glyph (the tests in `escape`) |
| `csi_entry` | done | its own state — see below. A `DEL` here is ignored, the row "`7F` DEL in `csi_entry` or `csi_intermediate`", and a GR byte under `ESC % @` is read as its 7-bit byte, the ISO 8859-1 row (both below) |
| `csi_param` | done | `CsiParams`. **A `:` ignores the whole sequence, counted, but in SGR and XTMODKEYS**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.3.1, "If 3/10 is received within a parameter string, the entire sequence up to and including the final character shall be ignored", and williams' `csi ignore`; `[[source: ecma48]]` 5.4.2 b allows 03/10 only inside one parameter. SGR's groups (`38:2::r:g:b`, `4:3`) and XTMODKEYS' one sub-parameter ("a mask of modifier bits to factor out of the parameter encoding", `[[source: ctlseqs]]`) are kept, the two xterm keeps. XTMODKEYS reads it as modifyOtherKeys' mask, `CSI > 4 ; Pv : mask m`, as xterm parses it; ctlseqs' own example, `CSI > 4 : 1 m`, is refused and counted, an open departure (the `CSI > 4 ; 2 m` row). Until then a parameter after `:` was read as if `;` had come, so `CSI 2:5 H` was CUP 2;5; keeping that would depart from the text, so the text is built (`a_colon_outside_sgr_ignores_the_sequence`, `sgr_and_xtmodkeys_keep_their_sub_parameters`) |
| `csi_intermediate` | done | intermediate recorded, in both feed paths. A `DEL` here is ignored, the row "`7F` DEL in `csi_entry` or `csi_intermediate`", and a GR byte under `ESC % @` is read as its 7-bit byte, the ISO 8859-1 row (both below) |
| `csi_ignore` | done | |
| `dcs_entry` | done | its own state — the marker rule, as `csi_entry`. **A C0 other than `CAN`, `SUB` and `ESC` is executed and the introducer goes on**, since 2026-10-23: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.5, "All other C0 controls are immediately recognized within escape, control, or control string introducer sequences. The escape, control, or control string introducer sequence continues where it left off" (PDF p. 144), as in a `CSI`; `[[source: williams]]` ignores them (354, 565) and so does xterm, practice; baud executes them, the standard's text. Until then they were ignored, so `ESC P LF $ q m ST` answered and moved nothing (`a_c0_in_a_dcs_introducer_is_executed_and_the_introducer_goes_on`, `a_shift_in_a_dcs_introducer_shifts_the_text_after_it`, `a_bel_before_a_dcs_body_rings_and_the_introducer_goes_on`); a `BEL` ended the string until 2026-10-09. **Under `ESC % @` a GR byte is read as its 7-bit byte**, here and in the two rows below: williams, "In all states, these codes are treated identically to GL codes 20-7F" (462); `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5, in "the introducer sequence to a Device Control String, the eighth bit will be ignored, and they will be treated as their 7-bit equivalent" (PDF p. 135); and `[[source: ecma48]]` §9, "10/00 to 15/14 shall have the same meanings" as "02/00 to 07/14". Until 2026-10-10 each state sent a GR byte to the body, so `ESC P A4 F1 m ST` was no DECRQSS and `90 B0 BB B2 F1` no Sixel (`a_gr_byte_in_a_dcs_prefix_is_read_as_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`, `a_gr_byte_in_a_sixel_introducer_is_read_as_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`, `a_gr_byte_in_a_dcs_prefix_takes_its_seven_bit_edge_under_latin1`; under UTF-8 nothing changed, `a_gr_prefix_under_utf8_is_what_it_was`). The texts agree on `A1-FE`; `FF`, which §9 stops short of and williams reads as `7F`, is ignored where `7F` is, and `A0` leads to the body as `20` does. `3A` here ignores the string, as in `dcs_param` |
| `dcs_param` | done | a C0 executed and the parameters going on, as in `dcs_entry`, where williams ignores it (361). Under `ESC % @`, `B0-B9` and `BB` read as `0-9` and `;` and are parameter bytes, and a Sixel's `Pb` is read from them (`dcs_entry`). **`3A`, and so `BA`, ignores the string**, since 2026-10-19: williams sends it to `dcs ignore`, "the character 3A appears anywhere" (589), and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.1 makes the parameter string "identical in syntax to the parameter string of a control sequence" (PDF p. 133), whose 3.5.3.1 says "If 3/10 is received within a parameter string, the entire sequence up to and including the final character shall be ignored" (PDF p. 129); `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.27 gives a DCS no parameter string. The string goes to the body unread, discarded at `ST` and counted, as one with a marker after a parameter does, so `ESC P 0:1 q` is no Sixel and `ESC P 1 : $ q m ESC \` answers nothing (`a_colon_in_a_dcs_prefix_ignores_the_string`). Until then it separated parameters as `;` does, which no text reads, so it was a departure once a CSI ignored a colon. xterm's own prefix parse takes `:` as the final and drops what follows (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`'s file, `parse_ansi_params`); see "The DCS prefix had the same merge" below |
| `dcs_intermediate` | done | `30-3F` after an intermediate does not reopen the parameters: the introducer runs on to its final byte, its bytes put to the body as before; a C0 executed, as in `dcs_entry`, where williams ignores it (368), after any intermediate or private marker up to the final byte, since 2026-10-23: XTGETTCAP's `+`, DECAUPSS's `!`, a `=` marker, a `?` or `:` after a parameter, a byte after `$` that is no final. They led straight to the body until then, so the C0 was put there (`a_c0_after_a_dcs_intermediate_or_marker_is_executed_and_the_introducer_goes_on`). Under `ESC % @`, `A4` is DECRQSS's `$` and `F1` its `q`, and `F1` alone a Sixel's (`dcs_entry`) |
| `dcs_passthrough` | done | payload collected for DECRQSS, DECRSPS, DECRSTS, DECLKD, XTGETTCAP, XTSETTCAP, XTGETXRES, ReGIS and Sixel. **The format effectors `08-0D` are payload and `DEL` is ignored**, williams' *put* and *ignore* (388), the two classes every text agrees on: `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.27 lets DCS's command string hold "00/08 to 00/13 and 02/00 to 07/14", `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.4 calls them "valid within control strings", and williams passes them "to the handler" (726); `DEL` williams ignores with no condition, STD 070 3.5.4.4 recommends ignoring "all other control codes not used within a command string", and ECMA-48 F.8.1 lets it be "removed from, a CC-data-element without affecting its information content". Only xterm stores it, practice not followed. Until 2026-10-10 the body took the `osc_string` row for both, so no handler saw a format effector and `DCS $ q m DEL ST` answered invalid (`a_dcs_body_puts_the_format_effectors_and_ignores_del`, `a_del_in_a_decrqss_pt_is_ignored`, `a_format_effector_in_a_dcs_body_reaches_the_handler_and_a_del_does_not`, `the_dcs_lf_del_atom_reaches_the_handler`; `an_osc_and_an_apc_keep_their_rows_for_a_format_effector_and_a_del` holds the other two bodies). **Both handlers drop a format effector**: DECRQSS as STD 070 3.5.4 recommends, "that these characters not affect the interpretation of the control string", so `m CR` names SGR as it did (`a_format_effector_in_a_decrqss_pt_changes_nothing`; xterm's `strcmp` answers invalid), and Sixel as STD 070-9 §9.1 requires, "These codes shall have no effect on the interpretation of Sixel Control strings". **`BEL` is payload and ends nothing**, williams' *put* (388): it ended the string, an `OSC`'s terminator in a DCS, until 2026-10-09, so `DCS $ q m BEL` answered and drew what followed; now only ST ends it (`a_bel_in_a_dcs_body_is_payload_and_only_st_ends_it`, `a_bel_inside_a_sixel_rings_and_draws_nothing`, `a_bel_does_not_terminate_a_dcs`). **Every other C0 is put to the handler too**, since 2026-10-19, williams' row whole ("C0 controls are also passed to the handler", 726), and each handler gives it the meaning DEC's practice does, "silently ignored" where it has none and "acted upon" where it has one (818-822): DECRQSS and DECRSPS drop every C0 before they read their data, as STD 070 3.5.4.4 recommends "that all other control codes not used within a command string be ignored by hardware", so `m BEL` and `m NUL` answer as `m`; a Sixel rings a `BEL` and does text mode's nothing for an `ENQ`, as 070-9 §9.1 gives each the "same action as in text mode" and ignores "All other C0 Codes"; and any other DCS stores them and is discarded whole. Until then the graph ignored `00-06`, `0E-17`, `19` and `1C-1F` and put `BEL` alone, which DECRQSS kept, so `m BEL` answered invalid and a Sixel `BEL` rang nothing (`every_c0_in_a_decrqss_pt_is_dropped`, `a_c0_or_gr_byte_in_a_decrsps_body_is_dropped_or_read_as_gl`, `another_dcs_holding_every_c0_is_discarded_whole`). **Under ISO 8859-1 a GR byte in a DECRQSS or DECRSPS body is its 7-bit byte**, since 2026-10-19: the data is a control sequence's characters (DECRQSS, STD 070 p. 5-196), whose GR STD 070 3.5.3 g reads as "the character from the corresponding row in columns 02 to 07" (PDF p. 129; its 3.5.5 truncates GR only "within escape, control, and control string introducer sequences", PDF p. 144, and 3.5.4.5 leaves a DCS body's to "the internal control string format"), ECMA-48 §9 gives GR in "the contents of the command string" "the same meanings as" 02/00-07/14, and williams (462) and ctlseqs read GR as GL in a control string, so `DCS $ q ED ST` answers as `m`; it was kept raw and answered invalid (`a_gr_byte_in_a_decrqss_body_is_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`). **A Sixel's handler drops every C0 it is put before its decoder**, ringing a `BEL`, and the graph drops `DEL`, so neither a `BEL` nor a `DEL` reaches a Sixel number; a stored `7F` split one until 2026-10-09, `!1 DEL 0~` drawing one column for ten, as a put `BEL` would (`a_c0_or_del_inside_a_sixel_number_splits_nothing`). xterm hands its Sixel parser only `CASE_IGNORE` bytes (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, 3408-3412), so not the `BEL`, which is `CASE_BELL`; the `DEL` is one, and ends a number there (`[[source: xterm-graphics-sixel]]` 541-555), a departure not followed. **A Sixel's handler drops a `SPACE`, inside a number too, and under ISO 8859-1 stores a GR byte as its GL byte**, since 2026-10-13: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §8.1, a command "is terminated by any non parameter code" and "Note Format Effectors (0/8 to 0/13) and SPACE (2/0) are ignored within sixel control strings" (PDF p. 919); §9.2, "2/0 (SPACE) Ignored"; §9.4, "The 8th bit is set to zero and the code is then processed as a GL code" (926), so `A0` is a `SPACE` and `FF` a `DEL`, both dropped; williams (462) and ECMA-48 §9 read GR as GL too. Until then `!1 SPACE 0~` drew one column where `!10~` draws ten, and a GR byte was stored raw and skipped, so `FE FE` drew nothing (`a_space_inside_a_sixel_body_is_ignored_and_splits_no_number`, `a_gr_byte_in_a_sixel_body_is_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`; under UTF-8 nothing folds, `under_utf8_a_gr_byte_in_a_sixel_body_is_not_read_as_gl`; `a_sixel_body_byte_is_kept_folded_or_dropped_as_070_9_section_9_says` holds `Sixel::body_byte` over all 512 pairs). **A Sixel keeps its chapter's rules for `SUB`, `CAN`, `ESC` and a C1**, since 2026-10-19 (the `DCS ... q` row): `SUB` is a blank sixel, 070-9 §9.1's "process as if a 3/15 code (blank Sixel) was received", over STD 070 3.5.4.4's and williams' end of the string, and a Sixel the other three cut off is placed from what it received, williams' unhook. **`SO` and `SI` shift, in this body and every other string**, since 2026-10-23: `[[source: ecma48]]` §9, "SO and SI have no effect on the interpretation of a control sequence, a control string or the operand of a single-shift control function, but they may, indeed, affect the meanings of bit combinations following in the data stream" (PDF p. 89), and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.1.4 the same (PDF p. 126). Neither reaches the handler, and GL is G1 or G0 for the text after the terminator; williams puts them here and ignores them in the other strings, and xterm ignores them, practice; baud follows the standard's text. They were dropped and the shift lost (`a_shift_inside_a_control_string_shifts_the_text_after_it`). **A C0 in the introducer is executed** (`dcs_entry`). **A string other than a Sixel that `CAN`, `SUB`, `ESC` or a C1 cuts off is discarded, unacted on**: VT510 Table 4-1 "Immediately cancels", `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.6's `CAN` ("this data shall be ignored"), and williams' VT220 to VT500 "cancel", whose *unhook* lets a handler "finish neatly" (728), which one that acts only at the terminator does by acting on nothing; STD 070 3.5.4.4 says they "terminate" a string "to minimize the effect of a lost String Terminator", read as ending it so the bytes after are not swallowed, not as running it. A Sixel and ReGIS keep what they drew as the data arrived, williams' "Cancelling a control string does not undo these effects" (501-503); nothing changed. **A `SUB` that cuts a string off draws the error character under the setting**, off by default since 2026-10-23 (the `1A` rows below). The row read `partial` from 2026-10-09 for that gap alone, and `done` before it with the gap unnamed |
| `dcs_ignore` | done | unhandled DCS consumed, never executed |
| `osc_string` | done | payload captured, bounded, and dispatched. **The one string `BEL` ends**, by `[[source: ctlseqs]]` ("XTerm accepts either BEL or ST for terminating OSC sequences", 2023-2024) where williams ignores it (407) (`a_bel_still_ends_an_osc`). **Under `ESC % @` a GR byte is kept as its 7-bit byte**, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5 drops its eighth bit in an OSC, its clipboard body included, so under `ESC % @` a title written in Latin-1, `ESC ] 0 ; caf E9 BEL`, is `cafi`; until 2026-10-11 it was stored as it came (the ISO 8859-1 row, below) |
| `sos_pm_apc_string` | done | payload consumed, never executed. **`BEL` is ignored and ends nothing**, williams' row (426) and ctlseqs' "Pt need not be printable characters" (432-433, 2278-2279); it ended the string until 2026-10-09 (`a_bel_in_sos_pm_or_apc_is_ignored_and_only_st_ends_it`). STD 070 3.5.4.5 drops a GR byte's eighth bit in an APC or PM too. An `APC G`, a kitty graphics command, has a reader since 2026-10-16 and takes the rule (`Paint::str_put`); every other body is kept and dropped unread at the terminator |

~~`sos_pm_apc_string` is a correctness and security gap.~~ Resolved: SOS, PM
and APC payloads are consumed by the graph's `SosStr` node and never executed,
and a DCS body by `DcsStr`, which hands it only to the handlers the
`dcs_passthrough` row above names. Until 2026-10-10 this named a
`StringIgnore`, which `baud-core` does not have. Note the
specified limit, which is not a hole: `[[source: williams]]` says a bare `ESC`
*cancels* a control string, and so does a C1 control (its anywhere rule, lines
612-615), so the guarantee is precisely that a payload containing no ESC, and
under ISO 8859-1 no byte in `80-9F`, is inert. The C1 half holds in the
`dcs_*`, `osc_string` and `sos_pm_apc_string` states since 2026-10-09; see
the anywhere block. **And until 2026-10-09 a `BEL` broke it too**: one body
node served all five strings and ended each on `BEL`, so a DCS, SOS, PM or
APC payload holding one was not inert past it. Only an `OSC` ends on `BEL`
now, the one string `[[source: ctlseqs]]` lets it end.

baud adds a `Utf8` state, which `[[source: williams]]` does not have — that
parser predates UTF-8 and is defined over bytes. Keeping it is deliberate; the
note in `docs/research/notes/c-vt-parser.md` records why.

### `csi_entry` was folded into `csi_param`, and the fold was the bug

The two states differ in one transition, and it is the one that matters. In
`csi_entry` the bytes `3C-3F` (`<`, `=`, `>`, `?`) are **collected** as a private
marker; in `csi_param` those same bytes go to `csi_ignore`. A marker is legal
only in the position immediately after `CSI`.

Folded into a single state, baud accepted a marker anywhere in the parameter
string — and then *executed* the result, which is the dangerous direction:

* `CSI 2 ? l` is malformed and must be discarded. baud read it as DECRST 2,
  reset DECANM and **switched the terminal into VT52 mode** — a protocol change
  produced by a sequence the spec says to throw away.
* `CSI 1 ? J` is malformed and must be discarded. baud read it as DECSED and
  erased the screen.

`a_marker_after_a_dcs_parameter_is_ignored` and its CSI counterpart pin both. Splitting the state also
closed two smaller holes in the same arm:

* **C0 inside a control sequence.** `[[source: williams]]` gives `csi_param` an
  `execute` action on `00-17,19,1C-1F` that returns to the same state. baud had
  no C0 branch, so a stray NUL fell through to `csi_ignore` and silently
  cancelled an unrelated SGR. core2's pair of C0-inside-a-CSI tests pinned the
  rule and the fact that
  *execute* means executed, not skipped.
* **`<` and `=` were dropped silently.** baud recognised only `?` and `>`, so
  the other two private namespaces fell to `csi_ignore` and vanished without
  reaching dispatch — a `parsed` failure, invisible to `Term::unsupported`.
  `Marker` now carries all four. `Csi::parse` rejects `Lt` and `Equals` before
  the standard table, because falling through would read `CSI < c` as a primary
  DA and **answer a query in the wrong shape**.

Both feed paths share one definition of the rule (`Marker::from_private`), so
the fast path and the state machine cannot drift on it — the divergence class
this parser has had before.

### The "anywhere" block

`[[source: williams]]` has a set of transitions that fire *regardless of the
current state*, and baud had none of them:

> On the VT220, VT420 and VT500, the C0 controls CAN and SUB cancel any escape
> sequence, control sequence or control string in progress and return to ground
> state.

`1B` ESC does the same, starting a fresh sequence rather than ending in ground.

In `baud-core2`, four arms each wrote `if b.is_c0() { self.execute(b) }` — and
`is_c0()` is `b < 0x20`, which **includes all three**. So an ESC arriving
mid-sequence was executed and the parser stayed in the parameter string:
`CSI 1 ESC [ 2 m` lost its escape, dispatched a nonsense `CSI 1 [`, and printed
`2m` as text. That core handled them once, ahead of the state match.

**`baud-core` took half of that fix, and this table said `done` for both
halves until 2026-09-27.** Its graph's `one()` takes `CAN`/`SUB` ahead of the
node functions; `ESC` it left to them, and `escape()` and the four `csi_*`
functions sent it to `c0()`, which consumed it and stayed in the sequence --
the same `CSI 1 [` and `2m`, and `ESC ( ESC 0` designating DEC graphics. The
row's test was the mnemonic `ESC`, which every suite mentions, so nothing could
see it. Now `one()` takes `1B` too, in `Escape` and the four `CSI` nodes, as
`Act::EscStart`, which clears the half-parsed sequence:
`an_esc_abandons_every_sequence_and_keeps_its_meaning_elsewhere` gates the edge
from every node, and `baud-core/tests/esc_abandons.rs` the behaviour, one test
a node, at read sizes 1, 3, 7 and whole.

The states that decline are the ones that must. Inside a control string ESC is
the first half of ST, so the payload has to survive until the next byte decides
between *terminator* and *cancel*; those nodes implement that themselves. A
character cut mid-sequence becomes a replacement and re-dispatches the ESC from
ground. And VT52 has no `CSI`: its escape restarts on a second ESC, and a
direct address takes one as a coordinate, as xterm's does.

| status | |
|---|---|
| `18` CAN, `1A` SUB — cancel from any state, control strings included | done — but in a Sixel's body, where DEC's Sixel chapter has its own rule (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §9.1): `SUB` is a blank sixel and the image goes on, and `CAN` places what the Sixel received before it ends it, since 2026-10-19 (the `DCS ... q` row). With `[screen] substitute_mark` on, a `SUB` in ANSI mode also draws the error character (the row below) |
| `1B` ESC — abandon and begin a new sequence | done — since 2026-09-27; this row read `done` before and was not, see above |
| `80-9F` C1 under `ESC % @`, inside a control string — cancel it, then execute, or enter `dcs_entry`, `csi_entry`, `osc_string` or `sos_pm_apc_string`; `9C` ends it as ST | done — since 2026-10-09, from the `dcs_*`, `osc_string` and `sos_pm_apc_string` states; see below. Every C1 byte from every string node acts as its 7-bit `ESC Fe` form at every read split (`a_c1_in_any_control_string_acts_as_its_seven_bit_form_under_latin1`), an `OSC` title a C1 cancels is not set (`a_c1_inside_an_osc_acts_under_latin1_and_is_not_stored`), the table holds it exhaustively (`a_latin1_c1_leaves_every_control_string_and_a_utf8_one_stays`), and under UTF-8 the same bytes stay payload (`under_utf8_c1_bytes_in_a_string_stay_in_the_payload`) |
| `80-9F` C1 under `ESC % @`, inside an escape or control sequence — cancel it, then execute, or enter `dcs_entry`, `csi_entry`, `osc_string` or `sos_pm_apc_string`; `9C` goes to ground | done — since 2026-10-09, later the same day, from `escape`, `escape_intermediate` and the four `csi_*` states, and so from an `ESC` inside a control string, which hands its next byte to `escape`; see below. Every C1 byte from each of sixteen such states acts as its 7-bit `ESC Fe` twin at every read size and every split into two reads, and the twin as the control alone from ground (`a_c1_in_any_escape_or_control_sequence_acts_as_its_seven_bit_form_under_latin1`); `ESC 85` is a NEL, `ESC ( 84` an IND that designates nothing, and a `CSI` a C1 interrupts does not dispatch (`a_c1_after_esc_or_inside_a_csi_acts_under_latin1`); the table holds it exhaustively (`a_latin1_c1_cancels_every_escape_and_control_sequence_and_a_utf8_one_does_not`); and under UTF-8 the same bytes are no controls: in a `CSI` what they were, and after `ESC` the ground's, the UTF-8 row "a byte `80-FF` after `ESC`" (`under_utf8_a_c1_byte_in_a_sequence_is_what_it_was`) |
| C0 in `csi_entry` — execute and **stay**, without consuming the marker position | done — `CSI <BS> ? J` is still DECSED |
| C0 in `csi_intermediate` | done — this arm had no C0 branch, so `CSI ! <NUL> p` lost its DECSTR |
| C0 in `csi_param` — execute and **stay**, the parameters kept | done — `CSI 2 ; 5 CR H` runs the CR and then a CUP to 2;5, as `[[source: williams]]`'s `csi param` executes `00-17,19,1C-1F` and stays (315) and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.3 e goes on "as if the intervening control code had not been received", at every read size and every split into two reads (`a_c0_inside_the_parameters_runs_and_the_sequence_goes_on`, `tests/csi_decline.rs`). **Until 2026-10-12 the off-by-default `two-pass-csi` arm ran it as a CUP to 2;2**: its walk declined at the CR without clearing the parameters it had stored, and the graph pushed them again from the `[`. The same file holds `DEL`, a byte `A0-FF` under `ESC % @` and `80-FF` under UTF-8, which declined the same way; it tests that arm only when built with it |
| `7F` DEL in `csi_param` | done — ignored, and the sequence goes on, as `[[source: williams]]`'s `csi param` ignores it (315): `CSI 5 DEL C` moves the cursor as `CSI 5 C` does, under both encodings and at every read size (`a_del_in_csi_param_is_ignored_and_the_sequence_goes_on`) |
| `7F` DEL in `csi_entry` or `csi_intermediate` | done — ignored, and the sequence goes on, as `[[source: williams]]`'s `csi entry` and `csi intermediate` ignore it and stay (306, 328), so `CSI DEL 5 C` moves the cursor as `CSI 5 C` does, `CSI DEL ? 25 l` hides it, the marker still legal after the `DEL`, and `CSI ! DEL p` is a DECSTR. Until 2026-10-11 each node took `7F` to `Consume` into `csi_ignore`, which dispatched nothing. Under both encodings, at every read size and every split into two reads (`a_del_in_any_escape_or_control_sequence_is_ignored_and_the_sequence_goes_on`; the table, `a_del_in_an_escape_or_control_sequence_is_ignored_and_the_node_stays`) |
| `7F` DEL in ground, ANSI and VT52 | done — ignored: no cell and no cursor move, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-3 §3.4.2 says for a 94-character set in GL ("shall be ignored"), with `[[source: vt510-ch7]]` § 7.2.5 and `[[source: williams]]`'s ground. Until 2026-10-08 it drew U+FFFD, a decoding error for a byte that is well-formed in UTF-8 (`del_in_ground_is_ignored`, at every read size). **With a 96-character set in GL it is the set's character at 7/15** (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.3.1; `a_96_set_in_gl_draws_space_and_del`), since 2026-10-19; VT52 has no 96-character set |
| SUB additionally displaying the DEC error character `␦` | done — **off by default, and a setting draws it**, since 2026-10-23, and a second setting names the character, DEC's reversed question mark by default. `Term::set_substitute_mark`; the app's `[screen] substitute_mark`, `"off"` by default, and `substitute_character`, U+2426 by default (`baud_domain::DEC_ERROR_CHARACTER`, note AE's AE8.1), any character one cell wide under every width the terminal can measure by (`ErrorCharacter::of`), anything else said in the config's notice and the default used. With it on, a `SUB` writes the character as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` writes it, "placing a reversed question mark in the display at the Active Position" (p. 5-132) by `insert_or_replace_character()` (pp. 5-140-5-141): at the cursor, in the current rendition and DECSCA attribute, shifting the line under IRM, the cursor moving on and wrapping under DECAWM; it is a cluster of its own because `SUB` is a control (`[[source: uax29]]` GB4, GB5); in the ground and wherever it cancels an escape sequence, a control sequence, a single shift or a control string, note 2's "the Substitute control function to be executed instead", and after the U+FFFD of a character it cuts short. A Sixel body keeps its blank sixel (the `1A` row above), and a `SUB` after the body's `ESC` cancels that escape sequence and draws the mark. **VT52 mode draws nothing**: no DEC text read names a mode for SUB's display, and the VT102 guide settles neither reading, so the mark is drawn in ANSI mode only; xterm 411 draws it in VT52 mode at its default ID, practice. Off, the default departs from DEC's VT220 to VT500 and from xterm 411 at its default ID, a choice the project made (`a_sub_draws_the_mark_where_it_arrives_only_when_turned_on`, `the_mark_takes_the_rendition_protection_insert_and_wrap_of_a_character`, `the_mark_is_a_cluster_of_its_own`, `a_sub_inside_a_character_writes_the_replacement_then_the_mark`, `a_sixel_body_keeps_its_blank_sixel_and_its_escape_takes_the_mark`, `can_and_vt52_mode_draw_no_mark`, `the_mark_is_the_embedders_character_and_resets_keep_it`; on all three draw paths, `substitute_mark_gate` in `cells.rs`). Detail below |

**The C1 controls are the block's third rule, and baud takes it from every
ANSI state.** `[[source: williams]]` (lines 612-615): "All C1
controls cancel any escape sequence, control sequence or control string in
progress and are executed. Control functions special to this parser, i.e. DCS,
SOS, CSI, OSC, PM and APC, cause a transition to their appropriate states." In
UTF-8 these bytes are continuation bytes and the rule cannot apply (the
Encoding section below). Under `ESC % @` the string node ended only on `9C`
and stored the other thirty-one, so `85` landed in a window title and the NEL
never ran, and the DCS prefix sent all thirty-two, `9C` included, into the
body. `one()` now gives a C1 inside a string the edge it takes from ground,
and `9C` ends the string. It cancels as `ESC` does: williams uses the one verb
for all four and asks that "the 7-bit (ESC Fe) and 8-bit representations of
C1 controls should act in the same way" (534-537). A cancelled string is not
dispatched, by any of the four, and xterm's `sos_table` reaches `do_osc` only
from `CASE_ST` (`[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]` 7781-7820,
`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 5288-5320); whether it should keep what it has done,
as williams' `unhook` and its "does not undo these effects" suggest, is open.

The rule's other half came later the same day. In `escape` a C1 was dropped
and the escape ended without it, so `ESC 85` lost its NEL, and in the `csi_*`
states it was dropped and, from `csi_param`, the sequence carried on, so
`CSI 2 ; 85 H` moved the cursor. williams says it again of `csi entry` and
the states after it (534-537): "C1 controls will cancel the sequence and then
be executed." `one()` now gives a C1 in `Escape` and the four `CSI` nodes the
edge it takes from ground, as the `1B` row above cancels from the same five;
`9C` there takes its ground edge, the `ESC \` it stands for, since there is
no string to end and the diagram draws it to ground with no action (line
268). xterm agrees in every ANSI table: `esc_table`, `csi_table`, `scstable`,
`cigtable` and the rest take `84` to `CASE_IND` and `85` to `CASE_NEL` (for
`esc_table`, `[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]` 6157-6196). So every ANSI state a
Latin-1 C1 can arrive in acts on it: ground, the strings, the sequences, and
an `ESC` inside a string, which hands the byte on to `escape`. The UTF-8
continuation states cannot be reached under Latin-1, where every character
is one byte. VT52 is not williams' parser and is not taken here: a Latin-1 C1
in VT52 ground still takes its ANSI edge, note AE's D3, which waits on DEC's
text for that mode (xterm's `vt52_table` ignores C1).

**A bare ST is no function, and is counted nowhere.** `[[source: ecma48]]`
§ 8.3.143 defines ST only as "the closing delimiter of a control string", so
a `9C` that arrives with no string open, from ground or from a sequence it
cancels, closes nothing, and neither does an `ESC \` there. williams draws
`9C / (no action)` (line 268) and says "there is never any work to do for
9C" (972-973). baud does nothing and counts nothing for either; both fell to
`Esc::Unsupported` and were counted as a function baud lacks until 2026-10-09,
after the sequence half above (`a_bare_st_closes_nothing_and_is_counted_nowhere`).
An ST that closes a string is the string node's, and is unchanged. A `9C` under
`ESC % @` in VT52 ground takes its ANSI edge (note AE's D3) and so is uncounted
too; VT52 has no control strings for it to close.

**The basis this row gave was wrong, and what SUB displays is a choice the project made: nothing by default, the error character under a setting.**

`[[source: williams]]` records that "SUB will also display the error character, a
reversed question mark", describing VT220 through VT500 hardware, and
`[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` p. 6-4 says it of DEC's printers: "SUB in printable
text causes the error character (a reverse question mark) to be printed."
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` documents `SUB` nowhere as producing a glyph. baud cancels
and writes nothing.

This row said xterm "does not write one", and that baud follows xterm here for
the reason it does on DECAWM's default. xterm 411 does write one, under
conditions, read from its source and not run (note AF §5(a)6, D5). A ground SUB
reaches CASE_SUB through the ground table (`[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]`
`VTPrsTbl.c:188`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]` `charproc.c:3646-3678`), and what
`dotext` goes on to write, after the translation, is:

- **nothing at decTerminalID 52, in any build**: CASE_SUB writes only when
  `terminal_id >= 100` (`:3665`);
- **code 2 at the other IDs, in a build without `OPT_WIDE_CHARS`** (`:3670`),
  below 32, so `xtermCharSetOut` passes it unchanged
  (`[[source: xterm-charsets-c]]` `charsets.c:395-396`);
- in a build with `OPT_WIDE_CHARS`, where CASE_SUB picks 0x2426 when
  `terminal_id > 200` and 0x2592 otherwise (`:3667-3668`):
  - **U+2426 at decTerminalID 420, in a UTF-8 locale, with ASCII in GL and
    GR.** 420 is xterm's default (`[[source: xterm-ptyx-h]]` `ptyx.h:398-400`;
    `charproc.c:516`), a UTF-8 locale selects UTF-8 mode at xterm's defaults,
    and `resetCharsets` then puts ASCII in both, so no translation runs. With
    another set in GL or GR the translation runs and still leaves it: in UTF-8
    mode `xtermCharSetOut` skips any character above 255
    (`charsets.c:384-393`);
  - **`&` outside UTF-8 mode, with ASCII in GL.** At ID 420 `resetCharsets`
    puts ISO Latin-1 Supplemental in GR, so the character goes through
    `xtermCharSetOut`, which takes its low eight bits, 0x26
    (`charsets.c:366-370`), and leaves that byte with ASCII in GL;
  - **U+2592 at the VT100-level IDs** xterm keeps (100, 101, 102, 131 and
    132; `charproc.c:11020-11033`).

The pinned files do not say whether a given build has `OPT_WIDE_CHARS`:
`xterm.h` includes `xtermcfg.h`, which is not pinned, when `HAVE_CONFIG_H` is
defined, and then defines it as 0 if nothing has set it
(`[[source: xterm-xterm-h]]` `xterm.h:43-45`, `:298-300`). The write path past
`dotext`, and xterm's drawing, were not read.

So "baud follows xterm" was wrong as written: under those conditions xterm
writes a glyph, and following xterm, the DECAWM precedent, would not give
"writes nothing". The row stayed **out** until it was decided, on 2026-10-23: keep writing nothing by default, and add a setting that draws the
mark, and one naming the character, DEC's by default (the row). DEC STD 070
says where and how: SUB "is processed by placing a reversed question mark in
the display at the Active Position" (p. 5-132, PDF p. 387), its algorithm
`insert_or_replace_character(&sub)`, which writes "current rendition" and
"current_attribute", shifts the line under IRM and advances "the active
position after entering character" with autowrap's last-column flag (pp.
5-140-5-141, PDF pp. 395-396); xterm's CASE_SUB writes through `dotext()` as
any text. Which codepoint the error character is no longer waits: U+2426 SYMBOL FOR
SUBSTITUTE FORM TWO, note AE's AE8.1. It is `baud_domain::DEC_ERROR_CHARACTER`,
emitted beside the national tables by `scripts/gen-nrcs-tables.py`, and
`the_dec_error_character_is_symbol_for_substitute_form_two` holds it to its
name in `[[source: unicode-data]]`. Since 2026-10-12 it is drawn at the
reserved positions of DEC Supplemental and JIS-Katakana, where
`[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` §A.2 and §A.12 say "The error character is printed
when one of these reserved characters is received by the device" and
`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-3 §3.4.3 says "The character code 10/0 in 8-bit
environments is treated as an unassigned graphic character code, and will cause
the error character ... to be displayed on conforming devices", read for a
set's reserved positions by analogy (**inferred**) (their rows below), and for
`A0` with a 94-character set in GR (note AE's AE8). Those positions draw
U+2426 whatever `[screen] substitute_character` names: the setting is SUB's.
The rest of Q-UNDEF (DEC Turkish Supplemental's hatched cells, Greek NRC 7/9
and 7/10) is open.

The reason given before that one was that this was "a rendering decision made
where no renderer exists". That was wrong too, and its correction stands: baud
stores codepoints, not pixels, so writing a glyph for SUB would be storing a
*character*, which it does all day. Nothing about the renderer prevents it.

The asymmetry this section called settling -- a glyph an application did not
send is indistinguishable afterwards from text it did, while writing none loses
nothing it can observe, because the cancellation itself is the visible
behaviour and is implemented -- is an argument for that decision, not a basis
the row can rest on.

### Encoding: UTF-8, or ISO 8859-1, and 8-bit C1 controls only under the second

`[[source: williams]]`'s parser has 8-bit C1 controls throughout — `9B` enters
`csi_entry`, `9D` `osc_string`, `90` `dcs_entry`, `9C` is ST. baud has them
only under `ESC % @` (ISO 8859-1), and a reader comparing against the state
diagram will notice. In UTF-8 the absence is forced rather than chosen:

> a C1 control can be mistaken for badly-formed UTF-8 when the terminal runs in
> UTF-8 mode because C1 controls are valid continuation bytes of a UTF-8 encoded
> (multibyte) value […] it is not possible to use a C1 control obtained from
> decoding the UTF-8 text, because that would require reprocessing the data.
> — `[[source: ctlseqs]]`

`80-9F` cannot be both a control and a continuation byte. A terminal picks one,
and picking UTF-8 makes the choice for every byte at once. So:

| | status |
|---|---|
| UTF-8 decoding, including the `Utf8` parser state williams does not have | done |
| ill-formed UTF-8 — **one U+FFFD per maximal subpart** | done — since 2026-10-12, the practice of `[[source: unicode-ch3]]` 3.9.6, "U+FFFD Substitution of Maximal Subparts", which "the Unicode Standard does not require": "The maximal subpart at that offset is replaced by a single U+FFFD", a maximal subpart (D93b) being "the initial subsequence of a well-formed code unit sequence, or ... a subsequence of length one". Table 3-7 narrows only the second byte, after `E0`, `ED`, `F0` and `F4`, so a lead and a second byte outside its column are two subparts, and every byte of an overlong or a surrogate is one: `E0 80`, `ED BF`, `F0 80` and `F4 BF` write two U+FFFD, `ED A0 80` and `E0 80 80` three and `F4 90 80 80` four, as `String::from_utf8_lossy` does. Until then a lead and the continuations after it wrote one however many subparts they held. In the ground, after a control, after `ESC` and `ESC (`, in VT52, in a run of each encoded length (the vector and scalar decodes of a three-byte run both), in a cluster, at every read size and every split into two reads; a byte no sequence begins with (`C0`, `C1`, `F5-FF`) no longer continues a run, where `F8 A0 80 80` after a four-byte character decoded as U+20000. ISO 8859-1 is unchanged (`the_oracle_and_baud_give_tables_3_8_to_3_11`, Tables 3-8 to 3-11 as printed; `every_probe_writes_the_replacements_from_utf8_lossy_writes` and the `every_probe_*` tests, every one-to-four byte probe over Table 3-7's byte classes; `every_lead_meets_every_byte_after_it_in_the_ground` and `every_lead_meets_every_byte_after_it_in_a_three_byte_run`, every byte boundary; `a_byte_no_sequence_begins_with_never_continues_a_run`; `latin1_writes_no_replacement_for_any_probe`; `a_split_lead_waits_for_the_second_bytes_it_admits`, the table; `the_second_byte_table_is_table_3_7` and `a_valid_lead_decodes_to_a_character_iff_it_admits_its_second_byte`, the domain's tables) |
| bytes `A0-FF` under UTF-8 are UTF-8, never a right half, whatever G0-G3 hold | done — note AE's Q5(a), decided 2026-10-10 (AE5 part 1). `[[source: unicode18-ch3]]` C4 has a process interpret a coded character sequence "according to the character semantics established by this standard", D92 gives every UTF-8 byte sequence its scalar value or makes it ill-formed, and `[[source: ecma35]]` 9.1 gives a 7-bit code no GR area. xterm's mapping, decoded U+0080-U+00FF through GR and raw bytes past the decoder whenever GR is not ASCII (`[[source: xterm-ptydata-c]]` ptydata.c:91-97), which shows `C3 A9` as `Ã©` after `ESC * 0`, is practice not followed. Every U+00A0-U+00FF and every raw `A0-FF` after `ESC ) * +` with `0` or `A`, and after `ESC ( 0` and `ESC ) 0 SO`, at every read size (`gr_bytes_under_utf8_are_utf8_whatever_is_designated`, a guard, red under either perturbation). LS1R-LS3R under UTF-8 set the slot GR invokes and nothing more, state DECCIR reports (Q5's other half, 2026-10-19) |
| a byte `80-FF` after `ESC`, or after `ESC` and intermediates — **in UTF-8** | done — since 2026-10-11. The byte cancels the sequence, with no function, and is dispatched again from the ground after a control, whose decoder takes it as any byte there: `a ESC E0 A4 83 b` writes `a`, U+0903 in a cell of its own (`[[source: uax29]]` GB4) and `b`; a stray continuation, a disallowed lead byte (0xC0, 0xC1, 0xF5-0xFF) or a lead cut off writes one U+FFFD per maximal subpart (the row above) and the byte that cannot continue it is the ground's, so `ESC A4 83 b` writes two U+FFFD and `b` and `ESC F5 b` one and `b`; a character a read cuts completes on the next. Until then `escape` and `escape_intermediate` dropped the lead with the sequence and each continuation of the well-formed U+0903 wrote a U+FFFD, which `[[source: unicode-ch3]]` 3.9.5 rules out when the stream is read as one UTF-8 conversion: a converter "must not consume the successor bytes as part of the ill-formed subsequence whenever those successor bytes themselves constitute part of a well-formed UTF-8 code unit subsequence"; read with the bytes inside a sequence belonging to the sequence, 3.9.5 says nothing of them. That the character is written rather than dropped with the sequence no text read decides: `[[source: williams]]` leaves the recovery "not specified" (128-129), `[[source: ecma35]]` NOTE 41 says recovery "is not covered by this Standard", and `[[source: ecma48]]` Annex D leaves error recovery to implementors; none of xterm, kitty, vte, foot, wezterm and ghostty prints it. The `ESC` inside a control string hands its byte to `escape`, so there the byte cancels the string too (`a_character_after_esc_is_written_and_the_escape_is_cancelled`, the decision's rows at every read size and every split into two reads, with its controls in `the_controls_for_a_character_after_esc`; `every_byte_after_esc_or_an_intermediate_is_the_grounds_after_a_control`, every `80-FF` bare, completed, truncated and followed by `80` or `BF`, after `ESC`, `ESC (`, `ESC $ (` and a string's `ESC`, against the same bytes after a control and bounded by `String::from_utf8_lossy`; `under_utf8_a_byte_80_ff_after_esc_is_the_grounds_after_a_control`, the table). The `csi_*` states and VT52's `ESC` are the row below |
| a byte `80-FF` inside a control sequence, in `csi entry`, `csi param`, `csi intermediate` and `csi ignore`, or after a VT52 `ESC` — **in UTF-8** | done — since 2026-10-14: the byte cancels the sequence, with no function, and is dispatched again from the ground after a control, as after `ESC` (the row above), so `a CSI 1 E0 A4 83 b` writes `a`, U+0903 in a cell of its own and `b`, `a CSI 5 E0 A4 83 C` writes the `C` and moves nothing, `CSI A4 83 b` writes two U+FFFD and `b`, and the VT52 `ESC` the same. Until then the four `CSI` rows dropped the byte, `csi entry` and `csi intermediate` going to `csi ignore` and the other two staying, so `a CSI 1 E0 A4 83 b` ran REP and wrote the `a` twice and `a CSI E0 A4 83 b` took the `b` as an ignored final, and `Vt52Escape` handed the lead to `Vt52::parse`, which names no such byte. `DEL` keeps its row in each `CSI` state. Under ISO 8859-1 nothing moves: `80-9F` are C1 controls and `A0-FF` GL bytes in a sequence, as they were, and after a VT52 `ESC` a high byte waits on note AE's D3 (`a_character_inside_a_csi_is_written_and_the_csi_is_cancelled`, the decision's rows at every read size and every split into two reads; `every_byte_inside_a_csi_or_after_a_vt52_esc_is_the_grounds_after_a_control`, every `80-FF` bare, completed, truncated and followed by `80` or `BF`, after `CSI`, `CSI 1`, `CSI SP`, `CSI 1 <` and a VT52 `ESC`, against the same bytes after a `NUL` and bounded by `String::from_utf8_lossy`; `under_utf8_a_byte_80_ff_inside_a_csi_or_after_a_vt52_esc_is_the_grounds_after_a_control`, the table; `under_utf8_a_byte_80_ff_inside_the_parameters_cancels_the_sequence`; the property atoms `csi-entry-lead`, `csi-param-lead`, `csi-inter-lead` and `csi-ignore-cont`). Not covered: the DCS prefix nodes, where a lead byte still falls into the body, and VT52's `ESC Y`, which takes any two bytes as its coordinates |
| 7-bit `ESC Fe` forms `ESC P`, `ESC [`, `ESC \`, `ESC ]`, `ESC X`, `ESC ^`, `ESC _` | done — `[[source: ecma48]]` § 5.3 specifies these as the representation of C1 in a 7-bit environment, so this *is* the C1 interface, not a substitute for one. `ESC \` is ST, which § 8.3.143 defines only as the closing delimiter of a control string; with none open it closes nothing and is counted nowhere, as `9C` is under `ESC % @` (`a_bare_st_closes_nothing_and_is_counted_nowhere`). Until 2026-10-09 both were counted as unsupported |
| 8-bit C1 introducers `90`, `9B`, `9C`, `9D`, `98`, `9E`, `9F` — **in UTF-8** | **out** — they are continuation bytes there; a stray one is a decode error and renders U+FFFD. Follows ghostty; diverges from alacritty and wezterm |
| the same introducers under `ESC % @` (ISO 8859-1) | done — no multi-byte sequence exists for them to be part of, so `9B` is unambiguously CSI. From inside a control string too, which each cancels (`a_c1_in_any_control_string_acts_as_its_seven_bit_form_under_latin1`, and the anywhere block above); until 2026-10-09 the string stored them and only `9C` ended it. And from inside an escape or control sequence, which each cancels (`a_c1_in_any_escape_or_control_sequence_acts_as_its_seven_bit_form_under_latin1`); until the same day `escape` dropped them and the `csi_*` states consumed them |
| 8-bit `84` IND, `85` NEL, `88` HTS, `8A` VTS, `8D` RI under `ESC % @` | done — each acts as its 7-bit form, `[[source: ctlseqs]]` pairing the two, at every read split (`eight_bit_ind_nel_hts_and_ri_match_their_seven_bit_forms`; `8A` since 2026-10-19, `eight_bit_vts_under_latin1_sets_a_line_stop`), and none inherits the last escape's intermediate (`a_c1_control_does_not_inherit_the_last_escapes_intermediate`). Until 2026-10-08 all four were counted as unsupported, and `84` after `ESC ( 0` replaced G0. `8E` and `8F` map to `ESC N` and `ESC O`, counted as those are until note AE's single shifts are built |
| character width from East Asian Width + general category, generated from pinned Unicode data | done — `AmbiguousWidth` is embedder policy, `[[source: uax11]]` § 5 |
| UAX #29 extended grapheme cluster segmentation | done — `ClusterBreak`, and all **766** cases of Unicode's own conformance suite pass |
| combining marks stored against their base cell | done — `Term::marks`, in `ClusterStore`, and two bounds, both stated. **What the store holds: 1024 clusters**, since 2026-10-13, with eight probes, a cluster's slot named from the cell's `Content` above U+10FFFF, and, **once all 1024 slots are used, a sweep of the live cells** -- both screens, the history and the rows past a narrower edge -- that frees every slot no cell names, at most once every 64 interns that wanted a slot, counted from the last sweep, so a store a sweep left with fewer than 64 slots free waits 64 interns from it whatever its cells name by then. The store **refuses** rather than evicts, a refused cluster keeping its base and counted in `marks_dropped`: a cluster whose eight slots all hold others is refused while slots elsewhere are free, so refusals begin long before 1024 are on cells (in a 40x10 window holding 500 one-mark clusters in its history and on its screen, 3,059 of 4,000 more were refused, with one sweep). On note AD's texts the store never fills, so nothing sweeps: the Hindi article refuses 349 points and the session 6,296, and on the 17 dumps no cell the 63-slot store drew right is drawn wrong, which a generated payload holds in a test (`every_cluster_the_base_kept_whole_is_kept_whole`). **What a cluster holds: a base and eight continuations** (`ClusterSlot::CAPACITY`), so an eleven-codepoint RGI family loses its tail -- an open departure from D60's "any number of nonspacing marks", which stays at eight. Genesis 1:1, 1:1-2 and 1:1-5 lose 0, 0 and 0 points where the 63-slot store, freed only by `RIS`, lost 0, 10 and 45 (`every_point_of_genesis_1_1_to_5_is_kept`, `a_session_past_the_store_sweeps_once_the_store_is_full`, `the_history_the_other_screen_and_the_rows_outside_a_region_keep_theirs`, `a_full_store_of_live_and_dead_clusters_frees_only_the_dead`). Since 2026-10-23 **a cluster that is a prefix of a sequence the Unicode Standard names takes no slot** -- UTS #51's RGI emoji sequences and the standardized emoji variation sequences (`[[source: emoji17-sequences]]`, `[[source: emoji17-zwj-sequences]]`, `[[source: emoji-variation-sequences]]`), up to a base and eight continuations: the cell names a node of a trie built from the pinned files, so of 3,295 of them on two screens at once 3,200 read back whole and the 95 longer than a base and eight continuations keep their first nine, as one alone does, where the store left 2,411 short, and a cluster that goes on past one is interned whole (`every_named_sequence_is_kept_however_many_are_on_the_screen`, `a_cluster_that_goes_on_past_a_named_sequence_keeps_every_codepoint`, `every_prefix_of_a_named_sequence_is_one_step`, `nothing_but_a_listed_prefix_is_a_step`). They used to be **discarded**. This row said "evicting", `baud-core2`'s table, until 2026-10-10 |
| one cluster per cell — ZWJ sequences, flags and Indic conjuncts as a single cluster | done — `ClusterBreak` consulted in `write.rs`'s `glyph`; continuations attach to the base cell, after printable ASCII too. Until 2026-10-10 a continuation that is not zero-width took a cell of its own there, a SpacingMark (GB9a) or an Extend with a width such as `U+09BE` or `U+1F3FF` (GB9), because `Paint::piece` and `Paint::blit` told the machine nothing came before the run; they tell it `ClusterBreak::after_ascii_run` now. And a control ends the cluster before it (GB4, GB5) **for a continuation that takes a column**: every edge that performs a C0 control but `CR`, `NUL` and `DEL` included, or ends an escape sequence, a control sequence (an SGR and a malformed one that `csi ignore` swallows included) or a control string, leads to `Node::AfterControl`, whose row tells the machine (`Act::EndCluster`) before the next character it is asked about; `CR` leaves the cursor at column 0, where `glyph` restarts the machine, or under DECOM at the left margin, where its arm tells the machine itself (since 2026-10-13, `cr_and_the_line_starts_under_origin_mode_go_to_the_left_margin`). Before, such a continuation after a line feed, a tab, a cursor move or an erase was dropped on an empty cell or joined a letter the cursor had stepped back over (`a_control_function_ends_the_cluster_before_it`, `every_way_out_of_a_control_leads_to_the_ground_after_a_control`). A `U+FFFD` the decoder writes for an ill-formed sequence is the character U+FFFD to the machine, as a literal `EF BF BD` is: class Other, so GB9a joins a SpacingMark to it and GB11 and GB12/13 do not reach across it, after a control too (`a_replacement_character_is_class_other_to_the_cluster_after_it`), and GB9b joins it to a Prepend before it, its width the literal's, East Asian Width A, as the literal U+FFFD is. Since 2026-10-13; until then it was written without asking the machine and always broke before it, so `a U+0600 80 Z` took three cells where `a U+0600 EF BF BD Z` takes two (`a_replacement_for_ill_formed_bytes_clusters_as_a_literal_one`, 25 characters before it, 7 after and 5 spellings of the bytes at every read size and every split into two reads, in the ANSI and VT52 grounds and under both ambiguous widths; `a_control_ends_the_cluster_before_a_replacement_for_ill_formed_bytes`; `a_replacement_that_attaches_is_counted`, `Term::replacements` counting it whether it takes a cell or attaches and not counting a literal one). `every_line_of_the_suite_clusters_through_the_terminal` feeds every line of `[[source: gbtest]]` through `Term` at every read size, its 211 with NUL, LF and CR as the terminal receives them, and pins what still departs. **A combining mark after a control takes a cell of its own**, since 2026-10-23: GB4/GB5 give it a cluster of its own, a defective combining character sequence, "a sequence of combining characters [that] appears at the start of a string or follows a control or format character" (D57, `[[source: unicode18-ch3]]`), and the core stores the mark alone in the cursor's cell (`Content::DEFECTIVE`, D52's "as if it were a base character"), which every render path draws on a no-break space, TUS § 5.13's "rendered as if they had a no-break space as a base character" (`[[source: unicode18-ch5]]`), and which a copy, a print, a search and DECRQCRA's full codes read as the mark alone, and a double click as a character of the word it is in (`a_mark_stored_alone_is_searched_as_the_mark_and_not_as_a_space`, `a_mark_stored_alone_is_part_of_its_word`; a search read it as a space and a word stopped at it until the same day); what follows it in the cluster joins it, and `REP` repeats it as one, rather than attaching to the cell before. **A default-ignorable one** (a ZWJ, a variation selector, a tag) draws nothing and has no cell to take, so it is dropped and counted in `marks_dropped`, and a mark after it starts its own cluster: `U+2764 ESC[m U+FE0F` no longer turns the heart into its emoji (the join departs from GB4/GB5 and no answer decided it). Until then each went to the character the last write put down, the cell before the cursor or the one a write in the last column pinned, or was dropped where that cell was empty (`a_combining_mark_after_a_control_takes_a_cell_of_its_own`, `what_follows_a_defective_mark_joins_it`, `a_default_ignorable_after_a_control_is_dropped`, `rep_repeats_a_defective_mark_as_a_cell_of_its_own`, `a_defective_mark_is_summed_and_printed_as_the_mark`, `a_mark_after_a_control_draws_on_a_no_break_space_in_its_own_cell`, `a_mark_after_a_control_copies_as_the_mark`; `a_mark_after_a_control_goes_where_it_goes_with_autowrap_set` and its guards in `tests/margin_pin.rs` now hold that it lands where a character would). A program that measures such a mark with `wcwidth` (0) finds the text after it a column right of where it expected; a mark straight after its base is unchanged. A zero-width codepoint of class Control, such as U+200B, and a Prepend still join the cell before the cursor. Three open departures remain: **REP's copy** never joins what came before it, `CSI b` being a control, where the text it expands to would (note AB, Q24); a Prepend joins the cell before it and printable ASCII after it takes its own (GB9b; note AB §7.1); and a cluster the store refuses loses its tail (note AB §7.2). Whether `NUL` and `DEL` end the cluster at all is open too (note AB, Q24) |
| `Emoji_Presentation=Yes` is two columns | done — regional indicators are EAW `N`, so a flag measured from East Asian Width alone is half-width |
| DECRQM `?2027` — grapheme clustering | done — answers `3`, permanently set, and it is true: a `U+FE0F` after a base `[[source: emoji-variation-sequences]]` lists widens it to two cells (keycap bases excepted); configured to one cell it answers `4`; no sequence changes it |
| `ESC % G` — select UTF-8, ISO 2022 | done — switches the live input encoding back to UTF-8 after `ESC % @`, and changes nothing while UTF-8 is live. This row said "satisfied; baud already decodes UTF-8", from before `ESC % @` was built, until 2026-10-10 |
| `ESC % @` — select ISO 8859-1 | done — switches the live input encoding, and with it what a key sends and, since 2026-10-21, what a paste sends: ISO 8859-1, xterm's conversion of a selection outside UTF-8, with no byte 0x80 to 0x9F, which the child would read as a C1 control (`a_latin1_paste_is_latin1_with_no_c1_byte`; the input table's `?2004` row). **Until `ESC % G` or `RIS`**: since 2026-10-24 a full reset returns the input to UTF-8, the state baud is made operational in (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105), the byte after it in the same read included, and the app sets the pty's `IUTF8` again (`a_full_reset_returns_the_input_to_utf8`, `a_title_after_a_full_reset_in_the_same_read_is_utf8`, `a_full_reset_sets_iutf8_again`); `DECSTR` keeps it (`a_soft_reset_keeps_the_input_encoding`). xterm's `ReallyReset` keeps its UTF-8 mode, a practice not followed |
| `CSI ? 1020 $ p` — DECRQM on xterm's `utf8` resource | done — mirrors the **live** encoding, `1` or `2`, as xterm does |
| `CSI ? 1021 $ p` — DECRQM on xterm's `cjkWidth` resource | done — since 2026-10-15, answers the configured ambiguous-width policy, `1` under `wide` and `2` under `narrow`, as xterm answers it with `MdBool` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5845-5853), not `3` or `4`. The policy is the app's `[text] ambiguous_width`, read at launch and again when the config is saved; `RIS` and `DECSTR` keep it (`decrqm_1021_reports_the_ambiguous_width_policy`). Read-only in `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: DECSET and DECRST of it change nothing and are counted in `unsupported_param`, and XTSAVE keeps no slot, as xterm's `dpmodes` and `savemodes` skip it (`decset_of_the_width_resources_changes_nothing_and_is_counted`, `a_mode_decset_does_not_act_on_is_counted_by_both`). Under `wide` a box-drawing or block character takes two cells and, since 2026-10-25, is drawn across both on every draw path: its lines run through the second cell, its verticals and corners stay at the first cell's centre at the one-cell weight, a block is its fraction of both cells (note V, V14 design step 4; `a_wide_box_line_runs_through_both_cells_and_joins_its_neighbours`, `a_wide_block_fills_both_cells`, `ambiguous_box_gate` in `cells.rs`) |
| `CSI ? 1022 $ p` — DECRQM on xterm's `emojiWidth` resource | done — answers `0`, not recognized: baud has no `emojiWidth` switch, and its `Vs16Width` neither narrows on `U+FE0E` nor widens a bare listed emoji, so `1` and `2` would each claim something false (`decrqm_1023_answers_set_and_1022_is_not_recognized`) |
| `CSI ? 1023 $ p` — DECRQM on xterm's `privateWidth` resource | done — answers `1`: Private Use Area characters (`E000..F8FF`, `F0000..FFFFD`, `100000..10FFFD`) take one column under either ambiguous-width policy, since 2026-10-15, as xterm's default `privateWidth` keeps them (`[[source: xterm-wcwidth-c]]` 887-888). `[[source: uax11]]` § 6.1 gives them `A` by default and § 2 invites a terminal to tailor; the embedded icon face is sized to one cell (`private_use_takes_one_column_under_either_policy`, at every read size and every split into two reads) |
| `ESC SP F` — S7C1T, send 7-bit controls | done — the default |
| `ESC SP G` — S8C1T, send 8-bit controls | done — replies switch to `9B` / `90` / `9C`, and keys to `9B` / `8F` (`a_key_follows_s8c1t_as_a_reply_does`). At Level 1 it is counted and changes nothing (the DECSCL row); `RIS` goes back to seven-bit since 2026-10-19, as DEC STD 070's Device Initialization resets the C1 transmission |
| `ESC SP L`, `ESC SP M`, `ESC SP N` — announce ISO 4873 Level 1, 2 or 3 | done — since 2026-10-19, at Levels 3 and 4: L and M put ASCII in G0 and ISO Latin-1 Supplemental in G1 and invoke G0 into GL and G1 into GR, N puts ASCII in G0 and invokes it into GL (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-3 §3.8.1); at Levels 1 and 2, counted: 070's algorithm acts there only with a Level 2 eight-bit extension, and baud implements none (`the_announcers_designate_at_level_three_and_above`). xterm sets an ANSI level and designates nothing, a departure not followed |
| GR bytes `A0-FF` treated as GL `20-7F` inside a sequence — **in UTF-8** | **out** — they are UTF-8 lead and continuation bytes. This row said `out` for both encodings until 2026-10-10, on a reason that holds under UTF-8 alone |
| the same under `ESC % @` (ISO 8859-1) | done — **every GR byte in an ANSI escape or control sequence is read as its 7-bit byte**, as `[[source: williams]]` treats GR as GL "in all states" (462) and in DEC's notes for both (760, 847, 900), `[[source: ecma48]]` §9 names "Parameter Bytes, Intermediate Bytes, and Final Bytes of a control sequence", `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5 calls its DCS rule "the same way 8-bit graphic characters are handled within control sequences", and 3.5.2 f and 3.5.3 g say it of escape and control sequences (PDF pp. 127, 129); `[[source: ecma35]]` 13.1 bars a sender from GR in an escape sequence and leaves its reception open (NOTE 41); and xterm's `csi_table` gives `A0-FF` the cases of `20-7F` (`[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]` 679-798). In a DCS prefix since 2026-10-10, `dcs_entry`, `dcs_param` and `dcs_intermediate` (`dcs_entry` above); in `escape`, `escape_intermediate`, the four `csi_*` states and the `ESC` inside a control string since 2026-10-11 (VT52's escape and direct address are note AE's D3, as the C1 rows' are), so `ESC A8 B0` designates DEC Special Graphics, `CSI BF B2 B5 EC` hides the cursor, `ESC B7` is a DECSC, `ESC DB` opens a `CSI`, `ESC DD` an `OSC`, `ESC DC` is the `ST` that ends a string, and a GR final ends `csi_ignore`. Until then `escape` dropped a GR byte and ended the sequence, `csi_entry` and `csi_intermediate` ignored the sequence, `csi_param` dropped the byte, so `CSI 1 B2 m` was `CSI 1 m`, and `csi_ignore` waited for a 7-bit final. `A0` is an intermediate as `20` is and `FF` is ignored as `7F` is, as in the prefix. The graph reads the byte through `InputEncoding::gr_as_gl`, and the actions that read it back read it so too: `Marker::from_private` names `BC-BF`, `Act::Inter` stores it 7-bit, the one path a GR intermediate takes, and `EscFinal` and `CsiFinal` clear its eighth bit in their arms (`StreamByte::seven_bit`), `StrStart` names `DD` an `OSC`, and `Digit`'s table gives a GR digit its GL digit's row. Masked in the intermediate's readers instead, as first built, it cost about twelve instructions a non-SGR `CSI` in `csi_final`, and vim read about 2% slower against the parent in two sessions of three; in `Act::Inter` the instructions retired a rep are the split's on vim and cat, the split's or 0.2% fewer on btop, and 0.3-0.5% fewer on asciiquarium (`a_gr_byte_in_any_escape_or_control_sequence_acts_as_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`, every `A0-FF` from sixteen states at every read size and every split, against its GL twin; `each_gr_byte_in_a_sequence_does_what_its_seven_bit_byte_does_under_latin1`, one case per mechanism with positive controls; `a_gr_byte_in_an_escape_or_control_sequence_takes_its_seven_bit_edge_under_latin1`, the table; under UTF-8 no byte is read as GL, and after `ESC` each is the ground's, `under_utf8_a_gr_byte_in_a_sequence_is_what_it_was`). **And in an `OSC` body**, a title's and an `OSC 52`'s among them: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5, "GR (8-bit) graphic characters in APC, OSC, and PM control strings will be treated as their 7-bit equivalent (the eighth bit will be ignored)", and ECMA-48 §9 gives "the contents of the command string" the same rule, so `ESC ] B0 BB title BEL` sets the title `title` (`a_gr_byte_in_an_osc_body_is_its_seven_bit_byte_under_latin1`, six framings; `an_osc_52_spelled_in_gr_is_the_one_spelled_in_gl_under_latin1`). An `APC` or `PM` body no handler reads (`sos_pm_apc_string`). A Sixel body's GR bytes are read as GL since 2026-10-13, and a DECRQSS `Pt`'s are the open call `dcs_passthrough` names. |
| ISO 8859-1 input, switched by `ESC % @` / `ESC % G` | done — `InputEncoding`; one byte, one character, no decoder state |
| encoding detection | done — `InputEncoding::detect` **reports**; it never switches the terminal, and returns `None` when the sample cannot decide |
| Translating the national replacement sets | **partial** — 14 sets, 164 substitutions: 163 from `[[source: xterm-charsets]]`, cited as xterm's, and Turkish 2/1, U+0131, typed from `[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` Figure A-26, which xterm's table lacks. Greek 7/9 and 7/10 wait on note AF's Q-UNDEF. The ten VT220 sets are held to DEC's own tables (`each_vt220_national_table_is_decs`). A cell keeps the codepoint its set translated the byte to when it was written, so designating another set changes nothing already on the screen; this said "the run records which set it was written under so promotion cannot change it", a mechanism that was `baud-core2`'s. **They act whenever designated, in every mode**, since 2026-10-24 by note AF's Q-DECNRCM (this keeps `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms; it departs from 070-3 §3.6.4.4 read as a gate): STD 070's SCS algorithm designates whatever its character set table matches with no mode test (p. 5-121), its note 5 lets a workstation emulator "recognize all NRC sets regardless of the current keyboard dialect" (p. 5-118), and its DECNRCM "is ignored when the North American or Dutch Keyboard Dialect is selected" (note 3, p. 5-126), baud having no dialect setting (the `?42` row). The VT220 and VT420 manuals gate the sets on national mode (`[[source: dec-special-graphics]]` §2.4.3, `[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]` §4.6.19 and Table 4-4's note, `[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 27), as does xterm at designation (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` `charproc.c:1929`): the product manuals' and xterm's practice, not followed. Since 2026-10-22 the sets designate at Level 1 too, into G0 and G1, as the DECSCL row reads it, where p. 5-119 enters the NRCS table only at Level 2 or above. This row's gap from 2026-10-12 to 2026-10-24 was that question; Greek 7/9 and 7/10 keep it **partial** |

`S7C1T`/`S8C1T` are about what the terminal **sends**, and both are implemented:
"this tells the terminal to send C1 control characters as 8-bit sequences, e.g.,
its responses to queries".

**Sending and accepting are different axes, and conflating them is a mistake
this document made first.** S8C1T was refused here on the grounds that emitting
`9B` would make baud's own output invalid UTF-8. That reasoning does not hold: a
reply is not UTF-8 text, it is a control stream the application asked for by
sending S8C1T, and an application that asks is prepared to read it. baud still
cannot *parse* an 8-bit introducer in UTF-8 — that half is forced by UTF-8 and
stands — and under `ESC % @` it parses all of them (the rows above); there was
never a reason it could not *emit* one. `C1Transmit` carries the
choice and CSI, DCS and ST introducers all follow it, a key's `CSI` and `SS3`
included: xterm writes both through the `unparseseq` its replies use
(`[[source: xterm-input]]`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]` charproc.c 9356-9373).

**`?1020` mirrors the live encoding, and that is a correction.** It answered
`3` — permanently set — for as long as `ESC % @` was refused, which was true
then. Implementing the switch made it false, so it moved in the same commit.
A mode report that outlives the fact it reported is precisely the failure this
document refuses elsewhere, and it is easiest to create by fixing the thing the
report describes and leaving the report behind. xterm answers `MdBool` from the
live `screen->utf8_mode` for the same reason.

dtterm's `is2` opens with `ESC SP F`, which is how the conformance fixtures
caught the first version of this: counting S7C1T as unsupported claimed baud had
refused something it already did.

> **~~Open decision, under active research; the survey will land in
> `docs/research/notes/`.~~** It landed: `notes/g-text-encoding.md`, which this
> file already cites below. Its § 3 table surveys seven terminals, and the
> `ESC % G` / `ESC % @` column is the answer — **xterm supports the selectors,
> gated on `wide_chars`; kitty, ghostty, alacritty, wezterm, foot and Windows
> Terminal have no entry at all.** kitty says why in the same table: "there is
> no actual advantage… now that UTF-8 encoding is universal".
>
> So baud, honouring both selectors, matches xterm and exceeds every other
> terminal surveyed. **No further encoding is indicated by the evidence**, and
> adding one would put baud alone in a dimension no application uses: a program
> emitting Shift-JIS converts to UTF-8 itself, because every terminal it might
> run in requires that.
>
> `Encoding` in `baud-domain/src/store.rs` remains the extension point, and its
> `is_byte_per_column` predicate is still written around "fixed-width
> single-byte encoding" rather than around ASCII — so if a user ever does ask,
> the shape is an embedder-supplied 256-entry table, which covers every ISO
> 8859 part, the Windows code pages and KOI8 with one mechanism. That is a
> different thing from a protocol feature and would not be selected by an
> escape sequence, because none exists to select it.

**~~Grapheme clustering is out, and it is the largest thing on this page that
is.~~** It is done, and this paragraph contradicted the two below it for as long
as they have existed — the opening said `U+1F469 U+200D U+1F4BB` gets **four**
columns and the closing said two. The closing is right.

`[[source: uax29]]` defines the extended grapheme cluster, the unit a *user*
would call a character. `Grapheme::is_break` implements it against the generated
break-property tables, including `InCB` for Indic conjuncts and
`Extended_Pictographic` for emoji sequences, and `tests/encoding/graphemes.rs`
runs every case of `GraphemeBreakTest.txt`.

The stale half is left visible rather than deleted because of what it was:
a *correct description of the old behaviour* that outlived it. Nothing flagged
it — `no_row_is_unfinished` reads table rows, not prose, so a paragraph can
claim the opposite of the paragraph after it indefinitely.

**Combining marks used to be discarded outright.** `baud-core2`'s `put_cp`
returned early on `Width::ZERO`, so `e` + `U+0301` stored `e` and the accent was
gone — not mis-measured, *absent*, and unrecoverable by a renderer, a selection
or a copy. A terminal must not lose a character it was sent. They are stored
now, in `ClusterStore`: **keyed on the cluster's own content**, with its index
riding in the cell's `Content`, above U+10FFFF, where the base it replaces is
kept in the slot, so it moves with the cell through every rotation, reflow
and screen switch. It rode in six bits of `CellFlags` until 2026-10-14. ~~Keyed on the cell they attach to, in a
table that evicts on collision~~ was `baud-core2`'s design, and one `viewport()`
