call made it lose a cell's marks. It is bounded at 1024 clusters
and eight continuations a cluster, sweeps out the clusters no cell names once
all 1024 slots are used, and **refuses** rather than evicts a cluster whose
eight slots hold others, because a wrong cluster puts somebody else's accent on your
character; repeats share a slot. `Term::cluster_marks` is what a
renderer reads, and `marks_dropped` counts the refusals.

**And the same table answers full cluster semantics, which needed no cell bits
at all.** The reasoning that said otherwise was looking at the wrong constraint:
a continuation does not need the *cell* to identify its cluster, it needs
somewhere to live, and the marks table already is that. `write.rs`'s `glyph`
asks `ClusterBreak` whether a codepoint continues what came before it, and a
continuation attaches to the base cell instead of taking one of its own. So
`U+1F469 U+200D U+1F4BB` is two columns rather than four, and a flag is two
rather than four.

One thing that fell out of it: **regional indicators are East Asian Width `N`**
— one column — while carrying `Emoji_Presentation=Yes`. A flag measured from
EAW alone is half as wide as every terminal draws it, and the fix is a property
lookup rather than a special case. This is the question the deleted
`EmojiWidth` config was gesturing at, answerable now only because the cluster's
base is known.

**The segmentation half is done and measured, and so is the storage.**
`ClusterBreak` implements the boundary rules and passes all 766 cases of
`GraphemeBreakTest.txt`, which is Unicode's own suite written by the people who
wrote the rules. So the question is no longer "can baud find cluster
boundaries" but "what would it cost to act on them", and half of that now has a
number:

| corpus | width only | + UAX #29 breaks | cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| ASCII | 1119.6 Mcp/s | 338.3 | −70% |
| Latin + combining | 509.4 | 128.0 | −75% |
| CJK | 168.8 | 56.6 | −66% |
| emoji ZWJ, flags, Indic conjuncts | 156.0 | 50.1 | −68% |

Read the ASCII row with care: it measures a path ingest never takes. Printable
ASCII is blitted by the run scanner and never becomes a `Codepoint` at all, so
segmentation would cost nothing on it. The number that matters is CJK — **169 to
57 Mcp/s, about 3x on the non-ASCII decode path**, which at three bytes per
codepoint is roughly 500 MB/s down to 170.

That is a real cost and not a disqualifying one. **The table priced
`baud-domain`'s boundary machine alone**, in a bench deleted with `baud-core2`
and before `ClusterBreak` became a transition table, so it is kept as the
ordering it was measured for rather than as a live number. ~~What still has no number is
storage~~ -- it has one: `ClusterStore` is the side table this paragraph
described, its index in the six `CellFlags` bits that were spare, and it costs
**about 9%** on cat-cjk and less-cjk and **about 2.5%** on vim-unicode, the
three recordings that carry clusters, and nothing measurable on the rest. It is no longer a blocker. The bound it was built with, 63 distinct
clusters freed only by `RIS`, became 1024 slots on 2026-10-14, with a sweep of the dead once all are used.

`docs/research/notes/g-text-encoding.md` § 4.4 sets out where the bytes would
come from and § 4.5 why there is no consensus to conform to: mode 2027 is a
draft, kitty's text-sizing protocol solves an overlapping problem differently,
and terminals disagree today.

DECRQM `?2027` therefore answers **`3`**, permanently set, which is what
`decrqm_reports_grapheme_clustering_as_permanent` pins. It answered `0` — not
recognized — for as long as clustering was unimplemented, and that was the
honest answer then: `2` would have told an application the mode existed and was
merely off, whereupon it would set it and believe it took effect. The row moved
when the implementation did, which is the only order that keeps the answer
true.

**Until 2026-10-01 the `3` was half false.** Mode 2027's text is about width
as well as clustering, and a text-default emoji followed by `U+FE0F` stayed
one cell. It is two cells now -- in place when the row has room and moved whole
to the next row at the right margin, as ghostty does. `vs16_width.rs` pins
it. The answer stays permanent rather than resettable because bubbletea v2
sends `?2027l` on exit, behind the shell.

**ISO 8859-1 is honoured; the rest of the legacy charsets are not.**
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` gives `ESC % @` and `ESC % G` as the two selectors, and
both work: `InputEncoding` decides how a byte above `0x7F` is read, and a
single-byte encoding needs no decoder state at all. This was refused here on
the grounds that baud "cannot leave UTF-8", which was wrong — the encodings
differ only in that one rule.

**A byte `A0-FF` under `ESC % @` is a GR code, read through the slot GR
invokes** (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.3.2), since 2026-10-19: at power-up GR
holds G2, DEC Supplemental, the factory value of DEC's user-preference
supplemental set (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` §3.6.4.3 and 070-4 p. 14). So `D7` draws `Œ`
where ISO 8859-1 has `×`, and 20 of the 96 positions differ from ISO 8859-1,
a departure from `[[source: ctlseqs]]` 312's "That is ISO 8859-1"; with
`ESC . A` in G2, or `ESC - A` and LS1R, GR is ISO 8859-1's right half. With a
94-character set in GR, `A0` shows DEC's error character and `FF` is ignored
(070-3 §3.4.3, §3.4.4; note AE's AE8, decided) (`gr_reads_through_g2_under_latin1`,
`a_96_set_in_gr_draws_a0_and_ff`).

**Detection reports; it does not switch.** `InputEncoding::detect` takes a
sample and answers one of three things, and the third is the interesting one:

* a UTF-8 BOM at offset zero is a signature, and decisive;
* anything ill-formed as UTF-8 rules UTF-8 out — ISO 8859-1 has no ill-formed
  bytes, all 256 are characters, so it is what *remains* rather than a guess;
* a sample of nothing but ASCII returns **`None`**, because the two encodings
  agree on every byte below `0x80`.

That third case is why detection cannot be a default. Most of what a terminal
sees is ASCII and decides nothing, and a rule that answered anyway would be
inventing evidence. Nothing in baud calls `detect` — an embedder does, and
decides — because guessing an encoding wrong corrupts text silently and gives
the application no way to find out, which is the failure this document refuses
everywhere else. xterm makes it a resource for the same reason.

A sequence truncated by the end of the sample is not evidence either: the next
read may complete it, and treating it as ill-formed would make the answer depend
on where the read boundary fell.

`80..9F` are never printed in Latin-1: ISO 8859-1 reserves them for C1
controls, and printing them would invent characters the application did not
send. Each is read as its 7-bit `ESC` form, so the introducers and IND, NEL,
HTS and RI act, and the rest are taken as their 7-bit forms are: counted,
but for `9C`, which with no string open is an ST that closes nothing and,
like `ESC \` there, is counted nowhere (since 2026-10-09). This said
they "stay ignored" because baud did not act on them, citing xterm's
`CASE_IGNORE`; they were in fact counted, IND, NEL, HTS and RI included.
Inside a control string each cancels the string and then acts the same way,
as `ESC` does; until 2026-10-09 the string kept all but `9C` as payload.
Inside an escape or control sequence each cancels the sequence and acts as
well, since later that day; until then `escape` dropped it and the `csi_*`
states consumed it (the anywhere block).

**The rest of the legacy charsets remain out.** The one charset mechanism
baud implements, `SCS`, is a different thing from an input encoding: a
designated set replaces a few positions of the 94 and leaves the stream's
encoding alone. `ESC ( 0` replaces `0x60..0x7E` through
`[[source: foot-vt100-0]]`'s table and keeps `CellFlags::GRAPHICS` on the cell
(and should replace `0x5F` too; see the DEC Special Graphics row below), and
the national sets replace a handful each through `[[source: xterm-charsets]]`,
and since 2026-10-12 DEC Supplemental, JIS-Katakana and JIS-Roman each index a
94-entry table, and since 2026-10-19 the six 96-character sets a 96-entry one
(their rows in the `ESC` table).
This paragraph said `SCS` "annotates rather than translates" and that the cell
keeps the byte; both stopped being true when the tables landed. Under ASCII,
`PackedCell::translate` returns before any table is read. Byte-level
transcoding of a whole encoding would put a table lookup on the per-byte ingest
path, against the rule that the working set stays in cache — and `iconv`
already does it, once, outside the hot loop.

### The DCS prefix had the same merge, and the same three bugs

`dcs_entry`, `dcs_param` and `dcs_intermediate` were one arm, exactly as the CSI
states were, and lost exactly the same three rules:

| rule | `[[source: williams]]` | was |
|---|---|---|
| `3C-3F` is a marker in `dcs_entry` and an error in `dcs_param` | "Occurrences of 3C to 3F in positions other than the first character of the parameter string cause the entire control sequence to be ignored" | accepted anywhere |
| `30-3F` after an intermediate is malformed | `dcs_intermediate` sends them to `dcs_ignore` | walked back into `dcs_param` and accepted the digit |
| C0 in the prefix is **ignored** | "C0 controls other than CAN, SUB and ESC are **not executed** while recognising the first part of a device control string" | sent to `dcs_ignore`, costing the sequence |

Note the third against the CSI rule directly above it: williams says "C0 controls
are executed immediately during the recognition of a control sequence", and *not*
executed during a device control string. Same byte, different state, opposite
action — which is the whole reason these are separate states and not one.

Unlike CSI there is no `[[source: ctlseqs]]` sub-parameter form to accommodate in
a DCS prefix, so `3A` followed williams unmodified and ended the sequence.
From the Sixel parameters' routing to `csi` until 2026-10-19 it did not:
`Dcs` collected `3A` as a separator and the sequence went on, so `ESC P 0:1 q`
was a Sixel. williams sends it to `dcs ignore` ("the character 3A appears
anywhere", 589), and so does `[[source: dec-std-070]]`, whose 3.5.4.1 makes
the parameter string "identical in syntax to the parameter string of a
control sequence" and whose 3.5.3.1 ignores a control sequence at a 3/10;
`[[source: ecma48]]` 5.4.2 b alone lets 03/10 separate within a control
sequence's parameter sub-string, and gives a DCS no parameter string. xterm
counts `:` among a Sixel's parameter bytes (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s
file, 3459). Found 2026-10-10; since 2026-10-19 `3A` ignores the string
again, as both texts say, and `a_colon_in_a_dcs_prefix_ignores_the_string`
pins it.

### The parameter limit is 35, and it is derived rather than chosen

A CSI may carry more parameters than a fixed array holds, and discarding the
excess is a legitimate limit -- every terminal has one, and xterm's `NPARAM` is
30. What is not legitimate is the two feeds discarding it *differently*.

They did, in `baud-core2`. Its fast path accumulated a parameter into a local
and stored it only under the capacity bound, so an over-capacity parameter was
parsed and dropped. Its state machine wrote each digit straight into
`params[nparam - 1]` with `nparam` pinned *at* capacity, so the separator was
swallowed and the next parameter's digits appended to the last stored one:
`CSI ...;61;62;63 m` stored 6263 where the fast path stored 62. The result
therefore depended on where a read boundary fell, which is a chunk-invariance
bug. core2's ingest property did not find it because its generator did not
emit sequences that long.

| | one read | byte at a time |
|---|---|---|
| `CSI 0;0;...;0;1;4 m` | `BOLD` | nothing |
| `CSI 1;2;...;63;64 m` | `0xffff` | `0x7fff` |

core2's repair was `ParamIdx::next_capped`, which saturates one *past*
`CAPACITY` so that "capacity reached" and "capacity exceeded" stay
distinguishable; it is still in `baud-domain` and nothing calls it. `baud-core`
has one parameter store for both paths, `Csi` in `baud-core/src/write.rs`, with
one slot past the capacity: its `sep` stops advancing there, so every later
parameter folds into a slot nothing reads and is dropped, and its `count`
clamps to the capacity on the way out. See `tests/sgr/param_overflow.rs`. This
paragraph named core2's mechanism as the current one until 2026-10-10.

The capacity itself is **39**, derived in `ParamIdx::CAPACITY` as the longest
parameter list baud emits, the DECRQSS SGR reply: a leading `0`, every
attribute in `Attrs::WIRE` but one blink rate and one underline style, which
replace their families, the style at the two slots of `4:n`, a font, and three
colours at the six elements of `48:2::r:g:b`, each element a slot. It read
`1 + Attrs::WIRE.len() + 1 + 3 * 5` = 35 here after the ideogram aspects
widened `WIRE`. Since DECRQSS
answers with something the application can send back verbatim, a reply the
parser cannot re-read is a reply that does not round-trip -- and at 16 it could
not. Bold with a foreground, a background and an underline colour already
answers with seventeen parameters, which is an editor drawing an undercurl, not
a corner case. Deriving the constant rather than writing a number is what stops
it going back out of step the next time `WIRE` grows, which is how it drifted in
the first place.

### C0 inside an OSC payload is ignored, not stored

`osc_string` takes the *ignore* action on `01-17,19,1C-1F`; only `20-7F` is
`osc_put`. `dcs_passthrough` is not the same: `[[source: williams]]` gives
`00-17,19,1C-1F,20-7E` its *put* action and `7F` *ignore* (388), and baud's DCS
body follows it for `BEL`, the format effectors `08-0D` and `7F`, and the
`osc_string` row for the other C0, where the texts conflict, as its row names.
baud appended every byte that was not a terminator, so a raw control byte
landed in the title — and OSC payloads are attacker-supplied, which is why this
one is in the threat model rather than only in the conformance table.

**This block broke chunk-invariance the moment it landed, and the property
caught it.** `baud-core2`'s fast path scanned an OSC straight to its
terminator, so it could not see a cancel in between while the state machine
could — the same bytes set a title or did not, depending on whether they fit
inside one `read(2)`. Its repair declined any OSC payload containing a C0
byte at all, since a path that scans to the terminator cannot see a cancel
and one that copies the payload wholesale keeps bytes that should be dropped.
`baud-core`'s `fast_esc` takes no control string at all: it folds `ESC [`,
an intermediate and a final, and a bare two-byte `ESC`, and leaves `P`, `X`,
`]`, `^` and `_` to the graph, which walks every string byte by byte (this
said the fast path scans an OSC until 2026-10-10).
`a_c0_in_an_osc_payload_is_ignored` pins it deterministically
rather than leaving it to a saved seed.

---

## Tier 1 — VT100/ANSI core

The set every terminal must implement. No application can be assumed to work
without these.

### C0 controls

| byte | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `0x00` NUL | ignored | done |
| `0x07` BEL | bell | done — counted (`Term::bells`) and, since 2026-10-14, **rung by the macOS app**: `NSBeep` ("Plays the system beep", `[[source: apple-nsbeep]]`) while the window is focused, or under the config's `bell = "flash"` the visual bell a `?5` pair shows, or since 2026-11-01 the colour a program set with kitty's `OSC 21 ; visual_bell=` (that row; not under Reduce Motion, nor in a minimized window) or under `"none"` neither; while it is not focused nothing sounds, `?1042` asks for attention (the Dock icon bounces) and `?1043` raises the window unless it is minimized, whatever the config says (rows below). `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.3: "BEL is used when there is a need to call for attention; it may control alarm or attention devices." Since 2026-10-20 **it sounds at DECSWBV's volume**: off is silent and low sounds as high (the `CSI Ps SP t` row); the margin bell rings through the same decision at DECSMBV's. **Since 2026-11-01 a `BEL`'s visual bell flashes at most twice a second**, as WCAG 2.3.1 has it: "no more than three general flashes and / or no more than three red flashes within any one-second period" (`[[source: wcag22]]`). A ring while the flash rises or holds its peak holds it there, so bells rung every frame are one flash however long they ring; a ring after the peak, and through a quiet after the flash ends, is absorbed, as xterm suppresses a bell inside `bellSuppressTime` (`[[source: xterm-man]]`); a flash and its quiet last half a second, except where a change of `?5` ends an other-video flash early: then the quiet runs from that change (`BellFlash::video_ended`), and the change itself shows the flash's video, so no extra transition is drawn. Counted on the frames the CPU path draws at 60 frames a second, on the dark and the light theme over a dense screen, as the other video and in white and red, rung every frame, every 100 and 200 ms and every half second, it passes the census's verdict and its strict reading, at most two flashes in a second, and the flash each ring period from one frame to sixty draws holds that bound (`bells_rung_fast_flash_at_most_twice_a_second`, `no_ring_period_flashes_more_than_twice_a_second`, `the_bell_is_below_the_flash_thresholds`). Before, a bell rung every 200 ms flashed the whole screen five times a second, as kitty's bell, restarted at each ring (`screen_bell()`, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-screen]]` 3146-3158), does: both fail the verdict (`without_the_rule_a_bell_rung_every_200_ms_fails`). **A `?5` pair no frame drew, what `tput flash` sends, is not held to the rule**: each pair still restarts the other video's flash, an open gap. Bells within one frame interval ring once, and a bell inside the interval is absorbed: **an open departure from DEC STD 070**, whose bells are "sounded individually at a rate of 4-5 per second" from "a bell buffer" of "10 entries" (070-6 § 6.6.5.1, p. 6-45, `[[source: dec-std-070]]`), found 2026-10-20 and not changed; xterm suppresses for `bellSuppressTime`, 200 ms by default (`[[source: xterm-man]]`), another alternative. The decision is `baud-app/src/bell.rs`'s `ring`, tested over all 216 inputs, and the AppKit calls a shim the source gates hold (`a_focused_window_does_what_the_config_says`, `an_unfocused_window_does_what_the_modes_say`, `a_minimized_window_is_not_raised_and_still_asks`, `reduce_motion_shows_no_flash_and_rings_nothing_instead`, `each_output_reads_only_its_own_inputs`, `bells_within_one_frame_ring_once`, `a_ring_flashes_from_the_next_frame`, `the_pump_rings_the_bell_before_it_can_return`); no bell was heard or watched on screen. **Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app rings it through the same decision**: the window is focused while the keyboard is its, xdg-shell saying nothing of a minimized window; the sound is the compositor's system bell, `xdg-system-bell-v1`'s `ring` naming the window, which "may be an audible sound, a visual feedback of some kind, or any other thing including nothing" (`[[source: xdg-system-bell-v1]]`), and nothing where the compositor does not offer it; the flash as on macOS; once a frame interval, the compositor's refresh or the gap between its frame callbacks; since 2026-10-21 `?1042` and `?1043` on an unfocused window are one request there, `xdg-activation-v1`'s `activate` with a token the window asks for, as foot marks a window urgent (`[[source: foot-2705e36-wayland]]` 2630-2658, 2830-2860): no Wayland request tells urgency from a raise, and "It's up to the compositor to display this information as desired" (`[[source: xdg-activation-v1]]`), whether it brings the window forward or marks it for attention; one token in flight at a time (`the_system_bell_rings_where_the_compositor_offers_it`, `the_linux_driver_rings_as_ring_decides`, `a_frames_length_is_the_gap_between_frame_callbacks`, `the_window_is_activated_with_a_token_it_asks_for_or_is_given`); tested over the fake compositor's socket only. **Inside a control string it rings only in a Sixel**: it ends an `OSC` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 2023-2024) and no other string, as `[[source: ecma48]]` 5.6 closes each with ST; it is put to a DCS body's handler and ignored in the DCS prefix and in an SOS, PM or APC, `[[source: williams]]`'s rows (354-426). A Sixel's handler rings it, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §9.1, "same action as in text mode", and every other DCS handler ignores it, since 2026-10-19 (`a_bel_inside_a_sixel_rings_and_draws_nothing`). Until 2026-10-09 it ended all five; see `dcs_passthrough` and `sos_pm_apc_string` above. xterm rings it in every string but an OSC (`CASE_BELL`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file 3691-3705), a departure from williams' *put* and *ignore* not followed outside a Sixel (`a_bel_ends_an_osc_and_no_other_control_string`) |
| `0x08` BS | backspace | done — at the left margin it stops inside the scrolling region; left of the margin, or on it on a row outside the region, it moves one column and stops at the first (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` BS, p. 5-63, notes 1-2 and the algorithm), since 2026-10-14 (`bs_left_of_the_left_margin_moves_one_column`, `bs_on_a_row_outside_the_scroll_area_passes_the_left_margin`, `bs_on_the_margin_rows_stops_at_the_left_margin`, `tests/vt420/tab_backspace.rs`); until then a cursor in either place did not move, or under `?45` reverse-wrapped to the row above. `[[source: vt510-chapter4]]`'s Table 4-1, "If the cursor is at the left margin, no action occurs", names no row. `?45` reverse-wraps only where it stops (`reverse_wrap_takes_over_only_where_bs_stops`). **After a full line it steps back from the last column**, since 2026-11-01, so the character it was sent to correct is the one overwritten: at a pending wrap the Active Position is the last column (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-D pp. D-13 and D-14) and BS "moves the Active Position backward ... by one column", as xterm's `CursorBack` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 152-158) and ghostty do; under `?45` or `?1045` with `?7` the wrap is the step and it stays on that column, xterm's `(rev || rev2) && screen->do_wrap` (153). Before, the wrap was always the step (`bs_and_cub_count_from_the_last_column`, `under_reverse_wrap_the_wrap_is_the_first_step`). ghostty passes the left margin on every row without `?45` and stops at it on every row with it, xterm stops at it on every row: both pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle` |
| `0x09` HT | tab | done — real stop table. Under a right margin left of the last column it stops at that margin when it starts inside the scrolling region at or left of it, or one past it at a pending wrap (`?7h`), and goes on to the next stop or the last column when it starts two or more columns past it or on a row outside the region (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` HT, p. 5-67, note 1), since 2026-10-14 (`ht_inside_the_margins_stops_at_the_right_margin`, `ht_right_of_the_right_margin_goes_to_the_next_stop_or_the_last_column`, `ht_on_a_row_outside_the_scroll_area_passes_the_right_margin`, `tests/vt420/tab_backspace.rs`); until then it passed the right margin unless DECOM was set. The region's margin rows count as inside, as note 1 and BS's algorithm have it, where HT's algorithm (p. 5-68) compares strictly (`ht_on_the_margin_rows_stops_at_the_right_margin`): open. At a pending wrap it leaves the cursor on the right margin with nothing pending, at either width: STD 070's D.6.1 (pp. D-13, D-14) lists `HORIZONTAL_TAB` among the controls that reset the Last Column Flag (`ht_at_a_pending_wrap_stays_on_the_right_margin`). ghostty keeps the wrap pending, and ghostty and xterm stop it at the right margin on every row, pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`. A cursor a move placed one past the margin is beyond it and goes on to the next stop, under `?7h` since 2026-10-15 (`ht_one_past_the_right_margin_with_autowrap_goes_to_the_next_stop`; note V's V10 step (d), the `CSI s` row), where it read as a pending wrap and was held on the margin, and under `?7l` before (`ht_one_past_the_right_margin_without_autowrap_goes_to_the_next_stop`) |
| `0x0A` LF | line feed | done — index only unless LNM; under LNM `CR` first, then the index (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` LF, p. 5-60; `lf_under_lnm_starts_at_the_left_margin`). Since 2026-10-30 LNM is **also a local Set-up feature** (070-6 §6.2.1 d): `[keyboard] new_line` is the power-up and `RIS` state and a change of it acts at once, and `DECSTR` leaves LNM, which its table lists "NOT affected"; the texts differ, as `soft_terminal_reset()`'s algorithm assigns "new line mode = NEW LINE OFF", and baud follows the table, as the VT510's DECSTR table, which omits LNM, does (`a_new_line_set_up_is_lnm_itself`, `a_soft_reset_keeps_its_own_table`) |
| `0x0B` VT | vertical tab | done — since 2026-10-19, **to the line a program set a line tabulation stop on**, the column kept, and `LF` where no stop follows, so a stream that sets none sees what it always did: `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.161 moves it "to the corresponding character position on the line at which the following line tabulation stop is set", and `[[source: vt510-chapter4]]` Table 4-1 treats VT "as LF", which is what it does with no stop to reach. Inside the scrolling region it reads stops to the bottom margin, outside it to the last line, as CVT's row says (`vt_moves_to_the_following_line_stop`, `vt_with_no_stop_following_is_a_line_feed`, `vt_inside_the_region_reads_stops_to_the_bottom_margin`). Its own action in the per-byte table, out of line |
| `0x0C` FF | form feed | done — index |
| `0x0D` CR | carriage return | done — under side margins to the left margin from at or right of it on a row inside the top and bottom margins, else to column 1 (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CR, p. 5-58; `cr_inside_the_fence_goes_to_the_left_margin`, `cr_outside_the_scroll_area_goes_to_column_one`, `cr_left_of_the_fence_goes_to_column_one`), since 2026-10-15; xterm and ghostty test the column only, pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle` |
| `0x0E` SO | shift out (G1) | done — inside a control string as well, for the text after it (the `dcs_passthrough` row) |
| `0x0F` SI | shift in (G0) | done — inside a control string as well (the `dcs_passthrough` row) |

### ESC sequences

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `ESC 7` | DECSC save cursor | done — **each screen has its own saved cursor**, since 2026-10-20, xterm's `sc[screen->whichBuf]` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`, `CursorSave`): a save on the alternate screen leaves the shell's, so a program that saves around its own drawing gives the shell's cursor back when it leaves (`a_save_on_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_primarys`). `[[source: vt510-decsc]]` lists seven items and `baud-core` saves them: cursor position, SGR attributes, the wrap flag, origin mode, the selective erase attribute, and of the character sets G0-G3 as slots and which slot GL and GR invoke (`a_cursor_restore_brings_back_the_slots_and_the_shift`, `decrc_restores_gr`), as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` §5.13.1 saves GR at Level 2. **A single shift is never pending when it runs**: the `ESC` of `ESC 7` cancels it (070-3 p. 3-81), so vt510's "Any single shift 2 (SS2) or single shift 3 (SS3) functions sent" is always empty, as 070's save buffer has no such field. **The primary's save follows its text through a resize**, since 2026-10-20 (note V, V4): a narrowing re-cut moves it with its cell and a shrink or a growth with its row, as the live cursor moves, onto the top row when its row went into the history (`decsc_follows_its_cell_through_a_narrowing_re_cut`, `decsc_follows_its_row_through_a_shrink_that_retires_rows`); the alternate screen's is not moved, its cells keeping their addresses (`the_alternate_screens_slot_is_not_moved_by_a_resize`); under `?47` the primary's slot is moved too, so a `?1049 l` that leaves restores it re-cut (`a_1049_exit_after_47_restores_the_primarys_save_re_cut`). **A pending wrap is saved**, since 2026-11-01, so a program that saves the cursor at the end of a full line and restores it gets the wrap back and the next character goes to the next line: the wrap flag is the pending wrap, DEC's Last Column Flag, which "should be saved when a Save Cursor operation is performed" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-D p. D-14) and which both DECCIRs report among "the same information saved through a save cursor" (Sflag bit 4, "autowrap pending"); a wrap waiting at SLL's line limit or kept on a column the margins moved is saved the same way, and a re-cut moves it with its character (`decsc_and_decrc_keep_a_pending_wrap`, `a_wrap_waiting_at_the_line_limit_is_saved_and_restored`, `a_saved_pending_wrap_rides_the_re_cut`). **DECAWM is not saved**: 070-5's `save_cursor` (p. 5-188) saves no Auto Wrap Mode and no DECCIR has a bit for it, as xterm's `DECSC_FLAGS` leaves `WRAPAROUND` out (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 461) and ghostty and foot do (note AE's Q12, decided 2026-11-01; `docs/research/notes/ae-code-extension.md`). Until then the mode was saved and restored, and the pending wrap dropped at full width. The row said `done` on the strength of the first two items until the manual was read rather than assumed |
| `ESC 8` | DECRC restore cursor | **partial** — restores what DECSC saved on the screen shown, clamped to the grid that exists now; on the alternate screen with nothing saved there it goes home, whatever the primary saved (`the_alternate_screen_does_not_restore_the_primarys_save`). **And into the margins when the restored origin mode is displaced**, line and column, the saved mode applied first, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `restore_cursor` (p. 5-190) does, since 2026-10-13 (`decrc_under_origin_mode_lands_inside_the_margins`, and `a_right_margin_column_restored_under_a_narrower_margin_is_clamped`, where a column saved on the right margin sits where the pending-wrap sentinel would); before, a restore after narrower margins left the cursor outside them under DECOM. **A saved pending wrap comes back pending**, since 2026-11-01, on the column it waited on, clamped to the grid and into the margins as a column is, and the next character wraps from there, as xterm's `CursorRestore` restores `do_wrap` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 494) and ghostty its `pending_wrap`; at full width the clamp dropped it before, so the next character overwrote the last (`decsc_and_decrc_keep_a_pending_wrap`, `a_restored_pending_wrap_wraps_on_the_corpus_screen`, note AE's plan test 28, with `?1049` and DECRSPS). **DECAWM is left as it is** (`decrc_leaves_autowrap_as_it_is`); origin mode is restored, which neither core did once (`a_cursor_restore_brings_back_origin_mode_and_leaves_autowrap`). **With nothing saved** -- at power-on, and after `DECSTR` or `RIS`, which empty the slot -- since 2026-10-11 it does what `[[source: vt510-decrc]]` lists: the cursor home, origin mode reset, the attributes off, and the default character-set mapping, ASCII in G0 and G1, DEC Supplemental in G2 and G3 as the user-preference supplemental set, GL on G0 and GR on G2 (`[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]` § 4.7, "the default character set mapping is established"; vt510's "the DEC Supplemental Graphic set into GR"); and the selective erase attribute off, which vt510's list omits and `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s reset save buffer (DECSC note 3, p. 5-187) and vt220's Table 4-10 hold. `DECAWM` is left as it is: 070's buffer has no wrap flag and no no-save list resets it. Until then it did nothing (`baud-core/tests/decrc_unsaved.rs`). The gap: `SCORC` with nothing saved, which stays inert until SCO's text is read, note AE's Q13 (`CSI s` / `CSI u` row) |
| `ESC D` | IND index | done — column preserved (was resetting it); scrolls the region at its bottom and nothing on the last row below it, as DECSTBM's row says; under side margins it moves only the rectangle the four margins bound (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3, "All scrolling, both vertical and horizontal, is limited to this area") (`lf_and_ind_at_the_bottom_margin_move_the_rectangle`), **and from outside the side margins too**: STD 070 p. 5-24 marks IND and RI "Continues to work outside these margins" for L/R and its algorithm tests the line alone (`ind_lf_and_ri_outside_the_side_margins_still_scroll_the_rectangle`). xterm (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`, `xtermIndex`) and ghostty (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-terminal]]`, `index`) move nothing from there: their departure, pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle` |
| `ESC E` | NEL next line | done — `CR` and then `LF`, so the line it leaves decides (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` NEL, p. 5-64; `nel_and_lnm_judge_the_line_they_leave`), as `LF` under `LNM` (p. 5-60), since 2026-10-15 |
| `ESC M` | RI reverse index | done — at the top margin under side margins moves only the rectangle the four margins bound (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3, "All scrolling, both vertical and horizontal, is limited to this area") (`ri_at_the_top_margin_moves_the_rectangle`), from outside the side margins too, as IND's row says. Since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps the window over the history (note V, V2): `RI` at the top of the primary takes a line back as SD does, and sends a window it reaches to the live screen at that byte (`a_take_back_that_reaches_the_window_sends_it_live_at_that_byte`) |
| `ESC c` | RIS full reset | done — the power-on state from either screen, every line single-width (`decaln_ris_and_deccolm_reset_every_line`), and it **empties the history**. xterm's `CASE_RIS` clears its saved lines where `DECSTR` keeps them (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`), and alacritty (`[[source: alacritty-reset-state]]`, `[[source: alacritty-grid-reset]]`) and foot (`[[source: foot-vt-ris]]`, `[[source: foot-term-reset]]`) do the same; the Linux console (`[[source: linux-vt-ris]]`) and sfte (`[[source: sfte-ris]]`) keep it. The whole primary ring is erased with the rotation kept, so a reset scrolls nothing and leaves nothing a reader of the ring or a later re-cut can bring back (`a_full_reset_clears_the_history_and_the_next_lines_refill_it`, `a_full_reset_leaves_nothing_hidden_below_the_window`, `a_full_reset_leaves_nothing_of_the_old_text_in_the_ring`, `a_full_reset_scrolls_nothing`, the captured `reset(1)` in `a_captured_reset_leaves_no_history`); it leaves the alternate screen before the soft reset, so what `?1049 h` saved does not survive it (`a_hard_reset_on_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_power_on_state`), and empties both screens' saved cursors, the initial state, where xterm's keeps the alternate screen's (`resets_and_the_two_slots`), and frees the `OSC 8` table (`a_full_reset_frees_the_link_table`). `DECSTR` keeps the history (`a_soft_reset_keeps_the_history`). It left the counts and reset the rotation until 2026-09-23, so the scrollback read rows the reset never wrote. Since 2026-10-09 it moves `Term::history_erasures`, as `CSI 3 J` does, and the macOS app puts a scrolled-back window on the live screen however the read falls (`an_erased_history_puts_the_window_on_the_live_screen_however_the_read_falls`). Since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps the window over the history (note V, V2), and the reset sends it live at its byte, from either screen, keeping where it stood for the first erasure an embedder watches (`Erasure::Reset(Screen, Looking)`; `an_erasure_sends_the_window_live_from_either_screen`, `a_reset_keeps_where_the_window_stood_at_its_byte`). **Since 2026-10-11 it moves `Term::resets`, and nothing else does** -- not `DECSTR`, `ED 2`, `CSI ? 3 J`, `CSI 3 J`, a screen switch, a scroll or a resize (`the_reset_count_moves_on_a_full_reset_and_nothing_else`) -- for what a reset owes an embedder's state over the screen, note V's V1: ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "initial state, i.e. the state it has after it is made operational" (`[[source: ecma48]]`) has no selection, and foot's hard reset cancels one (`selection_cancel`, `[[source: foot-term-reset]]`) as xterm's `RIS` disowns one through `ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, `[[source: xterm-scroll]]`), neither on `DECSTR`. When it moves across a read the macOS app drops the selection, drops the echoes it drew ahead of the shell as contradicted, settles the wheel's part-line, and reveals the thumb when the reset brought a window over the history to the live screen; a reset on the primary runs no screen-switch fade (`baud-app`'s `restart_tests`, the captured `reset(1)` at eight read sizes among them). `DECSCL`, which DEC's manuals call a hard reset in the VT320, VT420 and VT520 and a soft one in the VT220 and DEC STD 070 (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), soft-resets (the DECSCL row). Since 2026-10-19 `RIS` returns to Level 4 and seven-bit C1, and since 2026-10-24 to UTF-8 input after `ESC % @` and to `?1007` set (the `ESC % @` and `?1007` rows). **The width**, since 2026-10-14: after a program's request it asks back for the grid's width at the first request since power-on or the last `RIS`, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s Device Initialization takes the page width from NVM, the width the user stored (`ris_asks_for_the_width_before_the_first_request`); xterm asks for 80, practice departing from the text. **Since 2026-10-25 it takes the rest of what a program set**, which `DECSTR` keeps: both kitty keyboard stacks, XTSHIFTESCAPE, `?30`, the title and icon name, the badge and its colour, the user variables, the reported directory and its host, the colours a program set with `OSC 10`, `11`, `12`, `17` and `19`, which `OSC 21` and iTerm2's `SetColors` write too, and the text under the cursor, a font a program changed, asked back of the embedder, and an iTerm2 file still arriving in parts (each its own row; `ris_power_on.rs`). It rings no bell, where xterm's `bellOnReset` rings one by default: no text asks for it |
| `ESC H` | HTS set tab stop | done — since 2026-10-27, **a stop set right after a line is filled lands on the line's last column**: at a wrap waiting at the last column or a DECSLRM right margin, HTS, `CTC 0` and `TBC 0` set and clear the stop on the column the wrap waits on, the one `CPR` names, where the stop went one past it: past the line at the last column, out of sight until a wider window showed it, and one past the margin under DECSLRM: the active position is not advanced from "the last character position of the active line" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 6.1.6), and DEC's HTS sets the stop at "the Active Column", which the Last Column Flag leaves there (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` D.6.1, p. 5-69). The wrap still waits, HTS not being among the controls D.6.1 lists (`hts_and_tbc_at_a_wrap_waiting_at_the_lines_end_name_its_column`) |
| `ESC J` | VTS line tabulation set | done — since 2026-10-19, a line tabulation stop on the cursor's line, which CVT and VT then reach (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.162; `cvt_moves_to_the_line_vts_set_and_keeps_the_column`). None at power-on and after `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR` (`ris_clears_the_line_stops_and_decstr_keeps_them`); fixed to the screen's lines, as the character stops are to its columns. VT52's `ESC J` still erases (`vt52_esc_j_still_erases`). DEC's terminals and xterm have no line stops; ECMA-48's whole set is built |
| `ESC =` / `ESC >` | DECKPAM/DECKPNM | done — since 2026-10-30 **also a local Set-up feature**, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-6 §6.2.1 d asks: `[keyboard] application_keypad` switches the keypad of the windows open when it changes, and like the VT420's keypad field ("The setting is not saved in nonvolatile memory", `[[source: vt420-uu-ch5]]`) it is not a saved value, so power-up, `DECSTR` and `RIS` select Numeric whatever it says (`[[source: vt510-deckpam]]`; `a_change_of_the_set_up_acts_on_what_it_changes`, `launch_and_ris_take_the_saved_values_and_select_numeric`) |
| `ESC Z` | DECID identify terminal | done — **at Levels 1 and 2 it answers what `CSI c` answers**, through the same function, so the Sixel claim and `S8C1T` reach it, and `9A` under ISO 8859-1 is the same control: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4 IDENTIFY TERMINAL (PDF 232), "Levels: 1, 2", "This control is identical to a primary DA control with an omitted parameter or a parameter value of zero (0)"; `[[source: ctlseqs]]`, "Obsolete form of CSI c (DA)". **At Levels 3 and 4 it is not recognised, answers nothing and is counted**, since 2026-10-19: note b, "DECID is not recognized at Level 3 or above", and the algorithm, "Ignored above Level 2", which answers only at Level 1 or Level 2. baud powers up at Level 4 (the DECSCL row), so **a program sending `ESC Z` at power-up gets no answer** and `CSI c` still answers `?6c`; DECSCL 61 or 62 brings it back, and so does `ESC <`, which returns to Level 1, 070-0A EXIT VT52 EMULATION MODE (p. A-15), "causing the terminal to return to Level 1 operation" (the DECSCL row; `leaving_vt52_returns_to_level_one_seven_bit`, `decid_is_not_recognised_at_level_four`, `decid_is_not_recognised_at_level_three`, `the_c1_9a_is_not_recognised_at_level_four`, `decid_answers_at_level_one`, `decid_answers_at_level_two_in_eight_bits`). It answered at every level from 2026-10-16, when baud had no level state. xterm answers at every level, a departure from the text not followed; 070's known deviations record the VT300 family and the VT420 ignoring it at Level 2 too, where baud follows the text. In `[[source: ecma48]]`, ESC Z / 09/10 is 8.3.109 SCI, whose use "is reserved for future standardization", so DECID takes a representation ECMA-48 defines no behaviour for. DEC STD 070's own note a records the conflict in the sentence this row leaves out: "THIS CONTROL IS RESERVED FOR FUTURE STANDARDIZATION BY ISO AND ANSI. Conforming software shall use the primary Device Attributes (DA) control". In VT52 mode `ESC Z` is VT52's identify and answers `ESC / Z` (note c; `docs/protocols/vt52.md`). Counted as unsupported until 2026-10-16 (at Level 2: `decid_answers_the_primary_da`, `decid_answers_the_sixel_claim_as_da_does`, `decid_answers_in_eight_bits_after_s8c1t`, `the_c1_9a_under_latin1_is_decid`, `in_vt52_mode_esc_z_keeps_the_vt52_answer`) |
| `ESC I` | HTJ character tabulation with justification | done — since 2026-10-26, and `89` under ISO 8859-1, **a program can push what it wrote in a field up against the next tab stop**: "HTJ causes the contents of the active field ... to be shifted forward so that it ends at the character position preceding the following character tabulation stop. The active presentation position is moved to that following character tabulation stop. The character positions which precede the beginning of the shifted string are put into the erased state" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.61). The field is `EF`'s, from the stop at or before the cursor to the next, bounded by the line (4.2.40; the `CSI Ps N` row); its contents run from its start through its last position not in the erased state, wherever the cursor stands in it, so nothing leaves the field, and the cursor goes where `HT` takes it, the last column where no stop follows. In VT52 mode `ESC I` stays the reverse line feed. With BDSM reset, on a line holding an SDS or SRS string, the field's positions are the ones drawn, in both DCSM states as `HT` counts its stops, and their content moves between the cells drawn there, each keeping its string. **Under DECLRMM the field stays the line's**, not the margins', rather than bounded where `HT` stops: with margins 1 to 12 and stops at 9 and 17, `ab` written at 10 moves to 15 and 16, past the right margin, while the cursor stops at the margin, column 12, as `HT` does. No other terminal has HTJ, and DEC STD 070, whose margin table bounds ICH, DCH, IL, DL, DECIC and DECDC (p. 5-24), has neither HTJ nor EF (`under_declrmm_htjs_field_is_the_lines`, `htj_shifts_the_field_to_end_before_the_next_stop`, `htj_shifts_the_whole_written_field_with_the_cursor_inside_it`, `htj_on_an_empty_or_full_field_only_moves_the_cursor`, `htj_with_no_following_stop_ends_the_field_at_the_line_end`, `htj_moves_a_wide_character_whole`, `htj_shifts_the_positions_drawn`, `htj_as_a_c1_under_latin1_and_not_in_vt52`) |
| `ESC l` / `ESC m` | HP memory lock and unlock | **out** — counted, and no margin moves: a program that locks the lines above the cursor this way sees them scroll as before. `[[source: ctlseqs]]` has them "Memory Lock (per HP terminals). Locks memory above the cursor" and "Memory Unlock", and xterm moves its top margin to the cursor's line or the first (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 4759-4770), but `[[source: ecma48]]` 5.5, Table 5, leaves `ESC 06/12` and `ESC 06/13` unallocated, "reserved for future standardization and shall not be used", and no HP manual is pinned. The `xterm-256color` entry `TERM` names carries `meml` and `memu` (`[[source: terminfo-xterm-256color]]`), exempt in the terminfo conformance test for this reason. They stay counted where SGR 58/59 and DECID act on codes ECMA-48 reserves, because a pinned text assigns those; Table 5's "shall not be used" is the difference. They are not gated by an xterm resource either: xterm's `esc_table` maps them at `[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]` 6133-6134 with no `#if`, and the handler at `[[source: xterm-reset]]` 4759-4770 tests no resource. Only `ESC F` has a gate, `hpLowerleftBugCompat`. Whether to act on them stays open (`hp_memory_lock_and_unlock_are_counted_and_move_no_margin`) |
| `ESC V` / `ESC W` | SPA / EPA start and end of guarded area | done — since 2026-10-19, and `96` and `97` under ISO 8859-1. **A program can fence off part of the screen so that ED, EL and ECH leave it alone.** EPA guards every position from the one the cursor was at on SPA through the one it is at on EPA, written or not, in the order of the character path: the rest of the first row, the rows between whole, the last row to the cursor (`[[source: ecma48]]` 6.5.2.2, "The character position which is the active presentation position after receipt of SPA is the first character position of the guarded area. The character position which is the active presentation position before the receipt of EPA is the last character position"). While ERM is reset, its initial state, ED, EL, ECH, EA and EF spare a guarded position; set, they erase it and it stays guarded (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.4 and 4.2.38, the `CSI 6 h` row). DECSED and DECSEL erase a guarded character DECSCA left erasable and keep its guard, only DECSCA deciding them. The guard is a cell flag of its own, apart from DECSCA's, so a plain ED still erases DECSCA-protected text, where xterm and ghostty keep one bit and a mode naming the last control to set it. **Where no text decides**: a host write into a guarded position replaces the guard, as every write builds a fresh cell; EPA before SPA's position guards the positions between them; a second SPA starts the area again; nothing is guarded before EPA, so an erase between them erases; the start of an open area moves with its line through a scroll, IL, DL and a region's scroll, as 8.3.67 and 8.3.32 shift "the start ... of a qualified area", and is the screen's first position if it scrolled off the top; DECSTR forgets an open SPA, as xterm's soft reset does, and keeps ERM and the guarded positions (xterm's soft reset also sets `protected_mode = OFF_PROTECT` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`), so its guarded characters become erasable by ED, EL and ECH; baud keeps them spared, because DEC's soft-reset table names no guarded area); DECSC and DECRC do not carry an open SPA; a screen switch keeps the start as its screen position. Not asked, and built so: a DL or a region's scroll that deletes the start's line forgets the open SPA, as an image anchored there goes; a resize forgets it. A change of width keeps every guarded position, an empty one re-cut as line content as a styled blank is, so a 20-column guarded row narrowed to 12 is two rows and widens back to one (`a_guarded_empty_row_keeps_every_guard_through_a_reflow`). `RIS` removes every guard and an open SPA (8.3.105, "remove qualified areas"). **A print leaves out what is guarded**, since 2026-10-27: under GATM's GUARD, its reset state, every print of screen contents prints a guarded position as a space (the `CSI 1 h` row); it said GATM had nothing to act on, "since baud transmits no screen contents", while MC printed them (`spa_and_epa_guard_from_spa_through_the_position_before_epa`, `ed_el_and_ech_spare_a_guarded_position_under_erm_reset`, `under_erm_set_they_erase_a_guarded_position_and_it_stays_guarded`, `the_start_of_an_open_area_moves_with_its_line`, `a_recycled_row_carries_no_guard`, and the rest of `baud-core/tests/guarded_area.rs`). Open: reflow reads guarded empty positions as line content, so a guarded empty row narrowed from 20 columns to 12 takes two rows. A start pushed below the screen by a full-screen scroll down is EPA's last position, where IL pushing it out of the band forgets it |
| `CSI ? 2 l` | DECANM — enter VT52 | done — see [protocols/vt52.md](protocols/vt52.md) |
| `ESC # 8` | DECALN screen alignment | done — since 2026-10-14, `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s DECALN (pp. D-19 and D-20) in its order: Origin Mode absolute (`decaln_resets_origin_mode`), the rendition normal before the fill, the colours with it (`decaln_resets_the_rendition_before_the_fill`) and SAPV's variants kept, as `SGR 0` keeps them (`decaln_keeps_the_variants`), the screen filled with `E`, the top and bottom margins reset and the cursor at 1;1 (`decaln_fills_the_screen_and_homes_the_cursor`, `decaln_resets_the_margins`, `the_homes_under_origin_mode_go_to_the_origin`), every line single-width (`decaln_ris_and_deccolm_reset_every_line`). **And the side margins**, which the 1985 algorithm predates: "DECALN sets the margins to the extremes of the page" (`[[source: vt510-decaln]]`); `DECLRMM` stays, as no text resets it (`decaln_resets_the_side_margins_and_keeps_the_mode`). Open: whether the colours go with the four renditions STD 070 names, and the protection and link the `E`s carry. **partial** until 2026-10-14, while it kept DECOM, the rendition and the side margins |
| `ESC # 6` | DECDWL double-width line | done — since 2026-10-18, a line drawn twice as wide: the cursor's line holds half the columns, `end_of_line` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.6.1 and DECDWL, pp. 5-95, 5-97 and 5-98, and § 5.14.1, p. 5-209; `[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`; `[[source: vt510-decdwl]]`), each character two columns wide. What lay past the half is emptied and the cursor pulled back inside it (`decdwl_loses_what_lies_past_the_half_and_decswl_does_not_bring_it_back`, `decdwl_pulls_the_cursor_back_to_the_half`); text wraps at the half, and with `DECAWM` reset overwrites its last column (`a_double_width_line_wraps_at_its_half`, `the_pending_wrap_is_on_the_last_column_of_the_half`, `without_autowrap_the_half_s_last_column_takes_the_overflow`); every move stops there and a move onto the line lands inside it (`every_move_stops_at_the_half`, `a_move_onto_a_double_line_lands_inside_it`); `ICH`, `DCH` and insert mode shift within it (`ich_and_dch_shift_within_the_half`); none of the four controls leaves a wrap pending, D.6.1's list (pp. D-13 and D-14; `the_controls_end_a_pending_wrap`). The rendition is the row's, carried in its mark, so a scroll, `IL`, `DL`, `SU`, `SD`, the history and each screen keep it with the line, and the line a scroll brings in is single-width (pp. 5-210 and 5-211; `a_scroll_carries_the_rendition_and_brings_in_single_width`, `twelve_reverse_indexes_leave_half_of_a_double_height_box`, `each_screen_keeps_its_own_renditions`). A block a rectangle operation names stops at each line's own end, so it looks narrower on a double line, as the notes of DECFRA, DECERA, DECSERA, DECCARA and DECRARA say ("character positions 5 through 10 affected", pp. 5-169 to 5-176), and DECRQCRA, whose text (pp. 5-179 and 5-180) has no such note, by § 5.6.1's "character positions cannot be addressed which are more than half the current value of the right edge of the display" (p. 5-95): all six act on the line's own positions and never on what lies past them (`decfra_fills_a_double_line_to_its_end`, `decera_stops_at_a_double_line_end`, `decsera_stops_at_a_double_line_end`, `deccara_in_a_rectangle_blanks_a_double_line_to_its_end`, `deccara_in_a_stream_stops_at_a_double_line_end`, `decrara_stops_at_a_double_line_end`, `decrqcra_sums_a_double_line_to_its_end`); DECCRA writes a double destination line only to its end and reads a source position past its line's end as unoccupied, what a DEC terminal's copy gives (`deccra_copies_onto_a_double_line_up_to_its_end`, `deccra_reads_a_double_line_past_its_end_as_unoccupied`). xterm reads the screen's width for every row (`[[source: xterm-screen]]`). Everything the line shows is drawn at its size: an `SGR 52` ring, an input method's composition, the line as it slides out of the window under a smooth scroll (on the cell path, the only one that slides; its full-row slide is every path's frame one line up), and kitty's placeholders, which are characters (`an_encircled_ring_is_drawn_at_the_line_s_size`, `a_composition_on_a_sized_line_draws_as_its_text_would`, `a_placeholder_on_a_sized_line_is_drawn_as_its_character`). An image begun on the line, a Sixel or a kitty placement, is not text and is not stretched: it starts at the cursor's displayed place, the doubled character's left edge (070-9 § 11.1; `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`), text over it frees it by the display columns it covers, and the cursor goes right of it on the row it lands on (`a_sixel_on_a_double_line_starts_at_the_cursor_s_displayed_place`, `a_kitty_placement_on_a_double_line_starts_at_the_cursor_s_displayed_place`, `text_over_an_image_on_a_double_line_frees_it_by_the_columns_it_covers`, `sixel_scrolls_right_counts_a_double_line_s_characters`); xterm places it at the character position's single-width column (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]`). A kitty delete by the cursor's cell, `d=c` or `d=C`, takes every placement either display column of the cell meets, "all placements that intersect with the current cursor position" (`[[source: kitty-graphics]]`; `a_delete_by_the_cursor_on_a_double_line_takes_both_display_columns`). `IL` and `DL` carry such an image with its line, at the display column it began at (`il_and_dl_carry_an_image_on_a_sized_line_with_its_line`). `DECIC` and `DECDC` leave it where it is while the text shifts under it, as xterm and kitty do, the kitty text moving images only when the screen scrolls (`decic_and_decdc_leave_an_image_on_a_sized_line_where_it_is`), and shift the line to the right margin, so what they push past its end comes back on DECSWL, as xterm's does: their texts say "from the active column to the right margin" (pp. 5-150 and 5-151), are silent on a sized line and leave the algorithm "to be supplied", where ICH's and DCH's algorithms stop at the line's end (`decic_and_decdc_shift_a_sized_line_to_the_right_margin`). With `DECAWM` reset a wide character on the half's last column writes its lead alone there, nothing in the hidden half (the `?7` row). Not under `DECLRMM` (note 3). A width change keeps a sized line one row and whole, never joined to a neighbour, with what lies past a narrower half kept unseen: no text resizes a page but DECCOLM (`a_reflow_keeps_a_sized_line_whole_and_on_its_own`, `a_sized_line_narrowed_below_its_text_comes_back_whole`). vttest's double-size test and its Bugs B, E and L replay from its own bytes as the text says (`tests/vttest/`; `the_double_size_test_draws_each_rendition_at_both_widths`, `bug_b_scrolls_a_region_under_a_double_width_line`, `bug_e_moves_from_a_double_width_line_to_column_100`, `bug_l_loses_the_right_half_as_the_texts_say`). xterm keeps the cells past the half (`[[source: xterm-doublechr]]`) and vttest's Bug L calls their loss a "common misfeature" (`[[source: vttest-main]]`): practice departing from the text, which is followed. Counted unsupported until 2026-10-18 |
| `ESC # 3` / `ESC # 4` | DECDHL double-height line, top and bottom half | done — since 2026-10-18, the top or the bottom half of a line drawn twice as wide and twice as tall (pp. 5-99 and 5-100; `[[source: vt510-decdhl]]`), "used in pairs on adjacent lines" (`[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`): everything DECDWL's row says of the line and its half, and the half each draws. DECDHLB's algorithm assigns `DOUBLE_HEIGHT_TOP`; its description and purpose name the bottom half, and the bottom half is set (`each_control_sets_the_cursor_line`). vttest's frame of DEC line drawing on double-height pairs, and the twelve `RI`s that leave half of it, replay as the text says (`the_double_height_frame_and_the_half_that_remains`). Counted unsupported until 2026-10-18 |
| `ESC # 5` | DECSWL single-width line | done — since 2026-10-18: the cursor's line single-width, losing nothing (p. 5-96; `[[source: vt510-decswl]]`), so what DECDWL or DECDHL emptied stays empty (`decdwl_loses_what_lies_past_the_half_and_decswl_does_not_bring_it_back`); on a single-width line it asks for what is already true (`every_spec_esc_intermediate_form_is_handled_or_counted`). Counted unsupported until 2026-10-18 |
| `ESC ( ` / `ESC ) ` / `ESC * ` / `ESC + ` | charset designate G0-G3 | done — the mechanism: G0-G3 designated, invoked into GL by SI, SO, LS2 and LS3 and into GR by LS1R, LS2R and LS3R. At power-up and after DECSTR or RIS, G0 and G1 hold ASCII and G2 and G3 the user-preference supplemental set, DEC Supplemental, with GL on G0 and GR on G2 (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` §3.6.4.3, 070-4 p. 14), and after DECSCL 62 or 63 as well, Level 2 having the 8-bit Interface Architecture as an implicit extension (`levels_two_to_four_single_shift_the_supplemental_set`). **Since 2026-10-30 Level 1 has its own**: after DECSCL 61 all four hold ASCII, "ASCII in G0, G1, G2, and G3" (§3.6.4.1), so a single shift there draws ASCII where it drew DEC Supplemental (`level_one_single_shifts_draw_ascii`), and a DECRC with nothing saved loads them too (`a_full_reset_from_level_one_loads_the_level_four_sets`). What each set draws is in the rows below |
| `ESC - ` / `ESC . ` / `ESC / ` | designate a 96-character set into G1, G2, G3 | done — since 2026-10-19, the six `[[source: ctlseqs]]` lists, each translated from the ISO 8859 right half its final is registered as: `A` Latin-1, `B` Latin-2, `F` Greek, `H` Hebrew, `L` Latin-Cyrillic, `M` Latin-5 (`scripts/gen-iso96-sets.py`, from `[[source: iso8859-1]]` and the five others; `every_listed_96_set_is_translated`). In GL, 2/0 and 7/15 are the set's characters (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.3.1); in GR, 10/0 and 15/15 (8.3.2). An unassigned position draws DEC's error character (070-3 §3.4.3). Another final, or `ESC , F` (02/12, reserved), is designated `Other` or counted |
| `ESC n` / `ESC o` | LS2 / LS3 lock G2, G3 into GL | done — since 2026-10-19 (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.3.1; `ls2_invokes_g2_into_gl`, `ls3_invokes_g3_into_gl`), at every level DA could claim: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` §3.6.1.1 requires them at Level 1, where the VT220 and VT510 manuals withhold them. Since 2026-10-22, **from Level 2 of those DECSCL selects**, their own fields' "Levels: 2, 3", as the VT420's Table 4-1 ignores them at Level 1 (`[[source: vt420-rm]]`; `level_one_does_not_execute_a_level_two_function`; the DECSCL row) |
| `ESC ~` / `ESC }` / `ESC \|` | LS1R / LS2R / LS3R lock G1, G2, G3 into GR | done — since 2026-10-19 (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.3.2; `the_right_locking_shifts_move_gr`). A GR byte under `ESC % @` reads through the slot; under UTF-8, where no byte is GR's, the slot is state DECCIR reports |
| `ESC N` / `ESC O`, `8E` / `8F` under `ESC % @` | SS2 / SS3 single shift | done — since 2026-10-19: the next graphic character, 2/0-7/15 or under ISO 8859-1 10/0-15/15 stripped to seven bits, from G2 or G3 (`[[source: ecma35]]` 8.4; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 3-80/3-81; `ss2_draws_one_character_from_g2`, `the_eight_bit_single_shifts_and_a_gr_operand`); at Level 1 that is ASCII, since 2026-10-30, after DECSCL 61 and after `ESC <` (the DECSCL row, `level_one_single_shifts_draw_ascii`, `leaving_vt52_leaves_only_level_one_sets`). Anything else cancels the shift and is processed as if it had not come, SO and SI excepted, any number of which act and keep it (`a_control_or_sequence_cancels_a_pending_single_shift`, `shift_out_and_shift_in_between_keep_the_single_shift`). Under UTF-8 too (AE4, decided). A final after an intermediate, `ESC SP N`, is not a single shift at any read split |
| `ESC ( 0` / `ESC F` (VT52) | DEC Special Graphics glyph table | **partial** — `0x60..0x7E` translate through `[[source: foot-vt100-0]]`'s 31 entries, and the cell keeps `CellFlags::GRAPHICS`. **`0x5F` stays `_`**, where every DEC text read replaces it with Blank: `[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]` Table 3-9 ("ASCII codes 137 through 176", octal), `[[source: vt102-ug-ch5]]` Table 5-15, `[[source: dec-special-graphics]]` Table 2-4 and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` Appendix A, Table 2. None of the DEC texts read gives Blank a codepoint, so whether it stores U+0020 or U+00A0 is open. The gap is the same on the bulk run, the per-byte path and VT52 `ESC F` |
| `ESC ( A` | UK NRCS designation | done — reported as `Charset::Uk`, **not aliased to ASCII**, and `#` is `£` through `[[source: xterm-charsets]]`. It acts in every mode, as the national sets do; whether it should is the gap on the translation row, with STD 070's UK a Level 1 extension "selected in Set-Up to replace ASCII" (§5.8.1 note 3, p. 5-115) and xterm translating it without national mode at level 1 (`[[source: xterm-charsets-c]]` `charsets.c:404-406`) |

This row said "recorded, not translated" after the tables landed |
| `ESC ( <` / `ESC ( % 5` | DEC Supplemental | done — translated since 2026-10-12, through a 94-entry table (`dec_sets_data.rs`, indexed by `byte - 0x21`) that `scripts/gen-dec-sets.py` generates from `[[source: dec-special-graphics]]` Table 2-3's right half, read at generation: its 81 glyphs, and in its 13 empty cells DEC's error character, U+2426 (`DEC_ERROR_CHARACTER`), as `[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` §A.2 says ("The error character is printed when one of these reserved characters is received by the device") and Figure A-2 hatches, the two masks equal; for a video terminal, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-3 §3.4.3 states it for 10/0 ("treated as an unassigned graphic character code, and will cause the error character"), read for reserved positions by analogy (**inferred**); `[[source: vt510-ch7]]`'s control table has "Reserved 160 ... Error character generated". xterm's `map_DEC_Supp_Graphic` (`[[source: xterm-charsets]]`), practice, agrees but at 5/15, where it draws `_` and DEC has ß; the generator fails on any other difference. `<` is DEC Supplemental in `[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]` Table 4-4 and the UPSS in 070-3 p. 3-63, note AE's Q14, open; both readings draw this table while baud has no DECAUPSS, the UPSS defaulting to DEC Supplemental (`[[source: vt510-decaupss]]`). It draws where it is invoked into GL, from G0 or from G1 by `SO`; G2, its home, has no invocation here (note AE). Until then both finals drew their byte, which conforms under no reading. **Open beside it**: whether a set STD 070 §5.8.1 requires at Levels 2-4 acts while DA answers VT102 (note AF's Q-Level); this row takes the reading every other designation here takes, that a listed final acts at every level. It stays `done` beside Q-Level and the clustering question on the JIS-Katakana row, because neither is a gap in this set: Q-Level is asked of every final STD 070 §5.8.1 assigns a level, the national designations among them, whose row is `done`, and the clustering question is about the cluster machine, while this set's table is complete (`dec_supplemental_is_table_2_3`, the table against Table 2-3 read at test time; `every_dec_set_position_renders_as_its_table_says_on_both_paths`, every position alone and in a run, under `ESC ( <`, `ESC ( % 5` and `ESC ) % 5 SO`; `each_dec_set_draws_what_its_dec_text_says`; `a_dec_set_designation_is_chunk_invariant`; `rep_repeats_a_dec_set_character`; `decrc_restores_a_dec_set`) |
| `ESC ( I` | JIS-Katakana | done — translated since 2026-10-12: 2/1-5/15 are JIS X 0201's katakana, which `[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` §A.12 names ("specified by JIS X 0201-1976. There are 63 characters defined"), through `[[source: unicode-jis0201]]`'s 0xA1-0xDF, U+FF61-U+FF9F; 6/0-7/14 are reserved and draw U+2426 (§A.12; `[[source: vt382-rm]]` §2.4.6, "displays a reverse question mark", and Figure 2-10). xterm's `map_JIS_Katakana` agrees but at 7/14, which it leaves `~`. Each byte is one cell and ends any cluster, U+FF9E and U+FF9F among them, which are `Extend`: arriving as UTF-8 they would join the katakana before (`[[source: uax29]]` GB9), and through the set each is the character at its position, as DEC charts every code of a set as one character (that a VT382 displays it so is inferred); DEC Supplemental's U+00A9 likewise opens no GB11 lookback for a `ZWJ` after it. Whether a translated character should enter the cluster machine as the same codepoint in UTF-8 does is open. The row stays `done` beside that question and note AF's Q-Level (STD 070 §5.8.1 lists JIS-Katakana at "2x, 3x, 4x"), because neither is a gap in this set: the clustering question is about the cluster machine and covers DEC Supplemental's U+00A9 too, while this set's table is complete, and Q-Level is asked of every final STD 070 §5.8.1 assigns a level, the national designations among them, whose row is `done` (`every_character_set_writes_printable_ascii_as_class_other` names the three; `jis_katakana_is_jis_x_0201_and_reserves_columns_6_and_7`, against JIS0201.TXT read at test time; `every_translated_position_is_one_narrow_cell`; the Term tests in the row above) |

### CSI operations

Final bytes from `[[source: ctlseqs]]` § "Functions using CSI".

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `CSI @` | ICH insert blanks | done — since 2026-10-21 the Sixel and ReGIS pixels from the cursor to the right margin are cleared, as a VT340 was seen to clear them (the Sixel row; `a_shift_of_characters_clears_the_sixel_from_the_cursor`). **ICH leaves the cursor where it is**, DEC STD 070's note 3, "The Active Position is not affected by execution of this control" (p. 5-142), and xterm's, where `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.64 moves the active position to the line home: a curses program sends `ich1` "just before sending the character to be inserted" (terminfo(5)), and the character would land at the line's start. A departure from ECMA-48 8.3.64, taken for that reason (`ich_leaves_the_cursor_where_it_is`). An extent SEE chooses other than the line takes the shift along it (the `CSI Ps Q` row) |
| `CSI Ps SP @` / `CSI Ps SP A` | SL / SR scroll left and right | done — since 2026-10-19, **the screen's text moves left or right and the cursor stays**: `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.121 and 8.3.135, "the data appear to move to the left" and "The active presentation position is not affected", within the rectangle the four margins bound (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3, "All scrolling, both vertical and horizontal, is limited to this area"), wherever the cursor stands (xterm, sharing DECIC's code, does nothing with the cursor outside the margins). The columns brought in are blanked as SU and SD blank a line, the current background or the default under DECECM, where DECIC and DECDC take normal rendition by their own text, DECECM's "background color used when text is erased or new text is scrolled on to the screen" (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-48). On a double-width line the text moves to the right margin, as DEC's own horizontal shifts move it (DECFI and DECBI, "regardless of their line attributes", pp. 5-37 and 5-39), so what goes past the half is kept and the line made single-width shows it (it stopped at the half until then) (`sl_moves_every_row_left_and_blanks_the_right`, `the_margins_bound_the_shift_and_the_cursor_does_not_gate_it`, `the_columns_brought_in_take_the_scroll_fill`, `a_double_width_line_shifts_to_the_right_margin`). A prefixed form is counted |
| `CSI A` | CUU cursor up | done — **the top margin stops it from at or below it, DECOM or not**, else the first line (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CUU, notes 1 and 2, p. 5-41; its algorithm tests `line >= top_margin` and no mode), as xterm's `CursorUp` and ghostty's `cursorUp`, since 2026-10-15 (`tests/vt100/cuu_cud.rs`; `cuu_cud_and_cub_move_and_stop_at_the_edges`); until then a region bounded it only under DECOM. **It ends a pending wrap**, since 2026-11-01, so the next character lands on the column the cursor moved from and not back at the start of the line it left: D.6.1 lists CURSOR UP among the controls that reset the Last Column Flag (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. D-13 and D-14), and xterm's `CursorUp` calls `ResetWrap` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 284) (`cuu_and_cud_end_the_wrap`). VT52's `ESC A` keeps the page's edge (p. A-23); alacritty and sfte go to the page's edge, pinned as their departures in `baud-difftest` and `baud-sfte-difftest` |
| `CSI B` | CUD cursor down | done — **the bottom margin stops it from at or above it, DECOM or not**, else the last line (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CUD, notes 1 and 2, p. 5-43), as xterm's `CursorDown` and ghostty's `cursorDown`, since 2026-10-15 (`tests/vt100/cuu_cud.rs`; `cuu_cud_and_cub_move_and_stop_at_the_edges`); until then a region bounded it only under DECOM. **It ends a pending wrap**, as `CUU` does, since 2026-11-01 (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 263; `cuu_and_cud_end_the_wrap`). VT52's `ESC B` keeps the page's edge (p. A-24); alacritty and sfte go to the page's edge, pinned as their departures in `baud-difftest` and `baud-sfte-difftest` |
| `CSI C` | CUF cursor forward | done — from at or inside the right margin it stops there, from beyond it at the last column (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CUF notes 1-2, p. 5-45; `cuf_inside_the_fence_stops_at_the_right_margin`, `cuf_beyond_the_right_margin_stops_at_the_last_column`), since 2026-10-15. A cursor a move placed at right + 1 is beyond the margin and goes on, under `?7h` too since 2026-10-15 (`cuf_from_a_cursor_placed_one_past_the_margin_moves_on`; the `CSI s` row), where it read as a pending wrap; the sentinel a write leaves there stays on the margin (`cuf_at_a_pending_wrap_stays_on_the_right_margin`). With BDSM reset it steps by the positions as drawn, in both DCSM states, rightwards on the screen on a right-to-left line, as `CUB` steps leftwards (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.20, 8.3.18; the DCSM row) |
| `CSI D` | CUB cursor back | done — from at or right of the left margin it stops there, from left of it at column 1 (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CUB notes 1-2, p. 5-47; `cub_inside_the_fence_stops_at_the_left_margin`, `cub_left_of_the_fence_goes_to_column_one`), since 2026-10-15; ghostty without `?45` passes the margin, pinned. **After a full line it counts from the last column**, since 2026-11-01: `CUB 2` from a wrap pending on column 20 lands on column 18, as `BS` does and `HPB` counts, and at a right margin from the margin (the `0x08` row; `bs_and_cub_count_from_the_last_column`); the wrap was its first step before. Under `?1045` with `?7` set `CUB` wraps a count of columns up, and from the top margin round to the bottom margin's line (the `?1045` row) |
| `CSI E` | CNL cursor next line | done — the line it lands on starts as `CR` would (`cnl_and_cpl_start_at_the_left_margin`, `cnl_and_cpl_judge_the_line_they_land_on`), as xterm's, after moving as `CUD` does, so the bottom margin stops it (`cnl_and_cpl_stop_at_the_margins`, `cnl_and_cpl_stopped_at_a_margin_start_at_the_left_margin`, since 2026-10-15); `[[source: vt510-cnl]]` says only "the first character", and no DEC algorithm has a CNL: open |
| `CSI F` | CPL cursor preceding line | done — as `CNL`'s row, moving as `CUU` does (`[[source: vt510-cpl]]`; `cnl_and_cpl_start_at_the_left_margin`, `cnl_and_cpl_stop_at_the_margins`) |
| `CSI G` | CHA cursor char absolute | done — with BDSM reset to the cell drawn at the position, in both DCSM states (the DCSM row) |
| `CSI H` / `CSI f` | CUP / HVP | done — `CUP` moves the active presentation position and `HVP` the active data position (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.21, 8.3.63), the same cell but with BDSM reset on a line holding a string, where `CUP` takes the cell drawn at the position in both DCSM states (the DCSM row); under DECOM from the top and left margins, stopping at the bottom and right (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` CUP, p. 5-50; `cup_under_origin_mode_addresses_from_the_left_margin`, since 2026-10-13: the column was absolute) |
| `CSI I` | CHT forward tabulation | done — `HT` n times, so under side margins the right margin stops it as it stops `HT` (`cht_stops_at_the_right_margin_as_ht_does`), since 2026-10-14; `[[source: vt510-cht]]` names no margin and STD 070 has no CHT: open |
| `CSI J` | ED erase in display | done — 0, 1 and 2, each line erased to its own end, a double line's half; **at a pending wrap every one ends it**, since 2026-11-01, and 0 erases from the last column, the character the wrap waited after, and ends the row's soft wrap (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` D.6.1, pp. D-13 and D-14, and 070-5's revision item 5, p. 5-218; xterm's `ClearBelow`, `ClearAbove` and `ClearScreen` reset `do_wrap`, `[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 2223, 2311-2314 and 2367; ghostty's `eraseDisplay`): before, 0 erased nothing on the cursor's row and the next character wrapped (`the_erases_from_the_cursor_start_on_the_last_column_and_end_the_wrap`, `the_whole_display_erases_end_the_wrap`, `el_and_ed_from_a_pending_wrap_end_the_rows_soft_wrap`); a line erased whole is single-width again, the cursor's from its first column (ED 0) or through its last (ED 1) (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` ED note 1 and algorithm, pp. 5-156 to 5-158; `ed_sets_a_line_it_erases_whole_to_single_width`); `3`, the saved lines, is xterm's and has its row in Tier 3. `CSI 4 J` and above stay counted. Since 2026-10-14, on the primary narrower than its rows hold, each row it erases to the end loses what the line held past the edge too, so a growth shows no erased line's colour (`ed_while_narrow_clears_the_rows_it_covers_past_the_edge`). **The erase's colour is DECECM's** (the `?117` row): the current background, every other attribute cleared, at power-on, the screen background once set, which is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s empty rendition (ED, EL, pp. 5-154 and 5-156; ECH's "attributes set to normal", p. 5-152). Under DECECM reset an erased position keeps its background and loses every other attribute, reverse and the foreground included: `[[source: vt520-rm]]`'s ED, EL and ECH "clear all character attributes", as ghostty, foot and alacritty keep the background alone. **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list, is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI K` | EL erase in line | done — to the line's end, a double line's half, keeping its rendition (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` EL note 1, p. 5-154; `el_and_the_selective_erases_keep_the_rendition`). **At a pending wrap it erases from the last column and ends the wrap**, since 2026-11-01, so `CSI K` after a full line clears its last character and the next one lands there: "from the Active Column to the end of the Active Line (inclusive)" (p. 5-154), the Active Column the last (D.6.1, pp. D-13 and D-14), as xterm's `ClearRight` does, "with the right part cleared, we can't be wrapping", clearing the row's soft wrap (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 2311-2314), and ghostty; 1 and 2 end it too, `ClearInLine2`'s `ResetWrap` (2223). Before, 0 erased nothing and the next character wrapped (`the_erases_from_the_cursor_start_on_the_last_column_and_end_the_wrap`, `the_whole_line_erases_end_the_wrap`, `el_and_ed_from_a_pending_wrap_end_the_rows_soft_wrap`, `erase_to_start_with_a_pending_wrap_covers_the_whole_row`). Since 2026-10-14, `0` and `2` on the primary narrower than its rows hold clear what the line held past the edge, and `1` leaves it (`el_while_narrow_clears_what_a_bar_held_past_its_mark`, `el_1_while_narrow_leaves_what_the_line_held_past_the_edge`). **The erase's colour is DECECM's** (the `?117` row): the current background, every other attribute cleared, at power-on, the screen background once set, which is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s empty rendition (ED, EL, pp. 5-154 and 5-156; ECH's "attributes set to normal", p. 5-152). Under DECECM reset an erased position keeps its background and loses every other attribute, reverse and the foreground included: `[[source: vt520-rm]]`'s ED, EL and ECH "clear all character attributes", as ghostty, foot and alacritty keep the background alone. **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list, is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI Ps O` | EA erase in area | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program can erase within a guarded area, or between guarded areas, without touching the rest.** "EA causes some or all character positions in the active qualified area (the qualified area in the presentation component which contains the active presentation position) to be put into the erased state", 0 to the area's end, 1 from its start, 2 all of it (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.37). baud's qualified areas are SPA and EPA's guarded areas, a flag on each position, so the active area is the run, in the character path and across lines, of positions sharing the active position's guard: a guarded area, or the unguarded positions between two or between one and the page's edge, each line to its own end; with no guarded area, the page. **Under DCSM's PRESENTATION**, with BDSM reset, since 2026-10-26, the run is read over the positions as drawn, each line's, and the cells drawn at the positions it covers are erased where they stand: "the qualified area in the presentation component which contains the active presentation position" (8.3.37; the DCSM row; `ea_erases_the_area_drawn_at_its_position`); under DATA over the cells. Open departure: two guarded areas that touch read as one, where `[[source: ecma48]]` 6.5.2 ends a qualified area at the beginning of the next. Under ERM reset the guarded positions are spared, so EA inside a guarded area does nothing; set, they are erased and stay guarded. The blank is DECECM's. The first parameter only, the rest counted; a value past 2 is refused as ED's is. No DAQ, so no other kind of qualified area. On a double-width line each line stops at its half and the cells the half hides stay, as EL's do, `x <= end_of_line(line)` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-155).
