On the primary while it is narrower than its rows hold, since 2026-10-19 a row EA erases to its end is cleared past the edge first, as EL's is, so a wider screen later shows no trace of what was erased; until then it showed the old line's colour there (`ea_outside_a_guarded_area_erases_the_unguarded_run`, `ea_inside_a_guarded_area_follows_erm`, `ea_with_no_guarded_area_erases_the_page`, `ea_and_ef_read_their_first_parameter`, `ea_and_ef_on_a_double_width_line_stop_at_its_half`, `ea_while_narrow_clears_the_rows_it_erases_to_their_end`). |
| `CSI Ps N` | EF erase in field | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program can erase one tab-stop field of a line.** "EF causes some or all character positions of the active field ... to be put into the erased state" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.40), a field being "the character position at a character tabulation stop (beginning of the field) and the character positions up to, but not including, the character position at the following character tabulation stop" (4.2.40). A field stays in its line: the line's start bounds the positions before its first stop and its end those after its last, and with no stops the line is one field. **Under DCSM's PRESENTATION**, with BDSM reset, since 2026-10-26, the field is the one holding the cursor's presentation position, its stops at presentation positions as `HT` counts them, and the cells drawn there are erased where they stand: "the field which contains the active presentation position in the presentation component" (8.3.40; `ef_erases_the_field_drawn_at_its_position`); under DATA the cells' field. Guarded positions go as ERM says and the blank is DECECM's; the first parameter only, as EA. On a double-width line a field ends at the line's half, and its stops are the line's character positions, as HT's algorithm indexes its stops by the line's column (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-68). A field that reaches the line's end clears the line past a narrower edge first, since 2026-10-19, as EA does (`ef_erases_within_the_field`, `ef_fields_stay_within_their_line`, `ef_spares_a_guarded_position_under_erm_reset`, `ea_and_ef_on_a_double_width_line_stop_at_its_half`, `ef_while_narrow_clears_past_the_edge_when_its_field_reaches_the_end`). |
| `CSI L` | IL insert lines | done — nothing outside the region or the side margins (`[[source: vt510-il]]`, STD 070 p. 5-24; `il_and_dl_outside_the_side_margins_do_nothing`), a pending wrap counting as on the right margin (`il_at_a_pending_wrap_is_inside_the_margins`); under side margins it moves only the rectangle the four margins bound (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3, "All scrolling, both vertical and horizontal, is limited to this area") from the cursor's row (`il_and_dl_move_the_rectangle_from_the_cursor_row`). **And it puts the cursor on the left margin**, column 1 at full width (STD 070 IL note 2, p. 5-146; `il_and_dl_at_full_width_go_to_column_one`, `il_and_dl_inside_the_fence_go_to_the_left_margin`), as xterm and ghostty do and alacritty does not, since 2026-10-15; until then it left the column **Since 2026-10-21 the ReGIS page's pixels on the lines it moves go with them**, the line brought in cleared, as a Sixel already did by its anchor (the Sixel row; `the_lines_a_band_moves_take_the_page_with_them`). **Since 2026-10-25 the lines either side of what it inserts end**: the row above, the row its moved lines end on and, at the primary's top, the history's newest line, as `lines_bounded` ends them for IL at row 0, so a line it brings in under a wrapped row is a paragraph of its own, terminal-wg's rule for SD and SU ("wherever lines are removed or inserted, we ensure that the boundaries are all hard newlines"), which baud applies to IL and DL as well (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`; the SCP row; `su_sd_il_and_dl_end_the_lines_either_side_of_what_they_move`).. The cursor goes to SLH's line home once one is set, and SEE 1, 2 and 3 limit the lines shifted (their rows) |
| `CSI M` | DL delete lines | done — as IL's row, inverted (`[[source: vt510-dl]]`), the cursor to the left margin too (DL note 2, p. 5-148). **Since 2026-10-21 the ReGIS page's pixels on the lines it moves go with them**, the line brought in cleared, as a Sixel already did by its anchor (the Sixel row; `the_lines_a_band_moves_take_the_page_with_them`). **Since 2026-10-25 the lines either side of what it inserts or removes end**, the row above and the row its moved lines end on, so a line it brings in under a wrapped row is a paragraph of its own, terminal-wg's rule for SD and SU ("wherever lines are removed or inserted, we ensure that the boundaries are all hard newlines"), which baud applies to IL and DL as well (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`; the SCP row; `su_sd_il_and_dl_end_the_lines_either_side_of_what_they_move`).. The cursor goes to SLH's line home once one is set, and SEE 1, 2 and 3 limit the lines shifted (their rows) |
| `CSI P` | DCH delete chars | done — since 2026-10-21 the Sixel and ReGIS pixels from the cursor to the right margin are cleared, as a VT340 was seen to clear them (the Sixel row; `a_shift_of_characters_clears_the_sixel_from_the_cursor`). An extent SEE chooses other than the line takes the shift along it (the `CSI Ps Q` row) |
| `CSI S` | SU scroll up | done — **and it now moves the grid**; the grid is a ring and the screen a window into it, so the scroll copies nothing. Under side margins it moves only the rectangle and keeps nothing in the history (`su_moves_only_the_rectangle`, `the_whole_screen_under_side_margins_keeps_no_history`): the DEC texts make SU a pan (STD 070 p. 5-91), so what it does to data is ECMA-48 § 8.3.147's inside the area § 5.4.3 bounds. **Since 2026-10-25 the lines either side of what it inserts or removes end**, the row above and the row its moved lines end on, so a line it brings in under a wrapped row is a paragraph of its own, terminal-wg's "wherever lines are removed or inserted, we ensure that the boundaries are all hard newlines" (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`; the SCP row; `su_sd_il_and_dl_end_the_lines_either_side_of_what_they_move`). |
| `CSI T` | SD scroll down | done — **one parameter**; five is XTHIMOUSE. Under side margins as SU's row (`sd_moves_only_the_rectangle`). Since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps the window over the history (note V, V2): on the primary, a full-screen scroll down that takes back the window's line, or the last line of the history, sends the window to the live screen at that byte, at every read size (`a_take_back_that_reaches_the_window_sends_it_live_at_that_byte`). **Since 2026-10-25 the lines either side of what it inserts or removes end**, the row above, the row its moved lines end on and, at the primary's top, the history's newest line, so a line it brings in under a wrapped row is a paragraph of its own, terminal-wg's "wherever lines are removed or inserted, we ensure that the boundaries are all hard newlines" (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`; the SCP row; `su_sd_il_and_dl_end_the_lines_either_side_of_what_they_move`). |
| `CSI Ps ^` | SIMD select implicit movement direction | **out** — counted, and the screen does not scroll. The pinned `[[source: ecma48]]` gives `CSI Ps 05/14` to SIMD and SD `05/04`; `[[source: ctlseqs]]` keeps `CSI Ps ^` as SD, "a publication error in the original ECMA-48 5th edition (1991) corrected in 2003", and no pinned terminfo entry sends it. baud has no implicit-movement directions to select (note AB), so SIMD waits on them (`csi_caret_is_simd_and_counted_not_a_scroll`) |
| `CSI Ps ; Ps ; Ps ; Ps ; Ps T` | XTHIMOUSE initiate highlight tracking | done — the program's answer to a `?1001` press: it starts the region the app draws and the release reports, `func` 0 aborts, and one no press is owed changes nothing. It used to be read as SD and **scrolled the screen**. `a_tracked_release_reports_the_region_in_the_long_form`, `func_zero_aborts_and_the_release_is_normal`, `an_unsolicited_answer_is_ignored`, `the_answer_lands_the_same_at_every_read_split` |
| `CSI X` | ECH erase chars | done — **at a pending wrap it erases from the last column and ends the wrap and the row's soft wrap**, since 2026-11-01, as EL 0 does and xterm's ECH, `ClearRight`, does (the `CSI K` row); under ERM reset, a guarded position among the `n` is spared, as ED and EL spare one. Under ERM set it is erased and stays guarded (the `ESC V` row). **The erase's colour is DECECM's** (the `?117` row): the current background, every other attribute cleared, at power-on, the screen background once set, which is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s empty rendition (ED, EL, pp. 5-154 and 5-156; ECH's "attributes set to normal", p. 5-152). Under DECECM reset an erased position keeps its background and loses every other attribute, reverse and the foreground included: `[[source: vt520-rm]]`'s ED, EL and ECH "clear all character attributes", as ghostty, foot and alacritty keep the background alone. **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI Z` | CBT backward tabulation | done — since 2026-10-27, from a wrap waiting at the last column or a DECSLRM right margin it counts from the column the wrap waits on, as from the line limit's, so a stop on that column is not "the n-th preceding character tabulation stop" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.7) and the cursor goes to the one before it, where it stayed put; a cursor a move placed one past the margin counts from there (`cbt_from_a_wrap_waiting_at_the_lines_end_counts_from_its_column`, which holds `HT` and `CHT` from the same waits) |
| `CSI Pn Y` | CVT cursor line tabulation | done — since 2026-10-19, to the line of the n-th following line tabulation stop, the column kept (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.23; `cvt_counts_its_stops`). When the stops run out first, the bottom margin inside the scrolling region and the last line outside it, as HT stops at the right margin (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` HT note 1), and it never scrolls (`cvt_past_the_last_stop_goes_to_the_bottom_margin`) |
| `CSI Ps W` | CTC cursor tabulation control | done — since 2026-10-19, every parameter in order (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.17, "Notation: (Ps...)"): 0 and 1 set a character stop at the cursor and a line stop on its line, 2 to 6 clear what TBC 0, 1, 2, 3 and 4 clear, any other value counted (`ctc_sets_and_clears_both_kinds`, `ctc_walks_every_parameter`) |
| `CSI ? 5 W` | DECST8C tab stops every eight columns | done — since 2026-10-19, every stop cleared and one set at column 9 and every eighth after (`[[source: vt510-decst8c]]`; `decst8c_sets_a_stop_every_eight_columns`). Only the form the texts give: `CSI ? W` and any other value are counted, where xterm also takes a bare or 0 parameter (`decst8c_takes_only_five`) |
| ``CSI ` `` | HPA char position absolute | done — the active data position (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.57): the cell in every DCSM state, where `CHA` takes the position drawn (the DCSM row); a program under DCSM's DATA reaches a data column with it, as TR/53's worked example 2 reaches one with `CHA` (`tr53_example_2_data_lines_by_the_data_movers`) |
| `CSI a` | HPR char position relative | done — to "the last position on the line" (`[[source: vt510-hpr]]`) whatever the side margins (`hpr_passes_the_right_margin`), as xterm's `CursorSet` and ghostty; under DECOM confined (the `?6` row). **From a wrap waiting at a DECSLRM right margin it counts from the margin**, since 2026-10-27, as `HPB` does and as xterm's cursor column stays on the margin while the wrap waits: margins 1 to 10, ten letters, `HPR` to 11, where it went to 12 (`hpr_from_a_wrap_waiting_at_the_right_margin_counts_from_the_margin`); from SLL's line limit, from the limit (the SLL row). No DEC algorithm has an HPR: open |
| `CSI j` | HPB char position backward | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can move the cursor back a number of columns with ECMA-48's own control**: "moved by n character positions ... in the direction opposite to that of the character progression" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.58, `CSI Pn 06/10`, default 1; `hpb_moves_back_n_columns`). No DEC text and no ctlseqs entry has it, so it mirrors HPR: to the line's first position whatever the side margins (`hpb_passes_the_left_margin_as_hpr_passes_the_right`), confined under DECOM (`under_decom_hpb_and_vpb_stop_at_the_margins`), no reverse wrap (`hpb_does_not_reverse_wrap`). From a pending wrap it counts from the last column, where the cursor stands, and the wrap is gone, as `CUB` does since 2026-11-01 (`from_a_pending_wrap_hpb_counts_from_the_last_column`); `CUB` took the wrap as its first step before, which xterm's `CursorBack` does only under `?45` or `?1045` with `?7`, and X11R5's only with REVERSEWRAP and WRAPAROUND both (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 153, `[[source: xterm-x11r5-cursor-c]]` 81-83). Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI b` | REP repeat preceding char | done — **after every writer**: a run of UTF-8 text the bulk writer took and a character the cell table read now leave REP what they wrote, where sixteen UTF-8 `Ä` and `CSI 2 b` wrote nothing until 2026-10-19 (`rep_repeats_the_last_character_of_every_writer`, `rep_repeats_a_character_the_table_writes`, the property atom `rep`). **A copy made after GL's set changed is the character as drawn, counted as the code the original was received as**: German `[` drawn `Ä`, `ESC ( B`, `CSI 2 b` draws two more `Ä` that count 0x5B, ECMA-48 8.3.103 repeating "the preceding character", a graphic character being one "that has a coded representation" (4.2.43) (foot's line drawing (`[[source: foot-csi]]`, `[[source: foot-term-reset]]`) and kitty (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`) keep the character, ghostty re-maps its code (`[[source: ghostty-terminal-9bbb9b2]]`), and xterm repeats nothing after any control (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `doparsing`); `rep_after_a_set_change_copies_the_character_and_its_code`). After a cluster each copy is its first character (`rep_after_a_cluster_repeats_its_first_character`) |
| `CSI c` | DA device attributes | done — identifies as VT102, and adds Sixel (attribute `4`) only when the embedder opts in (`Term::set_sixel_claim`, the app's `[images] advertise_sixel`, **off by default**) **and** has provisioned image memory. Off by default because an application that reads the claim stops drawing its text fallback (for yazi, chafa's grid of block elements, box-drawing strokes and coloured spaces), and the claim was held back until that fallback was proven to render right -- which `baud-core/tests/image_preview.rs` now does, cell for cell, on two real chafa captures. A claim is only read by an application that decides from the DA: yazi takes a terminal brand from `TERM_PROGRAM` first, which baud sets to `baud` so an outer terminal's name does not decide it; an unprovisioned baud refuses every Sixel, so it answers `?6c` even when opted in. `the_primary_da_advertises_sixel_only_when_opted_in_and_provisioned` pins all three. **Only `Ps` 0 or omitted is answered**, since 2026-10-18: `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.24, "With a parameter value not equal to 0, DA is used to identify the device which sends the DA", and xterm answers only `GetParam(0) <= 0`; any other first parameter gets no reply and is counted in `unsupported` (`a_da_with_a_nonzero_parameter_is_not_answered_and_is_counted`). Until then `CSI 1 c` was answered as a request. **Open departure**: ReGIS is built and DA lists no attribute `3`, against 070-4's DA, "The rest of the parameters indicate the architectural extensions supported at the current operating level" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]`, PDF 221), its Table 1 registering "3 1-4 ReGIS Display" (PDF 223); baud lists Sixel's `4` there under its claim, and xterm sends `3` while its ReGIS is on (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 4165-4168). `3` stays out while DA1 answers as a VT102, `?6c` (the ReGIS row; note AW, section 7) |
| `CSI > c` | DA2 secondary device attributes | **out** — answered with silence and counted in `unsupported`. `[[source: ctlseqs]]` line 817 shapes the reply as `CSI > Pp ; Pv ; Pc c`, where `Pp` is a terminal *type* and `Pv` a firmware version. baud has neither honestly: it is not a VT220 and has no firmware, and an application parses whatever arrives as the report it asked for. That is the same refusal primary DA makes by under-claiming as a VT102, one step further. Recorded here because real capture proved it is asked for — vim sends it once in `tests/recordings/vim-unicode.raw`, and until that landed the table did not mention DA2 at all. `every_fixture_owes_the_same_reply` pins the silence *and* the count |
| `CSI > 0 q` | XTVERSION report the terminal's name and version | done — `Ps` 0, omitted included, answers `DCS > | Pt ST`, the one report baud sends that is not a CSI, as `[[source: ctlseqs]]` ("CSI > Ps q, Ps = 0 -> Report xterm name and version (XTVERSION)") shapes it; `Pt` is `baud(<version>)`, the name and the workspace version in xterm's own form, `XTerm(411)` (`[[source: xterm-version]]`), since 2026-10-16 (until then `baud`, a name with no version), and `TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION` is the same version (`xtversion_names_baud_and_its_version`, `the_environment_names_what_xtversion_reports`); any other `Ps` is unanswered (`xtversion_answers_with_a_dcs`, `xtversion_defaults_to_zero`, `an_unknown_secondary_query_is_not_answered`, `xtversion_does_not_collide_with_other_q_finals`, `baud-core/tests/xterm/keypad_version.rs`). The table had no row for it until 2026-10-08 |
| `CSI d` | VPA line position absolute | done |
| `CSI k` | VPB line position backward | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can move the cursor up a number of lines with ECMA-48's own control**: "moved by n line positions ... in a direction opposite to that of the line progression" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.159, `CSI Pn 06/11`, default 1; `vpb_moves_up_n_lines`), the column kept. It mirrors VPR: past the top margin to line 1 (`vpb_passes_the_top_margin_as_vpr_passes_the_bottom`), confined under DECOM, and a pending wrap left as VPR and CUU leave it. Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI e` | VPR line position relative | done |
| `CSI g` | TBC tab clear | done — all six of `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.154's values since 2026-10-19: 0 the character stop at the cursor, 1 the line stop on its line, 3 every character stop, 4 every line stop, 5 both kinds; **2, the character stops "in the active line", clears every character stop**, because baud's are the same in every line (TSM MULTIPLE; the `CSI 18` row). Any other value is counted and clears nothing (`tbc_1_clears_the_line_stop_at_the_cursor_and_nothing_else`, `tbc_2_clears_every_character_stop`, `tbc_5_clears_both_kinds`, `tbc_with_a_value_ecma48_does_not_define_is_counted`). `[[source: vt510-tbc]]` and xterm keep 0 and 3, "There are only two values for Ps"; ECMA-48's set is built |
| `CSI Pn SP d` | TSR tabulation stop remove | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can remove one tab stop by its column**: "TSR causes any character tabulation stop at character position n in the active line ... and lines of subsequent text in the presentation component to be cleared, but does not affect other tabulation stops" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.156). A character stop is in every line (TSM MULTIPLE, the `CSI 18` row), so the stop at column `n` goes from them all; position `n` is a presentation position, the column `HTS` sets a stop at with BDSM reset (the DCSM row). `Pn` has no default value, so an omitted or zero one clears nothing and is counted; a position past the line holds no stop. xterm and the DEC terminals have no TSR (`tsr_clears_the_stop_at_position_n_and_no_other`, `tsr_where_no_stop_is_set_clears_nothing`, `tsr_with_no_position_is_counted`, `tsr_with_a_private_marker_is_no_control`, `tsr_clears_the_stop_hts_set_at_the_presentation_position`) |
| `CSI Pn SP b` / `CSI Pn SP a` / ``CSI Pn SP ` `` / `CSI Pn1 ; Pn2 SP c` | TAC / TALE / TATE / TCC aligned tabulation stops | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can right-align, centre or decimal-align what it writes at a tab stop**: each sets "a character tabulation stop calling for" centring, leading edge or trailing edge alignment, or the centring of a target character, replacing "any tabulation stop previously set at that character position" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.151 to 8.3.153, 8.3.155). **The string is laid out again as each character arrives**: after `HT` or `CHT` lands on such a stop, each graphic character is put where the stop says, the string written so far moved back. The stop is the left edge of its cell, where TATE puts the first character's trailing edge, so TATE's string starts in the stop's cell, as an ordinary stop's; TALE's last character's leading edge is there, so its string ends in the cell before; TAC's string is centred on that edge, an odd length's extra column after it; TCC's string starts at the stop until the target arrives, which goes in the stop's cell, the text before it moved back, and what follows goes on after it; `Pn2` 32 when omitted, 32 to 127 or 160 to 255, "the code table position (binary value) of the target character in the currently invoked code": compared with the position a character was received at, so under DEC Special Graphics 113 finds the `q` drawn `─` and under the UK set 35 the `#` drawn `£`, and with a decoded character's scalar value. A format effector or a cursor movement ends the string, SGR and the other controls that move nothing do not: `CR`, `LF`, `VT`, `FF`, `IND`, `NEL` and `RI` end it themselves, where they scroll a line in under the cursor or leave it where it was; a string that would move back past the line's start, the left margin under DECLRMM, or on past its end goes on as plain text. `HTS` and `CTC 0` set an ordinary stop where an aligned one was, `TBC`, `TSR` and `CTC` clear one as any stop, and DECST8C, `RIS` and a restored DECTABSR or DECTSR leave ordinary stops; DECTABSR reports an aligned stop as a stop. An omitted or zero `Pn` and a target 8.3.155 does not permit are counted. Neither `[[source: ctlseqs]]` nor DEC STD 070 has them. With BDSM reset, on a line holding an SDS or SRS string, the stop is found at the cursor's presentation position and the string moves by the positions as drawn. **An erase or edit of the string's line ends it too**, since 2026-10-27, the decided rule's "and an erase or edit of the line", which until then was not built: ED, EL, ECH, ICH, DCH, IL, DL, EA, EF, DECSED, DECSEL, SU, SD, SL, SR, DECIC, DECDC, and DECERA, DECSERA, DECFRA and DECCRA's destination where their rectangle holds the line, `ICH` and `DCH` under SEE's page or qualified area where their shift runs through it, ED, EL, DECSED and DECSEL with a parameter list at each extent, each before it acts and past the guards that ignore it, so the text after it is plain; one that reaches only other lines, one the margins or the scrolling region make a no-op (IL and DL outside DECSTBM's region, ICH, DCH, DECIC and DECDC right of DECSLRM's), DECCARA and DECRARA, which change attributes, and DECDWL and DECDHL, which change how the line is drawn (`ESC # 6` on the string's line leaves the next character laid out), leave it open (`an_erase_or_edit_of_the_strings_line_ends_it`, `an_erase_of_another_line_or_an_attribute_change_keeps_it`). **A cursor movement ends it as it executes, wherever it leaves the cursor**, since 2026-10-27: the format effectors of 8.2.4 (`HPA`, `HPB`, `HPR`, `HVP`, `HTS`, `TBC`, `TSR`, `VPA`, `VPB`, `VPR`, `VTS`, PPA, PPB and PPR) and the cursor movements (`CUU`, `CUD`, `CUF`, `CUB`, `CUP`, `CNL`, `CPL`, `CHA`, `CHT`, `CBT`, `CVT`, NP, PP, `DECRC`, DECFI, `?1048`'s restore, VT52's moves and erases, and DECSTBM, DECSLRM, DECOM, DECCOLM and DECALN, which home the cursor, and DECRSPS's cursor restore and SPD's and SCP's `Ps2` 1), so `CUU` then `CUD`, a `CUP` to the cursor's own cell or `CUD` at the bottom margin ends it, where it ended only at the next character written elsewhere; DECSASD and a screen switch end it too, where a character on the alternate screen at the same row and column went on laying it out. No control leaves the cursor on another line with a string open, so an `IL` or `DL` there meets none. **A window resize that moves the cursor ends the string**, as DEC's page-size controls move the active position only when it no longer fits (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSCPP, DECSLPP): a narrowing that clamps the cursor or re-cuts its line, and a shortening that retires the rows above it; one that leaves the cursor where it was leaves the string's cells where they were, and the string goes on. The cursor is its cell: at a wrap waiting at the last column, a resize to the same width puts the cursor on that column and ends the string, and a widening leaves it one past, where `CPR` then names column 21, and keeps it (argued; no test pins the wait case) (`a_resize_that_moves_the_cursor_ends_the_string`, `a_cursor_movement_ends_the_string_wherever_it_leaves_the_cursor`, `a_control_that_homes_the_cursor_ends_the_string`, `a_screen_switch_ends_the_string`, `tac_centres_the_string_on_the_stop`, `tale_ends_the_string_at_the_stop`, `tate_starts_the_string_at_the_stop`, `tcc_puts_the_target_in_the_stops_cell`, `tcc_targets_the_space_by_default_and_latin1_letters`, `sgr_keeps_the_string_and_a_movement_ends_it`, `a_string_at_the_line_start_goes_on_as_text`, `cht_lands_on_an_aligned_stop`, `a_wide_character_is_centred_whole`, `hts_tsr_ris_and_decst8c_leave_ordinary_stops`, `a_missing_position_or_a_bad_target_is_counted`, `an_aligned_string_moves_by_the_positions_drawn`, `dectabsr_reports_an_aligned_stop`, `tcc_compares_the_code_table_position_received`, `a_format_effector_that_scrolls_ends_the_string`, `a_line_feed_in_or_below_a_region_ends_the_string`, `a_carriage_return_to_the_strings_column_ends_it`) |
| `CSI h` | SM set mode | done — LNM and IRM, and GATM, ERM, KAM, SRM, BDSM, DCSM and HEM (their rows, below). **Every mode in the list is set, in order**: `CSI 20 ; 4 h` sets LNM and IRM, as `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.125 gives the notation `(Ps...)` and xterm's `ansi_modes` walks every parameter; a mode baud does not act on, a hole and a bare `CSI h` are each counted in `unsupported_param`. Until 2026-10-19 only the first parameter was read (`sm_sets_every_mode_in_its_list`, `an_unrecognised_mode_in_the_list_is_counted_and_the_rest_apply`, `a_hole_in_the_list_is_counted_and_the_rest_apply`; the `sm-list` property atom) |
| `CSI 2 h` / `CSI 2 l` | KAM keyboard action mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can lock the keyboard**: while set, a key sends nothing and draws nothing (`[[source: vt510-kam]]`, "The terminal ignores all keystrokes that send characters to the host"; `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.11). Only the keys: a focus or mouse report, the wheel's cursor keys under `?1007` (`Term::wheel_key`) and a paste go on, the "part of the manual input facilities" ECMA-48 allows (`the_wheels_keys_are_neither_locked_nor_echoed`). The app's own menu shortcuts never reach `Term::key`, as VT510 exempts Set-Up, so the window can still be closed. Unlocked at power-on and by `RIS` and `DECSTR` ("Keyboard action KAM: Unlocked", `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`); `DECRQM` answers it (`kam_locks_the_keyboard_and_a_key_sends_nothing`, `kam_is_reported_and_unlocked_by_decstr_and_ris`). **And it is shown** since 2026-10-20: "Wait" on the indicator status line while the keyboard is locked, "the Wait indicator appears on the keyboard indicator line at the bottom of the screen" (`[[source: vt420-rm]]`, Unlocking the Keyboard; `[[source: vt510-kam]]`; `the_indicator_shows_wait_while_the_keyboard_is_locked`), a program showing the line with DECSSDT 1 (that row). With no status line, which baud powers up with, the lock is the embedder's to draw from `Term::keyboard_locked` |
| `CSI 12 h` / `CSI 12 l` | SRM send/receive mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **reset, a typed key appears on the screen as well as going to the program**: `[[source: vt510-srm]]`, "the terminal sends keyboard characters to the host and to the screen", and the key's bytes do there what they would from the program, an arrow's `CSI A` moving the cursor (`srm_reset_draws_a_typed_key_as_well_as_sending_it`, `the_echo_of_a_sequence_is_performed`). Only the keys, through a parser of the echo's own, so a key typed between the halves of a program's sequence lands on its own (`the_echo_does_not_land_inside_a_host_sequence`), and in the protocol the terminal is in, VT52's `ESC A` for Up in VT52 (`the_echo_follows_the_terminal_into_vt52`). **Set at power-on and by `RIS`**, VT510's "Default: No local echo" and xterm's, where `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.1 recommends the reset state: every pty echoes a key itself (`ris_sets_srm_and_decstr_keeps_it`); kept by `DECSTR`. Both app drivers draw the echo's frame at the key. **Open gap**: a control string the echo opens (Alt-`]` sends `ESC ]`, Alt-Shift-P `ESC P`) shares the host's string buffer |
| `CSI 18 $ p` | TSM tabulation stop mode, reported | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `4`, permanently reset: a character stop is in every line, MULTIPLE, the reset state (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.17), and cannot be made per-line, the one-state mode 7.1 allows ("some or all of the modes may have one state only"). `CSI 18 h` and `l` are counted (`tsm_is_reported_permanently_reset_and_cannot_be_set`) |
| `CSI 1 h` / `CSI 1 l` | GATM guarded area transfer mode | done — since 2026-10-27, **a print leaves out what a program guarded with SPA and EPA**: reset, GUARD, "Only the contents of unguarded areas in an eligible area are transmitted or transferred"; set, ALL, "The contents of guarded as well as of unguarded areas" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.7), a guarded area's contents being "excluded ... from transfer to an auxiliary input/output device" (6.5.2.2), a transfer MC initiates (6.6). Every print of screen contents is such a transfer, print page, screen, all pages, print line and autoprint; printer controller mode relays the host's bytes and is not one. Under GUARD each guarded position prints as a space, as an invisible character does, so what follows keeps its columns, and one at a line's end is left off as a trailing space is; every guarded position a print reaches is left out, an area only partly inside the print too, which 7.3.1 g leaves to the implementation. Built rather than the rows corrected, as baud builds a protocol whole. Reset at power-on and by `RIS` (7.1, 8.3.105); `DECSTR` keeps it, as it keeps ERM; DECRQM answers `1` or `2` for mode 1. Neither xterm's print nor DEC STD 070-7's reads a guard: practice, not followed. VT420 (Table 9-2) and VT510 (Table 4) list GATM as permanently reset, and xterm's DECRQM answers 4 for mode 1. baud answers 1 or 2 and lets SM 1 set ALL, as it does for HEM, which the same tables mark permanently reset. SATM, MATM and TTM are not built and SSA and ESA stay counted, so the active position is never in a selected area: under the modes' reset states 7.3.1 leaves the data stream's format undefined ("If the active presentation position is not within a selected area, the format of the data stream in the first and fourth case above is not defined by this Standard"), and a print takes the screen as their ALL states would (`under_gatm_guard_a_print_leaves_out_the_guarded_contents`, `every_screen_print_leaves_out_the_guarded_contents`, `a_guarded_area_partly_inside_a_print_is_left_out_there`, `decrqm_answers_gatm_and_ris_resets_it_and_decstr_keeps_it`; `every_spec_csi_form_is_handled_or_counted` sees `CSI 1 h` change a printed line, `CSI 1 l` being the state it starts in) |
| `CSI 6 h` / `CSI 6 l` | ERM erasure mode | done — since 2026-10-19: reset (PROTECT) at power-on and after `RIS`, as `[[source: ecma48]]` 7.1 recommends, and 8.3.105 lists "set the modes into the reset state" among what RIS "may imply, if applicable": ED, EL and ECH spare a guarded position (the `ESC V` row); set (ALL), "The contents of protected as well as of unprotected areas are affected by an erasure control function" (7.2.4). DECSTR keeps it, as neither DEC's soft-reset table nor xterm's names it. DECRQM answers `1` or `2` for mode 6 (`erm_is_set_reset_and_reported`). xterm has no ERM; built, as baud builds a protocol whole. EA and EF, the other two functions 7.2.4 names, follow it as ED does (the `CSI O` and `CSI N` rows). |
| `CSI 8 h` / `CSI 8 l` | BDSM bi-directional support mode | **partial** — since 2026-10-24, **set, Hebrew and Arabic lines are drawn right to left, from the start**: IMPLICIT, "All bi-directional aspects of data are handled by the device itself" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.1). Each row is drawn in the order the Unicode Bidirectional Algorithm gives its paragraph (`[[source: uax9-bidi]]`), the rows a soft wrap joins resolved as one, **each paragraph at the level its first strong letter gives it, falling back to the direction SCP or SPD set its lines to, or that direction outright with autodetection reset** (P2-P3, HL1; the SCP and `?2501` rows: a paragraph that starts in Hebrew or Arabic runs right to left, against the right edge, its erased cells laid out before its text, `[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-erased-cells]]`; **a paragraph holding a box-drawing character, U+2500-U+257F, is a table row**, laid out left to right with every rule in place and each cell between two rules a paragraph of its own at its own level, ordered inside its columns, UAX #9 section 3's table cells and HL4, a right-to-left cell against its cell's right side), on every draw path, and the cursor, the selection (copied in logical order, never a mirrored glyph), the find marks, the link underline and the mouse follow the drawn order (`docs/bidi.md`). Reset, EXPLICIT: every row in the order its cells hold, as before. **Set at power-on and by `RIS`**, since 2026-10-24: ECMA-48 7.1 recommends the reset state as the initial one, and its Annex D leaves "the initial state of a device upon power up including the settings of the modes" to the implementation; `RIS` returns the device "to its initial state, i.e. the state it has after it is made operational" (8.3.105), as VTE's does (`[[source: vte-modes-56bad78]]` 186, `[[source: vte-vteseq-cc-56bad78]]` 8315, `[[source: vte-modes-hh-56bad78]]`) and as SRM's row does here, where that clause's "set the modes into the reset state", one of what it "may imply, if applicable", would reset it. Kept by `DECSTR`, whose DEC table names no BDSM; `DECRQM` answers `1` or `2`, and DECTSR carries it for DECRSTS to restore (`decrsts_restores_what_every_tsi_control_reports`, from a state with it reset; `sm_8_sets_implicit_and_rm_8_resets_it`, `ris_returns_bdsm_to_set_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `bdsm_starts_implicit_and_decrqm_answers_set`; the three paths' pixels: `right_to_left_rows_draw_in_their_visual_order_on_every_arm`, and the cursor in each shape on an ordinary and a full reordered row, drawn there since 2026-10-24: `the_cursor_on_a_right_to_left_row_is_drawn_in_its_shape_on_every_arm`; the right edge: `a_right_to_left_paragraph_is_drawn_against_the_right_edge_on_every_arm`, `a_hebrew_paragraph_runs_right_to_left_against_the_right_edge`; a table row: `a_table_row_keeps_its_columns_on_every_arm`, `a_table_row_keeps_its_columns_and_each_cell_its_own_direction`, `a_dec_graphics_vertical_is_a_column_rule`). Terminal-wide, as ECMA-48's SM sets "the modes of the receiving device", where terminal-wg keeps it per paragraph (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-basic-modes]]`). Since 2026-10-25 **a status line in Hebrew reads right to left**: the host-writable line is laid out as a line of its own, under the direction SCP and `?2501` set on it (`a_hebrew_status_line_runs_from_the_right_edge`), and **a picture after a Hebrew caption sits after it**: a Sixel, kitty or iTerm2 picture on a row drawn right to left is placed mirrored by its anchor row's direction, unflipped (`a_picture_after_a_hebrew_caption_is_placed_mirrored`), and a kitty placeholder strip over its cells where they are drawn. Since 2026-10-27, **a finished paragraph is drawn at its words** while set (`[screen] wrap_words`, on by default; `baud_render::WordWrap`): no word split across two lines, and a run of the other direction the wrap would split given lines of its own in its own direction, a choice the project made and a departure from UAX #9's one level a paragraph for the moved lines (`docs/bidi.md`, "What you see"; `the_run_the_wrap_splits_gets_a_line_of_its_own_in_its_own_direction`, `a_re_broken_paragraph_draws_the_same_on_every_arm`); reset, nothing is re-broken (`with_bdsm_reset_nothing_is_re_broken`). **The gap**: a kitty picture placed relative to a placeholder picture (its parent virtual) is placed from the placeholders' stored column on a row drawn in another order; a predicted echo on a double-width line is not laid out as written. Since 2026-10-25 **the row leaving a smooth scroll** is drawn in the order it had as the window's first (`a_right_to_left_line_slides_out_in_the_order_it_was_drawn`, `the_leaving_row_is_laid_out_as_the_first_row_one_line_up`) and shaped as it was drawn still, joined, mirrored, marked and ligated, with BDSM set or reset (`a_line_sliding_out_is_shaped_as_it_is_drawn_still`), **a double-width line is laid out over its half**, UAX #9 3.4's display line, so a right-to-left paragraph there sits against the half's right edge (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.14.1; `a_double_width_right_to_left_line_is_drawn_within_its_half`, `a_double_width_right_to_left_line_is_laid_out_over_its_drawn_half`), and **an input method's composition and a predicted echo are laid out as written** in their cells, so their row is drawn as the same text written there (`a_composition_on_a_right_to_left_row_draws_as_the_text_written`, `a_predicted_echo_on_a_right_to_left_row_draws_as_its_echo`, `a_predicted_bracket_on_a_right_to_left_row_draws_mirrored_as_its_echo`). **Every mirrored character resolved right to left is drawn mirrored** (UAX #9 L4): its Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph, a cell carrying only bidi controls included (`a_bracket_carrying_a_bidi_control_draws_its_mirror`); that glyph under the marks a cell carries, `<` and U+0338 drawn as `>` and U+0338; and the 126 with no such glyph (`the_mirrored_characters_with_no_glyph_are_the_pinned_files_126`) as their own glyph flipped left to right in their span, a mark kept unflipped where the flip puts it (`a_mirrored_character_a_glyph_alone_cannot_draw_is_drawn_mirrored`, `a_turned_mirror_repaints_its_row`; the three paths' pixels, a flipped glyph against the left-to-right one mirrored: `mirrored_characters_draw_mirrored_on_every_arm`). **Arabic letters join** (`[[source: unicode-ch9]]` 9.2.2 and 9.2.3): set, over each paragraph's logical text across its soft wraps and within each level run (UAX #9 3.5), an erased cell between letters transparent, the lam-alef ligature centred over its two cells; reset, each row in the order it is displayed, as 9.2.2 states the rules, a Mongolian or Phags-pa word left to right, as those scripts run on a horizontal line (`[[source: unicode-ch13]]` 13.5, `[[source: unicode-ch14]]` 14.4; with the mode reset: `with_bdsm_reset_a_mongolian_word_is_joined_left_to_right`, `with_bdsm_reset_two_directions_of_joining_do_not_join`; the forms the face is given in logical order, exact against hb-shape on Noto Sans Mongolian and Noto Sans PhagsPa: `every_mongolian_and_phags_pa_fixture_joins_as_harfbuzz_does`; Arabic: `a_word_is_joined_in_its_logical_order_and_drawn_in_its_visual`, `a_soft_wrap_inside_a_word_keeps_it_whole`, `with_bdsm_reset_a_row_is_joined_as_displayed`; the three paths' pixels: `arabic_letters_draw_joined_on_every_arm`); since 2026-10-26 an input method's composition joins as its text written does, with itself and the letters beside it (`a_composition_of_arabic_draws_joined_as_written_on_every_arm`); Syriac's alaph forms are not drawn |
| `CSI l` | RM reset mode | done — as SM, every mode in the list reset (8.3.106; `rm_resets_every_mode_in_its_list`) |
| `CSI m` | SGR character attributes | done — see SGR below |
| `CSI n` | DSR device status report | done — 5 and 6; others unanswered, not guessed. 6's report is from the origin under DECOM, the column from the left margin since 2026-10-13 (`cpr_under_origin_mode_counts_from_the_left_margin`) |
| `CSI x` / `CSI 1 x` | DECREQTPARM request terminal parameters | done — since 2026-10-19. A program written for a VT100 that asks for the line settings is told them: `CSI 0 x` or `CSI x` and `CSI 1 x` are answered with DECREPTPARM, `CSI sol ; 1 ; 1 ; 120 ; 120 ; 1 ; 0 x` (`[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`): the request plus two, no parity, eight bits, 19200 both ways, the clock multiplier 1 and no STP flags. A pty has no line speed; 120 is the highest code the VT100 table defines, where xterm sends 128, a code the table does not have. A `<sol>` of 2 or 3 is a report, sent to a host and not to a terminal: counted, not answered (`decreqtparm_reports_the_line_the_vt100_table_can_name`, `a_decreptparm_sent_to_baud_is_not_answered_and_is_counted`). xterm answers only while it is a VT102 or below; baud identifies as a VT102 |
| `CSI ? 996 n` | colour preference query | done — since 2026-09-29, answered `CSI ? 997 ; 1 n` (dark) or `; 2 n` (light), `[[source: contour-palette-notifications]]`'s two replies and nothing else: the class of what `OSC 10`/`11 ; ?` would answer -- the override, else the embedder's default, of the colours shown, so under `?5` a dark theme answers light (since 2026-10-11, `the_996_answer_follows_the_colours_shown`; note Y § 7) -- by WCAG relative luminance of the background against the text (note Y, decision 5; `baud_domain::ColorScheme`, its linearisation a table held to `f64` in `baud-domain/tests/color_scheme.rs`). Silent where `OSC 11` would be, on a core told no colours. The DEC private `n`s are answered in their own row (Tier 2); any other stays unsupported and counted (`other_private_dsrs_stay_unsupported`). `the_query_answers_the_class_of_the_theme`, `the_query_follows_an_applications_override`, `tmux_starting_is_answered_in_order` (a tmux capture, `tests/scheme/`) |
| `CSI r` | DECSTBM scroll region | done — an `LF`, `IND` or wrap at a region's bottom scrolls the region whatever row that is, a region ending on the last row included (`lf_at_the_bottom_of_a_region_ending_on_the_last_row_scrolls_only_the_region`: it rotated the whole screen and retired the header rows above it), and on the last row *below* a region it neither scrolls nor moves (`lf_on_the_last_row_below_a_region_does_not_scroll`), both as `[[source: foot-linefeed]]`'s `term_linefeed` and alacritty do; `baud-difftest` carries both cases. On the primary a full-width region anchored at the top sends its scrolled line to the history whatever its height (`a_top_anchored_region_retires_its_line_whatever_its_height`), as `[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, kitty and wezterm do; a region in the middle, one narrower than the screen, and the alternate screen keep nothing. **It homes to the page origin**, line 1 column 1 with DECOM reset and the margins' corner with it set (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSTBM note 1, p. 5-25; `decstbm_homes_to_the_page_origin`); until 2026-10-13 it went to the region's top row either way. **An omitted or 0 bottom margin is the last line**: "default Pb: last line", and the algorithm reads a 0 as omitted (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSTBM, pp. 5-25 and 5-26); "Default: Pb = current number of lines per screen" (`[[source: vt510-decstbm]]`); a default is "assumed when no explicit value, or a value of 0, is specified" (`[[source: vt100-ug-ch3]]`); "default = full size of window" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); as xterm and ghostty read it, since 2026-10-13 (`a_zero_bottom_margin_is_the_last_line`, `a_zero_zero_region_is_the_whole_screen`, with `an_omitted_bottom_margin_is_the_last_line` the control); until then a 0 read as line 1, so `CSI 2;0 r` was parsed and dropped. **A bottom past the screen leaves the region alone**: STD 070's note 3 (p. 5-25), "this control will be ignored (not executed)", since 2026-10-13 (`a_bottom_margin_past_the_screen_leaves_the_region_alone`); until then it was clamped to the last line, as xterm and ghostty still do (pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`) |
| `CSI s` / `CSI u` | SCOSC / SCORC | done — one slot with `ESC 7` / `ESC 8`, the shown screen's, as xterm has it with DECLRMM reset (`scosc_and_decsc_share_a_slot`). **`SCORC` with nothing saved does nothing** (`scorc_without_a_save_is_inert`): its own text, ANSI.SYS's RCP (`[[source: msdos2-ansi-doc]]`), restores "the value it had when the console driver received the SCP sequence" and says nothing of a restore before one, and xterm sends `CSI u` through DECRC's `CursorRestore` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `CASE_ANSI_RC`) onto a slot it saved at start-up. Which to follow is note AE's Q13, open. ANSI.SYS's RCP restores the position alone, where xterm's and baud's restore everything DECSC saves. **Neither takes a parameter** (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1592-1593 and 1734; SCP `ESC [ s` and RCP `ESC [ u` in `[[source: msdos2-ansi-doc]]` 84 and 91, `[[source: mslearn-ansi-sys]]` 794-797 and `[[source: sco-osr507-screen-hw]]` 2595 and 2623): since 2026-10-14 one that is not a default makes the sequence `Unsupported`, counted, the cursor neither saved nor restored -- for `s` it is DECSLRM's shape, `CSI Pl ; Pr s`, "available only when DECLRMM is enabled" (ctlseqs 1595-1597; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSLRM note 4, p. 5-27), and `CSI 5 u` is no form at all (`scosc_with_a_parameter_saves_nothing_and_is_counted`, `scorc_with_a_parameter_restores_nothing_and_is_counted`, every read size and split); until then each saved or restored. **A defaulted parameter is the bare form**: ANSI.DOC's note 1, "The default value is used when no explicit value is given or a value of zero is given", and STD 070 § 3.5.3.1 (p. 3-24), so `CSI 0 s`, `CSI ; s` and `CSI 0;0 s` save (`scosc_with_every_parameter_defaulted_saves`, `scorc_with_every_parameter_defaulted_restores`), where xterm's `only_default` (charproc.c 2220, 4899 and 4913, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`) takes only a bare or single empty one, so `CSI 0 s` and `CSI ; s` save nothing there: xterm's departure. A colon makes it `Unsupported` too, § 3.5.3.1's "the entire sequence ... shall be ignored". Whether a non-default parameter is instead ignored and the SCOSC or SCORC run, § 3.5.1.3's "ignored as if that parameter were not received", is open |
| `CSI ? h` / `CSI ? l` | DECSET / DECRST | done — every parameter of a multi-parameter form is applied, in order (`decset_applies_its_list_in_order`) |

Private modes, one row each. A mode is **done** only when something acts on it;
for the input-side and renderer-side ones "acting" means an accessor an embedder
can read, which is the only channel a `no_std` core with no keyboard and no
renderer has.

| mode | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `?1` | DECCKM cursor keys | done — `Term::cursor_keys_application()`. Since 2026-10-30 **also a local Set-up feature** (070-6 §6.2.1 d): `[keyboard] application_cursor_keys` is the power-up and `RIS` state, "cursor key mode" being state 070-4's Device Initialization lets the user store, and a change of it acts in the open windows at once; `DECSTR` still resets it, its table's "reset (Cursor)" (`launch_and_ris_take_the_saved_values_and_select_numeric`, `a_soft_reset_keeps_its_own_table`) |
| `?2` | DECANM — **reset enters VT52** | done — from every conformance level, the power-up Level 4 included, read against the scope of 070-0A's "only recognized in Level 1 and Level 2" (`vt52_is_entered_from_every_level`); see [protocols/vt52.md](protocols/vt52.md) |
| `?3` | DECCOLM 132 columns | done — since 2026-10-14, **behind xterm's `?40`**, as xterm has it: while `?40` is reset it is ignored, as xterm's `srm_DECCOLM` is without `c132`, and counted (`Term::column_mode_refusals`; `deccolm_is_ignored_while_40_is_reset`, `a_deccolm_at_power_on_leaves_the_screen`). Allowed, it is `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `set_column_mode` and `reset_column_mode` (p. 5-72): the screen cleared unless `?95` is set, and with it every line single-width (`decaln_ris_and_deccolm_reset_every_line`), the top, bottom, left and right margins reset, the cursor at 1;1 whatever DECOM says, "even if the terminal was already in the selected state" (p. 5-71), and the width asked for, 132 or 80 (`Term::requested_width`; `deccolm_under_40_clears_resets_both_margin_pairs_homes_and_asks`, `deccolm_acts_again_in_the_state_it_selects`, `deccolm_under_origin_mode_homes_to_one_one`, `the_homes_under_origin_mode_go_to_the_origin`). `DECRQM` answers the bit; XTSAVE keeps it whatever `?40` says and XTRESTORE acts only under it, as DECSET does (`xtrestore_of_3_acts_only_under_40`). **The resize is the embedder's**: under `WidthHold::On` a read stops at the request, so the bytes after it land at the width asked for (`a_held_read_lands_the_rest_at_the_width_asked_for`, every read size and split), and they go on in the protocol the request's sequence left: a DECANM beside it, `CSI ? 2 ; 3 l` or `CSI ? 3 ; 2 l`, takes the rest to VT52 (`decanm_beside_a_held_deccolm_resumes_in_vt52`), and XTRESTORE of `?2;3`, which cannot replay `?2`, to ANSI (`xtrestore_of_2_and_3_holds_and_resumes_in_ansi`); the macOS app takes the columns, keeps its rows and then sizes the window to them (`baud-app`'s `width.rs`; `honour_resizes_inside_the_read_and_keeps_the_rows`, `a_program_width_lays_out_to_its_columns`), and refuses under `BAUD_GRID`; the Linux driver keeps its window and says so once (`a_request_is_decided_once_and_a_refusal_warns_once`). **And DECLRMM reset**, the VT510's "DECCOLM resets vertical split screen mode (DECLRMM) to unavailable" (`[[source: vt510-deccolm]]`), so `CSI s` is SCOSC again; STD 070's algorithm sets `left_right_margins_mode = SETABLE` instead, and xterm leaves the mode and calls that line an error -- the texts disagree, an open question. **partial** until 2026-10-14: the side margins stayed, and nothing asked for the width. Its clear, on the primary narrower than its rows hold, clears what the rows held past the edge as `ED 2` does (`deccolm_while_narrow_clears_what_its_rows_held_past_the_edge`, since 2026-10-14). **Since 2026-10-21 its clear takes the pictures too**: a Sixel and the ReGIS page lose their pixels on every row, as under `ED 2`, and the kitty placements on the screen go, as kitty's own DECCOLM set does, and on reset too, since DECCOLM clears the screen either way (`deccolm_s_clear_frees_a_sixel`, `deccolm_s_clear_clears_the_regis_page`, `deccolm_s_clear_deletes_a_kitty_placement`) |
| `?4` | DECSCLM smooth scroll | done — `Term::smooth_scroll()` |
| `?5` | DECSCNM reverse video | done — recorded by the core (`Term::reverse_video()`) and **drawn** since 2026-09-25. **Every cell draws with its foreground and background exchanged, an explicit `SGR` colour included**, since 2026-10-14, rather than xterm's palette swap: the mode "will affect which attribute bits from display memory are used to render the text foreground and background color" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.6.3.2, page 5-107, PDF 362). Bold brightens the foreground's index before the exchange, a cell's own `SGR 7` exchanges again, so it draws as on a normal screen, and faint dims the drawn text after both; no palette entry moves, and an `SGR 58` underline colour is drawn as set (`baud_domain::Video::drawn`). The contrast floor, `app_backgrounds = "opaque"` and the picture finder read the pair as drawn; under `?5` the finder, on by default, finds btop and cava as pictures (`under_reverse_video_only_btop_and_cava_draw_a_picture`), and whether it should read the drawn background is open. Until then baud drew xterm's `ReverseVideo` (util.c 3269-3345, `[[source: xterm-scroll]]`'s file), which swaps the defaults and palette entries 0 and 7 and 8 and 15 and leaves every other colour. The margin, scrollbar, cursor trail and screen fade take the default pair as a default cell shows it, on the frame that flips. Every background, the reversed default one included, keeps the window's alpha. `baud-render/tests/reverse_video.rs` (`every_cell_exchanges_its_colours_explicit_ones_included`, `the_canvas_paints_each_cell_exchanged`, `a_picture_is_found_by_the_background_drawn`), and the pixel oracle's `?5` scenes, cells and picture. **A `?5h` and `?5l` in one read are drawn too**, since 2026-09-25: on macOS `tput flash` sleeps its `$<100/>` and writes both in one burst (measured through a pty), so the mode reads as it did and no frame would show the flip. The core counts the changes -- the ones made where a frame could show them (`Term::reverse_flips`) apart from the ones made inside `?2026` (`Term::reverse_flips_held`), which a driver shows only once it stops honouring an update left open -- a frame that finds a count moved with the mode unchanged shows the other video, and the flash ends on the loop's own clock after the entry's padding: the `$<100/>` of the pinned `xterm-256color.ti`, parsed at build time by `baud_domain::terminfo` into a bounded `Delay` (`baud-core/tests/reverse_flips.rs`, `baud-app/src/bell.rs`, `baud-render/tests/video_flash.rs`, the pixel oracle's flash scene). A pair no frame drew is shown as the other video's flash once per pair, and is not held to WCAG 2.3.1's rate as a BEL's flash is (the BEL row), an open gap. Under the config's `bell = "flash"` a `BEL` shows the same flash on macOS, counted apart so a frame planned before it still sees it (`Bell::ring`, `a_ring_flashes_from_the_next_frame`, `a_ring_between_plan_and_commit_is_not_lost`; the `BEL` row). Each driver times an update from its own opening (`UpdateClock`, restarted by `Term::updates_opened`). The drivers' wiring -- the plan, the commit and the refusal, the hold, the due check and the Linux `poll` timeout -- is established by reading, since no test drives the loop, and a flash was not watched on screen. vim's `set vb` and bash's `bell-style visible` write the two halves 102 ms and 110 ms apart, in two reads, and frames draw each -- by reading the frame loop; the app was not watched doing either. **An `OSC 10`/`11` set, query or reset *while* reversed acts on the colour shown** since 2026-10-11 (note V, V17): a default cell drawn exchanged shows the stored background as its text and the stored foreground as its page, so baud maps the role to the stored slot through `baud_domain::Video`, the one table the renderer draws by -- `OSC 10` names the stored background and `OSC 11` the foreground, where xterm's handlers land on its swapped tables (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3568-3750) -- and a reply keeps the code asked (`baud-core/tests/reverse_osc.rs`, `a_dynamic_colour_set_under_reverse_draws_in_the_role_it_named`). Under the exchange the colour an `OSC 10` sets while reversed is also the text of every cell without `SGR 7` whose background is the default, an `SGR 31` cell's included (`osc_10_and_11_under_reverse_reach_an_exchanged_cell`). Before 2026-10-11, an `OSC 11` under `?5` moved the foreground drawn and its query answered the text colour. `CSI ? 996 n` follows, and `?5` sends no `997` (note Y § 7). **An `OSC 4` or `OSC 104 ; c` sent while reversed names the entry drawn**, since no entry moves under the exchange (`a_palette_override_is_drawn_where_its_entry_is_named`). **An `OSC 12` cursor colour equal to the text moves to the background on each change of `?5`** since 2026-10-11, as xterm's `ReverseVideo` does before its swap (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 3293-3295) and `ReverseOldColors` to the copy `OSC 12 ; ?` answers (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 6418-6431): every flip goes through `flip_reverse_video` -- `DECSCNM`, XTRESTORE and `RIS`, as each of xterm's calls `ReverseVideo` -- and the rule is `baud_domain::Video::cursor_across`. An unset cursor stays unset, being drawn in the text already, and so does one whose page a core without embedder colours cannot name. A flash draws the colour the flip would leave (`Term::cursor_color_in`, read by the renderer into `Renderer::block_paint`, which the pixel oracle's cell arm and the app's macOS cell path draw from; the oracle's `?5` scene flashes with an `OSC 12` equal to the text). `baud-core/tests/reverse_cursor.rs`, `a_flash_draws_the_cursor_colour_a_flip_would_leave`, `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws` |
| `?6` | DECOM origin | done — since 2026-10-13 the origin is "(Top Margin, Left Margin)" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECOM, p. 5-31): `?6h` homes there and `?6l` to line 1, column 1 (`setting_origin_mode_homes_to_the_top_and_left_margins`), `DECSTBM` and `DECSLRM` home to it (`decstbm_homes_to_the_page_origin`, `decslrm_homes_to_the_page_origin`), `CUP`/`HVP` count from it and stop at the right margin (CUP, p. 5-50; `cup_under_origin_mode_addresses_from_the_left_margin`), and `CPR` reports from it (p. 5-54; `cpr_under_origin_mode_counts_from_the_left_margin`). **And the cursor "cannot be moved above the Top Margin or left of the Left Margin"** (p. 5-31; `[[source: vt510-decom]]`, "cannot move outside of the margins"), since the same day for columns as for rows: `CR`, `NEL`, `CNL`, `CPL` and `LF` under `LNM` go to the left margin, `CUF`, `HPR`, `CUB`, `HT`, `CHT`, `CBT`, `CHA` and `HPA` stop at the margins, `DECRC`, `SCORC` and `?1049 l` clamp what they restore when the restored mode is displaced (`restore_cursor`, p. 5-190), a column on the right margin that a narrower `DECSLRM` leaves level with the new right margin + 1 included (`a_right_margin_column_restored_under_a_narrower_margin_is_clamped`), `DECCOLM` homes to the origin, which is line 1, column 1 once it has reset the margins, and `DECALN` resets the mode and the margins and homes there (`?1049 h` did until 2026-10-13, and leaves the cursor since: the `?1049` row), and VT52's moves keep it, each followed by a `CPR` (`tests/vt420/origin.rs`); until then `CPR` answered column 1 for a cursor left of the origin. `CHA`/`HPA` count from the screen's column 1 and are clamped, option (a'), where xterm counts from the left margin: no text settles which, an open question. Held against ghostty by the oracle's `decom:` fixtures, with ghostty's `CUB`, `DECRC` and `CHA`/`HPA` pinned |
| `?7` | DECAWM autowrap | done — the last column is the right margin from at or inside it and the screen's from right of the fence, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-139, "will not advance beyond the Right Margin or last column of the Active Line" (`without_autowrap_right_of_the_fence_writes_to_the_last_column`; the `CSI s` row). **A wide character that does not fit writes its lead alone on the last column**, since 2026-10-28, and nothing past it: the right margin, a double line's half and SLL's limit keep what they held, where the tail went one past each and `DECSWL` then showed it whole. No text gives a character two positions there (DEC STD 070 has none; ECMA-48 6.1.6's NOTE leaves it to the implementation; `[[source: vt382-rm]]` p. 10-4), so baud writes the lead alone, as it writes at the screen's edge already; xterm and ghostty write nothing (`on_a_double_line_s_last_column_the_lead_stands_alone`, `every_writer_keeps_the_double_line_s_half`, `at_the_right_margin_nothing_is_written_past_it`, `at_the_line_limit_nothing_is_written_past_it`, `the_cells_written_count_the_lead_alone`, `on_a_line_of_one_position_the_lead_stands_alone`, `the_table_read_keeps_the_same_columns`, `with_autowrap_set_the_character_wraps_whole`). **Open**: left to right, a pending wrap at the right margin survives `LF`, `VT`, `FF`, `IND`, `RI`, `ICH`, `DCH`, `VPA` and `VPR`, where 070-D pp. D-13 and D-14 resets it for every one of them but the last two (`[[source: dec-std-070]]`); `CUU`, `CUD`, `BS`, `CUB` and the erases end it since 2026-11-01 (their rows); `CUP 1;10 z LF y` on ten columns writes `y` two rows down |
| `?8` | DECARM auto-repeat | done — set at power-on, as `[[source: vt510-decarm]]` gives it ("Default: Repeat (set)"); `Term::key` drops a repeat while it is reset, on every layer. xterm ignores the mode (its `srm_DECARM` case: "ignore autorepeat"). `DECSTR` leaves it, as DEC's DECSTR table and xterm's soft reset both do. `a_repeat_is_dropped_while_autorepeat_is_reset`. Since 2026-10-30 **also a local Set-up feature** (070-6 §6.2.1 d): `[keyboard] auto_repeat` is the power-up and `RIS` state and a change of it acts at once (`launch_and_ris_take_the_saved_values_and_select_numeric`, `a_change_of_the_set_up_acts_on_what_it_changes`) |
| `?12` | cursor blink | done — since 2026-09-27 **a second input to the blink, *or*-ed with the style's own**, foot's rule (`[[source: foot-term-reset]]`'s file): `?12h` blinks a steady style and `?12l` cannot stop one that blinks, so the `?12h ?12l` vim sends at start and every `cnorm` leave a configured blink blinking. DECRQM answers the `?12` bit, reset at power-on whatever the style draws, as foot and xterm answer it; `DECSCUSR 0` and `7` leave it and both resets clear it. It is saved with the style at `?1049 h` and restored at `?1049 l` (`baud-core/tests/cursor_blink_rule.rs`, which replays vim's and helix's captured exits from `tests/cursor/`) |
| `?13` | xterm's `cursorBlink`, read-only | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can ask whether the user configured a blinking cursor**: `DECRQM` answers `1` or `2` from the style the embedder configured (`Term::set_initial_cursor_style`), as xterm answers its resource while it can still change (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`, `srm_CURSOR_BLINK_OPS`; note X recommends this read-only answer). DECSET and DECRST are counted, "set only via resource or menu" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `the_read_only_and_permanent_modes_do_not_move`) |
| `?14` | xterm's `cursorBlinkXOR`, read-only | done — since 2026-10-19: `DECRQM` answers `4`, permanently reset, because baud ORs `?12` with the style's blink (rule F) and never XORs; DECSET and DECRST are counted (`each_mode_answers_what_baud_does_at_power_on`) |
| `?18` | DECPFF print form feed | done — since 2026-10-20: "When Print Form Feed Mode is set, a Form Feed (FF) control character is transmitted to the printer port at the conclusion of each Print Page or Print Screen operation" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7 p. 7-37), and not after a print line ("DECPFF does not affect the print cursor line function", `[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 211); the MC row. Off at power-on and **reset by `RIS`**, "Default: No form feed", **kept by `DECSTR`**, whose list names it nowhere; set and reset with no printer, as a mode needs no printer port. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(PrinterOf(screen).printer_formfeed)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5544-5545); XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it, as xterm's `savemodes` does. Counted as unsupported before (`decpff_and_decpex_answer_decrqm_and_reset_with_ris`, `print_screen_ignores_decpex_and_decpff_adds_a_form_feed`) |
| `?19` | DECPEX print extent | done — since 2026-10-20: set, print page prints the whole page; reset, "only the area of the display within the scrolling region (Top Margin to Bottom Margin inclusive)" (070-7 p. 7-38), and print line only DECSLRM's margins (PRINT LINE); the MC row. **Reset at power-on and by `RIS`**, a source conflict (below), kept by `DECSTR`; DECRQM, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE as `?18`. Counted as unsupported before (`print_page_prints_the_scrolling_region_unless_decpex_is_set`, `print_line_prints_the_cursor_line_and_decpex_picks_the_margins`) |
| `?25` | DECTCEM cursor visible | done |
| `?30` | show scrollbar (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can hide baud's scrollbar and show it again**: "Show scrollbar (rxvt)" and "Don't show scrollbar (rxvt)" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Hidden, the macOS app draws none and nothing takes a press there, a drag in flight dropped; the overlay style's strip is cells, and the legacy style's gutter goes, the window narrowing by its width so the grid keeps its columns, as xterm's `ScrollBarOff` resizes its window through `DoResizeScreen` (`[[source: xterm-scrollbar-c]]` 101, 563-585), or, in full screen or with `[window] operations` off, the grid taking the columns (`a_scrollbar_change_resizes_the_window_where_it_may`, `a_hidden_scrollbar_keeps_no_gutter`). Shown at power-on, since baud has a scrollbar, where xterm's default has none, and `DECRQM` answers it as xterm's answers whether one is shown (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5553-5554; `the_scrollbar_is_shown_at_power_on`). **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset shows it again**: `RIS` returns to the state at power-on (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105) and `DECSTR` keeps it, where xterm keeps it across both, the scrollbar being its window's, a practice not followed, as keeping it would depart from 8.3.105. XTSAVE keeps it (`ris_shows_it_decstr_keeps_it_and_xtsave_restores_it`). The wheel still scrolls the history. **The Linux app draws a thumb too** since 2026-10-21, AppKit's overlay style drawn at the grid's edge, where macOS draws it at the window's edge, there being no system scroller setting to follow, and hidden by `?30` it draws none and takes no press (`a_jump_needs_a_drawn_thumb_and_a_shown_scrollbar`). Counted and dropped until 2026-10-20 |
| `?34` | DECRLM cursor right-to-left mode | **partial** — since 2026-10-26, **a program can write from right to left, as on a VT510 with a Hebrew keyboard**: "The next character position is defined as the current cursor position minus 1", and "DECRLM does not affect column numbering within the line", so the cells hold the characters in the order shown (`[[source: vt510-decrlm]]`). Under IRM "All characters from the current cursor position to the left margin inclusively are shifted one position towards the left"; with DECAWM a character at the left margin leaves a wrap waiting, and the next "appears at the right margin column of the next line", scrolling at the region's end; with it reset the margin is overwritten. `BS` moves right, `CR`, `NEL` and an `LF`, `VT` or `FF` under LNM go to the right-most column, `HT` to the previous tab stop, the margins DECLRMM's; a wide character takes the cursor's column and the one to its left; a combining mark joins the character the last write put down; `ICH`, `DCH`, `ED`, `EL`, `DECSED`, `DECSEL`, `ECH`, `CUP`, `HVP`, `CUF` and `CUB` are not affected. **Each row written so is drawn as its cells hold**, UAX #9 not applied to it though BDSM is set, so a Hebrew word reads as the VT510 shows it, and `?96` copies it in the order sent, until the row is erased whole: `ED`, `EL`, `ECH` or a fill from its first column to its end, a scroll or `IL` and `DL` bringing it in, or `DECSED` and `DECSEL` leaving no protected character on it end the mark and keep the row's path. One state for both displays; `DECSTR` resets it, as its table has it (`[[source: vt510-decstr]]`), and `RIS`. `DECRQM` answers it, XTSAVE keeps it, DECTSR carries it (version 6), and it acts at Level 4, the VT510's. While set the text goes one character at a time to the leftward writer, and the text path's writers never read it (`decrqm_answers_decrlm_and_decstr_resets_it`, `a_character_is_written_and_the_cursor_moves_left`, `at_column_one_it_wraps_to_the_right_margin_or_overwrites`, `a_wide_character_and_a_mark_go_where_the_last_write_was`, `under_irm_the_line_shifts_toward_the_left_margin`, `bs_cr_lnm_nel_and_ht_follow_the_direction`, `inside_side_margins_the_margins_bound_it`, `reset_with_a_wrap_pending_writes_on_from_the_margin`, `xtsave_keeps_decrlm`, `a_wrap_waiting_at_the_left_edge_does_not_depend_on_the_reads`, `under_unicode_8_a_mark_at_column_0_joins_the_last_write`, `a_row_erased_whole_is_no_longer_one_decrlm_wrote`, `a_row_erased_whole_past_the_edge_is_no_longer_one_decrlm_wrote`, `a_line_scrolled_in_is_not_one_decrlm_wrote`, `il_and_dl_bring_in_lines_decrlm_never_wrote`, `a_row_decrlm_wrote_is_drawn_as_its_cells_hold`, `a_row_decrlm_wrote_erased_whole_is_drawn_by_uax9_again`; the three arms' pixels: `a_leftward_row_draws_alike_on_every_arm`). **Since 2026-10-26 a SAPV form works under it too**: the form asked is the following character's alone, written leftward, under IRM the line shifting once a character, and 21 and 22 hold and release it as without DECRLM (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.108; `under_decrlm_a_form_is_the_following_characters_alone`, `under_decrlm_and_irm_a_form_shifts_the_line_once`), where every character after a form kept it; and Arabic it writes is joined as written, read from the right, with BDSM set as with it reset, where with BDSM set it was drawn unjoined (`under_decrlm_letters_join_as_written_and_take_the_forms_asked`; on every arm, `arabic_decrlm_wrote_draws_joined_with_its_forms_on_every_arm`). **Since 2026-10-26 a move or an edit ends the wrap waiting at the left margin**, as "The Last Column Flag should also be reset by executing any control which potentially changes the Active Position from the end-of-line position" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-D pp. D-13 and D-14): `CUU`, `CUD`, `VPA`, `VPR`, `VPB`, `LF`, `VT`, `FF`, `IND` and `RI`, on the row the write left or away and back, a line feed scrolling at the bottom included, and `ECH`, `EL`, `ED`, `DCH`, `ICH`, `IL`, `DL`, `DECSEL`, `DECSED` and the line renditions, so the next character is written where the cursor is (`a_move_or_an_edit_ends_the_wrap_waiting_at_the_left_edge`); a move away and back found it still waiting. `DECSTBM`, `DECOM`, `DECCOLM` and DECAWM reset end it too, through the home position they move the cursor to or the pin. `SUB`, which 070-5 writes as a reversed question mark through INSERT_OR_REPLACE_CHARACTER, draws nothing by default in baud and so leaves the wrap waiting. **Not covered**: `DECSC` and `DECRC` do not save the wrap waiting at the left margin, where they save a rightward one since 2026-11-01 (note AE's Q12); a `DECERA` or `DECSERA` over the whole row leaves the mark, its occupied cells becoming spaces, where `DECSED`'s spaces end it |
| `?35` | DECHEBM Hebrew/N-A keyboard mapping | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can switch the keyboard to Hebrew, as on a VT510 with a Hebrew keyboard**: "This control function sets the keyboard mapping to Hebrew/N-A and resets the mapping to North American" (`[[source: vt510-dechebm]]`). Set, a key the system's layout typed its own unshifted character on sends the letter the standard Israeli layout puts on that physical key, SI-1452's first level as xkeyboard-config's `il` layout transcribes it (`[[source: xkb-symbols-il-2ec770a]]`), DEC's own Hebrew key legends being figures: the key where the US layout has `a` sends shin on a US, AZERTY or QWERTZ layout, `,` sends tav. With Shift a key types SI-1452's second level, the US layout's but for six keys that type their mirror (`AE09`, `AE10`, `AD11`, `AD12`, `AB08`, `AB09`): the key where the US layout types `{` sends `}`, `(` sends `)` and `<` sends `>`, and each the other way, where the app names the key's place beside the character, as macOS does for every key; on the system's Hebrew layout, which types SI-1452 itself, a shifted key is sent as typed. A letter Caps Lock made a capital is sent as typed, as SI-1452's letter keys type their capital with Caps Lock, and a key held with Control, Alt, Meta, Super or Hyper is not mapped. Under 7-bit characters, DECHEM reset ("Reset (7-bit char)", `[[source: vt510-chapter4]]`), it "Makes DEC 7-bit Hebrew the keyboard character set", so a Hebrew letter, mapped or typed on the system's Hebrew layout, is sent as its code 60 to 7A, and "Causes received characters in the range of 60 to 7A (hex) to be displayed using the DEC 7-bit Hebrew when ASCII is designated or invoked", so ASCII in GL is drawn as that set, each display as DECHEBM is when it is written; reset, characters are drawn by GL's set again and what is on the screen stays. Under 8-bit characters, DECHEM set, a letter is sent as itself and received characters are not affected. **Only with a Hebrew keyboard**: "DECHEBM is only valid when a Hebrew keyboard language is selected", so it acts only while the system has a Hebrew layout enabled, as the app reads it, macOS's enabled keyboard input sources at start and at every focus gain and the Wayland keymap's layouts when the compositor sends one (`Term::set_hebrew_layout`). North American at power-on and after `RIS`; `DECSTR` keeps it, as its table does not list it (`[[source: vt510-decstr]]`). `DECRQM` answers it, XTSAVE keeps it, DECTSR carries it (version 7), and it acts at Level 4, the VT510's. **rxvt's and xterm's `?35`, "Enable font-shifting functions (rxvt)" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), is refused**: it cannot be counted apart, its bytes being DECHEBM's, so the refusal shows as xterm's `shiftFonts` resource staying unknown to XTGETXRES and a program's font changes staying under `[font] changes`, as rxvt-unicode's own `?35` gates Shift+Insert (`[[source: urxvt-command-c-5865a43]]`) and xterm's gates a program's `OSC 50` changes (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `decrqm_answers_dechebm_decstr_keeps_it_and_ris_resets_it`, `group_two_types_the_israeli_layouts_letter_on_the_keys_place`, `a_shifted_key_takes_the_second_level_as_the_apps_send_it`, `the_second_level_is_the_us_layouts_but_for_six_keys`, `without_a_hebrew_layout_it_does_nothing`, `under_seven_bit_characters_a_letter_is_sent_as_its_code`, `under_seven_bit_characters_ascii_in_gl_is_drawn_as_hebrew`, `the_main_display_draws_by_dechebm_set_on_the_status_line`, `below_level_4_it_is_counted`, `a_keymap_with_a_hebrew_layout_says_so`, `a_hebrew_language_makes_the_layout_present`). **Open, a departure**: the page's 7-bit behaviour also "when ... the terminal is set to VT100 mode", which at Level 4 is not reached, and baud acts at Level 4 only. **Open**: the apps do not say a key's shift level, so a shifted key takes SI-1452's second level only where the app names its place and it types the US layout's or SI-1452's character there. On Wayland a key whose unshifted character is the US layout's names no place and is sent as typed, so on a US layout Shift+`9` sends `(` and Shift+`[` sends `{`, a Hebrew layout's Shift+`9` and the ISO key's `<` arriving alike; on QWERTZ, Shift+`,`, which types `;`, sends tav on macOS and final pe on Wayland, and Shift+`8`, which types `(` where SI-1452 has `*`, is sent as typed; and on AZERTY, Shift where the US layout has `q` sends `/` on Wayland and `A` on macOS; SI-1452 has `)`, `}`, `>` and `Q` there |
| `?36` | DECHEM Hebrew encoding mode | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can switch to the VT510's Hebrew national mode, where lowercase letters are drawn as Hebrew**: reset, "(7-bit) national mode (NRC sets)", designates the DEC 7-bit Hebrew NRCS (`ESC ( % =`) into G0 to G3, so `` ` `` through `z` are drawn alef through tav; set, "DEC (8-bit) multinational mode", returns the sets to their power-up designations and invocations, as STD 070's DECNRCM algorithms do, "re-initializing the designated and invoked character sets to their default state" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-126 to 5-128; `[[source: vt510-dechem]]`). **Open, a departure**: "setting DECHEM has the same result as resetting DECNRCM" (`[[source: vt510-chapter4]]` Table 4-8, note 1), and `?42` stays ignored (note AF's Q-DECNRCM), so `?36 l` designates the Hebrew set while `?42 h`, whose result should be the same, changes nothing. Multinational at power-on and after `DECSTR` and `RIS`, as DECNRCM's reset state is, which "setting DECHEM has the same result as" (`[[source: vt510-chapter4]]` Table 4-8, note 1). Each display keeps its own, as DEC keeps it "for the main display and status line separately". `DECRQM` answers it, XTSAVE keeps it, and it acts at Level 4, the VT510's (`dechem_reset_designates_the_hebrew_nrcs_and_set_ascii`, `decstr_returns_dechem_to_multinational`, `the_status_line_keeps_its_own_dechem`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_keep_both`) |
| `?40` | allow 80 ⇄ 132 columns (xterm's `c132`) | done — since 2026-10-14: "Allow 80 -> 132 mode, xterm" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), the gate `?3` reads, reset at power-on as `Bres(XtNc132, ..., False)` gives it (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). `DECRQM` answers it, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->c132)` does (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); XTSAVE carries it; `RIS` resets it, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state", where xterm's reset keeps the resource; `DECSTR` keeps it (`deccolm_is_ignored_while_40_is_reset`, `ris_in_an_80_column_window_asks_for_80_and_resets_the_modes`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_carry_40_and_95`). It does not gate DECSCPP, which neither xterm nor STD 070 gates (the `CSI $ \|` row) |
| `?41` | more(1) fix (xterm's `curses` resource) | done — since 2026-10-20, **a pager written for terminals that wrap at once keeps the indent of a line after a full one**: set, a tab at a pending wrap with `?7` set takes the wrap first, then the tab, as xterm's `TabNext` (`[[source: xterm-tabs-c]]` 113-118; `set_a_tab_at_a_pending_wrap_wraps_and_then_tabs`): `IND`, scrolling at the bottom margin (`at_the_bottom_the_wrap_scrolls`), the screen's first column even under `DECLRMM` (`under_side_margins_the_tab_starts_from_the_first_column`), and the row left not marked as continuing, where xterm's own character wrap marks it (`the_row_left_is_not_marked_as_continuing`). CHT takes the wrap once. Nothing changes with no wrap pending or `?7` reset (`without_autowrap_the_tab_does_not_wrap`). Reset at power-on, the resource's default (`[[source: xterm-man]]`, `-cu`); `DECRQM` answers it (`decrqm_answers_the_more_fix`), XTSAVE keeps it, `RIS` resets it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as the xterm resource modes go (`xtsave_keeps_it_ris_resets_it_and_decstr_keeps_it`). Counted and dropped until then |
| `?42` | DECNRCM national replacement character set mode | done — since 2026-10-24, **recognised, and changes nothing**: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-126 to 5-128 puts the keyboard dialect's 7-bit NRC set in G0-G3 and "is ignored when the North American or Dutch Keyboard Dialect is selected" (note 3), and baud has no dialect setting, its keyboard a North American one, for which 070-6 p. 6-58 has "7-bit Characters mode is not available". So `?42 h` and `l` neither re-initialise the character sets nor are counted as a mode baud lacks, at every level, its field "1x, 2x, 3x, 4x (NRCS Extension)"; the national sets act whenever designated (the national sets row). Both of its algorithms, set_character_set_mode() and reset_character_set_mode(), run only "if (level_1_extensions[NRCS_EXT]==TRUE)" (p. 5-127), the field naming an extension at every level, and baud's DA1 answer claims none: the level row's Since(One) reads the `1x` as this table reads every `x`, an extension baud has (the DECSCL row), as the level `?42` is recognised from, and does not make DA1 claim the extension; without the extension neither body runs, and with it note 3 ignores the mode, so either way it changes nothing. **`DECRQM ?42` answers `0`**: "DEC Private modes that cannot be set or reset from the host will report as unknown" (p. 5-193). Note AF's Q-DECNRCM takes option (c), because it keeps STD 070's algorithms (pp. 5-119 to 5-128) and note 5. **An open departure**: it departs from 070-3 §3.6.4.4 if that prose is read as a gate, and reading no dialect as North American is inferred. (a) left it unrecognised and (b), the VT220 and VT420 manuals' and xterm's gate, departs from the SCS algorithm (p. 5-121). A keyboard dialect setting, which would make the mode reachable, is not built. Until 2026-10-24 it was `PrivateMode::Other` and counted (`decnrcm_is_recognised_and_ignored_with_no_keyboard_dialect`) |
