| `?43` | DECGEPM graphics expanded print | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can print its pictures large**: set, a ReGIS hard copy is sent expanded, reset compressed, "Default: Compressed" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), the Level 2 dump's grid 9 or 6 decipoints a pixel (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7 § 7.8.2.4). **The texts differ on a terminal with no printer**: the VT330/VT340 manual, "If you do not have a printer connected to the terminal, the terminal ignores these functions" (ch. 16, p. 255), and 070-7 § 7.8.2.2, which sets no such condition; baud records DECGEPM either way, following 070-7, as it records DECPEX, and the VT340's ignoring is not followed. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`; compressed at power-on and after `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`, which the VT330/VT340's Table 13-1 does not name; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. The VT340's other four graphics print modes, `?44` to `?47` (colour, colour syntax, background, rotation), are xterm's modes here (those rows), so the four are the user's `[screen] print_colour`, `print_rgb`, `print_background` and `print_rotated`, each off by default, as 070-7 § 7.8.2.1-7.8.2.3 lets the user select them, colour off by default. Counted as unsupported before (`offset_size_rotation_syntax_and_background`, `decgepm_resets_on_ris_and_survives_decstr`) |
| `?44` | margin bell (xterm's `marginBell`) | done — since 2026-10-20: "Turn on margin bell, xterm" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), "whether or not the bell should be rung when the user types near the right margin. The default is "false"" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). One of the margin bell's two switches, DECSMBV's volume the other (that row): set alone it rings nothing until a DECSMBV 2 to 8 arrives, and reset it disarms the bell, as xterm's `bellArmed = -1`. DEC assigns `?44` to the VT340's DECGPCM, a graphics print mode (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), which xterm takes only while its terminal identity is a VT125, VT240, VT241, VT330 or VT340 (`optRegisGraphics`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); baud's is a VT102, so `?44` is xterm's, and colour printing is the user's `[screen] print_colour`, which 070-7 § 7.8.2.1 lets "the user ... select" (the `?43` row). `Term::margin_bell()`. Off at power-on, **reset by `RIS`**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state", where xterm's reset keeps the resource, and kept by `DECSTR`; DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->marginbell)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5579-5580); XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. `decrqm_answers_44_and_xtsave_carries_it`, `turning_it_off_disarms_it` |
| `?45` | reverse wraparound | done — since 2026-10-20 to xterm's `CursorBack`, which defines this xterm mode (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` names it and no more; `[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 121-191): **backspace at the left margin goes to the end of the line above only when that line wrapped into this one**, and otherwise stays, so it walks back over a wrapped command line and not into unrelated output; **only while `?7` is set**, `REVERSEWRAP` with `WRAPAROUND`; **`CUB` walks the same way, its count included**, `CASE_CUB` calling `CursorBack` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). No top margin bounds it while `DECOM` is reset, only an unwrapped line and the screen's first line, where xterm would read a line of its history; **under `DECOM` the walk stops at the top margin**, as origin mode confines all cursor movement to the region (`[[source: vt510-decom]]`), where xterm's `rev` path reads no `ORIGIN` and leaves it, not followed (`under_origin_mode_reverse_wrap_stops_at_the_top_margin`). A failed step from below the scrolling region leaves the cursor on the line above, xterm's `if (row < bottom) ++row` as written. From a pending wrap `CUB n` moves n-1 columns, counting the pending wrap as one step as X11R5's `CursorBack` did with REVERSEWRAP and WRAPAROUND both (`[[source: xterm-x11r5-cursor-c]]` 81-84) and as `?1045` counts it; without the mode `CUB` and `BS` count from the last column (the `CSI D` row); today's xterm moves n-2 for n of 2 or more. `BS` (n = 1) agrees with xterm. Until 2026-10-20 it acted without `?7`, went into any line above but not past the top margin, and left `CUB` alone (`backspace_reverse_wraps_only_into_a_wrapped_line`, `reverse_wrap_needs_autowrap`, `cub_reverse_wraps_a_count_of_columns`, `reverse_wrap_crosses_the_top_margin_into_a_wrapped_line`, `a_failed_reverse_wrap_below_the_region_goes_up_a_line`) |
| `?46` | XTLOGGING start and stop logging | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can log everything it sends to a file** (note AV): "Start logging (XTLOGGING), xterm", reset "Stop logging" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), to the file `OSC 46` names. The log is the host's bytes from the one after `?46 h` up to and including `?46 l`, as xterm's `StartLog` takes `logstart = VTbuffer->next` and `CloseLog` flushes to it (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`): the core stops a read after each change (`Node::Held`, as at a page-width request), so each part the embedder feeds was parsed under one mode, and the app writes each part fed while started (`reads::feed`, `hostlog.rs`). The app opens the file on a writer thread, never the read loop: appended, created `0644`, opened non-blocking so a named pipe with no reader fails at once, and refused unless the real user owns it and neither group nor others may write it, xterm's `open_userfile`; `-` is the app's standard output; a name beginning `|`, which xterm runs as a command only when built with `ALLOWLOGFILEEXEC`, is refused and counted, as in xterm's default build; with no name the log is `baud.log.<host>.<yyyy.mm.dd.hh.mm.ss>.<pid>` after xterm's `Xterm.log` form, in local time as xterm's `localtime` gives it (`a_generated_name_is_in_local_time`), in the directory the shell started in, where a relative name is read too, and that name is kept for the next start with none, as xterm's `GenerateLogPath` keeps one name per process ("once opened we just reuse the same log name"), so a stop and a second unnamed start append to the first file. An open or write that fails stops the core's log (`Term::stop_log`) and is warned; the queue to the writer holds 4 MiB; past it a part is dropped and warned once. **When the shell's exit ends the app, or on Linux the window's close does, the log is finished first**: the app ends once the writer has written every part queued before and closed the file, as xterm's `FlushLog` writes each read before the next, and gives up on a sink that stalls at the loop's first turn two seconds on, warned; on macOS, Quit in the app's menu ends the process without waiting for it. `DECRQM ?46` answers `1` or `2`, and `4` while `[screen] logging = "off"` or in a core no embedder turned it on, when `?46` and `OSC 46` are refused and counted in `Term::logs_refused`. XTSAVE keeps it and XTRESTORE starts or stops through it, as xterm's `restoremodes` calls `StartLog`; `RIS` and `DECSTR` leave it, as xterm's resets do. VT340's Graphic Print Background Mode shares the number; baud prints no graphics (`the_log_runs_from_after_the_start_to_the_stop`, `decrqm_answers_the_mode`, `off_both_are_refused_and_counted`, `a_command_is_never_started`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_keep_the_mode`, `a_reset_leaves_the_log`; the app's `the_log_holds_what_the_host_sent_while_started`, `a_file_xterm_would_refuse_stops_the_log`, `a_full_queue_drops_and_counts`, `a_quit_finishes_once_the_last_parts_are_written`, `a_stalled_writer_is_given_up_on_at_the_bound`, `a_generated_name_is_kept_for_the_next_start`, `the_logging_setting_reaches_the_core`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `?1045` | extended reverse wraparound (XTREVWRAP2) | done — since 2026-10-19, **backspace at the left margin wraps up, and from the top margin round to the bottom margin's line**, each time to the line's end, while `?7` is set, as xterm's `REVERSEWRAP2` acts only with `WRAPAROUND` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`, `CursorBack`; whether it should also act without `?7` is unresolved). Above the top margin `CUB` walks up to the first line and stops at its left margin, not off the screen (`cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_wraps_a_count_of_columns`; backspace: `extended_reverse_wrap_goes_up_and_round_from_the_top`). **`CUB` wraps the same way, its count included**, as xterm's `CursorBack` serves both: each step past the left margin goes to the end of the line above and from the top margin round to the bottom margin's line, and from left of the left margin the walk turns at the screen's first column (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, `CASE_CUB`; `cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_wraps_a_count_of_columns`, `cub_under_extended_reverse_wrap_from_a_pending_wrap_and_in_margins`). Within a line a pending wrap is its first step, as xterm's `CursorBack` takes one off the count under either mode with `?7`, `(rev || rev2) && screen->do_wrap` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]` 153), where `CUB` without the mode counts from the last column; today's loop takes a pending wrap off the count twice, so for a count of two or more it lands a column right, not followed: it counts the wrap once. `DECRQM`, XTSAVE (slot 47); `DECSTR` keeps it and `RIS` resets it |
| `?47` | alternate screen, pre-1049 | done — switches buffers and does nothing else: no cursor save or restore and no clear, so the alternate screen shows what it held when last left, and the cursor stays where it is (xterm's `srm_ALTBUF`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). `DECRQM` answers from which buffer is shown, as for `?1049` -- baud's choice, since the pinned file records `whichBuf` only for XTSAVE and xterm's `DECRQM` code is not pinned. It was marked done while routed nowhere, and its one test passed on a no-op; every test in `baud-core/tests/legacy_alt_screen.rs` fails on a no-op. `RIS` erases the parked alternate surface, which xterm does not, because the reset frees the cluster and link slots its cells name. A resize while `?47` is up anchors the primary on the one shared cursor, which `?47 l` hands back. Each screen has its own saved cursor, as xterm keeps one per buffer, `sc[screen->whichBuf]` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`), since 2026-10-20 |
| `?1046` | allow switching to and from the alternate screen | done — since 2026-10-19, set at power-on. **Reset, a program cannot switch screens**: resetting it on the alternate screen goes back to the primary as `?47 l` does, and `?47` and `?1049` then do nothing, as xterm's do under titeInhibit (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `srm_ALLOW_ALTBUF`; `alt_screen_switching_can_be_turned_off`), and so do `?1047` and `?1048` (`modes_1047_and_1048_wait_on_1046`). `DECRQM` answers the mode's own sense, `1` while switching is allowed; xterm answers its titeInhibit bit, the inverse, a departure not followed. XTSAVE slot 48; `DECSTR` keeps it, `RIS` sets it |
| `?1047` | alternate screen, cleared on the way out | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program that uses `?1047` gets the alternate screen, and the screen it leaves is wiped**: set switches as `?47` does, showing what the alternate screen held, and reset clears it first, "Clear the screen first if in the Alternate Screen Buffer" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), xterm's `ToAlternate(xw, False)` and `ClearScreen` before `FromAlternate(xw, False)` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `srm_OPT_ALTBUF`). The cursor is neither saved nor restored. `DECRQM` answers which buffer is shown, as for `?47` and `?1049`; XTSAVE keeps that, slot 52, and a restore goes through DECSET's path, as `?1049`'s does (XT-6), so **an XTRESTORE that leaves the alternate screen clears it, where xterm's does not**: its `restoremodes` calls `FromAlternate(xw, False)` with no `ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, 8590-8600), a departure from xterm's practice. It was counted and dropped (`mode_1047_clears_the_alternate_screen_on_the_way_out`, `mode_1047_shows_the_alternate_screen_as_it_was_left`, `mode_1047_reset_on_the_primary_clears_nothing`) |
| `?1048` | save and restore the cursor | done — since 2026-10-19. Set is DECSC and reset DECRC, "Save cursor as in DECSC" and "Restore cursor as in DECRC" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), through the shown screen's slot, the one `ESC 7` and `ESC 8` use, as xterm's `CursorSave` and `CursorRestore` (`srm_SAVE_CURSOR`), so rxvt's `ESC 7` and `?47` pair brings the shell's cursor back. `DECRQM` answers whether a cursor is saved on the screen shown, xterm's `MdBool(screen->sc[screen->whichBuf].saved)` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5716-5717; `decrqm_1048_reads_the_shown_screens_slot`). **XTSAVE of it saves the cursor and XTRESTORE restores it**, xterm's `savemodes` and `restoremodes`, and foot's and ghostty's code. Since 2026-10-20 `?1049` saves into the primary's slot, so `?1049 h`, `?47 l`, `?1048 l` restores what `?1049` saved, as xterm's one `CursorSave` serves both (`the_1049_save_is_the_primarys_decsc`). It was counted and dropped (`mode_1048_saves_and_restores_the_cursor_as_decsc_and_decrc`, `decrqm_reads_1047_and_1048`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_of_1047_and_1048`). At power-on and after `RIS`, `DECRQM ?1048` answers `2` (reset); xterm answers `1`, because `VTRealize` saves both slots at home and its `RIS` (`ReallyReset`) saves the primary's again, an open difference |
| `?66` | DECNKM numeric keypad mode | done — since 2026-10-19, DECKPAM and DECKPNM as a mode, one state with them, so an application can ask with `DECRQM` which keypad it has (`[[source: vt510-decnkm]]`, "provided mainly for use with the request and report mode"; `decnkm_is_the_keypad_mode_and_decrqm_reads_it`). Numeric at power-on and after `DECSTR` and `RIS`, whatever `[keyboard] application_keypad` says (the `ESC =` row); XTSAVE keeps it |
| `?67` | DECBKM backarrow key mode | done — since 2026-10-19, **set, the Backspace key sends BS; reset, the default, DEL** (`[[source: vt510-decbkm]]`), and Control inverts it, xterm's documented `backarrowKey` and `[[source: kitty-keyboard]]`'s legacy table: Control-Backspace sends BS under the default where it sent DEL. `modifyOtherKeys` reports the byte the key sends, and VT52 follows it (`decbkm_chooses_what_the_backarrow_key_sends`, `decbkm_names_the_byte_modify_other_keys_reports`). Kept by `DECSTR`, whose table names it not, reset by `RIS` (`decbkm_is_reported_kept_by_decstr_and_reset_by_ris`) |
| `?69` | DECLRMM left/right margins | done — and set, every line of both screens single-width and DECDWL and DECDHL refused until it is reset (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-29; `declrmm_resets_every_line_and_refuses_the_double_controls`). **Reset, it keeps a wrap pending at a narrower right margin**, since 2026-11-01: the cursor still reports the margin's column and the next character goes to the next line's first column, where it went to the column one past the old margin. `reset_left_right_margins_mode()` (p. 5-30) assigns no position and no flag and D.6.1 (pp. D-13 and D-14) does not name it, as xterm and ghostty keep their flag; every move from there counts from that column, and the row the wrap leaves is not marked as continuing, the line having ended short of its end (`declrmm_reset_and_decstr_keep_a_wrap_pending_at_the_margin`, `a_kept_wrap_moves_from_its_column`, `a_wrap_taken_short_of_the_line_end_leaves_the_row_ended`) |
| `?95` | DECNCSM no clearing screen on column change | **partial** — since 2026-10-14: while set, `?3` does not clear the screen ("When enabled, a column mode change ... does not clear the screen", `[[source: vt510-decncsm]]`) and does everything else it does (`ncsm_withholds_the_clear_and_nothing_else`); DECSCPP never clears, as "This sequence does not affect the column mode change caused by the sequence, DECSCPP". Reset at power-on; `DECRQM` answers it, XTSAVE carries it, `RIS` resets it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`'s table does not name it. No level gate, where xterm answers it only at its VT510 level: note AF's Q-Level. **The gap**: what it keeps is re-cut by the embedder's `Term::resize`, which reflows the primary, where a VT510 "can lose data from page memory. Columns no longer present in page memory are lost" (`[[source: vt510-decscpp]]`) -- the DECSCPP row's gap |
| `?96` | DECRLCM right-to-left copy | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can make a copy read each line from its right edge**: "if \"ABCD\" is to be copied right-to-left ... the string is saved in the paste buffer as \"DCBA.\"" (`[[source: vt510-decrlcm]]`). Set, each line of a copy gives its cells in the order they are drawn from the right edge to the left, the lines in their order: a line nothing reorders comes out reversed, as on the VT510, and a Hebrew line UAX #9 draws right to left in the order it reads; a wide character and a letter with its marks stay whole. **The lines are the lines drawn**: on a finished paragraph the display draws at its words (`[screen] wrap_words`, `docs/bidi.md`), each display line from its right edge, the lines joined as a soft wrap joins rows, since 2026-10-28 (`decrlcm_reads_a_re_broken_paragraph_by_its_display_lines`); a block reads each display line it selects a cell of, a newline between each two, as the block's cells are drawn there (`decrlcm_reads_a_block_by_the_display_lines_it_selects`), and a paragraph the window keeps at the terminal's cut, at the top of what the history keeps, by that cut, the rows as they are drawn (`decrlcm_copies_the_cut_the_window_keeps_at_the_top_of_the_history`, `decrlcm_copies_a_kept_paragraph_beside_a_re_broken_one`). Left to right at power-on and after `RIS`; `DECSTR` keeps it, as its table does not list it (`[[source: vt510-decstr]]`). The core keeps the mode and the embedder's copy reads it (`Term::copy_direction`). `DECRQM` answers it and XTSAVE keeps it (`decrqm_answers_decrlcm_and_the_embedder_reads_it`, `decstr_keeps_decrlcm_and_ris_resets_it`, `decrlcm_copies_each_line_from_its_right_edge`, `decrlcm_reads_a_reordered_line_as_it_is_drawn`, `decrlcm_reads_a_history_line_from_its_right_edge`) |
| `?117` | DECECM erase colour | done — since 2026-10-19. **A program can choose whether an erase paints the colour it set or the screen's own background.** "Controls the background color used when text is erased or new text is scrolled on to the screen ... Set: erase to screen background (VT) ... Reset: erase to text background (PC)" (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-48). Reset, the factory default (Set-Up 2.9.7, p. 2-32) and the state after `RIS`, ED, EL, ECH and every line a scroll, IL, DL, ICH or DCH brings onto the screen take the current background, every other attribute cleared, the `bce` that `TERM=xterm-256color` promises (`[[source: terminfo-xterm-256color]]`); set, the default rendition. `DECSTR` keeps it (table 5-6 does not name it), `DECRQM` answers `1` or `2` and XTSAVE carries it; xterm answers `4` and never acts on it. DECSED, DECSEL and DECSERA keep each character's own rendition under either (`set_erases_to_the_screen_background`, `reset_erases_in_the_current_rendition`, `it_is_reported_kept_by_decstr_and_reset_by_ris`). Built to DECECM's text |
| `?1034` | eightBitInput — Alt/Meta add 128 | done — `Term::eight_bit_input()`, applied by `Term::key` under ISO 8859-1 only. What terminfo's `smm`/`rmm` send |
| `?1036` | metaSendsEscape — Meta prefixes `ESC` | done — `Term::meta_sends_escape()`, **set** at power-on |
| `?1039` | altSendsEscape — Alt prefixes `ESC` | done — `Term::alt_sends_escape()`, **set** at power-on |
| `?1035` | NumLock overrides the application keypad, xterm's `numLock` | done — since 2026-10-19, **set at power-on**, as the resource is: with NumLock on a keypad key sends its digit under DECKPAM too, ctlseqs' "Use the NumLock key to override the application mode", in both ANSI and VT52 (`mode_1035_lets_num_lock_override_the_application_keypad`). **On Linux the whole keypad leaves DECKPAM**, digits, decimal point, operators and Enter: the Wayland driver reports NumLock on every keypad key, the digits too, whose keymap type consumes it, as xterm tests NumLock against the event's whole state (`num_lock_takes_every_keypad_key_out_of_the_application_keypad`); a navigation key, which produces no text, keeps DECKPAM, as xterm overrides only a key with one byte of text (`a_navigation_key_with_num_lock_keeps_the_application_keypad`). **On macOS the mode has no effect**: a Mac keypad has no NumLock key (`0x47` is `kVK_ANSI_KeypadClear`), and the driver reports none. Its Alt half is nothing here: baud's embedder reports Alt itself, and xterm 411 finds the Alt modifier whatever the resource says. A modifier with no ctlseqs code (a lock, Super, Hyper) adds no modifier parameter to any base-layer key (`a_lock_alone_adds_no_modifier_parameter`). Kept by `DECSTR`, set again by `RIS` |
| `?1037` | Delete key sends DEL, xterm's `deleteIsDEL` | done — since 2026-10-19: set, the editing keypad's Delete sends DEL; reset, VT220 Remove, `CSI 3 ~` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). A modified Delete stays `CSI 3 ; m ~` and the numeric keypad's Delete is not the editing keypad's, as in xterm's input.c (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`; `mode_1037_makes_the_editing_delete_send_del`). Since 2026-11-01 **three states, as xterm's**: neither set nor reset, at power-on and after `RIS`, the keyboard decides, Remove on the default and VT220 keyboards and DEL on the legacy one; and **`DECRQM` answers set exactly when the editing Delete sends DEL** under the keyboard in force, reset under the termcap, Sun and HP keyboards, whose tables send their own, where xterm answers set (`input-coverage.md`'s keyboards section; `the_sun_delete_is_its_own_and_1037_answers_what_is_sent`). **XTSAVE and DECTSR keep what `DECRQM` answers**, so a restore brings back set or reset and never the unset state; under the termcap, Sun and HP keyboards a set `?1037` is saved as reset, and under the SCO keyboard a reset one as set, where xterm saves its three-state value, `DP_DELETE_IS_DEL` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 8256), a difference recorded and open (`xtsave_and_dectsr_keep_1037_as_decrqm_answers_it`). Kept by `DECSTR`, unset by `RIS` (`ris_returns_the_key_modes_to_power_on`) |
| `?1050` | terminfo/termcap function-key mode | done — since 2026-11-01 **each key of xterm's xtermcap.c table for which the xterm-256color entry (the `TERM` baud gives the child) has a string sends that string**, from a table compiled from the pinned entry and xterm's, whatever DECCKM, DECKPAM and DECBKM say; a key or modifier no row reaches keeps the default keyboard's encoding, the entry's `kb2`, keypad Begin, and `kcbt`, Shift-Tab, among them (`input-coverage.md`'s keyboards section; `the_termcap_keyboard_sends_the_entrys_strings`, `every_key_the_termcap_entry_names_is_sent_or_kept_for_a_reason`). One of the keyboard family below |
| `?1051` | Sun function-key mode | done — since 2026-11-01 the function and editing keys send ctlseqs' SUN column, `CSI 224 z` for F1, the arrows and the keypad's DECKPAM rows unchanged; under DECKPNM a navigation key on the keypad sends the Sun code of the key it duplicates (`the_sun_keyboard_sends_the_xterm_sun_entrys_keys`, `the_sun_keyboard_leaves_the_arrows_and_the_keypad`). **The six keyboard modes are one family**: DECSET selects one, DECRST of any returns to the default keyboard, DECRQM answers set for the one in force alone, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE keep the family as one value, DECTSR carries all six, `RIS` returns to the default and `DECSTR` keeps it (`the_keyboards_are_one_family_and_any_reset_returns_to_the_default`, `xtsave_keeps_the_keyboard_family_as_one_value`, `decrsts_restores_the_keyboard`, `ris_returns_to_the_default_keyboard_and_decstr_keeps_it`) |
| `?1052` | HP function-key mode | done — since 2026-11-01 ctlseqs' HP column, F1 `ESC p`, the arrows `ESC A`-`ESC D`, Page Down `ESC S` and Page Up `ESC T`; the HP 2392A's own table gives seven keys other codes, an open conflict: [Next] `ESC U` and [Prev] `ESC V` where baud sends `ESC S` and `ESC T`, Shift-Up `ESC S` and Shift-Down `ESC T` where baud sends `CSI 1 ; 2 A` and `CSI 1 ; 2 B`, Shift-Home `ESC F` where baud sends `CSI 1 ; 2 h`, Shift-Tab `ESC i` where baud sends `CSI Z`, and Tab `ESC I` where baud sends HT (`[[source: hp-2392a-rm]]`; `the_hp_keyboard_sends_the_xterm_hp_entrys_keys`, `a_modified_hp_key_is_shaped_by_the_modify_resources`) |
| `?1053` | SCO function-key mode | done — since 2026-11-01 the VT520's SCO Console keys, F1 `CSI M` and its Shift, Ctrl and Ctrl/Shift columns, Delete DEL (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` pp. 6-2 to 6-6; `the_sco_keyboard_sends_the_vt520s_function_keys`, `the_sco_keyboard_sends_the_vt520s_other_keys`) |
| `?1060` | legacy keyboard emulation, X11R6 | done — since 2026-11-01 F1-F4 `CSI 11 ~`-`CSI 14 ~`, Control adding 10 to a function key, modifiers as modifyKeyboard allows, Delete DEL (`the_legacy_keyboard_is_x11r6s`) |
| `?1061` | VT220 keyboard emulation | done — since 2026-11-01 Home Find `CSI 1 ~`, End Select `CSI 4 ~`, the keypad's `+` its `,`, its navigation keys their digits, Control adding 10 to a function key; Shift with a function key sends the key's own sequence while DECUDK is unbuilt (`the_vt220_keyboard_has_the_vt220s_editing_keypad`, `the_vt220_keypad_has_a_comma_and_no_navigation_keys`). At Level 1 and in VT52 mode every one of the six is recorded and the keyboard is the VT100's or the VT52's (`level_one_and_vt52_keep_their_own_keyboards`) |
| `?1010` | scroll to the bottom on output (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on, which is baud's window over the history as it was: output keeps the reader where they scrolled (note V, V2). **Set, a read from the program sends the primary's window live**, once a read, at the read's end: xterm's scrollTtyOutput does it as each read arrives, before parsing (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), a practice not followed: baud sends it once a read, at the read's end, so the read that sets `?1010` itself sends the window live and a read that ends on the alternate screen leaves the primary's window where it was (`scroll_on_output_sends_the_window_live_when_set`). xterm's resource is true by default; baud keeps its own. XTSAVE slot 44 |
| `?1011` | scroll to the bottom on a key press (rxvt) | done — since 2026-10-19, set at power-on, as a key has always snapped the window to the live screen here, as ghostty's `keystroke` and foot's view reset do (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-config]]`, `[[source: foot-input]]`); xterm's scrollKey defaults false, and baud keeps its own. Reset, a key leaves the window where it is; a paste still snaps. Read by the macOS app through `Term::resource`; XTSAVE slot 45 |
| `?1014` | xterm's `fastScroll` | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `3`, permanently set: baud draws at most one frame a display refresh and never one output already shifted off, which is what the resource selects ("suppressing screen refreshes for the special case when output to the screen has completely shifted the contents off-screen", `[[source: xterm-man]]`), and no switch could draw every intermediate scroll. DECSET and DECRST are counted |
| `?1041` | use the CLIPBOARD selection (selectToClipboard) | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on. **Set, a selection is copied to the clipboard as it is made**, as xterm's SELECT names CLIPBOARD rather than PRIMARY (`[[source: xterm-man]]`); reset, the macOS app copies a finished selection only under `[clipboard] copy_on_select = "on"`, since 2026-10-21 the default (note V, V13), the pasteboard being the one selection macOS has; `"off"` leaves the copy to `?1041` alone (`a_finished_selection_goes_to_the_pasteboard_unless_the_key_is_off`). Read by the macOS app; XTSAVE slot 46. Since 2026-10-20 the Linux app reads it for every use of SELECT, as xterm does: reset, a selection made goes to the primary selection and the middle button and Shift+Insert paste the primary; set, both use the clipboard instead, and the primary is not set; an `OSC 52` `s`, or an empty `Pc`, lands on the same one (`select_is_the_primary_until_1041`, `on_wayland_each_selection_named_is_its_own`) |
| `?1044` | reuse the data copied to CLIPBOARD (keepClipboard) | done — since 2026-10-19, `DECRQM` answers `3`, permanently set: xterm serves CLIPBOARD lazily and the resource makes it serve the copy it saved rather than a later selection (`[[source: xterm-button]]`, `ConvertSelection`); the macOS pasteboard holds the text baud wrote at the copy, so the clipboard always keeps the data copied. DECSET and DECRST are counted |
| `?2001`, `?2005`, `?2006` | readline mouse button-1, character-quoting, newline pasting | done — since 2026-10-19, reset at power-on: a click on the cursor's line moves the shell's point there, a paste is quoted byte by byte with the tty's literal-next character, a paste keeps its line breaks ([input-coverage.md](input-coverage.md)). XTSAVE slots 49-51. Under `?2004` a bracketed paste keeps its line breaks whatever `?2006` says. xterm's `_WriteSelectionData` turns LF into CR inside the brackets too unless `?2006` is set, and that is a departure, recorded as open. |
| `?2002`, `?2003` | readline mouse button-2 and button-3 | **out** — each changes a gesture baud does not have: a middle-button paste and a double-click of button 3 that deletes the selection, xterm's X conventions; on macOS the middle button pastes nothing and the right button is the menu's. Not recognised: DECSET counted, `DECRQM` answers 0 |
| `?1001` | highlight tracking | done — the fifth tracking level, exclusive with `?9`, `?1000`, `?1002` and `?1003` (ctlseqs' "five modes of the mouse protocols"), where it was a flag beside the level until 2026-10-11. Normal tracking's presses and releases; button 1's press is owed the program's XTHIMOUSE answer, the region the answer starts tracks the mouse within the program's rows (`Term::highlight`) and the macOS app draws it through the selection's highlight, and its release is reported as `CSI t` or `CSI T` in the encoding in force -- refused whole and counted where the encoding cannot name a position. Nothing waits on the answer. Under `?1016` the region's four positions are cells and the mouse's two pixels, the one reading both paragraphs allow; a long-form release with no pixel is refused and counted. Where the text is silent or reads two ways -- the short form's test, the SGR and urxvt forms, an empty row range -- the question is open. `a_press_is_reported_as_normal_tracking_reports_it`, `it_is_a_level_exclusive_with_the_other_four`, `the_end_is_held_to_the_rows_the_program_gave`, `under_sgr_pixels_the_region_is_in_cells_and_the_mouse_in_pixels`, `the_program_s_region_is_drawn_through_the_selection_highlight` |

### SGR

Enumerated against `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 and, for the extensions,
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` § "Character Attributes".

| parameters | status |
|---|---|
| 0 reset | done |
| 1, 2 bold/dim · 22 neither | done |
| 3 italic · 23 not | done |
| 4 underline, 21 double, **4:1..4:5** styled · 24 / 4:0 not | done — five styles, exactly one in force: straight, double, and `[[source: kitty-underlines]]`'s curly, dotted and dashed, each its own `Attrs` bit and drawn by the software compositor and the cell shader. A `4:n` *replaces* the family, and so do a bare 4 (the spec's backward-compatible 4:1) and 21 (`[[source: ecma48]]`'s "doubly underlined", the state 4:2 names), so 4:3 then 21 is double alone and 21 and 4:2 are one rendition (practice departs three ways and is not followed: vte reads 21 as bold off, `[[source: alacritty-vte-sgr]]`; the Linux console as a single underline, `[[source: linux-vt-sgr]]`; xterm holds 4 and 21 as two bits, `[[source: xterm-sgr]]`); 24 and 4:0 clear all five; an unknown n clears rather than aborts the walk. **4:3 is not 4;3** -- sfte flattens the colon and gets underline-plus-italic, measured -- and DECRQSS reports the three colon styles as 4:n so the reply round-trips. `each_colon_style_sets_exactly_its_own_bit`, `the_colon_form_is_not_the_semicolon_form`, `a_style_replaces_the_family_and_4_0_clears_it`, `twenty_one_replaces_the_family_in_either_order`, `twenty_one_and_4_2_are_one_rendition`, `a_colon_underline_style_round_trips`, `a_double_underline_after_another_style_reports_alone_and_round_trips` |
| 5, 6 blink · 25 steady | done — **each rate drawn at its own rate, and the two one aspect**, since 2026-10-13. `SGR 5` sets `Attrs::BLINK`, which the app blinks at 75 a minute (0.4 s shown, 0.4 s hidden, `BLINK_HALF_MS` on the display link's clock), inside `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117's "slowly blinking (less then 150 per minute)"; `SGR 6` sets `Attrs::RAPID_BLINK`, a bit of its own in the twenty-byte `StyleWord`'s `Attrs` (not the eight-byte cell), which it blinks at 150 a minute (0.2 s and 0.2 s, `RAPID_HALF_MS`), the floor of "rapidly blinking (150 per minute or more)". Integer const assertions beside the two halves hold both bounds, and `each_blink_rate_flips_at_its_own_rate` counts 75 and 150 hides across a minute of the clock. Each rate shows for half its cycle, as 5 always did: the text gives a rate and no duty cycle. **5 and 6 replace each other and the later wins** (`Attrs::BLINKS`, `SgrAttr::BlinkRate`): § 8.3.117 lists them as two rates of one aspect beside "25 steady (not blinking)" and closes "The usable combinations of parameter values are determined by the implementation"; 25 clears both, and so does 0 (`rapid_blink_is_its_own_rendition`, `the_later_blink_rate_wins`, `steady_clears_both_blink_rates`, `the_blink_rates_are_the_same_at_every_read_size`, every read size and every split into two reads, and `the_blink_rates_are_masked_replaces`, which holds the `sgr-table` arm's derived fold to the grammar's). Until 2026-10-14 6 set 5's bit and blinked at its rate, a stated departure, and DECRQSS answered `5` for it; it answers `6` and the answer round-trips (`decrqss_answers_6_for_rapid_blink_and_it_round_trips`). **Each rate hides its text only in its own off phase**, in the software compositor and in the cell shader, whose `blink_hidden` is the mask of the shapes the frame hides (`BlinkPhases::hidden`; `each_blink_rate_hides_only_in_its_own_off_phase`, `hidden_blinking_text_is_the_frame_of_its_spaces`, `a_hidden_cells_records_are_not_drawn`, and the pixel oracle's mixed-phase scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`), and **a frame knows which rates are on screen**, so a rate's flip repaints only while text at that rate is drawn and a slow-only screen builds no frame at the rapid flips (`Blinking::flips`; `a_rate_flips_a_frame_only_while_its_text_is_on_screen`, `a_blinking_row_keeps_blinking_through_incremental_frames`, `a_blinking_slot_off_screen_is_not_seen`, `a_rapid_slot_alone_is_seen_on_the_cell_path`, and `the_tick_takes_the_blink_phase_under_the_motion_preference` for the tick). 2.5 cycles a second is under WCAG 2.3.1's "more than three times in any one second period" (`[[source: wcag22]]`), whose flash is "a pair of opposing changes in relative luminance": one shown-and-hidden cycle is one flash, not two. **Under macOS's Reduce Motion both are held shown** (`text_blink`, `reduce_motion_holds_blinking_text_shown`, both rates, with the tick held to it by `the_tick_takes_the_blink_phase_under_the_motion_preference`): `[[source: apple-hig-accessibility]]` puts blinking animation under that setting, and WCAG 2.2.2 (`[[source: wcag22]]`) asks for a way to stop blinking that lasts past five seconds -- a departure from "slowly blinking" and "rapidly blinking" made at the system's request, stated here so it is not silent. **Under macOS's Dim Flashing Lights each off phase is drawn faint**, the text half way to its page as `SGR 2` mixes it, at the same two rates, so § 8.3.117's rates hold and each flip changes less of the page (`dim_off_phases`, `BlinkPhases::dimmed`; `dim_flashing_lights_draws_the_off_phase_faint_at_the_same_rates`, `a_faint_off_phase_draws_the_text_half_way_to_its_page`, `a_faint_phase_flips_frames_and_dims_only_hidden_phases`, and the pixel oracle's faint scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`): Apple's article asks that "an animation sequence with flashing lights in a game or app" be adjusted (`[[source: apple-mediaaccessibility-flashing-lights]]`), and a faint off phase was taken over steady text, a slower rate and an off phase held under WCAG's general-flash threshold. With Reduce Motion on as well the text is steady. The setting is read at launch and followed when its notification arrives (`the_flashing_lights_notification_marks_its_own_setting`, `a_local_centre_post_reaches_the_default_centre`). **The Linux app blinks both rates** since 2026-10-21, its one wait woken a millisecond past each flip of a rate on screen and nothing while none is (`effects::next_flip`, `the_next_flip_is_ahead_and_reads_the_new_phase`, `only_a_rate_on_screen_bounds_the_wait`, `blinking_text_wakes_at_its_own_flips_and_repaints_whole`). It holds both shown when the desktop portal's `reduced-motion` asks (`reduced_motion_holds_text_shown_and_turns_the_default_trail_off`); the portal has no Dim Flashing Lights counterpart to follow |
| 7 reverse · 27 positive | done |
| 8 conceal · 28 reveal | done |
| 9 strike · 29 not | done |
| 30-37, 39 foreground · 40-47, 49 background | done |
| 90-97, 100-107 aixterm bright | done — palette indices 8..15 |
| 38/48 `;5;n` 256-colour, `;2;r;g;b` truecolour | done — **a cell keeps its rendition while the cells name no more renditions than the table has slots** (65,536 at the app's provisioning): `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s SGR, page 5-104, "In no way does the selection of current rendition affect the rendition of characters already entered into the display". Since 2026-10-14 the rendition table sweeps -- at 7/8 load and before an eviction, at most once a sixteenth of its writes -- freeing every slot no cell of either screen or the history names, slot 0 never, and the renderer repaints a frame whose slots were reclaimed (`live_cells_keep_their_renditions_through_five_tables_of_churn`, `the_other_screens_cells_are_marked`, `the_historys_cells_are_marked`, `the_primarys_history_is_marked_from_the_alternate_screen`, `an_erased_rows_rendition_is_marked`, `a_churned_table_sweeps_before_it_fills`, `a_full_table_sweeps_before_it_evicts`, `a_sweep_is_chunk_invariant`, `a_swept_slot_reused_in_place_is_repainted`). Until then a session that had minted more renditions than slots evicted a named slot and recoloured its cells: yazi's chafa previews left 191 of 7,110 final cells differing from alacritty, 0 of 7,093 now. **Open**: with more distinct renditions named at once than slots it still evicts and recolours, as chafa printing into the shell does (81 cells of 7,199 differ); and a full table evicts without sweeping within a sixteenth of its writes of the last sweep, which happens only where the cells name more than 15/16 of the slots (`a_full_table_evicts_without_sweeping_within_a_sixteenth_of_its_writes`) |
| 38/48 `:5:n` / `:2:Pi:r:g:b` sub-parameter form | done — both the six-part form with a colour-space id and the five-part form without |
| 10-19 fonts | done — **text in `SGR 11` to `19` draws in the font `[font] alternatives` names for it**, on all three paths, and in the primary font for `SGR 10` and for a font no face is named for. `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 names "primary (default) font" and nine alternatives and says nothing of what any looks like; FNT (§ 8.3.53) would name each "according to a register which is to be established", so the setting is where they are named, empty by default. A font's bold and italic are its family's own faces, picked by the search the first of `fonts` takes, never synthesised, and a character the font lacks comes from the fallback chain, configured before embedded before the system's. The font is a rendition in `StyleWord`'s spare bits, so the eight-byte cell is unchanged, answered by DECRQSS and readable through `Term::style_of`; the renderer resolves it once a slot to a font the stack has faces for, so a stack with none keys every glyph as it did before. `each_alternative_font_draws_from_its_own_face`, `an_alternative_font_with_no_face_draws_the_primary`, `an_alternative_font_draws_its_own_styles`, `a_restack_that_names_a_font_redraws_its_text`, `the_alternatives_name_the_faces_of_sgr_11_to_19`, and the pixel oracle's `alternative_fonts_draw_alike_on_every_arm` |
| 58/59 underline colour | done — `StyleWord` grew to sixteen bytes; the *cell* holds a `StyleId` index and did not change |
| 20 Fraktur | done — **text under `SGR 20` draws in the Fraktur face `[font] fraktur` names**, on all three paths, its bold and italic from that family's own faces and what it lacks from the fallback chain. § 8.3.117 names the look, "Fraktur (Gothic)", and baud carries no Fraktur face, so **with none named, the default, 20 draws in the face the text would take without it**, and the setting's documentation says so: drawing the letters from the Mathematical Fraktur characters instead would be a per-glyph rule in the font chain and would use characters the Unicode Standard says "should not be used to represent styling of nonmathematical text" (`[[source: unicode18-ch22]]`), and the font chain takes no per-glyph rules, so the primary face draws it, and this row reads done with that default stated. Out of the box, then, 20 shows no Fraktur look. This stays **open** until a Fraktur face is embedded: an OFL face as an interim, or baud's own glyphs. With an alternative font set too, the Fraktur face is first and the alternative behind it, then the chain: 20 names a look the text defines and 11-19 a slot it leaves undefined. 23 clears italic **and** fraktur, as "not italicized, not fraktur" says. `sgr_20_draws_the_fraktur_face`, `fraktur_is_over_the_alternative_font`, `the_fraktur_key_names_the_face_of_sgr_20`, and the pixel oracle's `alternative_fonts_draw_alike_on_every_arm` |
| 26, 50 proportional spacing | done — **text under `SGR 26` draws at the cell pitch, exactly as it draws without it**, on all three paths. `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 reserves 26 "for proportional spacing as specified in CCITT Recommendation T.61" and 50 for its cancel; `[[source: itu-t61]]` defines 26 as "proportional spacing character pitch may be used", and its Note 1 leaves the choice to the receiving terminal: "the text that follows may be presented with proportional spacing at the recipient's option". baud is the recipient and keeps the cell pitch, the case the same note provides for: "For interworking with devices not capable of proportional spacing, no line or part of a line of text should contain more characters than are permitted by the current pitch value". Laying the run out at a proportional face's own advances would part the glyphs from the cells the cursor and a selection count, and a proportional face drawn a glyph a cell changes the face and not the spacing, so the cell pitch is kept over both. 26 is recorded, answered by DECRQSS and cleared by 50 and by 0. `sgr_26_is_recorded_but_changes_no_geometry`, `the_shared_reset_parameters_clear_both_attributes`, and the pixel oracle's `proportional_gate` |
| 51 framed, 53 overlined, 54, 55 | done — **drawn on all three paths**, the software compositor, the instance path and the cell shader, in the text's colour as the strike is: `SGR 58` is "the underline color" (`[[source: kitty-underlines]]`) and neither is an underline; ghostty draws its overline in the foreground too (`[[source: ghostty-generic-d9840f3]]`). 53 is a line across the top of the cell, one underline thickness tall, where ghostty puts it (`[[source: ghostty-metrics-d9840f3]]`). 51 is one box around each run of framed cells on a row: a line across the top and the bottom of every cell, and a side only where the run starts and where it ends. Both are choices the text leaves open, since § 8.3.117 says only "framed" and "overlined". 54 clears framed **and** encircled, 55 the overline. A framed cell whose text is hidden, concealed (`SGR 8`, "concealed characters") or in its blink's off phase, draws its background and nothing of its own, frame included, and the visible cells beside it close their box with a side at the seam. `an_overline_is_a_line_across_the_top_of_its_cells`, `the_overline_is_the_text_colour_and_not_the_underline_colour`, `a_framed_run_is_one_box`, `a_framed_run_closes_beside_a_hidden_cell`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate`; a glyph reaching into a framed or overlined cell takes the order 52's row gives, `reach_gate` |
| 52 encircled | done — **one enclosure around each run of encircled cells on a row, on all three paths**, in the text's colour as the frame is: the frame's top and bottom lines across the cells between the ends, and at each end a side at the run's edge between two quarter circles half a cell wide: a lone cell is a stadium; a run of two cells, a wide character among them, is a rectangle with rounded corners of radius half a cell's width. The curves keep to the corners, where the end glyph has less ink than the half ellipse crossed. A stroke at the cell's edge still touches ink at small sizes: 64 of 94 glyphs at 13 px on a lone cell. A half ellipse filling each end cell crossed most end glyphs' ink at 9-13 px, so rounded corners are drawn over it and over drawing 52 as 51's square box, as mintty does (`[[source: mintty-termout-3-7-8]]`). § 8.3.117 says only "52 encircled". The run is enclosed whole, rather than an ellipse a cell or one true ellipse a run: the Unicode Standard has "no defined way of indicating the application of a combining enclosing mark to more than a single base character" (`[[source: unicode18-ch22]]`), and 51 draws one box a run. The end is an anti-aliased mask of exactly one cell (`baud_render::boxes::draw_ring`), cached in the atlas once a slot has been encircled and drawn by the software compositor and the instance path as a procedural glyph is and by the cell shader from the glyph map under keys of its own, so the three sample the same bytes. A visible encircled cell beside a hidden one closes the run, as a frame does. A glyph reaching into an encircled cell lies under its outline when it is in that row or reaches down from the row above, and over it when it reaches up from the row below, on all three paths, as for every decoration: each row's glyphs and then its decorations, rows top to bottom (`[[source: css21-zindex]]`); a composition lies over it as over an underline. 54 clears it. `an_encircled_run_is_one_enclosure`, `an_encircled_end_keeps_to_its_corners`, `an_encircled_run_closes_beside_a_hidden_cell`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, `the_line_shape_bits_match_the_shader`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate`, `reach_gate` and `composed_decoration_gate` |
| 56, 57 | **out** — `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 reserves 56-59 for future standardization and `[[source: ctlseqs]]` assigns 56 and 57 nothing. 58/59 are done only because ctlseqs assigns *them* the underline colour, which is an extension over ECMA-48 rather than a reading of it. `sgr_56_and_57_are_reserved_and_set_nothing` |
| 60-63, 65 ideogram lines | done — `[[source: ecma48]]` § 8.3.117 defines 60-63 as "ideogram underline or right side line", "ideogram double underline or double line on the right side", "ideogram overline or left side line" and "ideogram double overline or double line on the left side", each named for two edges because which edge is marked follows the writing direction, and 65 "cancels the effect of the rendition aspects established by parameter values 60 to 64". **baud's text is horizontal, so they are drawn as the under and over lines**, on all three paths: 60 the straight underline and 61 the double, beside whatever `SGR 4` style is in force, 62 the overline and 63 two lines at the top, one thickness and a pixel apart. 60 and 61 take `SGR 58`'s colour as an underline does, and 62 and 63 the text's as the overline does. They stay a separate aspect from 4 and 21: 24 leaves them and 65 clears them. `Attrs` went to `u32` for the five bits. `tests/sgr/ideogram.rs`, `ideogram_lines_are_the_under_and_over_lines_of_horizontal_text`, `ideogram_double_lines_are_two_lines`, `sixty_five_and_fifty_five_cancel_their_lines`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate` |
| 64 ideogram stress marking | done — **a filled dot over each marked character**, on all three paths, in the text's colour. § 8.3.117 names it and gives no shape or side, so baud draws what the web draws for text with no language (`[[source: css-text-decor-3]]`): `text-emphasis-position`'s "Initial: over right", over in horizontal text; a filled `dot`, "Display small circles as marks. The filled dot is U+2022"; `text-emphasis-color`'s "Initial: currentcolor", the text's colour, so `SGR 58` does not reach it. A mark given only as filled or open is a circle in horizontal text ("If only filled or open is specified, the shape keyword computes to circle in horizontal typographic modes"); filled is U+25CF. The marks are "drawn once for each typographic character unit", so a wide character takes one, centred across its two cells, and are not drawn for "the Unicode separator classes (Z*)", for "any characters that belong to the Unicode P* general category and do not NFKD normalize [UAX15] to any of the following symbols" (`#`, `%`, `&`, `@`, `§`, `¶`, the per mille and per ten thousand signs and a few more, and their small and fullwidth forms, keep the mark), or for "the Unicode classes for control codes and unassigned characters (Cc, Cf, Cn)": a space, a comma, an ideographic comma, a soft hyphen or an unassigned codepoint takes none. The set is built from `[[source: gc-data]]` and `[[source: unicode-data]]`'s decompositions (`[[source: unicode17-ch3]]` D65) into a two-stage bit trie by `scripts/gen-stress-table.py`, read from Rust and as the same bytes by the cell shader, so a mark costs two loads. A cluster takes the mark unless its base is one CSS leaves bare; a space with combining characters (gc M*, `[[source: unicode17-ch3]]` D52) takes it, as CSS says. The cell shader sees a cluster's slot and not its base, so the overlay carries the renderer's answer for each cluster on screen. The cell has no ruby space above the line, so the mark is a square two line thicknesses on a side, centred, a side's length below the cell's top, over a dot under each cell, and over leaving it undrawn, as baud builds a protocol whole. 65 clears it. `a_stress_mark_is_a_dot_over_each_character`, `sixty_five_cancels_the_stress_mark`, `the_stress_mark_skips_what_css_leaves_bare`, `a_cluster_takes_the_mark_as_its_base_does`, `the_stress_mark_table_matches_the_shader`, `the_bare_mask_bit_matches_the_shader`, and the pixel oracle's `decoration_gate` |

**Fonts moved from `out` to recorded by measuring rather than assuming.** The row
said they needed cell bits that did not exist. A font is a *rendition*, and
rendition lives in `StyleWord` -- which carried sixteen bits of explicit
padding, put there because zerocopy rejects implicit padding. `PackedCell`
never had to grow, and the eight-byte cell was never the constraint. "A bit set
and never read" resolved the way it did for charset designations: baud records
the selection and `Term::style_of` is where a renderer reads it. Since
2026-10-23 the renderer does, and draws the font `[font] alternatives` names.

Every SGR parameter above is **done** or **out**. 10-19 and 20 read `done`
until 2026-10-18 while no path drew them, and were **partial** from that
day to 2026-10-23, until `[font] alternatives` and `[font] fraktur` named
their faces.
26 and 50 were **partial** from that day to 2026-10-23, until T.61 was read:
its Note 1 leaves proportional spacing to the recipient. 51, 53 and 60-64 read `done` over that gap too and are drawn since
2026-10-18, and 52, **partial** for part of it, since the same day. 6 was **partial** from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-14, while it blinked at 5's slow rate where § 8.3.117 asks 150 a minute or more. `CSI m` used
to be listed as `partial` on the strength of that last row, which conflated two
different things. `out` is a scope decision with a reason; `partial` is a row
that names what it does not do yet. A font selection that no renderer reads is
that second case, and the 10-19 row said so until its fonts were drawn, as
the 5, 6 row did until 2026-10-14.

**This was the largest `parsed` gap in the codebase and the table did not show
it.** `SgrAttr::fold` handled `Reset` and foreground only, falling through to "no
change" for `Set`, `Unset` and every `Bg` — because `StyleWord` was a bare `u32`
holding a 24-bit foreground and nothing else. Bold, italic, underline, reverse,
blink, conceal, strike and **all background colours** were parsed and discarded:
support in the parser, nothing on the screen. `StyleWord` now carries
foreground, background and attributes.

---

## Tier 2 — VT220 / VT420

In scope. Needed by real applications (vim, tmux, htop) but not by every one.

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `CSI ! p` | DECSTR soft reset | done — resets the DEC-defined state and **not** the alternate screen, mouse tracking or its encoding. Those are xterm extensions, so a DEC list cannot mention them and xterm decides: in `ReallyReset` all three are inside the `if (full)` RIS branch, `FromAlternate` next to `ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`). **It empties the shown screen's saved cursor and keeps the other screen's**, as xterm's soft reset writes `sc[screen->whichBuf]` alone, so a program that soft-resets on the alternate screen still gets the shell's cursor back at `?1049 l` (`resets_and_the_two_slots`). baud reset them until 2026-08-27, so a full-screen application recovering with `CSI ! p` was dropped onto the primary screen and lost what `?1049 h` had saved. **Of the mode word it restores only what xterm's soft reset restores**: DECTCEM, DECLRMM, DECOM, DECCKM, DECAWM, reverse wraparound and IRM, and releases `?2026`, which xterm lacks; LNM, `?4`, `?5`, `?8`, `?1004`, `?1034`, `?1036`, `?1039` and `?2004` stay, as they do there. It restores the keypad to numeric and `modifyOtherKeys` to its first value, which xterm does on both resets and RIS here did not. Until 2026-09-24 it restored the whole mode word, so a program recovering with `CSI ! p` lost its bracketed paste (`decstr_restores_the_modes_xterm_restores_and_keeps_the_rest`, `decstr_and_ris_restore_the_keypad_and_modify_other_keys`). It clears every `OSC 4` override and keeps the dynamic colours, as xterm's common path does (`both_resets_clear_every_palette_override`, since 2026-09-29). **It leaves the cursor where it is**, since 2026-10-14: "DECSTR has no affect on the Active Position" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4, DECSTR note c, p. 32), and the home in `[[source: vt510-decstr]]`'s and `[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]`'s tables is the saved cursor state's; it homed before (`decstr_leaves_the_cursor_where_it_is`, `decstr_under_origin_mode_leaves_the_cursor`). A pending wrap at the screen's edge survives it (`decstr_keeps_a_pending_wrap_at_the_screen_edge`), and since 2026-11-01 so does one at a narrower right margin, kept on the margin's column as `?69 l` keeps it, where it was left one column past it as a column (`decstr_keeps_a_wrap_pending_at_a_narrower_right_margin`). See `tests/xterm/soft_reset_scope.rs` |
| `CSI Pl ; Pc " p` | DECSCL select conformance level | done — since 2026-10-22 **a program can ask baud to behave as a VT100 (Level 1), a VT220 (2), a VT320 (3) or a VT420 (4), and baud then does only what that level defines**. Each control, mode, character set, SGR parameter and key carries the level that introduces it in one table built at compile time and indexed at dispatch (`baud_domain::LEVELS`): the lowest level its `[[source: dec-std-070]]` `Levels:` field names, an `x` an extension baud has, every higher level keeping it. A control above the level in force is not executed and is counted, DECSCL note 1's "the normal rule that Level 2 functions only work in Level 2 operation" (PDF 226): at Level 1 ICH, ECH, DECTCEM, DECSTR, DECSCA, the selective erases, LS2, LS3, the GR locking shifts, S7C1T, S8C1T and Terminal State Interrogation, below Level 3 SU, SD, DECSCPP and DECSLPP, below Level 4 the side margins, DECIC, DECDC, DECBI, DECFI and the rectangle operations (`level_one_does_not_execute_a_level_two_function`, `levels_two_three_and_four_add_their_functions`). The table is held to the standard: its fields transcribed from the pinned copy by `scripts/extract-std070-levels.py` (`baud-domain/tests/std070_levels.tsv`, its control `the_transcription_reads_the_fields_its_pages_show`), each row to its field (`every_row_is_its_fields_lowest_level`), each field to a row or a named reason it has none (`every_field_is_a_row_or_named_undispatched`), each row to the forms that perform it (`the_parsed_forms_name_their_rows`). One row departs from its own field, by its own algorithm: the announcers, at Level 3. LS2 and LS3 keep their fields' Level 2, as the VT420's "Control Functions Ignored in Level 1" lists them (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 4-1), where 070-3 §3.6.1.1 names them in Level 1 operation. The VT420's table was read beside the fields: it ignores at Level 1 what the fields put at Level 2 or above but for DECTCEM, SU, SD, DECSLPP, DECXCPR, DECLRMM, DECRQUPSS and the macro space report, which it does not list, a product's departure not followed. **A control no DEC level defines acts at every level** -- xterm's, kitty's, contour's, and ECMA-48's own such as CHA, VPA, REP and SL (`an_extension_acts_at_level_one`) -- and one only the VT510's or VT520's manual defines, DECSCUSR, DECSWBV, DECSMBV, DECST8C, DECNCSM, DECECM and the VT500 character sets, is Level 4's, the VT510's DECSCL making 62, 63 and 64 one "VT Level 4 mode"; the DEC controls only a VT220, VT340 or VT420 manual defines take the levels the VT420's Table 4-1 gives, the keyboard report Level 2's and DECBKM, DECSDM, DECGEPM and the data integrity and session reports Level 1's. **At Level 1**: SGR acts on 0, 1, 4, 5, 7 and 8 and on the parameters no DEC level defines, such as 3 and 23, and not on colour or 22, 24, 25, 27 and 28, §5.6.3's Color Text "at Level 2 or higher" and the VT520's Table 5-15 (`level_one_sgr_keeps_bold_and_drops_colour`, `level_one_drops_colour_from_a_repeated_sgr`); SCS designates ASCII, line drawing, UK, the NRC sets, JIS-Roman and Katakana, into G0 and G1 only, note 1, the NRC sets as Table 1's extension 9 and DECNRCM's "1x" and the VT340 and VT420 have them and the rest by SCS's own table (`level_one_designates_into_g0_and_g1_only`); DECRQSS, every string, DECRQM, DECRQPSR, DECRQTSR, DECRSPS and DECRSTS are not recognised, answer nothing and are counted, as the VT420 ignores them, the reading that keeps to DEC STD 070 (`level_one_has_no_terminal_state_interrogation`); under ISO 8859-1 each received byte loses its eighth bit "before any processing occurs" (070-3 §3.7.5.1), from the byte after the control that starts the strip to the byte after one that ends it, in one read or across reads, and under UTF-8 nothing is stripped (`level_one_strips_the_eighth_bit_under_latin1`); the keyboard is a VT100's (`docs/input-coverage.md`, the DECSCL row; `the_level_one_keyboard`); and since 2026-10-30 its soft reset loads Level 1's sets, ASCII in G0 to G3 (070-3 §3.6.4.1), so `ESC N A` draws `A` and not DEC Supplemental's `Á` (`level_one_single_shifts_draw_ascii`). The texts differ there: DECSCL's algorithm calls `soft_terminal_reset()`, whose body opens "if (conformance level != LEVEL_1)", the guard of DECSTR's note b ("ignored when operating at Level 1"), and so would leave the sets as they were, where §3.6.4 says its assignments "apply to the power-on and soft reset states" and DECSCL's note 3 that the reset "places the terminal into a known state"; baud follows the two. `RIS` from Level 1 returns to Level 4 before it loads the sets (`a_full_reset_from_level_one_loads_the_level_four_sets`). `ESC <` returns to Level 1 without a reset and, since 2026-10-30, **to Level 1's character sets**: a program that leaves VT52 and single-shifts draws ASCII, where after leaving VT52 from Level 2, 3 or 4 `ESC N A` drew the supplemental `Á` until the sets were reset. 070-0A's EXIT VT52 algorithm sets only the emulation mode, and its A.5.5 (p. A-11) leaves "Character Sets" with "undefined results and states both when entering and exiting VT52 Mode emulation"; at Level 1 "Only ASCII, national replacement characters, and DEC special graphics are available" (`[[source: vt220-rm-ch4]]` Table 4-3), and "In Level 1 operation character sets can only be designated into G0 and G1, with G2 and G3 containing the ASCII character set by default" (070-5 SCS note 1, p. 5-118). So a slot holding a set SCS could not designate there at Level 1 takes ASCII, by SCS's own level table: G2 and G3 always, G0 or G1 when it holds a set introduced above Level 1, and VT52's `ESC F` Line Drawing stays when it went into G0 or G1 (`leaving_vt52_leaves_only_level_one_sets`, `leaving_vt52_keeps_level_one_sets_in_g0_and_g1`). xterm restores the sets it saved on entering VT52 (`CASE_VT52_FINISH`'s `restoreCharsets()`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), so its G2 and G3 can keep the supplemental set at its level 1; not followed, because Level 1 holds only ASCII there. A `DECRC` after `ESC <` restores what a `DECSC` before VT52 saved, sets included, as 070-5 DECRC has it (`a_decrc_after_leaving_vt52_restores_the_saved_sets`). **Nothing changes at power-up**, Level 4, where every function but DECID executes (`level_four_executes_every_control_sequence_function`). Since 2026-10-19 **a program can select Level 1 to 4 and, above Level 1, seven- or eight-bit controls**, to `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4 DECSCL and `[[source: vt510-decscl]]`: `Pl` 61 to 64; any other is ignored and counted, "Attempts to select conformance levels that are unsupported by the device will be ignored". It soft-resets, note 3's "without affecting the data contained in the display", as the VT220 manual and xterm do, where the VT320 to VT520 manuals say a hard reset, and so unlocks the keyboard (`decscl_performs_a_soft_reset_and_keeps_the_screen`). `Pc` 0, 2 or omitted sends eight-bit C1 and 1 seven-bit, at Levels 2 to 4; Level 1 sends seven-bit whatever `Pc` says, "Level 1 operation is 7-bits only" (070-3 §3.6.4.1), and there `S8C1T` is counted and changes nothing, its algorithm's "if (conformance_level != LEVEL_1)" (`level_one_is_seven_bit_only`, `decscl_selects_the_level_and_the_c1_transmission`). **Level 4 at power-up and after `RIS`**, 070's `host_state_init`, "conformance level = LEVEL 4", and the VT510's factory default (`the_terminal_powers_up_at_level_four_seven_bit`, `a_full_reset_returns_to_level_four_seven_bit`). **Level 1 and seven-bit after `ESC <`**: 070-0A EXIT VT52 EMULATION MODE (p. A-15), "turns off the VT52 emulator and turns on the ANSI parser of the terminal, causing the terminal to return to Level 1 operation", which its revision A.9.2 (p. A-45) made "always", and 070-0C Category 2 (p. C-28), "even if the terminal had been set to LEVEL 2 mode prior to entering VT52 mode"; Level 1 being seven-bit, an `S8C1T` goes with it (`leaving_vt52_returns_to_level_one_seven_bit`). xterm's `CASE_VT52_FINISH` returns to its level 1 as well, "per vttest", and keeps an `S8C1T`, a departure not followed (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). **The primary DA answer does not follow it**: DA1 answers as a VT102, `?6c`, and xterm's DA follows its identity, not its level. DECRQSS `" p` reports it (the DECRQSS row), so **at power-up DECRQSS `" p` answers `64;1"p` while DA1 answers `?6c`**. **What the Level 4 power-up changes**, beside `RIS` returning to seven-bit C1 (070's Device Initialization): DECRQSS `" p` answers `64;1"p` where it was refused, and `ESC Z` gets no answer (the `ESC Z` row); since 2026-10-22, after DECSCL 61, 62 or 63 or an `ESC <`, the controls, keys and SGR parameters the level does not define do nothing, and a program that leaves VT52 with `ESC <` is at Level 1 until it sends DECSCL |
| `CSI SP q` | DECSCUSR cursor style | done — the shape and the blink an application asks for, and **drawn with its blink** since 2026-09-25: since 2026-10-15 at 333 ms a phase, or `[cursor] blink_interval_ms`, or on macOS the system caret's `NSTextInsertionPointBlinkPeriodOn` and `...Off` where the user has set them, with the hidden phase drawn by `[cursor] blink_animation` (the plasma glow by default, a fade, the fire, the ufo, or none; none under Reduce Motion), and held steady by macOS 15's non-blinking switch unless `[cursor] blink = true`, which changes the drawing and not the answer; Linux takes the same 333 ms default; only while the window has focus and restarted shown by output or a key -- foot's rules (`[[source: foot-term-reset]]`'s file) -- on the display link's clock or the Linux `poll` timeout (`baud-app/src/caret.rs`, `baud-render/tests/cursor_blink.rs`, the pixel oracle's off-phase scene). The power-on style is the embedder's (`Term::set_initial_cursor_style`, `[cursor] shape` and `blink`, a blinking block by default), and `Ps = 7`, `DECSTR` and `RIS` restore it, as xterm restores its resources. **The sources conflict on `Ps = 0`, and baud follows foot**: `[[source: ctlseqs]]` and xterm's `charproc.c` make it a blinking block, foot's `csi.c` resets it "to configured default" (`[[source: foot-csi]]`'s file). The two agree while the configured style is the default; `Ps = 1` stays the blinking block (`zero_and_seven_restore_the_configured_style_and_one_is_the_blinking_block`, `both_resets_restore_the_configured_style`). xterm's `cursorBlinkXOR`, which inverts an application's blink against the resource, is not followed. **The style is saved with the cursor at `?1049 h` and restored at `?1049 l`**, as kitty and wezterm do, and not by `DECSC`, which `[[source: vt510-decsc]]` does not list it for: helix ends its session on the alternate screen with `0 q`, the system entry's `Se` (`2 q`) and `cnorm`, and the shell gets its own cursor back. `DECRQSS` answers the style DECSCUSR set, not what `?12` draws |
| `CSI Ps SP t` | DECSWBV set warning bell volume | **partial** — since 2026-10-20, **a program can make the bell softer or silent**: `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-154, "1 Off; 2, 3, 4 Low; 5, 5, 7, 8, 0 or none High (default)", the warning bell "for errors and receipt of Ctrl G"; Table 4.7.3 and `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-148 agree. **0 and none are high**, as the three DEC texts give them, where `[[source: ctlseqs]]` and xterm's `CASE_DECSWBV` read them as off (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), a departure not followed; a value past 8 changes nothing and is counted. `Term::warning_bell_volume()`; DECRQSS answers `SP t` (its row). High at power-on, restored by `RIS`, which "Sets all features listed on set-up screens to their saved settings" (p. 5-184 of the VT510's), where xterm keeps it; a `BEL` sounds at the volume set when it arrived, though DECSWBV or `RIS` change it later in the same read; kept by `DECSTR`, whose Table 5-9 names it not. **On macOS off is silence and low sounds as high**: the app's `NSBeep` takes no volume, NSSound sends the system beep to it (`[[source: apple-nssound]]`), and the system sound services play "with no programmatic volume control available" (`[[source: apple-audioservicesplaysystemsound]]`), so no documented call plays the user's alert sound below its volume -- the gap this row is partial for. The volume reaches the sound alone: the flash, `?1042` and `?1043` act whatever it says, as xterm's volume reaches only its audible bell (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`), and the config's `bell = "none"` stays silent. **On Linux likewise**: `xdg-system-bell-v1`'s `ring` carries no volume (`[[source: xdg-system-bell-v1]]`), so off is silence and low sounds as high. `decswbv_reads_every_value_as_the_dec_manuals_give_it`, `a_volume_past_eight_is_refused_and_counted`, `ris_restores_the_volumes_and_44_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `each_bell_is_counted_at_the_volume_it_rang_at`, `a_bell_rings_at_the_volume_it_was_sent_at`, `the_volume_silences_the_sound_and_nothing_else` |
| `CSI Ps SP u` | DECSMBV set margin bell volume | **partial** — since 2026-10-20, **a typewriter's margin bell**: `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-138, "1, 0 or none Off (default); 2, 3, 4 Low; 5, 6, 7, 8 High", the margin bell "which sounds as the current position nears the right margin". **0 and none are off**, as DEC gives them, where ctlseqs and xterm's `CASE_DECSMBV` read 0 as high, not followed; past 8, counted. The VT520's Section 2.12.7 calls high the default of all three volumes, against the page, the summary table and the Set-Up's "Margin bell volume Off"; off is built. **It rings when both switches are on**, this volume and `?44` (its row), and then **as DEC STD 070 rings it** (070-6 § 6.6.5.2, pp. 6-45 and 6-46, `[[source: dec-std-070]]`): a normal keystroke, one whose first character is not `ESC`, `CSI` or `DCS`, arms it, and a character printed from a column at or left of RIGHT MARGIN - 8 to one right of it on the same line rings it, so on 80 columns the character written in column 72 -- eight columns, as `[[source: vt100-ug-ch1]]` and `[[source: vt102-ug-ch3]]` give them, where xterm's `nMarginBell` is 10. The right margin is the line's: a double-width line's half, or DECSLRM's. Rung once, it is disarmed until the next keystroke; the cursor moving to a new line disarms it -- an `LF`, a wrap, a scroll of the screen or of a region that holds its line, `IL`, `DL`, a change of screen -- and horizontal moves neither ring it nor arm it. A region scrolled above or below the cursor's line moves no text on it and keeps it armed, and a write that prints no character, DECDWL's or DECDHL's cleared half or a DECFRA or DECCRA rectangle, neither rings it nor disarms it. **A cursor that leaves the line and comes back before anything is printed stays armed**, where STD 070 disarms it: an open departure, since telling it needs a test on every row change. xterm rings it on the key instead, from the cursor where the key finds it (`AdjustAfterInput`, `[[source: xterm-input-c]]`). `Term::margin_bell_volume()`, and the rings counted in `Term::margin_bells`, which the macOS app sounds as a `BEL`, at this volume -- low as high, as for DECSWBV, the gap this row is partial for; the Linux app sounds it the same way. Off at power-on and after `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`. `decsmbv_reads_every_value_as_the_dec_manuals_give_it`, `the_character_printed_in_column_72_rings_and_no_other`, `the_margin_bell_needs_44_and_a_volume`, `a_cursor_key_does_not_arm_it`, `every_way_to_a_new_line_disarms_it`, `horizontal_moves_neither_ring_nor_disarm`, `every_writer_rings_it`, `the_right_margin_is_the_lines`, `a_write_that_prints_nothing_neither_rings_nor_disarms_it`, `a_scroll_away_from_the_cursors_line_keeps_it_armed`, `each_bell_is_counted_at_the_volume_it_rang_at`, `the_ringer_hears_each_bell_at_its_own_volume` |
| `CSI " q` | DECSCA character protection | done |
| `CSI ? J` | DECSED selective erase display | done — 0, 1 and 2; `3` is in Tier 3. At a pending wrap each ends it, and 0 starts on the last column and ends the row's soft wrap, as ED does (the `CSI J` row), since 2026-11-01. **A character DECSCA left erasable becomes a space in the colours and attributes it had, and an empty position stays empty**: "changed to the Space character (2/0). The character rendition and attributes associated with each position do not change ... empty character positions, are not affected" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-159 and 5-162; its algorithms assign the code alone). xterm writes a space in the current colours over every unprotected position, empty ones too; until 2026-10-19 baud wrote an empty cell in the current rendition (`decsed_turns_characters_into_spaces_in_their_own_rendition`, `an_empty_position_is_not_affected`, `decsed_reaches_past_the_rows_mark`). The space keeps the ECMA-48 guard (decided 2026-10-16) and an `OSC 8` link, and drops the line-drawing flag and a cluster's marks (`the_space_keeps_the_guard_and_the_link_and_drops_the_rest`). A wide character the cursor's column cuts goes whole, both halves spaces, as ED snaps (`a_wide_character_the_edge_cuts_becomes_two_spaces`); a protected one stays whole. The line renditions stay, note 3 (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-162), where its algorithm resets the cursor's line as ED's does; the note is followed (`el_and_the_selective_erases_keep_the_rendition`). **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list, is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI ? K` | DECSEL selective erase line | done — as DECSED, on the cursor's line (`decsel_turns_a_character_into_a_space_in_its_own_rendition`), a pending wrap included, as EL ends one (the `CSI K` row). **Every parameter, in order**, since 2026-10-19: `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms loop `for (n=0; n<parc; n++) switch(parv[n])` (EL p. 5-155, ED p. 5-157, DECSEL p. 5-160, DECSED p. 5-163), so `CSI 1 ; 0 K` erases the whole line; a value with no case, and a `3` in a list, is skipped and counted. `[[source: ecma48]]` gives ED and EL the notation `(Ps)` and says nothing of a second parameter (`ed_and_el_apply_every_parameter_in_order`, `a_value_with_no_case_is_skipped_and_counted`, `a_three_in_a_list_is_skipped`, `the_selective_erases_apply_every_parameter_in_order`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI $ p` | DECRQM request mode | done |
| `DCS $ q Pt ST` | DECRQSS request status string | done — an application can read back each setting baud acts on, as the control that sets it, `DCS 1 $ r <control> ST`, but SCP's character path, which it answers `DCS 0 $ r ST` because no DEC or xterm text lists it, and SLH's and SLL's positions and SEE's extent, answered as SCP is (`decrqss_answers_slh_and_sll_as_invalid`, `decrqss_answers_see_as_invalid`), and gets `DCS 0 $ r ST` for any other. Since 2026-10-22 **a colour is answered as T.416 writes it**, so neovim run over ssh, where `COLORTERM` does not reach it, finds 24-bit colour from the answer: one parameter sub-string, `:` between its elements, `38:2::r:g:b` with the colour space left empty as "a default value" and `38:5:n` for an index, `48` and `58` alike (`[[source: itu-t416]]` 13.1.8, the text ECMA-48 8.3.117 points 38 and 48 at), as xterm, foot and ghostty answer (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3280-3299, `[[source: foot-dcs-c]]` 318-410, `[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-terminal-zig]]`); neovim's probe, `SGR 48;2;1;2;3` then `$ q m`, turns truecolor on only for `48:2:` and the channels (`[[source: neovim-core-defaults-lua]]` 1037-1076; `neovims_truecolor_probe_is_answered_in_t416s_form`, `every_colour_reports_in_t416s_form`), and `38:5:n` for an index below 16 too, where xterm, ghostty and kitty write `3n` and `9n`; foot writes them only for a colour set as one of the sixteen, and answers `38:5:n` for one set by `38;5;n` (`[[source: foot-dcs-c]]` 322-336). It answered `38;2;r;g;b` and `38;5;n` before, the legacy spelling, whose `2` ECMA-48 5.4.2 reads as a parameter of its own and which neovim reads as six attributes. XTREPORTSGR and the rendition DECRSTS replays take the same spelling (`xtreportsgr_answers_a_direct_colour_in_t416s_form`, `decrsts_restores_a_direct_colour_in_every_slot`). Of `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s eighteen strings, fourteen answer: `m` SGR, `SP q` DECSCUSR, `" q` DECSCA, `r` DECSTBM, `s` DECSLRM, `$ \|` DECSCPP, since 2026-10-22 `t` DECSLPP, `1 $ r Ps t` with `Ps` the screen's rows and 24 on a shorter screen, by `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s "if (parv[0] <= 24) lines_per_page = 24" (p. 5-76) and because `CSI Ps t` below 24 is an XTWINOPS (`decrqss_answers_decslpp_as_the_lines_per_page`), `* x` DECSACE, since 2026-10-19 `" p` DECSCL, `6n ; 0 " p` or `6n ; 1 " p` above Level 1 -- `64;1"p` at power-up, while DA1 answers `?6c`, refused until then because answering "would claim a conformance level (note AP)"; it follows from the Level 4 power-up (the DECSCL row) -- and invalid at Level 1, where DECRQSS is no function, xterm's shape and refusal (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); since 2026-10-22 **at Level 1 no string is answered**, `" p` among them, DECRQSS being "Levels: 2x (TSI), 3-4" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-196), as the VT420 ignores it there (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 4-1), the reading that keeps to DEC STD 070; xterm answers the other strings, a departure not followed (`level_one_has_no_terminal_state_interrogation`, `an_unknown_setting_gets_the_invalid_reply`; the DECSCL row), and `> Pp m` and `> Pp f` for every key-modifier resource, answered `> Pp ; Pv m` and `> Pp ; Pv f`, a disabled one `-1` and modifyOtherKeys' mask `: mask`, as xterm's `do_dcs` writes them (`decrqss_answers_the_key_modifier_strings`, `every_resource_answers_as_xterm_reports_it`). xterm's `do_dcs` spells the `f` answer `>%d;%dm`, an XTMODKEYS (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`), which is not the string that sets it, and is not followed. Since 2026-10-20 `> t` and `> Ps t` answer XTSMTITLE's modes in force as the control that sets them, `> 0 ; 2 t`, and `> t` with none set, which sent back is XTSMTITLE's default (`decrqss_answers_the_title_modes_as_the_control_that_sets_them`); xterm's `do_dcs` answers a 0 or 1 a mode, which sent back sets the wrong modes, and is not followed. Since 2026-10-20 `$ ~` DECSSDT and `$ }` DECSASD answer the status line's type and the display selected, `1 $ r Ps $ ~` and `1 $ r Ps $ }`, "The Status Display Type (DECSSDT) and Active Status Display (DECSASD) may be queried through DECRQSS" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 p. 14-9; `decrqss_answers_the_type_and_the_display`). Since 2026-10-20 two the VT520's DECRPSS list names and ctlseqs' does not (`[[source: vt520-rm]]` pp. 5-103 and 5-104) answer too: `SP t` DECSWBV and `SP u` DECSMBV, `1 $ r Ps SP t` and `1 $ r Ps SP u` with `Ps` 1, 2 or 5 for off, low and high, the first of each band, on which DEC and xterm agree, never the 0 they read apart (`decrqss_answers_each_volume_as_the_control_that_sets_it`). The rest answer invalid because baud does not act on them: `) {`, `, \|` and `, }` (the VT525's colours), `* \|` DECSNLS, a `> Ps t` naming no title mode, and a key-modifier string naming no resource (`decrqss_answers_invalid_for_what_baud_does_not_act_on`); each such setting's control is counted as unsupported, across ctlseqs', STD 070's and the VT520's DECRPSS lists, so one that lands with its answer left invalid fails a test (`every_setting_decrqss_refuses_is_one_baud_counts`). `1` is valid and `0` invalid, as `[[source: ctlseqs]]` records the DEC manuals' errata (`baud-core/src/dcs.rs`) |
| `DCS + q Pt ST` | XTGETTCAP request termcap/terminfo string | done — since 2026-10-21 **a program can ask which bytes a key will send**, by its terminfo or termcap name, and gets the bytes the key sends in the modes in force: `DCS 1 + r name=value ST`, both in hex, every pair in one response, or `DCS 0 + r ST` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 545-582).
