The names are xterm's table, all 319 (`[[source: xterm-xtermcap-c]]` 70-244), compiled at build time into a hashed index that finds any name in one or two compares. `Co` and `colors` answer 256, `RGB` 8, and `TN` and `name` the `TERM` the app gives the child, `xterm-256color`, refused on a core no embedder told. A key answers the key as it would be encoded now -- DECCKM, DECKPAM, `modifyOtherKeys`, the kitty flags, DECBKM, `?1037` and `S8C1T` included -- the press xterm's own query simulates: `kf13` is F13, `CSI 25 ~`, as terminfo(5) and xterm's table name it, and `kcbt` at `modifyOtherKeys` 2 is `CSI 27 ; 2 ; 9 ~`, ctlseqs' own example for that level. A key that sends nothing in that state -- Help, Find, Select, Clear, Undo and F36-F63, which baud has no code for, F21-F35 before a kitty flag, every key under KAM -- answers the empty string and the list goes on, xterm's answer for Undo, Clear and KAM, and valid by the text's one definition of an invalid name, "one not found in xterm's tables". **Any other name is refused**, ending the list: the entry's output strings, numbers and booleans, `Ms`, `Tc` and the other extensions, which the text calls "intentionally not part of this interface" and which are "not found in xterm's tables", the conforming answer. A list cut at the 512-byte string buffer ends before the name the cut fell in; an answer that does not fit behind the replies pending is dropped whole and counted; after a parameter, `DCS 1 + q` is another DCS, as xterm's `do_dcs` reads it (`xtgettcap_answers_a_key_as_it_would_be_sent_now`, `xtgettcap_a_key_that_sends_nothing_answers_empty`, `xtgettcap_refuses_a_name_not_in_xterms_table`, `xtgettcap_a_cut_list_ends_before_the_name_the_cut_fell_in`, `every_name_in_xterms_table_is_found_and_no_other`). Where baud differs from xterm's practice: `ka1`, `kc1`, `ka3` and `kc3` answer baud's own keypad Home, End, PageUp and PageDown, where xterm remaps them onto the editing keys before it encodes them; `kcbt` at `modifyOtherKeys` 2 answers the live Shift-Tab, where xterm answers `CSI Z`; and a refused name gets no trailing `;` or echo, which xterm writes (R1). While a ReGIS one-shot input would take the key, the answer is still the key's encoding. neovim run over ssh asks `Tc;RGB;setrgbf;setrgbb` in one request when `COLORTERM` does not reach it (`[[source: neovim-core-defaults-lua]]` 1013-1027), and the list ends at `Tc`, refused, so it learns its 24-bit colour from DECRQSS instead (the `DCS $ q` row; `neovims_truecolor_query_is_refused_at_tc`). Before 2026-10-21 it was consumed and counted, unanswered |
| `DCS + p Pt ST` | XTSETTCAP set termcap/terminfo data | **out** — refused and counted in `Term::tcap_sets_refused`, and nothing changes. **Every retrieval takes the text's own failure path.** It has xterm "retrieve data from the terminal database" by the name it is given, and that database is the host's: xterm's `set_termcap` hands the name to `setupterm`, or `tgetent` in a termcap build. baud consults no host database, so no retrieval succeeds, and only "If successful" does the name override the one XTGETTCAP uses (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 535-542; `[[source: xterm-xtermcap-c]]` 48-51 and 624-657; note AM, sections 3 and 8.4). xterm's own code, as practice, changes no answer outside its tcap keyboard, `?1050`, whose table baud compiles from the pinned xterm-256color entry rather than loads, and never `TN`'s, which answers the `termName` resource under every keyboard (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 5225-5226). It is refused and counted (`xtsettcap_is_refused_and_counted`) |
| `DCS + Q Pt ST` | XTGETXRES request resource values | done — since 2026-10-22 **a program can ask what baud starts from by xterm's resource names**: `DCS 1 + R name=value ST`, both in hex, in xterm's `do_dcs` shape byte for byte (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 510-533; `[[source: xterm-misc]]`'s file, misc.c 5251-5306): the first name decides the `1` or `0`, each name is echoed as sent and a known one gets `=` and its value, a boolean `true` or `false` and an integer in decimal, upper-case hex as `x_encode_hex` writes it; the list ends after the first unknown name, echoed with the `;` after it if one was sent; an empty name, an odd count of hex digits or a byte that is not hex where a name starts ends it before that name, and with no first name there is no reply, as xterm sends none, and the list is counted in `unsupported_param`, as XTQMODKEYS counts a resource it does not have (`x_decode_hex`, `[[source: xterm-xstrings-c]]`; `xtgetxres_answers_a_resource_and_echoes_an_unknown_one`, `a_list_ends_after_its_first_unknown_name`, `a_list_with_no_first_name_gets_no_reply`). **Sixty-four names answer**, each as the value baud starts from, ctlseqs' "XTerm evaluates resources at startup time", so a program that sets a mode does not move its resource (`a_resource_does_not_follow_the_mode`): the seven `allow*` resources, `true` while `OSC 60` lists the category, from the same facts (`the_allow_resources_agree_with_osc_60`); the initial value of each of the thirty-seven modes baud has that starts from an xterm resource -- `appcursorDefault`, `appkeypadDefault`, `jumpScroll` (DECSCLM reset), `autoWrap`, `reverseWrap`, `c132`, `reverseVideo`, `marginBell`, `curses`, `scrollBar`, `cursorBlinkXOR` (`?14`, permanently reset), `alternateScroll`, `bellIsUrgent`, `popOnBell`, `metaSendsEscape`, `altSendsEscape`, `eightBitInput` and `eightBitMeta` (both `?1034`), `deleteIsDEL`, `backarrowKey`, `numLock`, `privateColorRegisters`, `sixelScrolling` (DECSDM reset), `sixelScrollsRight`, `titeInhibit` (`?1046` reset), `scrollTtyOutput`, `scrollKey`, `fastScroll` and `keepClipboard` (permanently set), `utf8` and `privateWidth` (read-only), `keepSelection`, `selectToClipboard`, `printerFormFeed`, `printerExtent`, `logging` and `oldXtermFKeys` (`?1060`, false: the default keyboard) -- each held to a fresh terminal's DECRQM answer by a list of xterm's own resource and mode pairs, read off `dpmodes`, `xterm_resources` and ctlseqs' DECSET list rather than off baud's table, which also requires the names whose mode baud does not recognise, `shiftFonts` (xterm's `?35`, which baud reads as DEC's DECHEBM) and `emojiWidth` (`?1022`), to be unknown (`every_mode_resource_is_the_fresh_terminals_mode`); `eightBitControl`, `titleModes` and the thirteen `modify*Keys` and `format*Keys` baud has, held to a fresh terminal's DECRQSS, XTQMODKEYS and XTQFMTKEYS answers (`the_setting_resources_are_the_fresh_terminals_settings`); and the five the embedder gives at start, `cursorBlink`, `cursorUnderLine` and `cursorBar` from the initial cursor style, `pointerMode` from the initial pointer mode and `cjkWidth` from the ambiguous width (`the_embedders_resources_answer_what_it_set`). **Every other name is unknown**, as xterm answers a name it has no resource for: `termName` among them, an application resource of xterm's and not the VT100 widget's, and `decTerminalID`, which would name a terminal identity (DA1 `?6c`). The answer follows `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` and is sent whole or dropped and counted in `reply_overflows`; a list cut at the string limit ends before the name the cut fell in, as XTGETTCAP's does |
| `CSI ? 6 n` to `CSI ? 85 n` | DEC private device status reports | done — since 2026-10-19 an application that asks a VT420 question gets the VT420 answer, each true of baud: **`?6`** DECXCPR, `CSI ? Pl ; Pc ; 1 R`, the cursor and its page, counted from the margins under DECOM as CPR is (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-55, PDF 310; `decxcpr_reports_the_cursor_with_its_page`, `decxcpr_counts_from_the_margins_under_decom_as_cpr_does`); **`?15`** `?10n`, ready, while the embedder has a printer; `?11n`, not ready, while it has none but had one since power-up or `RIS`, its prints queued; else `?13n`, "not connected" (070-7 pp. 7-32 and 7-33, PDF 710-711): since 2026-10-20 the macOS app has one while `[screen] printing` is on and macOS lists a printer, and the Linux app never, so the answer and what a print does agree, as 070-7 § 7.3.2 ties them; **since 2026-10-21 the answer is the printer's when it is asked**: on macOS the read stops at `?15` (`PrinterHold::On`), the printer list is read again off the main thread and the answer goes before the rest of the read is parsed, so a printer added or removed while baud keeps focus is seen at once, and a list that does not come back within 100 ms is answered from the last one (`a_held_dsr_15_is_answered_from_the_printer_the_embedder_finds`, `a_held_printer_question_waits_for_the_drivers_answer`) (`dsr_15_answers_ready_with_a_printer_and_not_connected_without`, `losing_the_printer_leaves_a_run_collecting`, `ris_forgets_a_lost_printer_and_keeps_a_present_one`; the MC row); **`?25`** `?23n`, "No User Defined Keys are present. The device does not support this extension", where xterm, which has them, answers `?20n` (070-11 p. 11-7, PDF 974: "A report will be given even if the UDK extension is not supported"), true while DECUDK stays out (its row below), and whether it is built, so that this answers `?20n` or `?21n` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1436-1438), is open; **`?26`** `?27 ; 0 ; 0 n`, the keyboard language "cannot be determined" and the keyboard ready (`[[source: vt510-dsr-kbd]]`), with no keyboard type since every `Ptyp` names a DEC keyboard; **`?55`** `?50n`, locator ready, and **`?56`** `?57 ; 1 n`, a mouse (070-13, PDF 1030; `[[source: vt3xx-tp]]` pp. 203-204), once the embedder says it has a pointer (`Term::set_locator_device`; both apps do: macOS always, Linux while the seat names one), and `?53n`, "No locator", and `?57 ; 0 n`, "Unknown Locator", until then, a DECRQLP or DECEFR answering DECLRP's "unavailable" (`a_core_with_no_pointer_has_no_locator`); **`?62`** DECMSR `CSI 0 * {`, no macro space (p. 5-185, PDF 440), in decimal where xterm writes `0000`; **`?63 ; Pid`** DECCKSR `DCS Pid ! ~ 0000 ST`, the checksum of no macro definitions (p. 5-181, PDF 436); **`?75`** `?73n` once after power-up or `RIS` and `?70n` after, 070-4's algorithm (PDF 240), where xterm always sends `?70n`; `?71n` reports a communication error, and a pty has no parity or framing to fail (`data_integrity_reports_the_power_up_once_and_then_ok`); **`?85`** `?83n`, one session (070-4, PDF 239). Each in the text's shape (`each_private_status_answers_what_baud_has`, `the_reports_follow_s8c1t`, `the_reports_are_the_same_at_every_split`). **Answered while DA says VT102**, as DECRQSS, DECRQM and DECRQCRA already are, since 2026-10-19, so the protocol is built whole while DA1 stays `?6c`; xterm as a VT102 answers `?6` without the page and nothing else. **A source conflict**: `[[source: vt420-rm]]` and `[[source: vt510-dsr-xcpr]]` print DECXCPR without the `?`, STD 070 and `[[source: ctlseqs]]` with it; baud follows STD 070. A `Ps` no text gives stays silent and counted (`an_unknown_private_status_is_silent_and_counted`) |
| `CSI Ps i` / `CSI ? Ps i` | MC media copy: print page, line, screen and all pages; autoprint; printer controller mode | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program's print opens the macOS print panel**, which shows what will print and names the window, and nothing reaches paper until the user presses Print (note AW, section 1). Built to `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-7, the Printer Port Extension, and `[[source: vt420-rm]]` chapter 11: `CSI i` or `CSI 0 i` prints the page, the scrolling region while DECPEX is reset, and `CSI ? 11 i` all pages, baud's page memory being the one page (xterm prints its saved lines too); `CSI ? 10 i` the composed display, whatever DECPEX says; `CSI ? 1 i` the cursor's line, within DECSLRM's margins while DECPEX is reset (070-7, PRINT LINE), the cursor not moving; each but the line ended by `FF` under DECPFF. **Autoprint**, `CSI ? 5 i` to `CSI ? 4 i`, prints each line the cursor leaves by `LF`, `VT`, `FF` or an autowrap, ended by `CR` and that character (070-7 § 7.3.7), `IND` and `NEL` printing nothing. **Printer controller mode**, `CSI 5 i` to `CSI 4 i`, sends the host's bytes to the printer and not the screen, but NUL, XON and XOFF and the sequences that turn it on and off (§ 7.3.6), where xterm matches only the literal four, `[[source: xterm-print-c]]`; any other sequence, `RIS`, `DECSTR` and `CSI 0 i` among them, is the printer's, as § 7.3.6 and DECSTR's note d say; `CSI 4 i` ends autoprint too (its reset algorithm); a sequence cut between reads is held for the next, so where the reads fall changes nothing (`a_controller_run_is_the_same_at_every_split`); under ISO 8859-1 the 8-bit `CSI 4 i` ends it. While it runs **the title says "Printing"**, as the screen is not updated. **What a job holds**: 070-7 § 7.4's stream -- `ESC [ 0 m`, `ESC # 5`/`6`/`3`/`4` at each line, `ESC [ 0 ; Ps m` at each change with bold 1, underline 4, blink 5 and reverse 7 and nothing else, invisible text as spaces, and a guarded position as a space under GATM's GUARD (the `CSI 1 h` row), trailing spaces of the normal rendition left off, `CR LF` a line -- in UTF-8 with no character-set designations, since a cell holds Unicode. **What one job is**: a print; an autoprint run, a print in the middle of it ending its job there; a controller run. The core keeps the finished jobs (`baud_core::PRINT_QUEUE`, more joined to the last after a form feed) in an area the embedder sizes, 2 MiB in the app, bytes past it dropped, counted and the job marked cut, a controller run going on to its `CSI 4 i`, as ending it at the bound would draw printer data on the screen, a departure; **a printer lost after it was there is "not ready"**, § 7.3.2's third state: `?15` answers `?11n`, a run keeps collecting and a new print is queued as a job, "print operations shall be queued", the app holding the jobs until macOS lists a printer again (`losing_the_printer_leaves_a_run_collecting`, `a_lost_printer_holds_the_jobs_until_one_is_back`), and `RIS` forgets a printer that is gone (`ris_forgets_a_lost_printer_and_keeps_a_present_one`). **The app** takes the jobs after each read, at most one panel at a time, those finishing meanwhile waiting as one, each on a new page, up to 2 MiB, lays each out off the main thread in the system's monospaced font sized to the widest line, a double-height pair printed once at double size, and shows the panel as a sheet once the read's callback has returned (`the_print_panel_is_always_shown_as_a_sheet`). **With no printer since power-up or `RIS` every print function is ignored and counted** in `Term::print_refusals`, never entering controller mode (§ 7.3.2): `[screen] printing = "off"`, which is read at launch, no printer listed since launch, the Linux app, and a core no embedder told. VT52's `ESC ]`, `ESC V`, `ESC ^`, `ESC _`, `ESC W` and `ESC X` are the same functions ([protocols/vt52.md](protocols/vt52.md)). `RIS` ends an autoprint run, its job kept. **Pictures print through ReGIS's hard copy** (the ReGIS row): "Graphics to Printer", `CSI ? i` and `CSI ? 0 i` (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), is accepted and changes nothing, a hard copy always going to the printer, since 2026-10-21 (`graphics_to_printer_is_accepted_and_to_host_refused`); **a print screen stays text** where a picture is on the screen, 070-7 § 7.6.2's "may be transmitted as sixels" being a guideline. Refused and counted apart: the out-of-scope row's kinds. Counted as unsupported before (`with_no_printer_every_print_function_is_ignored_and_counted`, `printer_controller_mode_hides_output_and_collects_it`, `autoprint_prints_each_line_the_cursor_leaves`, `the_stream_carries_renditions_line_sizes_and_hidden_text_as_spaces`, `vt52_print_commands_print`, `the_area_bound_cuts_a_job_and_counts_the_bytes`) |
| `CSI & u` | DECRQUPSS request user-preference supplemental set | done — since 2026-10-19, answered `DCS 0 ! u % 5 ST`, DEC Supplemental (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-125, PDF 380): the set `ESC ( <` draws, and the only one baud's repertory can make the UPSS while DECAUPSS changes nothing (`decrqupss_answers_dec_supplemental`). "Takes no parameters", so `CSI 1 & u` is no request and is counted (`a_parameter_on_decrqupss_or_decrqde_is_no_request`) |
| `DCS Ps ! u Dscs ST` | DECAUPSS assign user-preference supplemental set | **partial** — consumed as a control string and not acted on. The one assignment baud's repertory can honour, `0 ; % 5`, leaves the UPSS what it already is; the other STD 070 allows, `1 ; A`, ISO Latin-1 supplemental (p. 5-123, PDF 378), has the 96-character designation and GR invocation it needs since 2026-10-19, and waits on note AE's Q14 reading of `ESC ( <`. **The gap** is that assignment and the parser edge that recognises the string; recorded rather than built here since 2026-10-19 |
| `CSI " v` | DECRQDE request displayed extent | done — since 2026-10-19, answered DECRPDE `CSI Ph ; Pw ; 1 ; 1 ; 1 " w`: every line and column of the one page, shown from its first cell (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-88, PDF 343; `decrqde_answers_the_whole_page`). Takes no parameters, as DECRQUPSS |
| `CSI s` (DECSLRM) | left/right margins | done — with DECLRMM (`?69`). **An omitted or 0 `Pr` is the page's right border**: "default Pr: Last Column", and the algorithm reads a 0 as omitted (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` DECSLRM, pp. 5-27 and 5-28); "Default: Pr = 80 or 132 (depending on the page width)" (`[[source: vt510-decslrm]]`); as xterm (charproc.c 4891-4894, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`) and ghostty (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-terminal]]` 2610) read it, since 2026-10-13 (`a_bare_decslrm_sets_the_margins_to_the_page_borders`, `a_zero_right_margin_is_the_page_border`, `an_omitted_right_margin_is_the_page_border`, with `an_omitted_left_margin_is_column_one` the control); until then it decoded as column 1, so `CSI s` and `CSI 5 s` were parsed and dropped. An inverted or one-column pair is rejected. **A `Pr` past the page leaves the margins alone**: STD 070's note 3 (p. 5-27), "this control will be ignored (not executed)", since 2026-10-13 (`a_right_margin_past_the_page_leaves_the_margins_alone`); until then it was clamped to the last column, as xterm and ghostty still do (pinned in `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`). Margins act for writing, for `ICH`/`DCH`/`DECIC`/`DECDC`, and since 2026-10-12 for every vertical scroll: `SU`, `SD`, `IND`, `RI`, `LF`, a wrap at the bottom margin, `IL` and `DL` move only the rectangle (`[[source: vt510-decslrm]]`, "sets the left and right margins to define the scrolling region"; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` § 5.4.3 and its `scroll_up`/`scroll_down`, § 5.14.2-3), on either screen, the whole screen and a top region included, and keep nothing in the history (`tests/vt420/rect_scroll.rs`). **A row that wraps at margins narrower than the page keeps them** (`baud_domain::WrapMargins`, beside its mark, carried by a scroll, `IL` and `DL` and into the history, and under those margins by the rectangle's scroll, a wrap at the bottom margin, `IL` and `DL`, with the rectangle's cells; dropped when the row wraps again at the page's edge, when it wraps no longer, a newline or a fill to its end ending it, and when it is erased whole; `a_wrap_at_side_margins_keeps_the_margins`, `the_margins_move_with_their_row`, `a_wrap_at_the_bottom_margin_carries_the_margins_up`, `il_and_dl_inside_the_rectangle_move_the_margins`, `a_later_wrap_at_the_page_edge_drops_the_margins`, `a_row_that_no_longer_wraps_has_no_margins`, `a_later_wrap_writes_its_margins_and_an_erase_drops_them`; a change of width that re-cuts the row keeps the mark and not the margins, and the row is read whole, where its cells outside the margins it wrapped at are not its paragraph's: open, `a_change_of_width_that_re_cuts_the_rows_reads_them_whole`), so with BDSM set its paragraph is laid out inside them and the cells outside them each as a paragraph of their own, since 2026-10-28 (`docs/bidi.md`; `a_paragraph_inside_side_margins_is_laid_out_inside_them`, `a_paragraph_the_rectangle_scrolls_keeps_its_margins`), where UAX #9 read the whole rows and moved another pane's letters across the screen. A wide character a side margin cuts is erased whole first, and until 2026-10-21 an image moved when its anchor was inside the rectangle: no text settles either, both open; the images' rule is below. Held against ghostty by `bench-others/ghostty-oracle`. **Line starts and moves honour the fence with DECOM reset**, since 2026-10-15: `CR`, `NEL`, `CNL`, `CPL` and `LF` under `LNM` by `CR`'s rule, `CUF` and `CUB` stop at the margins, `IL` and `DL` put the cursor on the left margin (`tests/vt420/line_starts.rs`; their rows), and `HPR` passes the right margin (its row); `HT` and `BS` stop at them by where the cursor starts since 2026-10-14. **A cursor a move put right of the fence writes on to the screen's last column**, since 2026-10-15 (note V's V10 step (d), C2-min): `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s insert-or-replace algorithm bounds a write by the right margin only from at or inside it, else by the line's end (pp. 5-139 and 5-140), and xterm's `dotext` takes `last_col` past `rmargin` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, charproc.c 6991-6995 and 7055-7058). A write that does not fit there wraps to the left margin, and with `DECAWM` reset the last column is the screen's; the cursor reports its own column and no wrap is pending. **At exactly right + 1 a move places the cursor** (`baud_domain::CursorCol`, a tag only cold code sets), so `CUP`, `HVP`, `CHA`, `HPA`, `HPR`, `VPA`, `CUB` and `BS` land on that column, `IL`/`DL`, `ICH`/`DCH` and `DECIC`/`DECDC` are inert there, `CUF` and `HT` go on from it, and `DECSC`/`DECRC` keep it placed; the sentinel a write leaves there is still a pending wrap on the margin (`tests/vt420/corner.rs`, 18 cases at every read size and split). `ICH` and `DCH` right of the fence are ignored, ICH note 2 (p. 5-142) and DCH note 3 (p. 5-144); they underflowed there, a panic in a debug build. Rows outside the top and bottom margins keep the right margin, where STD 070 gives the line's last column and the first column (p. 5-139), as xterm does: open. Under DECOM it homes to the margins' corner, `CUP` addresses from the left margin and every move stays inside the margins, since 2026-10-13 (the `?6` row). **Since 2026-10-21 the pictures in the rectangle move with it and the rest stay**: the ReGIS page's pixels in the rectangle move (`a_rectangle_scroll_moves_the_page_only_inside_the_margins`), and a Sixel across a side margin is split, the part inside moving as an image of its own (`a_sixel_across_a_side_margin_is_split`, `a_sixel_inside_moves_and_one_outside_stays`), the part moving its whole height by its anchor as a whole image in a band does; where the arena has no room for the part, its pixels move within the image instead, only its rows inside the band, and pixels a move takes past the image's top, moving up, or past the band's bottom or the image's, whichever comes first, moving down, are lost (`with_no_room_to_split_the_pixels_move_within_the_image`) |
| `CSI ' }` / `CSI ' ~` | DECIC / DECDC | done — the columns brought in are empty in the normal rendition, "blank with normal rendition" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-150 and 5-151; `[[source: vt510-decic]]`, "no visual character attributes"), where ICH and DCH take the current colours; until 2026-10-19 DECIC and DECDC took them too (`decic_and_decdc_bring_in_columns_with_normal_rendition`). Every row of the region shifts to the right margin, a double line's past its end, and an image stays where it is (the `ESC # 6` row) |
| `CSI Pn $ \|` | DECSCPP select columns per page | **partial** — since 2026-10-14: the width asked for (`Term::requested_width`), by `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `set_columns_per_page` (p. 5-74), `Pn` up to 80 -- omitted and 0 among them -- is 80 and anything past it 132, where xterm takes 0, 80 and 132 and ignores the rest (`decscpp_reads_its_parameter_as_std_070_does`). Nothing else here: no clear, no margin moved, no cursor moved, and `?3` not moved, since the algorithm changes `columns_per_page` and not `column_mode`, where xterm sets `IN132COLUMNS` (`decscpp_asks_without_40_and_leaves_the_screen`). **Not gated by `?40`**, as neither text nor xterm gates it -- the `?40` gate is `?3`'s alone. Under `WidthHold::On` the read stops at it, as at `?3` (`decscpp_holds_and_two_requests_stop_a_read_twice`); `RIS` after a request asks back for the width the window had before it (the `ESC c` row). `DECRQSS` for `$ \|` answers the page the grid is, by the same rule -- up to 80 columns is 80, wider is 132 -- where xterm answers the width last asked (`decrqss_answers_decscpp_with_the_grids_page`). `CSI ? Pn $ \|` is no control and asks nothing. **The gap**: the change is the embedder's `Term::resize`, which reflows the primary and resets all four margins, where STD 070 (p. 5-73) keeps the cursor and the right margin unless they lie past the new edge, preserves columns 1-80 and clears 81-132 |
| `CSI Ps $ ~` | DECSSDT select status line type | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can open a line of its own under the screen**, or ask for baud's: `[[source: vt420-rm]]` pp. 219-221 and `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 (pp. 14-4 to 14-13). 0 no line, 1 the indicator, 2 the host-writable line; an omitted `Ps` is 0, the VT420's "0 (default)" and 070-14's "If Ps is omitted, it is assumed to be 0", as xterm's code reads it, where ctlseqs' annotation marks 1; any other value is counted. **The line is a row beside the main display, never one of its rows**: `Term::geometry` and every size a program asks for stay the main display's, and the window grows a row to hold it and gives it back (`Term::status_rows`), or, in full screen, in a window as tall as the screen allows, maximised on Wayland or with `[window] operations = "off"`, the grid gives the row up and takes it back when the line goes, and under `BAUD_GRID`, which pins the grid, the window and the grid both keep their size (`the_status_line_grows_the_window_where_it_can_change_size`, `a_window_as_tall_as_the_screen_gives_the_grid_row_up`, `a_cell_resize_keeps_the_status_lines_row`). The line's resize comes before the window operations of the read that showed it, so `CSI 8 ; 30 t` in the same read gives thirty rows and the line, and a move keeps the line's row (`the_status_lines_frame_comes_before_a_pumps_requests`, `a_wayland_window_grows_by_the_line_first`). **No line at power-on**, 070-14's "No Status Line (factory default)", where the VT420 powers up with the indicator; `RIS` "Erases and exits the status line" to none. A type changed is initialized, "the Host Writable (Remote) Status Line is cleared", and a type other than host-writable leaves the line, "the Status Display will be exited" (p. 14-9). **The indicator** is the terminal's state in reverse video, "the reverse of the Main Display background" (p. 14-5), at fixed columns with a space between (§14.2.1.2): the page and the cursor's line and column, `1(LL,CCC)` as in the VT340's example, the VT420's first two fields (`[[source: vt420-uu-ch4]]` Table 4-2), the page always baud's one where 070-14 asks for it "if more than one page is available"; "Wait" while KAM locks the keyboard, the VT420's keyboard indicator; and the locator's position while DEC locator reporting is on, 070-14's Graphics Input Position Indicator, "only visible when graphics input is enabled" (p. 14-7), so it goes with the pointer event whose report turns a one-shot DECELR off, as it did only at the next event until 2026-10-21 (`the_locator_field_goes_when_a_one_shot_report_disables_the_locator`), a frame drawn for every pointer event while the indicator is shown, on both drivers, as "The interval between the change in the state of a feature and the update of the appropriate indicator shall not exceed 80 milliseconds" (pp. 14-5 and 14-6; `a_move_redraws_the_indicator_line`). **And the printer**, since 2026-10-21, at column 42 where the VT340's line has it, in the VT420's words: `Printer: None` with no printer since power-up, `Ready`, `Not Ready` once it is lost, `Auto Print` while autoprint is on and `Controller` during a printer controller run, a run's mode shown over the port's state, drawn again as soon as the embedder's printer changes (`the_indicator_shows_the_printer_and_the_print_mode`). No session, edit-mode or modem field: baud has one session and no local editing, and the VT420 leaves the modem field "blank, unless you have a modem connected", as 070-14's is "not displayed if Modem Controls are not selected", and a pty has none (`the_indicator_shows_the_page_and_the_cursor_in_reverse_video`, `the_indicator_shows_wait_while_the_keyboard_is_locked`). Level 1 does not have it, "DECSSDT and DECSASD do not work in Level 1 (VT100 mode)" (p. 14-13; `level_1_does_not_have_them`); a storage that lends no row has none and counts it (`a_storage_that_declines_the_row_has_no_line`, `the_line_writes_the_row_the_embedder_lends`). Every renderer draws it below the main display, the software and instance paths from the converted row and the cell shader from the window copy's row after the screen's, its glyphs by records (`the_frame_grows_a_row_for_the_line_and_gives_it_back`, `the_indicator_is_drawn_in_reverse_video_below_the_main_display`); a smooth-scroll slide does not run while it is shown |
| `CSI Ps $ }` | DECSASD select active status display | done — since 2026-10-20: 0 the main display, 1 the host-writable line, which "must be selected through DECSSDT for DECSASD to be able to select" it (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-14 p. 14-11; `decsasd_selects_the_line_only_when_it_is_host_writable`); any other value is counted. **The line is a second display of one row** with "its own ANSI text state and settings" (§14.2): 070-14's list of what each keeps, the active position and its pending wrap, the rendition and DECSCA, origin mode, G0-G3, GL, GR and the single shifts, and the `DECSC` slot, which the VT420 keeps "separate ... for the main display and the status line" (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 271); the character sets each display's where the VT420 shares them, DECAWM shared and its pending wrap each display's, and DECTCEM each display's, 070-14 item 14 (`the_line_keeps_its_own_rendition_charsets_and_saved_cursor`, `autowrap_is_shared_and_the_pending_wrap_is_each_displays`). Tabs, IRM and the width are shared (`tabs_and_irm_are_shared`). On the line "only the column parameters in cursor positioning control functions ... are significant", CUU and CUD do nothing, CPR reports line 1 (`only_the_columns_position_the_cursor_on_the_line`), ED, EL and ECH erase it alone (`an_erase_on_the_line_erases_the_line_alone`), and a scroll erases it, "a single line Host Writable Status Line is erased", retiring no line into the history (`scrolling_on_the_line_erases_it_and_keeps_no_history`). DECANM is ignored there (`decanm_is_ignored_on_the_line`); DECCOLM clears it and homes its cursor (`deccolm_clears_the_line_and_homes_its_cursor`); DECSTR and DECSCL leave it, not erased, and reset its own state where it waits as they reset the main display's (`the_resets_exit_the_line_as_070_says`, `decstr_resets_the_lines_own_state`). The cursor is drawn on the line while it is selected, on every path (`the_host_writable_line_draws_its_text_and_its_cursor`, `the_cursor_is_drawn_on_the_status_line_while_it_is_selected`, `the_cell_path_draws_the_status_line_as_the_instance_path_does`). What names a line of the main display does not reach the line: a Sixel, ReGIS or kitty image and an `OSC 133` mark sent there are counted and not acted on, and text and erases there free none of the main display's images, on either screen (`the_line_leaves_the_main_displays_images_alone`); the alternate screen, DECLRMM's single-width pages and the history's erase act on the main display (`declrmm_from_the_line_resets_the_main_displays_lines`); the selection and the margin bell are left where they are; and the rendition table's and the cluster store's sweeps keep what the line names (`a_sweep_keeps_the_lines_renditions`, `a_cluster_sweep_keeps_the_lines_clusters`). A press over the line is a press past the grid's last row, clamped to it as over the padding, and the locator reports it outside. xterm's `OPT_STATUS_LINE` ignores on the line DECDHL, DECSWL, DECDWL, CUU, CUD, VPA, VPR, ED, XTHIMOUSE and SD (both `CASE_TRACK_MOUSE`, which reads a single nonzero parameter as scroll-down, charproc.c 4030-4036), DECSTBM, DECALN, XTSMGRAPHICS, CAN, SUB, SPA, EPA, SU, IND, CPL, CNL, NEL, RI, the locator's controls and the rectangle operations (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, lines 3557-3598), where 070-14 has ED and a scroll act there: a departure not followed. baud lets DECDWL and DECDHL act on the line (`StatusLine::rendition`), where xterm ignores them and 070-14 says nothing of them |
| `CSI Pts;Pls;Pbs;Prs;Pps;Ptd;Pld;Ppd $ v` | DECCRA copy rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. A program can move a block of text and its colours to another place on the screen in one control, the cursor staying put (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-169). The copy is taken as the source stood before it began, so a block moved onto itself does not smear (`deccra_copies_an_overlapping_rectangle_as_it_was`); a destination past the page edge is clipped there. baud has one page, so the page parameters all name it, "treated as the last page" |
| `CSI Pch;Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ x` | DECFRA fill rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. The block fills with one character in the current colours and attributes (p. 5-170). `Pch` 32-126 goes through the character set in use, so after `ESC ( 0` a 113 draws a line; 160-255 is the Latin-1 supplement under either input encoding; anything else is ignored, as the text says, and counted. xterm also takes Unicode values, an extension baud does not take. A fill, and a DECCRA, over a picture frees it, as text written over one does; DECERA and DECSERA leave it, as ED and EL do, where xterm clears it under all four. The filled cells take DECSCA's protection as a written character does, as xterm's ScrnFillRectangle keeps PROTECTED in its ATTRIBUTES mask; the text names only "the current renditions set through the SGR command". **One character a position under the wide ambiguous-width policy too**, as a DEC position holds one character and xterm's `ScrnFillRectangle` writes one a column: a filled U+00B0 takes each column where the same character written takes two (`decfra_fills_a_position_each_under_wide_ambiguous_width`) |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ z` | DECERA erase rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Written cells become spaces and empty cells stay empty, all in the default rendition (p. 5-171, "clearing all character attributes"). xterm keeps the current SGR instead, and baud's own ED, EL and ECH erase to the current background alone under DECECM reset (the `?117` row). A wide character an edge of DECERA, DECSERA, DECFRA or a DECCRA destination cuts goes whole, its outside half a space in its own colours that keeps DECSCA's protection and loses an ECMA-48 guard and an `OSC 8` link (`the_half_a_cut_leaves_keeps_its_protection_alone`); DECCARA and DECRARA change only the half inside. **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases** (the Sixel row; `each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`). |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr $ {` | DECSERA selective erase rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Only characters DECSCA left erasable become spaces, keeping their colours; protected and empty cells are untouched (p. 5-172). The protection read is DECSCA's. The space keeps an ECMA-48 guard and an `OSC 8` link, as DECSED's does (`decsera_keeps_the_guard_and_the_link`). **Since 2026-10-21 it clears the Sixel and ReGIS pixels in the positions it erases and keeps them over a protected or unoccupied one** (the Sixel row; `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`). |
| `CSI Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr;Ps.. $ r` / `$ t` | DECCARA / DECRARA change and reverse attributes in a rectangle | done — since 2026-10-18. Bold, underline, blink and reverse change or flip across the block without touching the text or its colours (pp. 5-173 to 5-176). Each parameter acts as its SGR namesake does, so 24 clears any underline style and 22 faint too; values the text does not list, xterm's 8 and 28 among them, are ignored. DECCARA with no valid parameter clears the four; DECRARA with none does nothing, as STD 070 says, where the VT420 and VT510 manuals reverse all four. A cell is given a new rendition of its own, so text outside the block that shared one keeps it, and a selection on the rows stays, as in xterm |
| `CSI Ps * x` | DECSACE select attribute change extent | done — since 2026-10-18. 0 and 1 make DECCARA and DECRARA run as a stream of text from the first corner to the second, skipping empty cells; 2 makes them a rectangle, empty cells turned into blanks (p. 5-177). DECRQSS answers it (`*x`, `[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 12-4); RIS returns it to the stream and DECSTR keeps it, as its table does not list it |
| `CSI Ps # y` | XTCHECKSUM select checksum extension | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask for xterm's other checksums of the screen**: "The bits of Ps modify the calculation of the checksum returned by DECRQCRA" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Bit 0 leaves the sum positive (`bit_0_does_not_negate`), bit 1 leaves out the video attributes (`bit_1_leaves_the_attributes_out`), bit 2 counts an unwritten cell as a space (`bit_2_counts_an_unwritten_cell_as_a_space`), bit 3 omits unwritten cells even under bit 2, as ctlseqs says, where xterm's code reads it "do not skip uninitialized cells" (`bit_3_omits_unwritten_cells_even_under_bit_2`), bit 4 counts whole code points, a line-drawing cell its Unicode value and U+FFF0 to U+FFFF as themselves, where xterm skips that range (`bit_4_counts_whole_code_points`); a code and its weights wrap at sixteen bits, as xterm's `int` total does before `checksum &= 0xffff`, so a bold U+FFFD sums 0x007D (`bit_4_weights_wrap_at_sixteen_bits`). Combining marks count under bit 2 and not bit 4, as xterm's code adds them (`marks_count_under_bit_2_and_not_bit_4`). A bare `Ps` is 0 and bits above 4, the manual's bit 5 among them, are ignored, as xterm's code reads five (`a_bare_ps_is_0_and_high_bits_are_ignored`). `RIS` and `DECSTR` restore 0 (`both_resets_restore_the_default`). Ignored and counted below Level 4, as xterm's `vtXX_level >= 4` (`below_level_4_it_is_ignored_and_counted`). Honoured with `[screen] checksum` off, which then answers nothing, as a default xterm allows `SetChecksum` and refuses `GetChecksum` (`with_the_checksum_off_it_is_honoured_and_nothing_is_answered`); `OSC 61 ; allowWindowOps` no longer names it. Counted and dropped until then |
| `CSI Pi;Pg;Pt;Pl;Pb;Pr * y` | DECRQCRA request checksum of rectangular area | done — since 2026-10-18. Answered `DCS Pi ! ~ xxxx ST`, four capital hex digits (pp. 5-179 and 5-181), which vttest reads screens back with. No DEC text defines the sum, so it is xterm's DEC mode, the one vttest expects: each written cell's code (a character written through GR or a single shift counts its 7-bit stored code, below), a written space included, plus weights for bold, blink, reverse, underline, invisible and protection, empty cells left out, negated. Any underline style adds 0x10 and either blink 0x40; xterm's csDEC adds 0x10 only for its single-underline bit (SGR 21 sets ATR_DBL_UNDER, which it does not count). **Answered by default, and a setting turns it off**, since 2026-10-19. The embedder sets it (`Term::set_checksum_report`, `ChecksumReport::On` or `Off`), the app from `[screen] checksum`, `"on"` or `"off"`. Off, every request goes unanswered, whatever its corners, and is counted in `checksums_refused`, as xterm sends nothing with `GetChecksum` among its disallowed window operations (`CASE_DECRQCRA`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); a default xterm keeps it off (`[[source: xterm-man]]`): a program can ask one cell at a time and read back the screen, other programs' output included. No sequence turns it back on, `RIS` and `DECSTR` included, and `DSR ?63`, which sums no macro definitions and reads nothing of the screen, is answered either way, as xterm's is (`decrqcra_goes_unanswered_and_counted_with_the_checksum_off`, `no_reset_turns_the_checksum_back_on`; the app's `the_screen_checksum_is_answered_unless_turned_off`, `the_checksum_setting_reaches_the_core`). XTCHECKSUM changes how the sum is taken (its row). Inverted corners get no reply, as the text says. Since 2026-10-19: **a program checking a form it drew in a national character set gets the sum a DEC terminal gives**, each character counted as the code it was received as -- German `[` drawn as `Ä` counts 0x5B, as DEC STD 070's display stores the code and the set (p. 5-140) and xterm's `charSeen` counts it -- where it counted the drawn character's low byte (`decrqcra_counts_a_national_character_as_the_code_received`). A character sent as UTF-8 was not received as a GL code and counts its own value whatever set GL holds, a UTF-8 `Ä` 0xC4 under German as under ASCII, and REP's copy of it counts the same (`decrqcra_counts_a_utf8_character_as_itself_under_a_national_set`). Line drawing already counted its byte, a departure from xterm, which counts its own internal codes there. The UK `£` counts 0xA3 as xterm's does. A request with a page and no corners sums the whole page under DECOM too, "If the final four parameters are unspecified, a checksum of page Pp is generated" (p. 5-179; `decrqcra_of_a_page_under_decom_sums_the_whole_page`). A position DECERA, DECSERA, ED, EL or ECH left unoccupied counts nothing, as the texts leave it unoccupied (pp. 5-153 to 5-157, 5-171, 5-172); xterm marks the positions DECERA, DECSERA, EL 1 and an ECH ending mid-line erase as drawn, and counts each as a space, which is its departure, and ED, EL 0 and EL 2 leave them undrawn there too (`decrqcra_leaves_out_what_an_erase_leaves_unoccupied`). Since 2026-10-19 too, **every set that replaces a code counts the code received**: DEC Supplemental's `@` drawn `À` counts 0x40 and its `W` drawn `Œ` 0x57, JIS-Katakana's `1` drawn U+FF71 0x31 and JIS-Roman's `\` drawn `¥` 0x5C, where they counted the drawn character's low byte or 0x1B, so all 63 katakana summed alike (`decrqcra_counts_the_code_received_under_every_set`). Open: a reserved DEC Supplemental position counts DEC's error character, 0x1B, as xterm's `unmap_DEC_Supp_Graphic` does, where CODE_RECEIVED would count the code sent; the cell does not keep it. DEC Technical and the other sets that draw their byte, and Greek's unreplaced 7/9 and 7/10, count that byte, where xterm counts its own codes and 0x1B. A character sent as UTF-8 while line drawing is in GL counts itself, a UTF-8 `─` 0x1B, as xterm's `charSeen` does, where it counted `q`'s 0x71; the cell keeps the line-drawing flag, note AE Q16(a) (`decrqcra_counts_a_utf8_character_as_itself_under_line_drawing`). Combining marks are left out of the sum, as xterm's default sum leaves them (`decrqcra_leaves_combining_marks_out`). **A character written through GR or a single shift counts the 7-bit code STD 070's display stores through its set** (p. 5-140), since 2026-10-19: DEC Supplemental `D7` drawn `Œ` counts 0x57 as its `W` does, and a 96-character set's character its code, Latin-1 Supplemental's `A` drawn `Á` 0x41 (`the_checksum_counts_the_stored_code_through_gr_and_single_shifts`); xterm counts 0xD7 and 0xC1 |
| `CSI Ps $ w` | DECRQPSR request presentation state report | done — since 2026-10-19. `Ps` 1 answers DECCIR, `DCS 1 $ u Pr;Pc;Pp;Srend;Satt;Sflag;Pgl;Pgr;Scss;Sdesig ST`, and 2 DECTABSR, `DCS 2 $ u` the columns of the tab stops `/` between them `ST`; 0 and any other value are ignored (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-198 to 5-204; `[[source: vt510-decrqpsr]]`, `[[source: vt510-deccir]]`, `[[source: vt510-dectabsr]]`). A program can read the cursor state and the tab stops and put them back with DECRSPS. DECCIR reports the current state of the kinds DECSC saves: the screen position, bold, underline, blink and reverse, the selective erase attribute, origin mode and a pending wrap, GL, GR, the set sizes and each slot's designation as sent, `<` for the user-preference set (070's own default report, `1;1;1;@;@;@;0;2;@;BB<<`, p. 5-203, read off the page; `the_default_report_is_std_070s_example` and a test a field). The single-shift bits are 0, the `CSI` having cancelled any shift. Answered whatever level DA claims, as DECRQSS is, where STD 070 gives the reports "Levels: 2x (TSI), 3-4" and xterm answers at level 3 and up. The reply buffer holds the longest DECTABSR, a stop at every column of the widest screen (`the_longest_dectabsr_is_sent_whole`) |
| `CSI Ps $ u` | DECRQTSR request terminal state report | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can save most of the terminal's settings in one reply and put them back with DECRSTS**: `Ps` 1 answers DECTSR, `DCS 1 $ s D...D ST`, at most 256 characters as STD 070 guarantees (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-206, 5-207; `[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 207), in baud's own layout, since "The format of the data is firmware revision dependent": a version nibble, then every mode DECSET and SM act on -- XTSAVE's sixty-one private modes and IRM, LNM, ERM, KAM and SRM -- every setting DECRQSS answers, DECSCA's among them in the cursor information DECCIR reports, the input encoding `?1020` reports, and the shown screen's `DECSC` save, which `?1048` reports is there and no control reads back, as the VT420 manual's "the entire state of the terminal, except for user-defined key definitions and the current soft character set" has it (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` pp. 245-247; `the_saved_cursor_is_restored`, `the_input_encoding_is_restored`), one nibble a byte in `4/0..4/15` as the VT420's guideline has it, and two checksum bytes, the 2's complement of the 8-bit sum of the data a nibble each, high first (`decrqtsr_answers_a_report_in_std_070s_shape`). The page size is carried, DECSCPP's and DECSLPP's. The tab stops are carried where they fit the bound, a screen up to 248 columns (`the_report_is_the_layouts_length`), and on a wider screen when they are every eighth column; other stops on a wider screen are left to DECTABSR, which reports them at any width (`the_tab_stops_are_restored_where_the_report_holds_them`). **Not carried, open**: the other screen's `DECSC` save, which is the primary's `?1049` save while the alternate screen is up, and so on the alternate screen a restore leaves the primary's saved cursor as it finds it; and the state no TSI control reports, the screen's contents, soft fonts, user-defined keys and macros, as STD 070 leaves out "large data items that are not practical to report". `Ps` 2 is DECCTR (its row); 0, omitted and any other `Ps` are ignored, "no report sent" (`decrqtsr_ignores_any_other_report`). The answer follows `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` and is sent whole or dropped and counted in `reply_overflows` |
| `CSI 2 ; Pu $ u` | DECCTR colour table report | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can read the 256-colour palette and restore it with DECRSTS**: `DCS 2 $ s Pc;Pu;Px;Py;Pz/... ST` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-109; `[[source: vt520-rm]]` p. 5-161), a group for each colour baud knows -- the program's `OSC 4` colour, else the embedder's palette -- and none for a colour neither gave, as `OSC 4`'s query answers nothing for it (`decctr_reports_each_colour_the_core_knows`). `Pu` 2 reports RGB in percent and 1 HLS on DEC's wheel, blue at 0, red at 120 and green at 240; 0 and omitted report HLS, the VT340's default, "0 or none HLS (default)" (`[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 206), where the VT520's page calls 0 illegal; any other `Pu` is ignored (`decctr_reports_hls_by_default_on_decs_wheel`). Percent holds 101 levels, so a colour restored from a report is the nearest of them, which reports the same again (`decrsts_restores_the_colour_table`). The VT520's DECCTR page prints the answer as `DCS 2 $ p`, a copy error: its own DECTSR page and STD 070 give `$ s` |
| `CSI Ps ; Pu ' z` | DECELR enable locator reports | done — since 2026-10-20, **a forms or menu program written for DEC terminals can take the mouse as DEC's text locator** (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13, PDF 1013-1036; `[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1842-1853): `Ps` 1 reports, 2 reports once and then disables ("a single locator report (DECLRP) shall reset locator reporting mode to disabled", note 1 of DECLRP, a request's answer and a filter's included), 0 disables; `Pu` 1 reports in the device pixels `?1016` uses and 0 or 2 in cells. "Undefined values for Ps or Pu causes the entire sequence to be ignored", counted, where xterm reads them as off and cells (`decelr_with_an_undefined_value_is_ignored_and_counted`). **One of ctlseqs' mutually exclusive mouse protocols**: enabling replaces `?9` and `?1000`-`?1003`, a DECSET of one of them replaces the locator, a DECRST of any turns it off, and `Ps` 0 turns off whichever is in force, as xterm's one `send_mouse_pos` does (`decelr_and_the_xterm_mouse_protocols_replace_each_other`). It cancels a filter rectangle (`decsle_0_and_decelr_cancel_the_filter_rectangle`). `RIS` disables it and its events, units and rectangle; DECSTR, whose table (070-4 p. 31) names none of it, keeps it (`ris_disables_the_locator_and_decstr_keeps_it`). Under it the pointer's presses go to the program, Shift still selects as for the other protocols, and the wheel scrolls as with no protocol, since the locator has no wheel, where xterm reports its wheel as the fourth button and as event 10. Both drivers feed it: the macOS app and the Linux seat, through `gesture.rs`. Not reportable: DECRQSS answers its final invalid, as neither 070-5's list nor ctlseqs' names it (`decrqss_does_not_read_the_locator_controls`) |
| `CSI Pm ' {` | DECSLE select locator events | done — since 2026-10-20: 1 and 3 report button down and up transitions, 2 and 4 stop them, in order across the parameters; 0 or none, the power-up choice, reports only requests "and also cancels any pending filter rectangle"; a value the text does not give is counted (`decsle_selects_which_button_transitions_report_a_declrp`, `decsle_with_an_undefined_parameter_counts_it`) |
| `CSI Pe ; Pb ; Pr ; Pc & w` | DECLRP locator report, sent by baud | done — since 2026-10-20, what baud sends; one a host sends baud is counted, as no control it acts on (`a_received_declrp_is_counted`): the event (1 request, 2-9 a button down and up, left, middle, right and fourth, 10 outside the filter rectangle), the buttons held after it ("1-Right 2-Middle 4-Left 8-Fourth"), the row and the column from 1:1. **No `Pp`**: "The page coordinate may be omitted if the locator is on page one", and baud has one page; xterm omits it too. **The position is omitted where it is undefined**, "Pr and Pc shall be omitted" (note 3): off the text area, a pixel the embedder did not give, and under DECOM in cells outside the margins, which 070-13 § 3.1 gives as its example; under DECOM cells count from the margins, as CPR's do, and pixels from the text area. xterm sends `CSI 0 & w` for an undefined position and ignores DECOM. `CSI 0 & w` alone is "locator unavailable". The fourth button is the first past three, macOS's button 3 and the Wayland seat's `BTN_SIDE`. A press outside a filter rectangle reports the press first, "by increasing event code number" (§ 3). A report the buffer cannot take is dropped and counted, and a one-shot DECELR waits for the next, from the pointer and from DECRQLP or DECEFR alike (`a_dropped_decrqlp_answer_leaves_a_one_shot_decelr_on`, `declrp_names_each_button_and_the_buttons_held_after_it`, `a_press_with_the_position_undefined_omits_the_coordinates`, `under_decom_cells_count_from_the_margins_and_pixels_do_not`, `a_press_outside_the_rectangle_reports_the_press_first`, `a_report_that_does_not_fit_is_dropped_and_a_one_shot_waits`, `declrp_writes_its_three_shapes`, `declrp_follows_s8c1t`) |
| `CSI Pt ; Pl ; Pb ; Pr ' w` | DECEFR enable filter rectangle | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask to be told when the pointer leaves a rectangle**, once: event 10 with where it went, or with the position omitted when it left the text area, and the rectangle ends. Omitted corners are the pointer's own, so no corners reports any move to another cell or pixel; a rectangle that does not contain the pointer reports at once. With the pointer away, a rectangle with a corner given reports at once with no position and one with none waits for the pointer to come back (note 2); with the locator disabled, or no pointer told (`a_core_with_no_pointer_has_no_locator`), it answers `CSI 0 & w` (note 4), where xterm is silent. **A rectangle whose top is below its bottom is taken as given**: it contains nothing, so it reports at once, where DEC's area operations ignore such a rectangle and xterm swaps its corners. xterm turns the locator off when a button is pressed or DECRQLP arrives while a rectangle is armed, "This might be a bug"; 070-13 says no such thing, and it stays (`decefr_reports_once_when_the_pointer_leaves_the_rectangle`, `decefr_takes_omitted_corners_from_the_pointer_and_reports_at_once_outside`, `decefr_reports_the_pointer_leaving_and_waits_for_it_to_come_back`, `decefr_with_the_locator_disabled_reports_it_unavailable`, `decefr_with_no_pointer_reports_at_once_or_waits_by_its_corners`, `a_request_or_a_press_inside_the_rectangle_leaves_the_locator_on`) |
| `CSI Ps ' \|` | DECRQLP request locator position | done — since 2026-10-20: `Ps` 0, 1 or none answers DECLRP event 1 with the pointer's buttons and position, omitted where undefined; with the locator disabled, or no pointer told, event 0, "to avoid timing out the application" (p. 8), DECLRP's "not enabled, or physically not present"; any other `Ps` is counted (`decrqlp_answers_unavailable_until_decelr_enables_the_locator`, `decrqlp_reports_the_pointer_and_its_buttons_in_cells_or_pixels`, `decrqlp_with_an_undefined_ps_is_ignored_and_counted`, `a_one_shot_decelr_is_disabled_by_its_first_report`) |
| `ESC 6` / `ESC 9` | DECBI / DECFI back and forward index | done — since 2026-10-18. The cursor steps one column, and at the left or right margin the text inside the margins slides over by one column instead, a blank column with no attributes coming in (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-37 to 5-39). It slides from any row, as the algorithm tests the column alone and the table marks both "not affected" by the top and bottom margins; xterm slides nothing from a row outside them. On a double-width line DECFI stops at the line's last column, as its algorithm cuts the column back to `end_of_line` (p. 5-38); until 2026-10-19 it stepped into the half past it (`decfi_on_a_double_width_line_stops_at_the_lines_end`). From a single-width row, the slide moves a double-width row to the right margin too, its hidden cells with it, as note 1 of each says, "Lines of text are shifted one column regardless of their line attributes", and as the scroll_left and scroll_right routines they call move every row to the right margin (§ 5.14.4 and § 5.14.5, pp. 5-212 and 5-213; `decfi_and_decbi_shift_a_sized_line_to_the_right_margin`) |

DECSCA, DECSED and DECSEL land together on purpose: the two erases without the
protection are silent aliases for ED and EL, and an application that protected a
region would watch it vanish.

Protection is **not** an `Attrs` bit. `[[source: vt510-decsca]]`: "DECSCA does
not effect visual character attributes set by the select graphic rendition (SGR)
function" -- so it sits in its own field of `StyleWord`, where a renderer
reading attributes cannot mistake it for one. It *is* part of the interning key,
so protected and unprotected text are distinct renditions and the flag survives
in the template table without widening the 8-byte `PackedCell`.

Two rules follow, and both are pinned by tests. A plain ED/EL erases protected
characters -- only the private forms are selective. And the blank an erase
leaves behind does not inherit the protection, or the first DECSEL under
`DECSCA 1` would protect everything it just cleared. A selective erase leaves
a space in the character's own rendition and protection, which is erasable
because only erasable characters become one.

---

## Tier 3 — xterm extensions in universal use

In scope because applications assume them, not because DEC specified them.

| seq | name | status |
|---|---|---|
| `OSC 0/1/2` | window/icon title | done — stored in the core (`Term::title`, `Term::icon_name`), and **set on the window** by both drivers since 2026-09-24, where until then nothing read it. **Not drawn on macOS**: the window crate's titled glass window hides its title text, so a program's title reaches the Window menu, Mission Control and accessibility, not the titlebar. On Wayland it reaches the compositor, whose titlebar shows it only under server-side decorations, which baud does not negotiate. The app reads the bytes in the encoding the core kept with them (`Term::title_encoding`, the XTSMTITLE row) and turns controls into spaces (`baud-app/src/title.rs`). **When a program sets no title, the window's title is the folder and the program running in it**, `~/src/baud — vim`, since 2026-10-21, a deep folder as its last three after an ellipsis, as kitty's and ghostty's shell integrations spell a prompt's title: the folder the shell's `OSC 7` names when it names this machine (the `OSC 7` row), else the foreground program's own, and the program by its argv[0], read from the pty's foreground process group (`TIOCGPGRP` on the master; macOS's `proc_pidinfo` and `KERN_PROCARGS2`, Linux's `/proc`; `baud-app/src/fg.rs`), read at the group's id as a pid, the group leader, as wezterm and alacritty read it; a pipeline whose leader has exited reads nothing, and the title keeps the half it knows (note AK 2.2, UNVERIFIED there). A program's title wins outright, as "Change Window Title to Pt" says, so nothing is added to one (note AK question 2); the derived title is read once a frame when a read, a key or, on macOS, a process fork, exec or exit owes it, never on a timer (`many_owed_checks_are_one_and_an_unowed_one_is_none`, `the_foreground_reads_name_the_program_and_its_folder`). **The icon name is the minimized window's title on macOS** since 2026-10-21 (`OSC 1`, the icon half of `OSC 0`, and `OSC 3`'s icon properties): AppKit's `miniwindowTitle`, set through the crate's `ns_window` after every title set, which "automatically changes the minimized window's title as well" (`[[source: apple-nswindow-miniwindowtitle]]`); with none the minimized window shows the title (note AK question 8; `the_icon_name_is_the_minimized_title_after_every_title_set`). Wayland's `xdg_toplevel` has no icon name (`[[source: wp-xdg-shell]]`), so the Linux app keeps it stored. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset empties the title and the icon name**, for the window to show its own until a program names one again, and `DECSTR` keeps both: the state at power-on has neither (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105), where xterm keeps both, a practice not followed (`ris_empties_the_title_and_icon_name_and_decstr_keeps_them`) |
| `OSC 4` | set colour palette | done — **every `c ; spec` pair of a sequence is applied, in order**, since 2026-10-18: "Any number of c/spec pairs may be given", and a `?` in any pair is answered in its own reply where it stands, with the query's terminator (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). **Since 2026-10-25 every query in a read is answered, however many the read carries**: baud's app sets `ReplyHold::On`, so a read whose answers fill half the reply buffer stops after the control that filled it, the answers go out, and the rest is parsed -- herdr's whole palette, four rounds of it, at any read size (`a_read_of_questions_is_answered_whole_and_in_order`, note AG, section 1). The buffer holds two of the longest answer one control can owe, XTGETTCAP's 10,412 bytes, and one `OSC 4` can owe at most a `?` in each pair its 512-byte body holds. An embedder that leaves the hold off has an answer that does not fit dropped whole and counted in `reply_overflows`, never cut. As xterm's `ChangeAnsiColorRequest` walks them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 2993-3023), a bad index, an index with no spec or a spec no reader takes ends the walk and keeps the pairs before it, counted once; an index of 256 to 260 names one of xterm's special colours, set or asked for as `OSC 5` does (the `OSC 5` row), and the walk goes on (`osc_4_applies_every_pair`, `a_query_in_any_pair_is_answered_in_turn`, `a_bad_pair_stops_the_walk_and_is_counted`, `a_special_colour_by_osc_4_is_set_and_the_walk_goes_on`); it was counted before 2026-10-19. Only the first pair was taken before. Including the `?` query, **answered for every entry** since 2026-09-29, as xterm's `ReportAnsiColorRequest` does: the application's override, else the embedder's table (`Term::set_default_palette`, pushed with the default colours by both apps), else nothing on a core no embedder told (`baud-core/tests/palette_query.rs`). It answered only overridden entries before. **Both resets clear every override** since 2026-09-29, as xterm's `ReallyReset` calls `ResetAnsiColorRequest` in its common path, and `DECSTR` leaves `OSC 10`/`11`/`12` as it does, where `RIS` clears them since 2026-10-25 (the `ESC c` row; `baud-core/tests/palette_reset.rs`, `a_soft_reset_returns_an_overridden_entry_to_the_theme`); they kept every override before, so a program reset after recolouring the sixteen left its colours on everything drawn after it. **A colour name is resolved as the X server resolves it**, since 2026-10-18: XParseColor sends a name to the server (`[[source: libx11-parsecol-b8d5a70]]` 114-139), which ignores case and nothing else (`[[source: xserver-oscolor-98aed9d]]` 852-884), over X.Org's rgb.txt (`[[source: xorg-rgb-0d2caec]]`, 782 names), built into a table at build time and found by two hashes and one comparison; the sixteen names of xterm's FAQ resource file and its `lime green` set what they name (`xterms_own_colour_names_resolve`, `every_name_in_rgb_txt_resolves_in_any_case`). A name the server lacks is left alone and **counted** as unsupported, as every name was until then (`a_name_the_server_lacks_is_counted`). So are the three sample names a stock libX11 installs in its client-side `Xcms.txt` -- `cms red`, `cms green`, `cms blue`, "provided just as an example" (`[[source: libx11-xcms-txt-b8d5a70]]`, installed by every build, `[[source: libx11-xcms-makefile-b8d5a70]]` 88-89) -- which XParseColor tries before the server, case and spaces ignored (`[[source: xparsecolor-obs-xcms-names-out]]`, and with no database `[[source: xparsecolor-obs-xcms-names-nodb]]`): the Xlib manual reads "zero or more client-side databases", their number and content "implementation-dependent" (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]` 533-537), and baud keeps none. **`rgbi:` and the six device-independent forms are colours** since 2026-10-18 (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]` 637-695): `CIEXYZ:`, `CIEuvY:`, `CIExyY:`, `CIELab:`, `CIELuv:` and `TekHVC:` converted to the sRGB surface baud draws on, CIELAB and CIELUV as CIE 015:2018 defines them (`[[source: cie-015-2018]]`), TekHVC, the conversions and the gamut clip as libX11's Xcms computes them (`an_in_gamut_spec_is_the_texts_colour`, `an_out_of_gamut_spec_keeps_its_hue_and_value`, `clip_matches_libx11_under_its_own_profile`). libX11 converts through a 1990 CRT profile when the server publishes none and reads CIELAB and CIELUV at a hundredth of CIE's scale; both depart from CIE 015:2018 (`[[source: cie-015-2018]]`) and the Xlib manual (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]`), which baud follows. A value off the manual's grammar or range is refused and counted (`a_spec_off_the_grammar_or_the_range_is_refused`). **A short `#` form is its channels' high bits, and a prefix is read in any case**, since 2026-10-18, as the Xlib manual's "Color Strings" defines them (`[[source: xlib-ch06-b8d5a70]]`): `#fff` draws `#f0f0f0`, `#3a7` is `#3000a0007000`, and `RGB:FF/00/00` is red. Every form is read to XParseColor's sixteen bits and drawn as its high byte, the pixel a 24-bit X server gives it; 607 specs run through libX11 on Xvfb are held line by line (`every_spec_reads_as_the_oracle_drew_it`, `[[source: xparsecolor-obs-rgb-out]]`). `rgb:1/2/3/4`, which libX11's reader takes, is refused, as the manual's grammar has three fields. **Under `?5` an entry set, queried or reset is the entry drawn wherever a cell names it**, since 2026-10-14, when every cell's colours exchange and no entry moves (decided 2026-10-13; the `?5` row; `a_palette_override_is_drawn_where_its_entry_is_named`); under xterm's palette swap, drawn until then, an override of entry 7 was drawn where a cell named 0 |
| `OSC 10` / `OSC 11` | default foreground / background | done — **under `?5` each names the colour shown in its role**, `OSC 11` the stored foreground, and the reply keeps the code asked (the `?5` row, since 2026-10-11). Set from any spec `OSC 4` takes, read by the renderer before its theme (`Term::dynamic_color`), and the `?` query answered in the setter's shape, **four hex digits a channel** (`rgb:efef/efef/efef`), which is xterm's reply form: `rgb:` accepts one to four digits in what is *sent*, but readers parse the reply at fixed offsets, and yazi read baud's two-digit `rgb:ef/ef/ef` as a failure it reports as a dark background whatever the theme, until 2026-09-22 (`yazi_reads_a_light_background_as_light_and_a_dark_one_as_dark`). The `OSC 4` reply takes the same width. **A query on a fresh terminal answers the embedder's theme**, which both apps push in through `Term::set_default_colors` -- the query every theme-aware program sends to learn light from dark; a core that was never told says nothing, the `OSC 4` refusal for the same reason. **Each spec after the first sets the next colour**, since 2026-10-18: "Each successive parameter changes the next color in the list. The value of Ps tells the starting point in the list" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), so `OSC 10 ; fg ; bg ; cursor` sets all three and `OSC 11 ; bg ; cursor` starts at the background; an empty field passes over its colour, each `?` is answered in its own reply, in turn (within the reply buffer's cap, as the `OSC 4` row says), and a number baud keeps no colour for -- the pointer's 13 and 14, Tektronix's 15, 16 and 18, which baud does not draw -- and the specs past 19 are counted while the walk goes on, as xterm's `ChangeColorsRequest` walks them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3680-3703; `each_spec_after_the_first_sets_the_next_colour`, `an_empty_field_leaves_its_colour`, `each_query_in_the_list_is_answered`, `a_colour_baud_does_not_keep_is_counted_and_the_walk_goes_on`). Only the first spec was taken before. `osc_10_and_11_set_the_dynamic_colours`, `osc_11_query_answers_the_embedders_default`, `dynamic_colours_are_chunk_invariant`, and the pixels in `baud-render/tests/dynamic.rs`. **A full reset returns both to the embedder's**, since 2026-10-25: a program's colour is not part of "the state it has after it is made operational" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105), where xterm's `ReallyReset` keeps every dynamic colour (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`), a practice not followed; `DECSTR` keeps them, as its list names neither (`ris_returns_the_text_colour_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `ris_returns_the_page_colour_and_decstr_keeps_it`) |
| `OSC 110` / `OSC 111` | reset default foreground / background | done — clears the application's override and leaves the theme's default, which is what reset means; under `?5` of the role shown, as xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3718-3750; `osc_110_and_111_under_reverse_reset_the_colour_shown`, since 2026-10-11). `an_override_wins_the_query_and_reset_restores_the_default` |
| `OSC 12` / `OSC 112` | cursor colour | done — **stored and read** now that a renderer draws a cursor colour, which is the day the old counted-not-stored deferral named. The block cursor fills with it and the bar or underline cursor takes it, on all three paths -- `Renderer::convert_row` for the software and instance arms, the cell shader for the cell arm, which `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws` gates pixel-for-pixel. A spec is read as `OSC 4` reads it -- a colour name, the `#` and `rgb:` forms, `rgbi:` and the CIE and TekHVC forms -- since 2026-10-18 (`xterms_own_colour_names_resolve`), and the `Cs` the three `xterm` profiles carry replays with `red` in the conformance suite. The `?` query answers in the code-12 shape and `OSC 112` resets it. **A configured cursor colour** -- `[palette] cursor`, or a theme file's -- is drawn while no program has set one, answers the query and is what `OSC 112` returns to, since 2026-10-22: xterm reports the colour in use, at start its `cursorColor` resource, and its `ResetColorsRequest` allocates that resource again (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3568-3595, 3718-3750); with none configured the query answers nothing and the reset returns to none, as before. A configured colour equal to the text moves on a `?5` change and with a new `OSC 10` colour as a program's does (`a_configured_cursor_colour_is_drawn_answered_and_what_112_restores`, `a_configured_cursor_colour_equal_to_the_text_moves_as_a_programs_does`). The text under a block keeps a configured cursor text colour under a program's `OSC 12`, as kitty, alacritty, ghostty and foot keep theirs. `osc_12_sets_and_reads_the_cursor_colour`. **A colour equal to the text moves to the background on a `?5` change** (the `?5` row, since 2026-10-11). **A cursor colour equal to the text follows a new `OSC 10` colour**, since 2026-10-19, when the same sequence sets no cursor colour, as xterm's `ChangeColors` moves it (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 3062-3070), so a program that painted the cursor in its text colour keeps it visible; before, an `OSC 10` left it where it was (`a_cursor_colour_equal_to_the_text_follows_osc_10`). `OSC 110`, the reset of the text colour, does not move it: xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` goes through `ChangeColors` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3743-3747) and does, and only `OSC 10` was built. **A full reset returns it to the configured colour**, or to none, since 2026-10-25, as `OSC 10`/`11` go (that row); `DECSTR` keeps a program's (`ris_returns_the_cursor_colour_and_decstr_keeps_it`) |
| `OSC 17` / `OSC 19` | highlight background / foreground: the selection's colours | done — since 2026-10-18. **A selection takes the colours a program sets**: "Ps = 1 7 -> Change highlight background color to Pt", "Ps = 1 9 -> Change highlight foreground color to Pt" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), each drawn whenever it is set, on all three paths (`Hilite`, `Resolved::drawn`, the cell shader's `colors()`): the page alone draws each selected cell's own text on it, the text alone draws on the reversed page, both draw the text on the page, and neither is reverse video, the default. That is kitty's practice (`[[source: kitty-conf]]`) and the `highlightColorMode` true rows of xterm's manual; xterm decides that mode once at startup and, configured with nothing, never draws a runtime `OSC 19` -- baud draws each when set. No per-cell exception: xterm draws a cell whose own colour equals the highlight reversed, code its manual and ctlseqs do not state. Under `?5` each names the colour sent, the selection being drawn after DEC's exchange, where xterm's handlers trade them under its own `reverseVideo`. A glyph in a highlight colour takes that colour's contrast curve row. The `?` query answers in `OSC 10`'s shape with the query's terminator: the colour set, else the embedder's configured colour (the selection colours `Term::set_theme` is given, since 2026-10-21), else the colour a reverse-video selection is drawn in: the text shown for `17`, the page shown for `19`, whether or not the other is set -- as a default xterm answers, silent on a core never told its colours. `RIS` returns both to the configured colours, or to reverse video, since 2026-10-25, as `OSC 10`/`11` go (that row), where xterm's `ReallyReset` touches no dynamic colour (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`), and it kept the application's before; `DECSTR` keeps them (`ris_returns_the_selection_page_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `ris_returns_the_selection_text_and_decstr_keeps_it`). No DEC mode covers them, so DECRQM has none to report. A change repaints the frame. `osc_17_and_19_set_the_selection_colours_and_117_and_119_reset_them`, `a_highlight_query_answers_the_colour_the_selection_is_drawn_in`, `a_configured_selection_colour_answers_and_is_what_117_and_119_restore`, `ris_and_a_theme_flip_keep_the_configured_selection`, `osc_17_and_19_under_reverse_name_the_colour_sent`, `a_selection_is_drawn_in_the_highlight_colours_each_when_set`, `an_incremental_frame_after_osc_17_equals_a_full_repaint`, the pair scene of `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`. Before, both were counted as unsupported. **A configured colour is drawn**, since 2026-10-21, on all three paths and in both apps: the `[palette]` `selection` and `selection_text` keys, else a theme file's selection colours, else reverse video, the default (note V, V13); a program's colour wins over each while set; the find field, its current match and the `?1001` region draw in them too. `a_theme_selection_colour_is_drawn_until_a_program_sets_its_own`, `the_configs_selection_wins_over_the_files_and_none_takes_it_away`, `osc_17_and_19_answer_the_themes_selection`, the theme scene of `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`. **In a macOS window that is not key, reverse video is AppKit's unemphasized pair**, since 2026-10-22: `unemphasizedSelectedTextBackgroundColor` and `unemphasizedSelectedTextColor`, "e.g. when the associated control/view/window is not key" (`[[source: sdk27-nscolor-h]]` 232-235), resolved under the window's appearance; the find field's marks and the `?1001` region, drawn in the selection's colours, take it too. A colour of its own, the program's or the configured one, is drawn as it is. The core is not told the pair, so while the window is not key the `?` query answers the colours a key window draws. `a_reverse_video_selection_takes_the_unemphasized_pair_while_not_key`, `a_selection_colour_of_its_own_is_unchanged_while_not_key`, `the_unemphasized_pair_is_appkits_under_each_appearance` |
| `OSC 117` / `OSC 119` | reset highlight background / foreground | done — since 2026-10-18, and since 2026-10-21 **back to the embedder's configured colour**, "their default (resource) values" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), as xterm's `ResetColorsRequest` allocates the resource again (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3718-3750); reverse video where none is configured. `osc_17_and_19_set_the_selection_colours_and_117_and_119_reset_them`, `a_configured_selection_colour_answers_and_is_what_117_and_119_restore` |
| `OSC 8` | hyperlinks | done — **a listing links every entry the table can hold, and links again once the old ones are gone**: `ls --hyperlink` of a 37-entry directory linked 34 entries and of a 227-entry one, 101 of its 202 targets, until 2026-10-31, and after 127 targets every later link of the session was plain text until a reset; it links 37, and 127, the most a cell can name at once, and a listing after `clear` links again. A target probes sixteen of the 127 slots, each slot keyed by its `id` and address so two ids with one address stay two links as `[[source: osc8-spec]]` asks; when every slot it may take is held, the table is swept of the slots no cell, open link or parked display names. **A sweep reads the rows that hold links, not the buffer**: a linked cell marks its row, and a sweep reads each marked row to its mark, so in the app's storage at 80x24 a listing after `clear` links 127 targets however many listings came before, where reading the buffer's 940,000 cells left it plain until 235 KB more came. It is paid for in bytes read, four cells a byte, counted; an `ED 3` of rows that held links, or a reset, pays off what the sweeps before it spent from a quarter of the bytes read and not yet spent on a payoff, and a sweep that frees nothing makes the next wait for more links, so the sweeps read at most five cells a byte beside a fixed allowance, and the hostile streams the tests run spend at most 7.5-8.0 ns a byte more than with no sweep (an `ED 3` that paid off the whole debt let a stream sweep nearly every 65 bytes, 29-43 times slower than the base with a short history, and a reset that did let one read 57 cells a byte; `sixty_distinct_targets_on_one_screen_are_all_linked`, `a_target_past_the_table_is_linked_once_nothing_names_the_old_ones`, `two_ids_with_one_uri_are_two_links_even_from_one_home_slot`, `a_link_the_parked_display_holds_open_outlives_a_sweep`, `a_listing_after_clear_links_in_the_apps_storage`, `a_clear_pays_for_the_sweep_after_it`, `a_program_on_the_alternate_screen_keeps_its_links_over_a_full_history`, `a_listing_past_the_table_sweeps_a_few_times`, `a_sweep_reads_at_most_four_cells_a_byte`, `an_erase_of_the_history_pays_off_at_most_a_cell_a_byte`, `a_reset_pays_off_at_most_a_cell_a_byte`, `every_linked_cell_is_on_a_row_a_sweep_reads`). **A slot something names is never given another target**, so a cell never resolves to an address other than the one written under its index (`no_cell_ever_resolves_to_another_links_target`); a link still without a slot, the 128th named at once, is refused, and a clipped address is refused too, both counted in `links_refused` / `links_clipped`. A press holds its link with the table's epoch (`Term::link_epoch`), so a release over a cell that took a freed slot opens nothing (`a_press_on_a_link_whose_slot_was_given_away_opens_nothing`). **Activated in `baud-app` by Cmd-click**, and on Linux since 2026-10-21 by Ctrl-click, which opens with `xdg-open` and reveals through the file manager's `ShowItems` (`a_ctrl_click_on_a_link_opens_it`): `http`, `https` and `mailto` are opened, a `file` link on this host is revealed, in Finder on macOS and by the file manager's `ShowItems` on Linux (the opener would *launch* an application bundle), everything else is refused and counted; Cmd-Shift-click is a selection, a repeat click is swallowed whole, and a tracking child never sees the gesture. `a_cell_never_resolves_to_a_uri_other_than_the_one_written_under_its_index`, `a_link_whose_uri_may_have_been_cut_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_four_schemes_are_admitted_and_nothing_else_is`, `a_cmd_click_on_a_link_arms_on_the_down_and_opens_on_the_up`; see security note. **Since 2026-10-22 holding Cmd (Ctrl on Linux) over a link shows where it goes**: every cell naming its slot underlined, as the spec underlines cells sharing a URI and a nonempty `id` (`[[source: osc8-spec]]`), and its address in a bar at the bottom, a byte outside printable ASCII shown as `\xNN`, shortened to keep the end of its host, with the reason in words when a click will not open it (`the_preview_shows_every_byte_a_target_holds`, `every_cell_naming_the_link_is_underlined`, `ctrl_held_over_a_link_previews_it`, `the_link_preview_is_drawn_moved_and_cleared_without_a_trace`); the bar is drawn over a program's images, as kitty draws its hyperlink target after them (`[[source: kitty-hyperlink-target]]`; `baud_s_own_overlays_are_drawn_over_an_image`) |
| `OSC 52` | clipboard | done — **writes honoured, reads deliberately refused** (silence, counted in `clipboard_reads_refused`; see security note). Since 2026-10-10 (note V, V11): the body after `52;` leaves the 512-byte OSC buffer for two caller-provided areas (`Owned::with_clipboard`), is decoded as it arrives by `baud-domain`'s RFC 4648 decoder, and is published at the terminator (`Term::clipboard`, `Term::clipboard_writes`), whatever the read boundaries (`baud-core/tests/clipboard.rs`, the `osc52-*` property atoms). `Pc`'s twelve selections are kept as a set and an empty `Pc` is `s 0`; a `Pd` that is neither base64 nor `?` **clears** the selection, ctlseqs' rule. A copy is refused whole and counted, never cut, when the storage has no area, `Pc` names nothing ctlseqs knows, or it outgrows the area (`Term::clipboard_refusals`).
