The decoder accepts an unpadded final quantum and non-zero pad bits, both allowed by RFC 4648 3.2 and 3.5 and both a choice. macOS puts a write naming `c`, `p` or `s` on the general pasteboard when it is UTF-8, under `[clipboard] osc52` (on by default); `q` and the cut buffers have no counterpart and are refused (`baud-app/src/clip.rs`). Since 2026-10-20 Linux provisions the area and puts a write on each selection it names, through `wl_data_device` (`[[source: wayland]]`) and the primary-selection device (`[[source: wp-primary-selection-unstable-v1]]`): `c` the clipboard, `p` the primary, and `s` -- an empty `Pc` too -- xterm's SELECT, the primary while `?1041` is reset (foot, alacritty and kitty send an empty `Pc` to the clipboard, `[[source: foot-2705e36-osc]]`, `[[source: alacritty-vte-ansi]]`, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-clipboard-py]]`, so a tmux yank, whose `Pc` is empty (`[[source: tmux-3-5a-window-copy]]`), lands on the primary here as in xterm). A write names the serial of the last key or click, as foot names its last keyboard serial, `set_selection` asking for "the event that triggered this request" and a program's write having none; a copy the window could not make is said once (`the_linux_driver_polls_after_every_read`). Since 2026-10-21 a write that comes while the window lacks the keyboard is sent the same way, counted and said once, and a cancel of it before the keyboard returns is counted and said: neither protocol's `set_selection` asks for the keyboard, GNOME's compositor refuses a client without it and cancels the source (`[[source: mutter-47-data-device]]`), and wlroots takes it unless a later copy came first (`[[source: wlroots-0-18-2-data-device]]`); it is set at once rather than held until the keyboard returns, where foot and alacritty drop it (`[[source: foot-2705e36-osc]]`, `[[source: smithay-clipboard-0-7-2-state]]`) and kitty sends it (`[[source: kitty-0-43-1-wl-window]]`) (`a_copy_without_the_keyboard_is_sent_and_its_cancel_said`, `a_copy_without_the_keyboard_and_its_cancel_are_counted`) |

at a redraw the erase stands for it, the cursor going to the start of the block's emptied first row. **Since 2026-10-28, a completion list stays on the screen** in a shell that marks its input with `B` or `I`: an initial mark that arrives with the cursor past the end of the input's line -- bash, and zsh when its list does not fit below the prompt, printing the prompt again under the list -- is that prompt drawn again, not a redraw: nothing is erased, the cursor stays where the shell put it and the command keeps one entry in the prompt log, while a mark with the cursor on the input's line or above it, where a shell that redraws puts it, still replaces the block (a row the input wrapped onto is its line when the input's first row has scrolled above the screen, where zsh's count clamps and readline redraws from the input's last row), as does the first initial mark after a resize, wherever the cursor is, since readline's redraw on `SIGWINCH` starts on the input's last line, below any line break inside it, and any initial mark in the zone before a `B` or `I`, where the input is not known (`bash_keeps_its_list_above_the_prompt_it_prints_again`, `zsh_keeps_a_list_that_does_not_fit_below_its_prompt`, `a_mark_below_the_lines_under_the_input_erases_nothing`, `a_redraw_from_the_row_the_input_wrapped_onto_replaces_the_block`, `a_tall_input_redrawn_after_a_shrink_replaces_the_block`, `a_redraw_on_the_last_row_of_a_tall_input_replaces_the_block`, `a_line_break_in_the_input_redrawn_after_a_resize_replaces_the_block`; recorded zsh, bash, fish and nushell in `tests/completion/`). `A`/`N` start a command and `P` (`k=i`) a prompt, `k=s`/`k=c`/`k=r` later lines, `B` and `I` the input, `C` the output, `D` the end; an `I` input ends with its line, read at the next mark or resize (`an_i_input_ends_with_its_line`). Options reset at each new command, as kitty's `screen.c` does: `redraw=0` skips the redraw erase and lets a resize re-cut the prompt as output, as kitty does (`a_redraw_mark_under_redraw_0_erases_nothing`, `a_prompt_under_redraw_0_is_recut_like_output`); `redraw=last` erases as `1`, since an initial mark reaches a block under it only where the shell redraws a line that begins with one ; `cl=` (any value read as `line`, which the text permits), `move-keys=`, kitty's `click_events=` and `special_key=` are what a click at the prompt sends (`a_click_sends_what_the_a_asked`; the app's input-coverage row), and a prompt re-cut under `redraw=0` keeps its block start and its input on their cells (`a_recut_prompt_keeps_its_input_where_it_began`). `D`'s status is kept on the prompt its command began with -- `err=` first, a `D` with no `C` cancelled as iTerm2 reads it (`d_keeps_its_status_on_the_prompt_it_ends`), the command found by `aid` through nested ones (`a_nested_command_takes_its_status_by_aid`) -- and read through `Term::prompts`. **Since 2026-10-22** it is drawn when `[screen] exit_status` is on: a disc, a cross or a ring in a strip of its own beside the prompt's row, for success, failure and a cancelled command, nothing for a command not reported ended, read each frame from the prompts on the window shown (`Term::prompts_from`), so a mark keeps to its line through scrolling, a reflow and the history, and none on the alternate screen (`a_mark_shows_each_status_the_shell_sent`, `a_mark_follows_its_line_through_the_history_and_a_reflow`, `the_alternate_screen_has_no_marks`, `the_marks_differ_without_their_colours`). The prompts are kept by line, follow a re-cut, and are forgotten on the history lines `CSI 3 J` erases, all of them at `RIS`, and when a prompt is drawn above them, as after a clear, the primary's alone (`a_clear_takes_the_prompts_it_erased`, `a_jump_lands_on_its_prompt_after_a_reflow`, `erasing_the_history_forgets_its_prompts`, `a_full_log_forgets_its_oldest_prompts`); `Term::jump_to_prompt` moves the window to one (`a_jump_goes_to_the_previous_and_the_next_prompt`). kitty's `C;cmdline=` and `cmdline_url=` are unread: nothing in baud shows a command line. The zone the marks open is acted on by `Term::resize`, which does not re-cut the rows a shell will redraw, and counted in `semantic_marks`. **Each screen has its own**: the zone and the block start are swapped by `?1049` as the kitty stacks are, the alternate screen's start fresh on each entry, and the primary's zone alone decides whether the primary re-cuts (`a_mark_on_the_alternate_screen_does_not_redraw_the_primarys_block`, `a_block_opened_on_the_alternate_screen_is_not_redrawn_on_the_primary`, `a_mark_on_the_alternate_screen_does_not_stop_the_primary_re_cutting`). Since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps the window over the history (note V, V2): a redraw mark whose block retreats over the history sends a window it reaches to the live screen at the mark's byte (`a_take_back_that_reaches_the_window_sends_it_live_at_that_byte`). **Since 2026-10-24, with `[screen] prompt_at_bottom` on, a prompt lands on the window's last row**: once the shell has drawn it, at its input mark (`B` or `I`), on the primary with no scrolling region or side margins, the cursor walks down over the blank rows directly below it, the rows from the block's first to the cursor's moving with it as under `IL`, so a prompt of any height lands with its last line on the last row and output stays where it was written, except that a window shrunk at the prompt sends its top rows into the history, as it does with the setting off for any cursor on the last row, and a growth brings them back; a prompt with no `B` or `I` is not moved, rather than moving the output down onto the prompt or drawing the page bottom-aligned; the proposal's `A` asks only a fresh-line, so this is the embedder's choice, off by default. The block start, the input, the prompt log and a selection follow the rows; turned on at a prompt it pins that prompt at once. Recorded bash and zsh hold it (`a_prompt_lands_on_the_last_row_only_with_the_setting_on`, `a_growth_at_a_prompt_keeps_it_on_the_last_row`, `a_zoom_gesture_leaves_one_prompt_on_the_last_row`, `a_tall_prompt_keeps_the_output_through_a_zoom_at_its_largest`, `a_two_line_prompt_lands_whole_and_moves_no_output`, `a_two_line_prompt_redrawn_moves_no_output`, `an_input_mark_at_the_end_of_the_line_pins_as_b_does`, `nothing_but_a_prompt_mark_on_the_primary_moves_the_cursor`, `turning_it_on_at_a_prompt_pins_that_prompt_at_once`) **Since 2026-10-31 baud marks the prompts of the shells it starts** (`[shell] integration`, on by default; `baud-app/src/shell.rs`), so a stock shell's latest output is finished text like any marked shell's: zsh through `ZDOTDIR`, bash through `--posix` and `ENV` and fish 3 through `-C`, each startup handed back to the user's own files at once. Each prompt gets a `D` for the command before it, with its status, and an `A;aid=<pid>`, with the `aid` the text suggests: in zsh both from the last `precmd` hook, in bash the `D` from the first `PROMPT_COMMAND` entry and the `A` from the last, in fish 3 the `D` from `fish_postexec` and the `A` from the `fish_prompt` event. zsh and bash get `P;k=i` and `B` around `PS1` and `P;k=s` and `B` around `PS2`, so every redraw of the prompt carries them, and all three a `C` as a command starts; a prompt no command ran from gets its `D`, as the text has a cancelled input end. **An open departure: a fish 3 prompt has no `B`**, which the text says a prompt "must end with": fish 3 draws its prompt from the user's own `fish_prompt`, and a function of baud's in its place is what `funcsave` would then write over the user's file, so baud leaves it alone and does not know where fish 3's input begins (`funcsave_saves_the_users_own_fish_prompt`, `a_fish_prompt_reads_the_status_as_without_baud`). fish 4 sends its own and gets none, and startup files that already mark the prompt keep theirs (`a_stock_zsh_paragraph_is_drawn_at_its_words`, `zsh_exit_codes_reach_the_marks`, `a_bash_completion_list_stays`, `a_zsh_that_marks_keeps_its_own`, `zsh_startup_runs_as_without_baud`). |
| `OSC 9 ; Pt` | iTerm2's notification | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can post a notification**: "OSC 9 ; [Message content goes here] ST" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the whole body the message, shown as a desktop notification while the window is not focused, since no text says when: through the notification center on macOS, from the app's bundle and once you allow it, and the session bus's notification server on Linux; at most four at once and then one a second (`the_bound_lets_four_through_then_one_a_second`), and none with `[screen] notifications = "off"`. A body in one of ConEmu's forms is ConEmu's (its own rows), so `9 ; 9 ; dir` at a prompt posts nothing (ghostty reads the same, kitty posts it). `osc_9_posts_its_message`, `conemus_other_forms_post_nothing` |
| `OSC 9 ; 4 ; st ; pr` | ConEmu's progress | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can show how far along it is**: "0: remove progress ... 1: set progress value to pr (number, 0-100) ... 2: set error state ... pr is optional ... 3: set indeterminate state ... 4: set paused state, pr is optional" (`[[source: conemu-ansi-escape-codes]]`), and iTerm2's bare `OSC 9 ; 4` that clears it; a pause with no `pr` keeps the percentage shown (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]` 3.7), a percentage past 100 is 100, as Windows Terminal clamps it (`[[source: wt-adapt-dispatch]]`). A state no text defines, or a `pr` that is no number, changes nothing and is counted. | `OSC 99 ; metadata ; payload` | kitty's desktop notifications | **partial** — since 2026-10-21, **a program can post a notification with a title, a body, buttons and an icon, update it, close it and ask what baud shows** (`[[source: kitty-desktop-notifications]]`). **One gap is open.** On macOS a program that asked is told `p=close ; untracked` when the post is made, before permission is known; if permission is then refused, it was told of a notification that never showed. Neither the token nor the compositor's answer can be known when a program asks, so Linux still claims `report` alone, and it and the buttons only where the server lists `actions` (`support_claims_what_the_platform_delivers`). On macOS, which does not say when one closes, `p=alive` names what Notification Center still holds, read every two seconds while any is shown (`the_macos_driver_keeps_alive_true`); on Linux the server's `NotificationClosed` takes it out. The query goes unanswered on Linux until the session bus's notification server answers, and with none, and on macOS outside the app's bundle or once you refuse permission. The query `p=?` is answered with what the embedder says it delivers (`Term::set_notice_support`) and nothing else, in order with the read's other replies, and not at all while it has said nothing or notifications are off (`the_query_answers_what_the_embedder_delivers`), `buttons` among its payloads only where they are shown (`the_query_claims_buttons_only_where_they_are_shown`); a click reports only under `a=report` (`a_click_reports_only_when_asked`), a close under `c=1`, `untracked` where the platform cannot tell (`closes_are_reported_tracked_or_untracked`). Refused and counted (`Term::notice_refusals`): no area, no support, malformed metadata -- a key not one letter, or a value outside the text's character set with the `=` its base64 values pad with and the `?` of its own `p=?` -- a chunk or an id too long, `e=1` that is not base64, a notification with nothing in it, more than eight in one read |

It is drawn over the cells and the images and under baud's own overlays, the find field, the link preview's bar, the thumb and the fade, as kitty draws it before its scrollbar and hyperlink target (1983-1988), on all three paths alike (`a_coloured_bell_draws_its_colour_over_every_pixel`, `a_coloured_bell_draws_alike_on_every_arm`, `the_bell_is_drawn_first_of_the_overlays`), and mixed in the frame's blend: in linear light under the linear blend, as kitty's layered path mixes it, and in encoded numbers under the default, a departure in how the colour mixes and not in its alpha (`under_linear_blending_the_tint_mixes_in_linear_light`). With no colour set, an empty value or a reset, the bell is the other video, as before, baud's "Automatic color selection based on current screen colors" (`an_automatic_bell_draws_the_other_video`); a program's own `?5` pair is the other video whatever the colour, since it is reverse video and not the bell (`a_pair_is_the_other_video_whatever_the_bell_colour`). Built: foreground and background are the colours `OSC 10`/`11` set, selection_background and selection_foreground `OSC 17`/`19`'s, cursor `OSC 12`'s, a number `OSC 4`'s entry, and cursor_text the text under a block cursor, drawn on all three paths over the theme's (`the_text_under_the_cursor_is_set_asked_and_reset`). A query answers the program's colour, else the configured one, else empty, "the value must be empty" for a colour with no defined value, so a selection drawn in reverse video answers selection_background= where `OSC 17 ; ?` answers the text colour (`a_selection_made_automatic_answers_empty`, `a_terminal_told_nothing_answers_empty`), and so does a selection a theme draws in each cell's own text or page, which has no one colour, while `OSC 17`/`19`, which have no empty answer, answer the default page or text in its place (`a_selection_in_each_cells_colours_answers_empty`, `a_selection_in_each_cells_colours_answers_osc_21_empty`). A query of a key baud does not know answers `?` and is not counted, as DECRQM's "not recognized" is not; a set or a reset of one is counted. **The reply is kitty's to the byte**: one `OSC 21` for a sequence's queries and none for a sequence that asks nothing, each key as sent, once, where first asked, with what the last ask found, as kitty's dict keeps them, a colour as `rgb:rr/gg/bb`, and always ended by `ESC \`, as kitty's write_escape_code_to_child ends every OSC (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-screen]]` 1728-1741; `a_query_answers_in_kittys_shape`, `a_key_asked_twice_is_answered_once_with_the_last_value`, `a_key_is_echoed_as_sent`, `the_reply_ends_in_st_whatever_ended_the_request`). An empty value is the terminal's automatic colour, reverse video for a selection and the cell's colours for the cursor and the text under it, over a configured colour (`an_automatic_colour_wins_over_the_configured_one`); the default colours take none, "Not applicable", counted. **A palette entry takes no empty value either**, since 2026-10-22: "Setting the ANSI color table colors to dynamic is not allowed" (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-color-stack]]` 82), so the record is refused and counted, the entry keeps its colour, a later query in the same sequence answers that colour, and the sequence's other records still apply (`a_palette_entry_takes_no_dynamic_value`). Until then baud followed kitty's code, which sends an entry of the sixteen back to its configured colour and gives one past them xterm's fixed colour (`[[source: kitty-window-py]]` 621-629, and kitty's own test, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-tests-screen]]` 1978-1981), a departure not followed; ghostty reads every empty value as a reset (`[[source: ghostty-f54340a-osc-kitty-color]]`), practice not followed. A bare key resets (`a_palette_entry_is_set_and_reset`). **A value is read by kitty's text**: `rgb:` scaled, `#` as the high bits, `rgbi:` clipped to 0 to 1 and then read as baud's `OSC 4` reads it, the intensities converted to device values through the sRGB profile, so rgbi:0.5 draws `bc`, where ghostty scales linearly (0.5 to `7f`, `[[source: ghostty-f54340a-color]]` 701-708), kitty's colour names, an `@` alpha checked and dropped; kitty's parser reads `rgb:` by its first two digits, refuses every `rgbi:` and takes `oklch(`, `lab(` and a comment the text does not name, its departures (`a_value_is_read_by_kittys_text`). **Under `?5` foreground and background name the colour shown in that role**, set, asked and reset, as `OSC 10`/`11` do (note V, V17), so `background=?` and `OSC 11 ; ?` never disagree; kitty's code reads its stored profile, a practice not followed (`under_reverse_video_the_colours_shown_answer`). A number names the stored entry under `?5`, as `OSC 4` does, since no entry moves under the exchange. **A sequence of any length is honoured**: routed at `21;` as `OSC 52` is and read a record at a time, so the 256 entries are set and asked in one (`every_entry_is_set_and_asked_in_one_sequence`); a record longer than `OSC_MAX` is refused and counted (`a_record_past_osc_max_is_refused_and_counted`). **Nothing changes until the terminator**, so a sequence `CAN` or an `ESC` cancels changes nothing (`a_cancelled_sequence_changes_nothing`). Only printable ASCII is kept, as kitty strips the rest before it splits (`a_byte_past_printable_ascii_is_dropped`). XTPUSHCOLORS keeps the text under the cursor with the dynamic colours (`the_colour_stack_keeps_the_text_under_the_cursor`). The number is kitty's, not DECSWT's (the DECSWT row). A full reset takes back every colour it set, the text under the cursor included, since 2026-10-25, and the visual bell's since 2026-11-01, and `DECSTR` the palette entries alone, as for the sequences each key stands for (`ris_returns_the_cursor_text_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `ris_returns_the_visual_bell_and_decstr_keeps_it`); kitty's soft reset clears every colour a program set (`do_screen_reset()`, `[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-screen]]` 209-266), practice not followed, since `DECSTR`'s list names none of them |
| `OSC 1337 ; RequestAttention=Pv` | iTerm2's attention request | done — since 2026-10-21, **a program can ask for the user's attention**: "yes to request attention by bouncing the dock icon indefinitely, once to bounce it a single time, or no to cancel a previous request" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the Dock icon bounced until baud is active, once, or the request cancelled on macOS, and the launcher's `urgent` set on Linux. Since 2026-11-01, **`fireworks` sets off fireworks at the cursor**: "If it is fireworks then fireworks explode at the cursor's location", the cursor's cell as the request arrives handed to the embedder (`Term::fireworks`), and drawn on every path -- Metal, the CPU renderer and the Linux driver -- as iTerm2 draws them (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-findcursorview]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptytextview]]`): yellow, red and white stars thrown in every direction from the cursor, four bursts a second, spinning, growing and fading out over a second, a hole of 20 points round the cursor, each later burst from where the cursor has moved, and gone a quarter second after a hold of a second from the last request. Four departures from iTerm2's practice, none from the text: iTerm2's numbers flash more than three times a second over more of the field than the general flash threshold allows, so for WCAG 2.3.1 baud throws the slow stars at 40 points a second rather than 100 and half as many fast ones, under the threshold for one show, one held by repeated requests and two at once (`bauds_fireworks_are_below_the_flash_thresholds_and_iterm2s_are_not`, `held_and_overlapping_fireworks_are_below_the_flash_thresholds`); each star is drawn over the frame where iTerm2's emitter adds the stars together first; they are drawn inside baud's window, where iTerm2's overlay spans the screens; and a window scrolled back stays where it is, as baud's `?1010` rule keeps it for any output while that mode is reset (note V, V2), where iTerm2 scrolls the cursor into view. Under Reduce Motion the stars are held still and faded out. A cursor trail lit while they burn is not drawn: it is held until it lands, as under the launch splash. iTerm2's source reads any other value by `boolValue` and has a `flash` the page does not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`); baud follows the page and counts them. `request_attention_reaches_the_embedder`, `fireworks_reach_the_embedder_at_the_cursor`, `fireworks_are_drawn_at_the_cursor_and_end_by_themselves`, `the_fireworks_play_from_a_request_before_the_first_frame_and_go`, `the_drivers_hand_the_fireworks_to_the_renderer`, `the_fireworks_draw_alike_on_every_arm` |
| `OSC 1337 ; CursorShape=N` | iTerm2: the cursor's shape | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can make the cursor a block, a bar or an underline and leave its blink alone**: "where [N]=0, 1, or 2. 0: Block 1: Vertical bar 2: Underline" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the blink kept as iTerm2's `terminalSetCursorType` keeps it; a value that is not a number is 0, as iTerm2's `intValue` reads it, "Bogusly, non-numbers are treated as 0" (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`); one outside 0 to 2 changes nothing, as in iTerm2, and is counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`). `cursor_shape_sets_the_shape_and_keeps_the_blink` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ClearScrollback` | iTerm2: erase the history | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can erase the lines behind the screen**: "To erase the scrollback history" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the erasure `CSI 3 J` makes, the screen kept. iTerm2's source clears the screen as well and keeps the prompt (`clearBufferSavingPrompt:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-terminal-delegate]]`), a departure from the page, so baud follows the page. `clear_scrollback_erases_the_history_and_keeps_the_screen` |
| `OSC 1337 ; CurrentDir=dir`, `OSC 1337 ; RemoteHost=user@host` | iTerm2: the shell's directory and its host | done — since 2026-10-22, **the window's title follows a shell that reports its folder this way too**: "Reports the current directory" and "Reports the user name and hostname", `OSC 7` being "a combination of RemoteHost and CurrentDir" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). The directory is kept as sent, with no percent-decoding, on the host the last `RemoteHost` named after its last `@` (iTerm2's split, "Windows UPN format includes an @ in the user name", its control characters dropped as there, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-mutable-state]]`), this machine until one is named or when one is empty; a `RemoteHost` alone leaves the directory unknown until the next `CurrentDir`. An empty `CurrentDir` leaves none and is counted with `OSC 7`'s refusals (`Term::directories_refused`) -- iTerm2 asks the process then, which the core cannot see -- and so is one cut at the 512-byte body, one that does not fit beside its host, and any after a host past 255 octets. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset forgets the host** and `DECSTR` keeps it (`ris_forgets_the_remote_host_and_decstr_keeps_it`). `current_dir_and_remote_host_name_the_directory` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ReportCellSize` | iTerm2: the cell size in points | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program that draws images can ask how big a cell is**: "OSC 1337 ; ReportCellSize=[height];[width];[scale] ST" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the cell's device pixels over the backing scale the embedder tells (`Term::set_backing_scale`; macOS the window's, Linux 1), each number as iTerm2 writes it, `%0.2f` with the trailing zeros trimmed to one (`17.0;8.5;2.0`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-nsstringiterm]]`), ended with the query's terminator, which the page's "ST means either BEL (hex code 0x07) or ESC \\" allows (iTerm2 always sends `ESC \`). Silent, and counted under its key, until the embedder has told both the cell and the scale: a number baud does not know would be a wrong answer. `report_cell_size_answers_in_points` |
| `OSC 1337 ; ReportVariable=base64` | iTerm2: report a session variable | done — since 2026-10-22, answered in iTerm2's shape, `ESC ] 1337 ; ReportVariable=base64` (`reportVariableNamed:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100output]]`). **A program that asks for a variable gets the empty value, so what a shell set never reaches whatever reads the terminal**: every name is answered with it, a `user.` variable a program set with `SetUserVar` included, iTerm2's answer for a variable the user has not allowed, as iTerm2 denies each until the user allows it (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-nagging]]`). So every name is answered empty, the variable stored for an embedder to read alone, `Term::user_var`. The answer would go to whatever reads the terminal then, a remote shell over ssh included. Ended with the query's terminator (iTerm2 sends BEL). A name that is not base64 of UTF-8 gets no reply, as iTerm2 sends none, and is counted under its key. `report_variable_answers_with_the_empty_value`, `set_user_var_is_kept_and_reported_empty`; the app's `a_user_variable_is_never_read_back` |
| `OSC 1337 ; Notification=title=base64;message=base64` | iTerm2: a notification | **partial** — since 2026-10-22, **a program written for iTerm2 can post a notification, shown as an `OSC 9` one is**, and since 2026-11-01 **with its subtitle and a picture from a file on your machine**: the key the pages do not name, read as iTerm2's source reads it, `key=base64` pairs split on `;`, `title`, `subtitle`, `message` and `image` decoded (`screenPostUserNotification:rich:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`), posted under `OSC 9`'s rules: `[screen] notifications`, while the window is not focused, a message alone standing as the title, an empty one refused. A value that does not decode is none, as there. **The subtitle** is the post's subtitle on macOS, as iTerm2 sets it (`notifyRich:`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-notification-controller]]`; `[[source: macos-sdk-unnotificationcontent-h]]`), and a subtitle with no title leaves the title empty, as there. Linux's `Notify` has a summary and a body and no subtitle (`[[source: freedesktop-notification-spec]]`): the subtitle opens the body as a paragraph of its own, a blank line before the message -- "Each line is a paragraph" -- as Chromium sets a notification's context line (`[[source: chromium-fc2ff62-notification-bridge-linux]]`), and with no title it is the summary, the one text argument the text does not call optional. **The picture**: `image` is a path, as iTerm2 reads it with `dataWithContentsOfFile:`, so no URL is ever fetched, its trailing white space and newlines trimmed as there, by the set the system builds: "all the whitespace and newline characters" (`[[source: apple-nscharacterset-whitespacesandnewlines]]`), the whitespace "Zs and CHARACTER TABULATION (U+0009)" (`[[source: apple-nscharacterset-whitespaces]]`), the two together Unicode's White_Space, and U+200B ZERO WIDTH SPACE, which the system's set holds on macOS 27.0 though the text does not name it. It is read only when the notification is shown, through the reader kitty's `t=f` reads through, with every check that row lists, at most 10 MB, Apple's limit for an image attachment (`[[source: apple-unnotificationattachment]]`), and never from an OSC cut at its 512 bytes, whose path, cut on a base64 quantum, would name another file (counted under the key). On macOS a copy of a PNG, JPEG or GIF that baud's own decoders read, within the pixels `[images] pixels_mb` lets an image decode to, is attached -- never the file itself, which the system moves into its store; one that does not decode is left out and the text posts, as iTerm2 posts it, where the system would not schedule a request whose attachment is "corrupted, invalid, or of an unsupported file type" (`[[source: apple-unnotificationattachment]]`), and it is counted. On Linux its bytes show as an `OSC 99` icon's do, a PNG, JPEG or GIF of at most 1024 pixels a side as `image-data`. Nothing about it goes back to the program, which gets no reply, no id and no report. A picture alone posts on macOS when it shows; Linux, with no summary to send, does not post it, and counts it. **The gap is the picture's formats**: iTerm2 converts whatever its sandboxed decoder reads to a PNG (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-image]]`); baud's own decoders read PNG, JPEG and GIF, the three the system attaches, and it has no sandboxed one for the rest, so another format is not shown on either platform; and a file baud's decoders read that the system's do not, such as a JPEG cut short, which baud's decoder shows as far as it arrived, may still be refused by the system on macOS, its text with it, and on macOS the picture is checked by decoding it whole on the main thread, so a 24-megapixel photo near the default `[images] pixels_mb` holds the window for about a quarter of a second while it is read (218 to 276 ms measured for a 6000x4000 JPEG of 3.25 MB). `iterm2_notification_posts_its_title_and_message`, `an_iterm2_subtitle_and_picture_reach_the_macos_post`, `an_iterm2_picture_that_does_not_decode_is_left_out_on_macos`, `an_iterm2_picture_is_read_for_linux_and_a_bare_one_is_not_posted` (Linux's summary), `the_drivers_drop_and_log_what_delivery_counts` (Linux's drop and count), `an_iterm2_subtitle_opens_the_body_or_stands_as_the_summary` |
| `OSC 1337 ; UnicodeVersion=n` | iTerm2: which Unicode version widths follow | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program written for older width tables can ask for them, and an emoji Unicode 9 widened takes one column again**: "iTerm2 by default uses Unicode 9's width tables. Nine is baud's own tables, Unicode 17's, which iTerm2's "9" tables are generated from too (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-character-width]]`); eight is East Asian Width 8.0's wide and ambiguous sets (`[[source: eaw-data-8]]`), with the unassigned code points its header defaults to wide and zero width unchanged. A regional indicator is 8.0's one column, and a flag, two of them, is widened to two columns where they join, wrapping whole at the right margin, as iTerm2's source draws it: "A flag is a pair of regional indicators. It occupies two columns no matter how fullWidthFlags and the Unicode version are set ... A lone indicator is not a flag and keeps the width those settings give it" (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screenchar]]`). Keeping a lone indicator wide too would depart from it, so the text is followed; `[text] ambiguous_width` applies under either. 0 is the default. A push keeps the version under its label, the text after the first space; a pop takes entries off until one has its label, or the top one with none, and restores that version, as iTerm2's `popUnicodeVersion:` does, so a label no entry has empties the stack and changes nothing. Cells already written keep their widths. Built whole, as baud builds a protocol. | `OSC 1337 ; File=args, MultipartFile, FilePart, FileEnd` | iTerm2: inline images and file downloads (`[[source: iterm2-images]]`) | **partial** — since 2026-10-22, **`imgcat` and every program written for iTerm2's pictures shows a PNG in the terminal**: "ESC ] 1337 ; File = [optional arguments] : base-64 encoded file contents ^G", and its multipart form, `MultipartFile`, `FilePart`s and `FileEnd`, iTerm2's own from 3.5. The base64 streams into the PNG scratch from the `:`, and from each `FilePart`'s `=`, so a picture of any size up to the scratch arrives whole across reads. `inline=1` shows it; "width ... height ... N: N character cells. Npx: N pixels. N%: N percent of the session's width or height. auto: The image's inherent size", and `preserveAspectRatio`, "it will fill the specified width and height as much as possible without stretching", sized by iTerm2's own arithmetic in device pixels, one image pixel a device pixel for `auto` as its Retina default draws (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-inline-image-helper]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-image-info]]`): the picture drawn from its cells' top-left; wider than the columns right of the cursor, the whole screen's width with the height scaled, as iTerm2 does; taller than 255 rows, capped and widened as iTerm2 does. Placed at the cursor, a line feed before each row after the first, the cells' text erased as iTerm2's image characters replace it, the cursor right of the picture on its last row. The arithmetic decoder is T.81's own procedures, which decode its K.4.1 test sequence to its bits (`the_k41_test_sequence_decodes_to_its_decisions`), and libjpeg-turbo's arithmetic test image decodes to the bytes of its Huffman twin (`[[source: libjpeg-turbo-314]]`; `the_arithmetic_test_image_decodes_to_its_reference`, `every_arithmetic_form_decodes_to_its_reference`). **A sample of more than 8 bits draws at its high eight, as a 16-bit PNG's does**, its colour turned at its own precision first, and a lossless one of fewer is scaled to the full range, as a sub-byte PNG grey is (`twelve_bit_samples_draw_at_their_high_eight_bits`, `every_lossless_predictor_decodes_to_the_source`, `every_lossless_precision_decodes_to_its_reference`, `a_difference_of_half_the_range_decodes`). libjpeg-turbo writes no arithmetic-coded or subsampled lossless file, so those are Thomas Richter's T.81 implementation's (`[[source: richter-libjpeg]]`; `arithmetic_lossless_decodes_to_the_source`, `a_subsampled_lossless_scan_decodes_to_its_reference`); a lossless component halved across is upsampled as a DCT one is, by the IJG's triangle filter, as that implementation upsamples a lossless frame the way it upsamples a DCT one, where libjpeg-turbo repeats each sample. Anything else -- PDF, a text file iTerm2 would set as a document -- and data that is not base64 draw iTerm2's broken-image placeholder at the size asked, 35 by 36 pixels for `auto`: baud's own, a frame and its diagonals in the foreground colour, iTerm2's picture being GPL-2.0. Counted under the key, as is a file past the scratch, refused whole. Two more gaps: a `File=` header longer than the 512-byte body is never routed and is counted, and a change of cell size leaves a picture at its old pixel size, where iTerm2 redraws its image cells at the new size. `an_inline_png_is_placed_at_its_own_size`, `a_width_in_cells_resamples_with_the_aspect`, `a_multipart_file_is_the_same_picture`, `a_download_and_a_file_past_the_scratch_are_refused`, `what_does_not_decode_draws_the_placeholder`, `the_cells_are_the_pictures_and_text_clears_one`, `a_file_and_a_kitty_png_take_the_scratch_in_turn`, `an_inline_jpeg_is_placed_as_it_decodes`, `a_jpeg_process_not_built_draws_the_placeholder`, `the_exif_orientation_turns_the_picture`, `a_malformed_file_never_panics`, `a_malformed_arithmetic_wide_or_lossless_file_never_panics`, `an_inline_gif_plays_its_frames`, `a_gif_frame_waiting_for_input_moves_on_a_key`, `a_gif_frame_waiting_for_input_moves_on_a_press`, `a_gif_frame_waiting_for_input_moves_on_any_press`, `a_gif_that_ends_on_a_zero_delay_ends_showing_it`, `text_over_an_animated_gif_clears_every_frame`, `a_gif_too_big_for_the_memory_is_refused_whole`, `a_gif_plain_text_extension_is_counted`, `imagemagicks_streams_decode_to_its_reference`, `a_malformed_stream_never_panics`. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset abandons a file in parts**, its parts and `FileEnd` after it counted with none open, and `DECSTR` keeps it open (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105; `ris_abandons_a_file_in_parts_and_decstr_keeps_it`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; Capabilities` | iTerm2: report the terminal's features as a feature string (`[[source: iterm2-feature-reporting]]`) | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can ask what baud does in one question and get a true answer**: "OSC 1337 ; Capabilities ST. The terminal will respond with: OSC 1337 ; Capabilities = {FeatureString} ST", the codes in the table's encoding and in the order iTerm2's encoder writes them (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100output]]`), ended with `ESC \`. The two pages conflict on `ST`: this one defines it as "a synonym for `ESC 0x5c`", where the escape-codes page has "`ST` means either `BEL` (hex code 0x07) or `ESC \`" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), and iTerm2's source ends this report with BEL. `ESC \` is the one ending both pages allow, so the text is followed; always sending BEL would be a departure. Each feature is claimed only while baud does it: `T3`, both 24-bit forms; `M`, the mouse modes with `?1006`; `Sc7`, DECSCUSR 1 to 6 and 0 for the configured style; `Ts3`, title stacks and titles; `B`, `F`, `Gs`, `Go`, `Sy`, `H` and `P`, bracketed paste, focus reports, strikethrough and overline drawn, synchronised output, hyperlinks and progress; `U` while UTF-8 is the input, since it "Indicates if UTF-8 is accepted", and not after `ESC % @` selects ISO 8859-1, as claiming it always would be a departure; `Lr` at Level 4 alone, where DECSLRM is executed; `Cw` while `[clipboard] osc52` writes reach a clipboard; `No` while notifications are delivered; `Sx` while the Primary DA claims Sixel; `Aw` with `[text] ambiguous_width` wide; `Uw17`, or `Uw8` after `UnicodeVersion=8`, and none under ISO 8859-1, its 0 being "No unicode support is claimed". `FILE` while image memory is provisioned, since 2026-10-23, when JPEG began to draw: "the OSC 1337 File protocol for transfer/display of files such as images or video, including those using lossy compression", a second `F` after `Sx` as iTerm2's encoder writes it; its code is `FOCUS_REPORTING`'s too, so a reader saw an `F` before, and programs read it as inline display (`[[source: grok-build-2bdd1d6-terminal-image]]`), while downloads stay refused (while PNG alone drew, it was not claimed). `capabilities_report_what_baud_does_now`, `features_encode_in_iterm2s_order` |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetMark` | iTerm2: a mark to jump back to | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can mark a line, and Cmd-Up and Cmd-Down stop at it as they stop at the shell's prompts**: "The Set Mark (cmd-shift-M) command allows you to record a location and then jump back to it later ... The following escape code has the same effect as that command" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). The mark is the cursor's line, put among the prompts in line order and erasing none, with no exit status, so no mark is drawn beside it; a line already in the log keeps its entry, and a prompt redrawn over the mark's row takes it with the row. iTerm2's source reads one argument, `saveScrollPosition`, as "the entire region of visible lines" (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-terminal-delegate]]`): it marks the window's top line, so the jump, which puts a mark at the window's top, shows the region as it was; any other argument is the cursor's line. On the alternate screen, which keeps no history and where a jump does nothing, it is counted under its key and marks nothing. `set_mark_is_a_line_the_prompt_jumps_stop_at`, `a_mark_goes_in_line_order_and_reports_nothing`, `a_mark_in_a_full_log_forgets_the_oldest` |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetUserVar=name=base64` | iTerm2: a user variable | done — since 2026-10-22, **a shell or script can leave a value for later, as iTerm2's shell integration does**: "Sets the value of a user-defined variable ... [Ps1] is the key. [Ps2] is the base64-encoded value" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). The variable is `user.<name>`; a bare name, or a value that is not base64 of UTF-8, unsets it, and a name with a `.` is refused, "which is not allowed", as iTerm2's `UserVariableAssignment` reads it (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-user-variable]]`). An embedder reads it (`Term::user_var`); `ReportVariable` answers it with the empty value. Sixteen are kept, each name to 64 bytes: a seventeenth name, a longer name and a message cut at the 512-byte body are refused and counted under the key, never another variable evicted. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset clears them** and `DECSTR` keeps them: a terminal made operational holds none (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105; `ris_clears_the_user_variables_and_decstr_keeps_them`). `set_user_var_is_kept_and_reported_empty`, `user_vars_past_the_table_are_refused_and_counted` |
| `OSC 1337 ; Copy=:base64` | iTerm2: copy to the pasteboard | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can put text on the clipboard this way too**: "the base64-encoded string to copy to the pasteboard" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), the general one, through `OSC 52`'s path and under its rules: streamed past the 512-byte body from the `:` on, refused whole past the area, put on the pasteboard as an `OSC 52` write to `c` is. Data that is not base64 copies nothing, as iTerm2's decode leaves it nothing to copy, and is counted under its key. **A copy from inside tmux reaches the clipboard too**: iTerm2's segmented copy, which the pages do not name and its own `it2copy` sends whenever `TERM` is tmux's -- `Copy=2;uid`, a `Copy=3;uid:base64` for each line of the base64, `Copy=4;uid` (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) -- is built: the pieces are decoded into the area as they come, whatever output falls between them, and copied at the end; a piece under another `uid`, or a `Copy=2` or `3` with none, abandons it, and an `OSC 52` or a whole `Copy=:` while it is open, which takes the same area, abandons it too, each counted. `copy_puts_the_text_on_the_clipboard`, `a_segmented_copy_is_copied_at_its_end` |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetColors=key=value` | iTerm2: change a colour | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program written for iTerm2 can recolour the text, the page, the cursor and the text under it, the selection, bold text and the sixteen ANSI colours**: each `key=value` of a list split on `,`, as iTerm2's source splits it, sets the colour `OSC 10`, `11`, `12`, `17`, `19`, `OSC 5 ; 0` and `OSC 4` set for `fg`, `bg`, `curbg`, `selbg`, `selfg`, `bold` and `black` to `br_white`, and their resets undo it (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`). "RGB (three hex digits)" and "RRGGBB", alone (sRGB) or after `srgb:`, `p3:` or `rgb:`; P3 and `rgb` -- macOS's Generic RGB, what iTerm2's source makes of "the device-specific color space" -- converted to sRGB as a theme file's are. `curfg` sets the text under a block cursor, as `OSC 21 ; cursor_text` does. `badge` sets the badge's colour, opaque, as iTerm2's source makes a hex colour (`set_colors_sets_the_badge_colour`). `link`, `underline`, `tab`, `preset` and the source's `match` name colours baud keeps none of, and each such pair is counted, as an `OSC 4` pair baud cannot honour is; so is a pair off the form and the source's `i:N`, a binding that tracks a palette entry from then on, which baud has none of. A colour space none of the three, which the page says a space "should be one of", is read as sRGB, as iTerm2's source reads it in its default space. `set_colors_sets_each_colour_named`. A full reset takes back every colour it set, the badge's and the text under the cursor included, since 2026-10-25, and `DECSTR` the palette entries and `bold` alone, as for `OSC 4` and `OSC 5` (`ris_returns_the_cursor_text_and_decstr_keeps_it`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; HighlightCursorLine=yes|no` | iTerm2: the cursor guide | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can highlight the row the cursor is on**, and `[cursor] guide = "on"` does it for every program: "This shows or hides the cursor guide" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), "a horizontal rule that indicates the vertical position of the cursor" (`[[source: iterm2-doc-profiles-colors]]`). The value is read as iTerm2 reads it, `value.length ? [value boolValue] : YES` (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`): none is yes, and any other is `NSString`'s `boolValue` as swift-corelibs-foundation scans it (`[[source: swift-corelibs-28249ce-nsstring]]`, `[[source: swift-corelibs-28249ce-scanner]]`; `bool_value_reads_as_foundation_scans`). What a program set holds until `RIS`, which returns the setting's, as iTerm2's `screenDidReset` sets the profile's again; `DECSTR` keeps it, as iTerm2's `softReset` does (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`; `highlight_cursor_line_shows_and_hides_the_guide`). It is drawn as iTerm2 draws it: iTerm2's default colour, calibrated RGB (0.650, 0.910, 1.000) at 0.25 (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-profile-preferences]]`), over the frame's width one cell high, with a line one point high at the row's top and bottom filled twice (`[[source: iterm2-7ce037e-text-drawing-helper]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-cursor-guide-renderer]]`), over the backgrounds and the images under the text and under the text and the cursor (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-metal-driver]]`), a block cursor's cell left out, on all three drawing paths (`the_guide_fills_the_cursor_row_under_the_text`, `the_guide_moves_on_incremental_damage`, the pixel oracle's guide scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`, `the_cursor_guide_is_off_unless_turned_on`). It hides with the cursor (`?25 l`), as iTerm2's drawing without Metal, `iTermTextDrawingHelper`, does; its Metal path draws it over a hidden cursor's row, which baud does not follow. While the window looks back into the history it stays on the cursor's line, lowered with the scroll, for as long as that line is in view, as both of iTerm2's paths draw it (`drawCursrGuideIfNeededInRect`, iTerm2's spelling, `[[source: iterm2-7ce037e-text-drawing-helper]]`; `loadCursorInfoWithDrawingHelper`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-metal-per-frame-state]]`), and on the status line's row while DECSASD puts the cursor there (`the_guide_hides_with_the_cursor`, the pixel oracle's look-back guide scene). `[cursor] guide` is off by default, as iTerm2's profile has it (`KEY_USE_CURSOR_GUIDE`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-profile-preferences]]`). A trail's flash cap counts the guide over the page as a ground (`a_repeat_pass_over_the_cursor_guide_is_capped_against_it`). Open: the colour is iTerm2's default, with no setting of its own, and the guide reaches the renderer's frame, not the window's padding beyond it |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetBadgeFormat=base64` | iTerm2: the session badge (`[[source: iterm2-badges]]`) | done — since 2026-10-22, **a program can put a large label in the top right of the window, behind the text**, such as the host or the branch, and `[screen] badge = "off"` hides it (on by default, as iTerm2 draws every badge a program sets). The format is decoded as iTerm2's `screenSetBadgeFormat` decodes it and kept, and one that is not base64 of UTF-8 or that calls a function is refused and counted under its key, the badge left as it was, as iTerm2 refuses it (`set_badge_format_keeps_the_format_and_refuses_a_call`). **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset clears it and its colour**, which iTerm2's reset keeps (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`), and `DECSTR` keeps both: the state at power-on has neither (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105; `ris_clears_the_badge_and_its_colour_and_decstr_keeps_them`). It is an interpolated string, split into its text and the expressions in `\(…)`, those nested in an expression's strings included, and its text's eleven escapes expanded, as iTerm2's documentation gives them (`[[source: iterm2-doc-scripting-fundamentals]]`), `\u` also taking white space before its four characters or a `0x` in them, which the documentation does not cover (`a_format_splits_into_text_and_expressions`, `the_documented_strings_split_as_documented`, `the_documented_nesting_is_one_expression`, `escapes_expand_as_iterm2_expands_them`, `each_documented_escape_expands`, `a_format_evaluates_part_by_part`, `the_documented_strings_evaluate_as_documented`). A call is seen wherever the documentation's syntax puts one, in a nested expression of any length too: a call is refused at any depth, and a format that calls nothing in either reading is accepted however deeply it nests (`a_function_call_is_seen_where_iterm2_runs_one`, `the_documented_calls_are_seen_and_the_rest_are_not`, `a_long_or_deep_nesting_is_read_to_its_end`, `the_call_check_reads_every_depth_a_format_can_reach`). The split follows sixteen `\(` deep inside strings and reads one deeper as its string's text, a departure, since the documentation sets no limit; a call the split reads is refused too, but for one sixteen levels deep after a `\(` the split reads as text, where the two readings part and the reading of every depth decides. **Since 2026-11-01 a badge computes its text as iTerm2's does**, such as `\(user.env == "prod" ? "PRODUCTION" : "dev")` or `\(rows * columns) cells`: each expression is read on the documentation's grammar -- numbers, strings with expressions of their own, arrays, `path[index]`, paths to variables, and the operators from the ternary down to unary `!` and `-` at the documented precedence (`[[source: iterm2-doc-scripting-fundamentals]]`) -- and where the page is silent it takes iTerm2's values (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-subexpression]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-expression-evaluator]]`): a string that reads as a number counts as one, `+` joins no strings, a comparison is 1 or 0, null orders as nothing, and a number is written as `NSNumber` writes a double, `%0.16g` (`[[source: apple-nsnumber-stringvalue]]`; `the_documented_expressions_evaluate_as_documented`, `the_operators_bind_in_the_documented_order`, `values_are_written_as_iterm2_writes_them`, `each_kind_meets_each_operator_as_iterm2_has_it`, `names_and_numbers_are_read_as_iterm2_reads_them`, `a_nested_string_is_interpolated`, `the_deepest_nesting_a_format_can_hold_is_read`). A reference to an undefined variable standing alone is empty, as iTerm2's lax policy makes it, and one an operator is given fails the badge unless written with `?`, as iTerm2's code and tests read that policy (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-advanced-settings]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-expression-parser-tests]]`; `an_undefined_variable_alone_is_empty_and_an_operand_fails`); a badge whose expression fails shows a bug and the reason, in baud's words, as iTerm2 shows its own, and one naming itself shows `[Cycle detected]` (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`; `a_fault_fails_the_string_and_no_expression_is_text`, `a_cycle_and_a_failure_draw_as_iterm2_draws_them`). The variables are the Variables reference's (`[[source: iterm2-doc-variables]]`): a user variable as `SetUserVar` kept it, `columns`, `rows`, `name` and `presentationName` (the window's title as shown), `terminalWindowName`, `terminalIconName`, `jobName`, `jobPid`, `pid`, `tty`, `path` (the last `OSC 7` or `CurrentDir`, else the foreground's folder), `hostname` and `username` (the host and user the last `RemoteHost` or `OSC 7` named, `RemoteHost` split at its last `@` as iTerm2 splits it, each carried from the message before as iTerm2's `setHost` carries it: `user@` keeps the host named before and a host alone the user, each empty before any, and an `OSC 7` naming a host keeps the user for the same host, its ASCII case aside, and is no user for another; before any, this machine's user, `$USER`, and its name as `gethostname` gives it, where iTerm2 starts with `NSUserName()` and `[NSHost fullyQualifiedDomainName]`, which can differ, such as `name.local` against a name the DNS gives; `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-screen-mutable-state]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`), `homeDirectory` (`$HOME`), `shell` (the name of the login shell baud starts when no command is given, `$SHELL`'s else the system's, by its last path component as iTerm2 names the user's shell), `uname` (the system's name as `uname` gives it, as iTerm2's is without ssh integration), `creationTimeString` (when the session started, as iTerm2 writes it, `yyyyMMdd_HHmmss` in local time), `mouseReportingMode`, `applicationKeypad`, `bellCount`, `selection`, `selectionLength`, `sshIntegrationLevel` (0), `iterm2.pid` and `iterm2.localhostName`, each also under `session.`; a tmux or trigger variable is undefined, as iTerm2's is without the feature, and every other -- a profile's, `lastCommand`, `autoLogId`, which iTerm2 sets for every session, a tab's or a window's -- is drawn as an undefined one is and counted (`ItermCounts::badge_unevaluated`; `the_badge_draws_a_user_variable_a_program_set`, `every_listed_variable_is_answered_as_the_table_says`, `the_session_variables_are_the_sessions_facts`, `a_session_starts_with_this_machines_facts`, `the_creation_time_is_written_as_iterm2_writes_it`, `the_host_user_and_path_are_the_last_named`, `the_session_host_and_user_carry_as_iterm2_carries_them`, `a_badge_naming_what_baud_cannot_answer_is_counted`). Departures: strings are ordered by code point, where Foundation holds canonically equivalent ones equal; a path takes no white space around its dots; an expression nested past 512 levels, deeper than a badge baud keeps can nest, is unreadable; `creationTimeString` is in the Gregorian calendar, where iTerm2's formatter, given none, takes the user's (`[[source: apple-dateformatter-calendar]]`); `shell` stays the shell baud starts when `ShellIntegrationVersion` names another, which iTerm2 takes, since baud does not act on that key; `shell` is `$SHELL`'s, where iTerm2 asks `iTermOpenDirectory` for the user's; an `OSC 7` naming the host before keeps its user when the two differ by ASCII case alone, where iTerm2's `caseInsensitiveCompare:` folds the case of any letter; an `OSC 7` naming `localhost` names no host, as RFC 8089 reads it (`[[source: rfc8089]]`), where iTerm2 records it as the host; and an `OSC 7` baud refuses -- another scheme, a user or a query in the URI -- names no host, where iTerm2 reads the host of any URL. It is laid out as iTerm2's `iTermBadgeLabel` lays it out (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-badge-label]]`): split at its newlines, wrapped where Unicode's line breaking algorithm allows (`[[source: uax14]]`, built for it; `every_line_break_test_case_breaks_where_it_says`), right-aligned in a box half the window wide and a fifth high, at the largest whole point size from 4 to 100 that fits, by iTerm2's search, 30 points from the top and 10 from the right (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-advanced-settings]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-indicators-helper]]`; `a_badge_wraps_as_word_wrapping_does`, `the_size_is_the_largest_that_fits`, `the_badge_sits_in_the_top_right_under_the_text`). It is drawn in the embedded bold face whatever font the text is in, as iTerm2's badge has a face of its own, `KEY_BADGE_FONT` (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-profile-preferences]]`; a configured font does not change it; `the_badge_is_drawn_in_its_own_face`, `the_badge_face_is_never_the_texts`, `every_stack_draws_the_badge_in_the_embedded_bold`), in iTerm2's default red at the quarter iTerm2 draws it at (`the_badge_colour_is_iterm2s_default_in_srgb`), or in the colour `SetColors=badge=` sets, opaque as iTerm2 makes it (`set_colors_sets_the_badge_colour`, `the_badge_is_the_programs_format_evaluated`), under the text and over the backgrounds on all three drawing paths, leaving out a drawn block cursor's cell and an input method's composition as iTerm2 draws them over it (`the_badge_leaves_the_cursor_and_a_composition_alone`, the pixel oracle's badge scene in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`, `the_badge_changes_on_incremental_damage`, `the_badge_keeps_two_sizes_in_the_atlas`, `the_badge_is_on_unless_turned_off`); a trail meeting it draws no repeat pass (`a_repeat_pass_over_the_badge_draws_nothing`). **Since 2026-11-01 the badge is outlined as iTerm2 outlines it**: each glyph stroked in the background colour -- `OSC 11`'s, else the theme's, which reverse video does not swap (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptytextview]]`, `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-color-map]]`) -- opaque inside the alpha the badge is drawn at, a line 2% of the point size wide, as `NSStrokeWidthAttributeName: @-2` asks (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-badge-label]]`, `[[source: apple-ct-stroke-width]]`), centred on the glyph's outline, with miter joins bevelled past a limit of 10 and each contour joined at its start, as Quartz strokes a path (`[[source: apple-quartz2d-paths]]`; `a_stroke_straddles_the_outline_and_a_square_corner_is_mitred`, `a_corner_past_the_miter_limit_is_bevelled`, `a_contour_strokes_alike_either_way_round`, `the_badge_is_outlined_in_the_background_colour`), on all three drawing paths alike (the outlined badge in the pixel oracle's badge scene, the CPU path's frame of it included, in `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`). A colour glyph is not outlined, as CoreText strokes none, bitmap or `COLR`: drawn with a stroke width of -2, Apple Color Emoji's 😀 and Bungee Tint's `AB` at 100 points change no pixel, where JetBrains Mono's `AB` changes 1446, and it is drawn inside the alpha the badge is drawn at, as iTerm2 draws the whole label inside it, on every path alike (`a_colour_glyph_is_drawn_inside_the_badges_alpha`, and the colour glyph in the oracle's badge scene). And on a DECDWL or DECDHL line the hole the badge leaves for a composition is two cells wide for each of its cells, as the composition is drawn two wide there (`the_badge_leaves_the_cursor_and_a_composition_alone`) |
| `OSC 104` | reset palette | done — **every entry named is reset**, since 2026-10-18: "Any number of c parameters may be given", and with none "the entire table will be reset" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); one that is no number ends the list, as xterm's `ResetAnsiColorRequest` stops (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3050-3083), and 256 to 260 reset the special colours, as do no parameters at all, xterm's reset walking its 261 colours (`osc_104_resets_every_entry_it_names`, `osc_104_resets_the_palette`, `osc_105_and_104_reset_the_special_colours`). **A number past 260 is passed over and counted, and the list goes on**, since 2026-10-19, as xterm's `ResetOneAnsiColor` ignores it (3036-3046): `104;1;999;2` resets 1 and 2 (`osc_104_goes_on_past_a_number_out_of_range`); baud stopped there before. **An index is its digits, however many**, in `OSC 4` and `OSC 104` alike: `0001` is entry 1, as xterm's `atoi` and `strtol` read it (3001, 3064), where three digits were the most baud read before; a sign or a space is no number, the grammar's `c` being digits, where xterm's readers skip them (`an_index_with_leading_zeros_is_its_number`). A list of more than one entry was refused and counted before |
| `OSC 5`, `OSC 105`, `OSC 6` / `OSC 106` | xterm's special colours (bold, underline, blink, reverse, italic) and their modes | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can show bold, underlined, blinking, reversed or italic text in a colour of its own instead**, as xterm's colorBD to colorIT do. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 5 ; c ; spec -> Change Special Color Number c", any number of pairs, also "by adding the maximum number of colors ... in an OSC 4 control" (256 to 260); "Ps = 1 0 5 ; c -> Reset Special Color Number c", all with none; "Ps = 6 ; c ; f -> Enable/disable Special Color Number c ... OSC 6 is the same as OSC 1 0 6", `c` 0 to 4 the five modes and 5 colorAttrMode. A spec is read as `OSC 4` reads it, a `?` answered in the shape it was asked, `OSC 5 ; c` or `OSC 4 ; 256+c` (xterm's `ReportAnsiColorRequest`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]` 2636-2659), a colour nobody set being the text foreground, drawn and answered so, colorRV's page included; `OSC 104` with no parameter and both resets clear them, as xterm's reset of its 261 colours does, and `RIS` turns the modes off (ECMA-48 8.3.105), where xterm keeps its resources, not followed. **What a mode does**: a cell whose foreground is default, or any cell under colorAttrMode, takes the first special colour whose mode is on and whose attribute it carries, blink, then bold, underline and italic, as xterm's `MapToColorMode` orders them (`[[source: xterm-xterm-h]]`), and the attribute is no longer drawn -- no bold face, no underline, no blink, no italic -- "displayed in color or as bold characters" (xterm(1)); a reversed cell whose page is default, or any under colorAttrMode, takes colorRV as its page and is not exchanged, so its text keeps its colour on a colorRV page, as xterm draws it: `setExtendedBG` gives it colorRV (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file 1140-1150) and `ReverseOrHilite` is false for a reversed cell outside the selection while colorRVMode is on (`[[source: xterm-ptyx-h]]` 1603-1607), so `updatedXtermGC` makes no exchange; a reversed cell with a page of its own keeps its exchange outside colorAttrMode, as xterm(1)'s "displayed only when no ANSI colors have been set" reads, where xterm's `ReverseOrHilite` drops the exchange on every reversed cell, an open departure from xterm's code that follows its manual. **Applied where a rendition is resolved for drawing**, so turning a mode on recolours what is on the screen: the manual's "should be displayed in color", and xterm's own `extract_fg` maps at draw time a cell whose colours are both default (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` util.c 5680-5690), where its `setExtendedFG` bakes a cell with only a default foreground at `SGR` time (charproc.c 1110-1135). xterm's `OSC 6` handler stops at 4 and ignores colorAttrMode's 5, which ctlseqs lists; ctlseqs is followed. The `c ; f` walk is xterm's: an empty field before a `;` is 0, a `c` with no `f` or a field that is no number ends it, counted, and a `c` no mode has is passed over, counted (`osc_5_sets_and_answers_the_special_colours`, `osc_105_and_104_reset_the_special_colours`, `osc_6_and_106_turn_the_modes_on_and_off`, `the_resets_clear_the_special_colours`; the pixels' rendition in `baud-render/tests/special_colors.rs`: `a_bold_cell_takes_colorbd_and_loses_its_face`, `turning_a_mode_on_recolours_what_is_on_the_screen`, `each_attribute_gives_way_to_its_colour`, `an_explicit_colour_wins_unless_attr_mode`, `a_reversed_cell_shows_colorrv`, `an_unset_colorrv_is_the_text_foreground`). All four were counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 22 ; Pt` | pointer shape | **partial** — since 2026-10-19, **a program can change the mouse pointer**, as a text editor shows the I-beam or a game a crosshair. "Change pointer cursor shape to Pt. The parameter Pt sets the pointerShape resource. If Pt is empty, or does not match any of the standard names, xterm uses the resource's default" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`): the core keeps one of the 77 names of xterm's table, "adapted from <X11/cursorfont.h>" and matched as `strcmp` matches (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 889-975), for the embedder (`Term::pointer_shape`); an empty or unknown name is the embedder's own pointer, and an unknown one is counted. xterm's code returns on an empty `Pt` ("found no data", 4136-4160) and keeps the shape it had, where the text is followed. `RIS` returns the embedder's pointer, as ECMA-48 8.3.105's initial state, and `DECSTR` keeps it; xterm's reset keeps it, not followed (`osc_22_sets_the_pointer_shape`, `an_empty_or_unknown_osc_22_is_the_embedders_pointer`, `ris_returns_the_embedders_pointer`). **The gap**: the macOS app sets each name as the nearest of AppKit's cursors -- the I-beam for "xterm", the pointing hand for "hand1" and "hand2", the crosshair for the crosses, the open and closed hands for "fleur" and "sizing", not-allowed for "X_cursor" and "pirate", the contextual-menu cursor for "question_arrow" -- and the arrow for the rest, the resize arrows among them, AppKit's resize cursors being deprecated (`baud-app/src/pointer.rs`); a session that names no shape sets no cursor but the pointing hand over a link a click opens (since 2026-10-29, below). Whether AppKit keeps the cursor set over the window, whose view has a tracking area and no cursor rects, is unobserved: no test runs the set call. Since 2026-10-20 the Linux driver sets each name as the nearest of `cursor-shape-v1`'s shapes (`[[source: wp-cursor-shape-v1]]`), named with the pointer's last enter serial: "xterm" the text cursor, the hands the pointer, "watch" wait, "question_arrow" help, the crosses the crosshair and "plus" the cell, "fleur" move, "X_cursor" and "pirate" not-allowed, and the side, corner and double-arrow names their resize shapes, 25 names in all, the other 52 the arrow; with no `OSC 22`, the text cursor, xterm's `pointerShape` default (`[[source: xterm-man]]`), because Wayland leaves the image undefined until the client sets one (`[[source: wayland]]`, `wl_pointer.enter`). Since 2026-10-20 a compositor without the cursor-shape global gets a pointer baud draws itself, so the image is never left undefined, as `wl_pointer.enter` asks ("a client should respond to this event by setting an appropriate pointer image with the set_cursor request"; `[[source: wayland]]`): an I-beam for the text shapes, a pointing hand for `pointer` (since 2026-10-29) and an arrow for the rest, 24 logical pixels, GNOME's `cursor-size` default (`[[source: gnome-interface-schema-2417a27]]`), black in a white rim, drawn at the window's `preferred_buffer_scale` and set with the enter's serial; no cursor theme is read (`each_name_takes_its_nearest_shape`, `the_pointer_reports_by_frame_and_takes_its_shape`, `an_old_pointer_reports_at_once_and_no_global_draws_its_own`, `text_is_the_beam_pointer_the_hand_and_the_rest_the_arrow`). Since 2026-10-29, while the click's chord is held over a link a click opens, both apps show the platform's pointing hand over the program's shape, and the program's shape comes back when the chord is let go or the pointer leaves the link: ctlseqs orders nothing here, xterm having no links, and Ghostty and kitty do the same (`[[source: ghostty-d9840f3-surface]]`, `[[source: kitty-mouse-c-0-43-1]]`; `docs/input-coverage.md`, the link row; `the_hand_shows_while_a_click_would_open_the_link`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 60`, `OSC 61 ; Pt`, `OSC 62 ; Pt` | XTQALLOWED, XTQDISALLOWED, XTQALLOWABLE: which optional features are allowed | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can ask which of xterm's feature categories baud honours**, and gets a true answer in xterm's words. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: `OSC 60` replies "OSC 6 0 ; Pt ST where Pt is a comma-separated list of the allowed optional runtime feature categories", `OSC 61 ; cat` "the optional runtime feature subcategories which would be disallowed if the main feature is disabled", `OSC 62 ; cat` "the optional runtime feature subcategories which correspond to the main feature category". baud has no resources and no switch for any of them, so its fixed behaviour is written in xterm's own model, where a subcategory is allowed when its category is or when the category's disallowed list leaves it out (`AllowWindowOps`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`): `OSC 60` names the categories baud honours whole, `allowMouseOps` and `allowTitleOps` (the mouse since 2026-10-20, when the DEC `Locator` was built), and `allowFontOps` once the embedder has told the font with changes on; `OSC 61` names, for the others, what baud refuses -- for `allowColorOps` `SetColor` and `GetColor`, because `OSC 10` to `12`, `17` and `19` are honoured but xterm gates them under the same two as the pointer and Tektronix colours, `OSC 13` to `16` and `18`, which baud refuses (`AllocateTermColor` and `ReportColorRequest`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]` 6456 and 3572); for `allowWindowOps`, since 2026-10-21, **exactly the window operations the app does not do**: a manipulation and `SetWinLines` unless the embedder carries out every form of it (`Term::set_window_abilities`) and the window operations are on -- macOS does them all, Linux all but `RestoreWin`, `SetWinPosition`, `RaiseWin`, `LowerWin` and `MaximizeWin`, whose one-axis forms xdg-shell lacks, and, since 2026-10-21, `SetWinSizePixels` and `SetWinSizeChars` too while the compositor's `xdg_wm_base` is below version 4 or not yet bound, since `configure_bounds` is "since 4", a size in pixels is held to it and a 0 in pixels or in cells, "the display's height or width", is taken from it (`osc_61_answers_what_the_bound_xdg_wm_base_allows`) (a subcategory done in part is refused, as `SetColor` is: `SetWinSizePixels`, and `SetWinSizeChars`, whose 0 needs the bounds too), and a core no embedder told none -- a report until the fact it answers from is told (`GetWinState`, `GetWinPosition`, `GetWinSizePixels`, `GetScreenSizeChars`, each by xterm's gate; `GetWinSizeChars` always), the title reports, `GetSelection` (`OSC 52`'s read), `SetXprop` (`OSC 3`) while the window operations are switched off, `GetChecksum` while DECRQCRA is switched off, and `SetSelection` on a storage with no clipboard area, which refuses every write, or while the app's `[clipboard] osc52` is off (`Term::set_osc52_writes`), where it named every manipulation and `SetWinLines` whatever the app did and `SetSelection` only on a storage with no area (`osc_61_names_the_window_operations_the_embedder_does_not_do`, `the_window_abilities_reach_the_core_from_both_drivers`, `the_abilities_are_what_each_driver_does`); for `allowTcapOps` both of its, and for `allowFontOps` both until the embedder has told the font, and `SetFont` while `[font] changes` is off (the `OSC 50` row) -- and `OSC 62` xterm's table for the category, as a default build compiles it, and since 2026-10-20 `StatusLine` last in `allowWindowOps`', as an `OPT_STATUS_LINE` build lists it, baud having the status line, which `OSC 61` never names: with the operations off the grid gives the line its row (the DECSSDT row; `the_feature_queries_name_the_status_line_truthfully`). The paste controls are the embedder's to filter, and `OSC 60` never names `allowPasteControls`; `OSC 61 ; allowPasteControls` is empty until the embedder says what it replaces (`Term::set_paste_controls`), and since 2026-10-21 both apps say xterm's default, so it answers `NUL,EOT,ENQ,BS,ESC,DEL,STTY`, xterm's list in xterm's order (`osc_61_names_the_paste_controls_the_embedder_replaces`). Shapes are xterm's `report_allowed_ops` and `unparse_disallowed_ops` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file 10666-10722): the query's terminator, no `;` before an empty list, the category matched in any case with the alias `allowWinOps`, an empty list for a name that is no category; `OSC 60` with data and a bare `61` or `62` are answered with silence, as `do_osc` returns, and counted. The list answered is baud's own, over silence and over xterm's default values (`osc_60_names_the_categories_baud_honours_whole`, `osc_61_names_what_baud_refuses`, `osc_62_names_every_subcategory`, `a_malformed_feature_query_is_counted`, `the_longest_feature_reply_fits`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `CSI ? 1049 h/l` | alternate screen buffer | done — `?1049 h` saves the cursor "as in DECSC", switches to the alternate screen and clears it (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), and **leaves the cursor where it was**: the text names no move, and xterm's set arm is `CursorSave; ToAlternate; ClearScreen` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, 7732-7745), whose `ClearScreen` does not home (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`'s file, 2356-2383). Since 2026-10-13 the cursor is left where it is, which conforms, rather than homed as a stated departure; until then a first `?1049 h` homed, to the origin under DECOM (`entering_leaves_the_cursor`, `entering_the_alternate_screen_under_origin_mode_leaves_the_cursor`). `ClearScreen` resets the wrap (`ResetWrap`), and so does the entry: a cursor in pending wrap at the screen's edge stays in the last column with nothing pending (`entering_from_a_pending_wrap_drops_the_wrap`); one pending one past a right margin narrower than the screen is kept, the sentinel and not a placed cursor, and the next character wraps (counted by a scratch harness at all 31 read configurations of `?69h`, `CSI 1;10 s`, `CUP 9;10`, `a`, `?1049 h`, `b`). **`?1049 l` brings a pending wrap back**, since 2026-11-01, through the restore `DECRC` makes, so the next character after a full-screen program exits goes to the next line where the shell's cursor waited (`decsc_and_decrc_keep_a_pending_wrap`; the `ESC 8` row). **The save is the primary's `DECSC` slot**, since 2026-10-20, xterm's `CursorSave` into `sc[screen->whichBuf]` (`[[source: xterm-cursor-c]]`): an `ESC 7` before `?1049 h` is overwritten by it, and `ESC 8` on the primary after `?1049 l` or `?47 l` finds it (`the_1049_save_is_the_primarys_decsc`); the cursor's style and `?12` ride beside it, `?1049` only. A second `?1049 h` on the alternate screen clears it and saves the alternate screen's cursor into the alternate screen's slot, leaving the primary's (`a_second_1049_set_saves_into_the_alternate_slot`), and does not reset a pending wrap made on the alternate screen, where `ClearScreen` would: `CUP 9;200`, `a`, `?1049 h`, `CUP 9;200`, `c`, `?1049 h`, `b` puts the `b` at 10;1 at all 36 read configurations. `?1049 l` restores the cursor and rendition (`the_cursor_is_saved_and_restored`, `the_rendition_is_saved_and_restored`) **as `DECRC` does, on the primary too and with nothing saved**: "restore cursor as in DECRC", xterm's `CursorRestore` after `FromAlternate`, so a `?1049 l` with nothing saved, a stray `rmcup` at a shell prompt included, puts the cursor home with the rendition normal, where it did nothing until 2026-10-20 (`leaving_with_nothing_saved_restores_as_decrc_does`, `leaving_when_not_on_the_alt_screen_restores_as_decrc`). The restore does not empty the slot. **Its clear takes the pictures too**, since 2026-10-21, as xterm's `ClearScreen` does: `?1049 h` on the alternate screen, after `?47 h`, frees the Sixel there (`the_1049_clear_on_the_alternate_screen_frees_its_sixel`), and entering from the primary leaves the primary's (`entering_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_primary_s_sixel`) |
| `CSI ? 2031 h/l` | colour palette update notifications | done — since 2026-09-29. Reset at power-on and DECRQM answers the bit, so neovim's handshake subscribes (`neovims_handshake_subscribes_and_re_queries`); **nothing is sent on setting it**, as tmux learned the hard way. While set, an embedder colour change that moves the dark/light class sends one `CSI ? 997 ; 1\|2 n`: `Term::set_theme` and `Term::set_default_colors` compare the class before and after the whole change, so a class crossed inside one call is not reported (`a_class_crossed_inside_one_call_is_not_reported`). An application's own colour escapes send nothing and change the next `?996 n` answer. `RIS` clears it; `DECSTR` keeps it -- baud's rule, since xterm has no `?2031` (`ris_clears_the_mode_and_decstr_keeps_it`). The macOS app's OS light/dark flip under `theme = "auto"` reaches it through `tell_defaults` with `Overrides::Drop`, dropping the application's `OSC 4`/`10`/`11`/`12`/`17`/`19` colours -- only when the resolved appearance changes, since AppKit also calls back for accessibility variants of the same one (`palette_switch`, `a_flip_drops_the_applications_colours_and_reports_once`). Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app's does too, on the Settings portal's `SettingChanged`; the portal's answer at launch, which races the shell's first output, keeps them (`the_launch_answer_keeps_a_programs_colours_and_a_change_drops_them`) |
| `CSI ? 2048 h/l` | in-band resize notifications | done — reports `CSI 48 ; rows ; cols ; height_px ; width_px t` on enablement, on re-enablement, after every resize, and **after a cell-size change even when the grid did not move**, carrying what the terminal **has** rather than what was asked for. The pixel fields are the text area -- the grid times the cell size `Term::set_cell_size` was given -- and zero until one is given, which `[[source: inband-resize]]` defines as the answer of a terminal that cannot report them; "A terminal MUST report pixel sizes if it is capable", and "Font size changes can also affect the text area size", which is why a zoom reports. Until 2026-09-22 the fields were always zero. `the_inband_report_carries_pixels_once_the_cell_is_known`, `a_cell_size_change_reports_even_with_the_grid_unchanged` |
| `CSI 11 t`, `CSI 13 t`, `CSI 13 ; 2 t`, `CSI 14 t`, `CSI 14 ; 2 t`, `CSI 15 t`, `CSI 16 t`, `CSI 18 t`, `CSI 19 t` | XTWINOPS reports: window state, position and size, the text area, the cell, the screen | done — **a program can ask where the window is, how big it and the screen are, and whether it is minimized**, since 2026-10-19: `CSI 1 t` or `CSI 2 t` (open or iconified), `CSI 3 ; x ; y t` for the window's position and for the text area's (x first, a negative position as its sixteen-bit two's complement, ctlseqs' "Negative values correspond to 32768-65535"), `CSI 4 ; h ; w t` for the window with its frame, `CSI 5 ; h ; w t` for the screen in pixels and `CSI 9 ; rows ; cols t` for the screen in characters, its pixels over the cell's as xterm divides them (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file, `window_ops`). Each answers from what the embedder hands `Term::set_window`, every position and size in the device pixels `CSI 16 t` answers in, and **is silent and counted where it handed nothing**, never a made-up number (`the_window_reports_answer_from_what_the_embedder_handed_over`, `a_report_the_embedder_cannot_answer_is_silent_and_counted`, `the_window_and_screen_answers_have_ctlseqs_shapes`). The macOS app reads AppKit before every read it feeds -- the frame, the view taken to the screen, the window's screen and the primary one, measured from the primary screen's top-left (`[[source: apple-nswindow-frame]]`, `[[source: apple-nswindow-converttoscreen]]`, `[[source: apple-nsscreen-frame]]`, `[[source: apple-nsscreen-screens]]`; `the_window_facts_are_appkits_geometry_in_device_pixels_from_the_top`); the Linux app knows only its surface, so it answers `CSI 14 ; 2 t` and nothing else of the window -- xdg-shell tells a client neither its position nor that it is minimized, and no `wl_output` is bound. **The text area and the cell**: `CSI 4 ; h ; w t` (text area in pixels), `CSI 6 ; h ; w t` (one cell in pixels) and `CSI 8 ; rows ; cols t` (text area in cells), in `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s shapes, height first. **The two pixel answers need the cell size and are silence and a count without it**, the way an unanswerable `DSR` is; the cell count is always answered. Any other second parameter is undefined and counted. This is the reply an image client sizes its pictures from -- yazi sends `CSI 16 t` in its detection burst and read nothing back before 2026-09-22, so it drew every Sixel at a fixed 600x900. `the_window_queries_answer_from_the_cell_size_and_the_grid`, `without_a_cell_size_only_the_cell_count_is_answered`, `yazi_reads_the_cell_size_from_its_detection_burst`, `every_other_t_is_refused_or_not_xtwinops` |
| `CSI 22 ; Ps t`, `CSI 23 ; Ps t` | XTWINOPS title stack: save and restore the window title and icon name | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program that sets its own title can put the shell's back when it exits**, as vim does with `CSI 22 ; 2 t` and `CSI 23 ; 2 t`. `Ps` 0 both names, 1 the icon name, 2 the title (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), an omitted `Ps` 0 as xterm reads it; a third parameter from 1 to 10 stores or reads that slot without pushing or popping, and one past 10, outside ctlseqs' range, is counted.
