The stack is xterm's (`xtermPushTitle`, `xtermPopTitle`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `MAX_SAVED_TITLES`, `[[source: xterm-ptyx-h]]`): ten slots, the eleventh push over the oldest, a pop of an empty stack doing nothing, a half a push did not take filled from the nearest older slot, and a half still empty leaving that name -- so vim's icon-name restore, the second of its two pops, finds nothing and leaves vim's icon name, as it does in xterm (`vims_title_push_and_pop_restore_the_shells_title`, `a_half_the_push_did_not_take_comes_from_an_older_slot`, `the_eleventh_push_overwrites_the_oldest`, `a_slot_from_one_to_ten_reads_and_writes_without_pushing`, `the_title_stack_is_chunk_invariant`). `RIS` and `DECSTR` keep it (`the_resets_keep_the_title_stack`). Since 2026-10-25 `RIS` empties the title, so a pop after `reset` brings back a title pushed before it. |
| `CSI # S` | XTTITLEPOS report the title stack's position | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask how many titles it has saved**: answered `CSI Pn ; Pm # S`, the pushes not yet popped and the stack's ten levels (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 664), as xterm's `xtermReportTitleStack` writes them, so `Pn` passes 10 once a push has overwritten the oldest slot (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`). A direct slot moves neither (`xttitlepos_reports_the_pushes_and_the_depth`, `a_direct_slot_leaves_the_title_position`). |
| `CSI > t`, `CSI > T` | XTSMTITLE / XTRMTITLE title modes, `CSI > Pm t` and `CSI > Pm T` | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can send its title in hexadecimal, and send a UTF-8 title under `ESC % @`**. Mode 0 decodes `OSC 0`, `1` and `2` from hexadecimal, either case, as xterm's x_decode_hex and `ChangeGroup` do: a body that is not an even count of hex digits changes nothing and is counted, and the name ends at its first byte below 32 (`[[source: xterm-xstrings-c]]`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `a_hex_title_is_decoded_while_the_mode_is_set`, `a_title_that_is_not_hex_changes_nothing_and_is_counted`). Mode 2 reads a title as UTF-8: under UTF-8 every title is, set or reset, as xterm's `utf8Title` resource follows the encoding (`under_utf8_mode_2_changes_no_title`); under `ESC % @` it keeps the GR bytes of an `OSC 0`, `1` or `2` body, the text's "window/icon labels", where xterm keeps `0` and `2` alone, so the text is built (`under_latin1_the_utf8_mode_keeps_a_titles_gr_bytes`, `a_kept_gr_title_is_chunk_invariant`). Each name keeps the encoding its bytes are in, UTF-8 where they are valid under mode 2 or in hexadecimal and ISO 8859-1 otherwise, on the title stack too (`Term::title_encoding`, `a_hex_title_is_utf8_only_where_it_is_valid`, `the_title_stack_keeps_each_titles_encoding`). **Modes 1 and 3, the query modes, are refused and counted with the title reports** they shape, never recorded (`the_query_modes_are_refused_with_the_title_reports`); a value past 3 is counted. With no parameter both return to the default, none set, as the text says; xterm's code never reaches that branch. `RIS` resets them and `DECSTR` keeps them, as xterm's resets do (`ris_resets_the_title_modes_and_decstr_keeps_them`). DECRQSS answers them (the DECRQSS row). |
| `CSI # {`, `CSI # }` | XTPUSHSGR / XTPOPSGR save and restore the rendition, and their aliases `CSI # p` and `CSI # q` | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can save the colours and attributes it is drawing in and put them back**, ten levels deep (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 1525-1568 and 1871-1953), as xterm's `xtermPushSGR` and `xtermPopSGR` keep them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`): a bare push saves the whole rendition, an underline colour, a font and the attributes xterm lacks among it (`a_bare_push_and_pop_restore_the_whole_rendition`, `the_aliases_push_and_pop`); a list saves the groups it names, 1 bold to 9 crossed-out, 21 double underline, 30 and 31 the colours (`a_list_push_restores_only_its_groups`, `the_colour_groups_are_thirty_and_thirty_one`), and `CSI 0 # {` none (`a_push_of_zero_saves_nothing`); 10 and 11, SGR's fonts, name no group, where xterm reads them as 30 and 31, a departure from the text, so the text is built (`the_colour_groups_are_thirty_and_thirty_one`). 5 restores both blink rates, one aspect (the `SGR 5, 6` row; `five_restores_the_blink_rate`); 4 and 21 restore xterm's two underline bits on baud's one underline style (`four_and_twenty_one_restore_xterms_underline_bits`); what xterm does not have is restored only by a bare push. An eleventh push stores nothing and its pop restores nothing, as xterm's stack counts on; a pop of an empty stack is counted (`the_stack_is_ten_deep`). **`RIS` empties the stack and `DECSTR` keeps it**: RIS returns to the initial state, where nothing is saved, as it empties XTSAVE's cache; xterm's resets keep it, a departure from 8.3.105, so the text is built (`ris_empties_the_rendition_stack_and_decstr_keeps_it`). |
| `CSI # \|` | XTREPORTSGR report the rendition a rectangle shares | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask what colours and attributes a block of the screen has in common**: answered `CSI 0 ; Ps... m`, every attribute, colour and font all its cells share, in DECRQSS's spelling, where xterm reports its own subset (`xtermReportSGR`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`; `xtreportsgr_answers_the_attributes_every_cell_shares`). The rectangle is read as DECRQCRA's is, from the origin under DECOM and clamped to the page (`xtreportsgr_counts_from_the_origin_under_decom`); one holding no cell answers `CSI 0 m`, as xterm's does (`an_empty_rectangle_answers_zero`). |
| `CSI # P`, `CSI # Q` | XTPUSHCOLORS / XTPOPCOLORS save and restore the palette | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program that recolours the terminal can put the colours back as they were**, ten palettes deep (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 644-656): a palette is the 256 `OSC 4` colours, the five special colours and the dynamic ones, `OSC 10`, `11`, `12`, `17` and `19`, as xterm's `ColorSlot` holds them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`). A pop puts each back through its setter, so the screen repaints only where a colour changed (`a_pop_restores_the_palette_the_specials_and_the_dynamic_colours`, `a_pop_that_recolours_moves_the_colour_epoch`). Each parameter is one push or pop, a bare one the current entry, as xterm's loop takes them (`each_parameter_is_one_push`); 1 to 10 stores or reads that palette "without pushing" or "without popping", the current entry left where it is, as the title stack's direct slots are; xterm's `xtermPushColors` and `xtermPopColors` move it, a departure from the text, so the text is built (`a_direct_palette_leaves_the_current_entry`). An eleventh push, a pop of an empty stack, a palette never stored and one past 10 change nothing and are counted (`the_stack_is_ten_deep_and_refusals_are_counted`). **`RIS` empties the stack and `DECSTR` keeps it**, as for the rendition stack (`ris_empties_the_palette_stack_and_decstr_keeps_it`). |
| `CSI # R` | XTREPORTCOLORS report the palette stack | done — since 2026-10-20, answered `CSI ? Pu ; Ps # Q`, the current entry and the palettes stored, with the `?` xterm's `xtermReportColors` writes, which keeps an echoed answer from popping two palettes: a departure from ctlseqs' "same form as XTPOPCOLORS", `CSI Pm # Q` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 658-660), taken because it keeps an echoed answer from popping two palettes (`xtreportcolors_answers_the_entry_and_the_palettes_stored`, `the_report_follows_s8c1t`). The palettes stored are counted, the text's "number of palettes stored on the stack", which is xterm's high-water mark wherever only bare pushes were made. |
| `CSI 1 t` to `CSI 10 t` | XTWINOPS window operations: de-iconify, iconify, move, resize in pixels, raise, lower, refresh, resize in cells, maximise, full screen | done — since 2026-10-19, **a program can move, resize, minimise, raise, lower, maximise or full-screen the window**: built, on by default, and `[window] operations = "off"` turns them off. Each form takes the parameters `[[source: ctlseqs]]` gives it: 1, 2, 5, 6 and 7 none, 3 `x ; y`, 4 `height ; width` in pixels, 8 `height ; width` in characters, 9 `Ps` 0 to 3, 10 `Ps` 0 to 2, an omitted `Ps` 0 as xterm's `zero_if_default` reads it (`window_ops`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file). For 4 and 8, "Omitted parameters reuse the current height or width. Zero parameters use the display's height or width": an omitted or empty one keeps the size, 0 is the screen's, as xterm's `optional_param` and `RequestResize` read them. A move's 32768-65535 are -32768 to -1, the sixteen-bit two's complement `CSI 13 t` reports a position in, so the position reported, sent back, is where the window goes; xterm reads its move through X's signed `Position`. **Refused and counted in `unsupported_param`**, as forms ctlseqs does not give: 12 and 17, 9 ; 4 and up and 10 ; 3 and up, where xterm's `RequestMaximize` falls through to a restore, and a parameter more than a form takes. The core cannot move a window: it queues each request for the embedder (`Term::window_requests`, sixteen between drains, a request past them dropped and counted in `Term::window_requests_dropped`) and changes nothing itself, the grid included; a resize reaches the grid through the embedder's `Term::resize`, as the user's own does, so the rest of the read that asked is parsed at the old size, where xterm resizes before it parses on. Off (`Term::set_window_ops`, `WindowOps::Off`), nothing is queued and each is counted in `Term::window_ops_refused`, as xterm performs none it disallows; no sequence turns them back on, `RIS` and `DECSTR` included, and the reports and the title stack answer either way (`each_window_operation_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `a_form_ctlseqs_does_not_give_is_counted_and_not_queued`, `omitted_and_zero_parameters_reach_the_embedder_as_sent`, `with_the_operations_off_nothing_is_queued_and_each_is_counted`, `no_reset_turns_the_window_operations_back_on`, `a_full_queue_drops_and_counts_the_rest`; the domain's `the_window_operations_parse_to_their_names`, `a_resize_tells_omitted_from_zero`, `a_move_reads_its_coordinates_as_the_report_writes_them`). **macOS** performs each with AppKit once the callback that asked has returned: `deminiaturize:`, `miniaturize:`, `orderFrontRegardless`, which takes no focus, `orderBack:`, `toggleFullScreen:` when it changes the state asked for, and `setFrame:display:` for the rest. 3 puts the frame's top-left where `CSI 13 t` measures it; 4 sizes the content view, the text and its padding, as xterm sizes its VT100 widget, the text and its border; 8 the content a grid that size fills; each resize keeps the top-left and is no larger than the screen's visible frame, as xterm's `limitResize` of 1 limits one to the display's size, and since 2026-10-21 stays inside that frame top to bottom, a window that grows down past its bottom moving up rather than going under the Dock, as AppKit's `constrainFrameRect(_:to:)` holds a window's top edge and height to the screen (`a_frame_that_grows_stays_inside_the_visible_frame`; the status line's row too), and with no screen to measure against a resize in pixels is refused and one in cells is held to the largest grid. The requests one read brings are each measured on the window the ones before them leave, since AppKit performs none until the callback returns, so a move then a resize keeps the move. 9 resizes to the visible frame, both ways, its height or its width, saving the frame once for 9 ; 0 to return to, as xterm's `RequestMaximize` saves one; 7 draws the next frame whole. In full screen a move, a resize and a maximise do nothing, and a `BAUD_GRID`-pinned grid keeps the window's size (the app's `a_move_lands_where_the_report_measures_from`, `a_pixel_resize_sizes_the_content_view`, `a_cell_resize_gives_the_grid_asked_for`, `a_maximise_saves_the_frame_a_restore_returns_to`, `a_toggle_is_called_only_when_it_changes_the_window`, `the_reported_position_moved_to_is_where_the_window_was`, `the_window_operations_run_after_the_callback_that_asked`, `with_no_screen_a_pixel_resize_is_refused`, `a_batch_is_measured_on_the_window_each_request_leaves`). **Linux** does what xdg-shell has (`[[source: wp-xdg-shell]]`): 2 `set_minimized`, 9 ; 1 and 9 ; 0 `set_maximized` and `unset_maximized`, 10 `set_fullscreen` and `unset_fullscreen`, 10 ; 2 by the state the last configure carried, 7 a frame drawn whole, and 4, 8 and DECSLPP a size the window chooses while floating, held inside the compositor's `configure_bounds` (`wayland_does_what_xdg_shell_has`, `the_window_operations_reach_the_toplevel_as_its_requests`, `a_floating_window_takes_its_own_size_and_a_maximised_one_does_not`). Unbuilt there, each counted and warned once with its reason: 1, since `set_minimized` has no undoing, "nor is there any way to unset minimization on this surface"; 3, since `move` is "an interactive, user-driven move" that "must be used in response to some sort of user action"; 5 and 6, since xdg-shell has no stacking request; 9 ; 2 and 9 ; 3, since `set_maximized` has no axis; a resize while maximised, where the size "must be obeyed", or in full screen, where it "is a maximum"; and a size in pixels, 0 or given, before the compositor has sent its bounds, the one size of the screen xdg-shell tells a client; one in cells is held to the largest grid then. Full screen on 10 is ctlseqs' "Change to full-screen"; xterm 411's `window_ops` calls `RequestMaximize` there, the same resize as 9 ; 1, which is its departure. Nobody has watched any of it on a screen: the app's halves are proven as data and by source gates, as no test opens a window |
| `CSI Ps t` with `Ps` 24 and up | DECSLPP set lines per page | done — since 2026-10-19: `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s "Ps >= 2 4 -> Resize to Ps lines (DECSLPP), VT340 and VT420. xterm adapts this by resizing its window", as xterm's `RequestResize(xw, code, -1, True)` does, the columns kept: a request for `Ps` rows, the same `Term::window_requests` entry as `CSI 8 ; Ps t`, through the same setting, `[window] operations`. `[[source: vt510-decslpp]]`: "If the number of lines specified is not one of the values supported by the implementation, then the next higher supported number of lines per page is assumed. If the number of lines specified exceeds the maximum number supported, then the maximum number supported is used"; baud's page is its screen, every count from 24 to the window's largest is one it supports, and a count past the screen is the screen's, as the macOS app holds a resize inside its screen and the Linux one inside the compositor's bounds once it has sent them. Since 2026-10-22 DECRQSS `t` answers the page as the control that sets it, the screen's rows and 24 on a shorter one (the DECRQSS row). A count with a second parameter is refused, as a form ctlseqs does not give; 23 and below are XTWINOPS's own forms (`each_window_operation_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `the_other_requests_carry_what_ctlseqs_gives_them`, `a_cell_resize_gives_the_grid_asked_for`) |
| `DCS Pa ; Pb ; Ph q ... ST` | Sixel graphics | done — decoded into caller memory with no external decoder (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` is the raster grammar, `[[source: ctlseqs]]` the DCS envelope), placed at the cursor and drawn by all three render paths. **A colour register takes RGB and HLS**: HLS since 2026-10-11, with DEC's hue origin, "Blue: 0 degrees Red: 120 degrees Green: 240 degrees" (`[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`), by the double hexcone of `[[source: css-color-4-hsl]]`, within one percent of the VT340's sixteen default colours on that chapter's Table 2-3 (`hls_defines_reproduce_the_vt340_default_colour_map`, `the_hls_hue_origin_is_blue_at_zero_red_at_120_green_at_240`); an HLS value past its range ignores the whole command, `Pc` included, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §8.1.3 (`an_hls_define_out_of_range_is_ignored_whole`). It was counted and left unpainted until then. **A sixel pixel is drawn at its pixel aspect ratio where the two DEC texts agree**, since 2026-10-11: `P1` omitted, 0, 1 or 5 is 2:1, 3 is 3:1 and 9 is 1:1 (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`; `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §7.1), and a raster command's whole `Pan:Pad` before any data or `$` or `-` overrides it, an omitted or zero one reading as 1 (070-9 §8.1.2) -- `a_sixel_pixel_is_drawn_at_its_pixel_aspect_ratio`, `the_macro_parameter_sets_the_aspect_where_both_dec_texts_make_it_whole`, `a_raster_ratio_before_the_data_overrides_the_macro`. Every image was 1:1 before. **A ratio that is not a whole number is preserved**, since 2026-10-23: `P1` 2, 4, 6, 7 and 8 are 070-9 §7.1's 450, 250, 150, 130 and 112 to 100, and a raster `"3;2` 1.5:1, sixel row `y` starting at device row `y` times the ratio rounded half up once, so 4.5:1 takes rows of 5 and 4 in turn; 070-9 §12, "The pixel aspect ratio should be preserved if possible", over "the average grid size over not more than 30 pixels" (PDF pp. 928-929), where the VT330/VT340 manual rounds each to a whole number, that terminal's own fallback. A ratio below one, `"1;2`, is 1:1, §12's "never less than the smallest imageable unit", and a `P1` past 9 is read as omitted, 2:1, 070-3 §3.5.1.3's "unimplemented selective parameter values ... shall be ignored as if that parameter were not received" (PDF p. 125). All three were 1:1 (`a_ratio_that_is_not_whole_is_preserved`). **baud's grid fallback, as 070-9 §12 asks a device to state it** ("Conforming devices must clearly state in their product documentation how grid sizes fall back", PDF p. 928): one sixel column is one device pixel whatever the envelope's third parameter (ctlseqs' `Ph`, the VT3xx chapter's `P3`, not the raster command's) or the macro parameter's grid size asks, as the VT300's grid "is fixed", and the aspect ratio is drawn on it as above. A third parameter above zero is counted in `sixel_grids_fixed` (`a_horizontal_grid_size_falls_back_to_one_grid_and_is_counted`); it was dropped unseen. §12's general rule, that a device "selects an actual grid size that will come closest to but does not exceed the specified grid size, but never less than the smallest imageable unit of the device" (PDF p. 928), is not computed: no text maps a decipoint to a screen pixel and no physical pixel size reaches the core, so the fixed one-pixel grid is the stated fallback, over scaling by the display's points. **`P2` 0 or 2 sets the positions the data leaves at zero to the background**, since 2026-10-22, and so does an absent or empty `P2`, which is 0 (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` 192-203 and 302-303): over the raster extent `Ph` by `Pv` when a raster command before the data sends one, else from the image's corner to the right and bottom margins in force, the extent `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §10 starts a Sixel with and what a VT340 clears (`[[source: vt340test-p2effect-sh]]` 6-7, 12-13), to the screen's edge on a side the cursor is past, stopped at the screen's edges in display mode, and over the data's reach on a side no cell size measures (`a_raster_extent_is_filled_where_the_data_left_zero`, `without_a_raster_the_fill_reaches_the_margins`, `a_cursor_past_a_margin_fills_to_the_screen_edge`, `display_mode_stops_the_fill_at_the_screen_edges`, `with_no_cell_size_the_fill_is_the_datas_reach`, `p2_1_keeps_the_zero_positions_clear`); **and since 2026-10-22 the fill goes on as the image scrolls**: data that runs past the rows left to the bottom margin scrolls the screen, and the rows it scrolls in are filled across the fill's width from the band that overran them to the data's last row rounded up to a whole cell, the rows left counted as filled, so a raster extent shorter than them leaves the rows between as they were, as Windows Terminal's `_fillImageBackgroundWhenScrolled()` does, "to match the behavior of the original hardware terminals" (`[[source: wt-sixel-parser]]`; `the_fill_goes_on_as_the_image_scrolls`, and the pixel oracle on all three paths), where the fill stopped at `Pv` or the margins before (open: Windows Terminal goes on filling at every graphics new line, an empty trailing band included, where baud fills to the data's reach; and with the cursor below the bottom margin, the fill reaches rows below the screen that never scroll in, where Windows Terminal stops extending the image once no height is left); in the default background as `OSC 11` leaves it, read each frame, and unreversed under `?5`, as a VT340 fills with colour-map entry 0 on a reversed screen (`[[source: vt340test-background-select-2-png]]`) and xterm's graphics read no reverse video (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]`); opaque over the text under it, alike on all three paths (the pixel oracle, `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`). The cursor goes below the data, not the fill (`the_fill_does_not_move_the_cursor_further`); text on the fill alone clears it in the cells it writes, as a VT340 writes text into its one bitmap, and text on the data frees the image as before (`text_on_the_fill_clears_its_cells_and_keeps_the_image`, `text_on_the_data_frees_the_image`); an erase clears it in the cells it erases (`an_erase_clears_the_fill_in_the_cells_it_erases`); a hardcopy prints it as the background (`a_sixel_fill_prints_as_the_background`); and a filled image is counted in `sixels_filled`. **On the see-through window the fill is drawn as `[glass] sixel_backgrounds` says**, since 2026-10-22: opaque by default, as on an opaque window and as foot draws it (`[[source: foot-sixel-c]]`); `"dim"`, the same colour at the window's alpha over what is under it; `"clear"`, at that alpha in place of what is under it, as erased cells look; under `?5` each keeps the colour unreversed, as foot's register 0 reads, on all three draw paths (the pixel oracle's `sixel_fill_gate`, `each_fill_on_the_glass_draws_incrementally_as_a_full_repaint`). Until then there a `P2` of 0 drew as 1 does. Until 2026-10-22 every zero position was transparent and a `P2` of 0 or 2 was only counted. **An undefined colour register paints the foreground shown** since 2026-10-15, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §10, "Sixel color: Contrasting to background. Color numbers: All unspecified color numbers image as the default Sixel color" -- the embedder's default foreground, the theme's background under `?5`, and nothing while the core has not been told one, over the VT340's colour map, which xterm and foot load: yazi paints every fully transparent pixel with register 0, which it never defines (`[[source: yazi-sixel]]`), so its previews of images with transparency now show the foreground there (`an_undefined_register_paints_the_foreground_shown`). Every undefined register was transparent before. A register number past 255 is mapped back to 255, which 070-9 §8.1.3 requires ("the unimplemented index must be mapped back into the supported range") and fixes no mapping for; counting it or wrapping it stays open. **The row the cursor ends on**: it is the row below the image, where `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]` sets the text cursor "to the current sixel cursor position", which a VT340 rounds up to the row it falls in (`[[source: vt340test-textcursor-sh]]`), and 070-9 §11.3 allows only "an adjustment less than one character height ... in a downward direction"; baud takes the VT340's row. **The column after an image is the one it began in**, 070-9 §11.3, and a VT340 keeps it (`[[source: vt340test-textcursor-sh]]`), where the VT330/VT340 manual's "the text cursor is set to the current sixel cursor position" reads the other way, a conflict between the texts settled for 070-9 and the VT340 (`the_advance_keeps_the_column_where_the_image_started`); a zero repeat count is one, 070-9 §8.1.1, and so is an omitted one, `[[source: dec-pplv2-pm]]` p. 5-26 (`a_zero_repeat_draws_one_column`). **An image follows the row it was placed on**: scrolling into the history, a shorter screen retiring its row and a taller one pulling it back, a narrower screen re-cutting the lines above it, and a reader scrolled back to it. Its column is kept, and it is cropped at the edge rather than moved. It goes when its last row does -- the `ceil(h / cell_h)` rows it is drawn over, the rows text written over it counts, leaving the far end of the history or the alternate screen's top -- or when its anchor is blanked by a reverse scroll or dropped by a reflow that did not fit, and each is counted in `placements_expired`. So a tall image whose top line scrolled off the screen keeps its lower rows there, as "The top line of the sixel image may scroll off the screen" (`[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`, "Sixel Scrolling Enabled") describes and xterm draws (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]` 1787-1808), since 2026-10-18; it went whole with its anchor row before (`a_tall_sixel_goes_when_its_last_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_tall_sixel_on_the_alternate_screen_goes_with_its_last_row`, `a_taller_cell_takes_a_sixel_whose_rows_are_then_all_gone`). A cell height the embedder has not given is one row. `CSI 3 J` keeps one with a row on screen, as xterm's erase of the saved lines touches no graphic (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, `case 3`; `erasing_the_history_keeps_a_sixel_with_rows_on_screen`). **Each screen owns its images**: the shell's are not drawn over a full-screen program, and the program's are deleted when it leaves. Text over an image frees it. **A Sixel is decoded as its body arrives** (since 2026-10-14): each byte the handler keeps paints into the gap of the image arena the `q` claimed, the largest free one, and no copy of the body is kept, so **what limits an image is its pixels** -- the arena (`[images] pixels_mb`, 95 MiB by default since 2026-10-16, 4 MiB before), the 65535 a coordinate holds each way and the slot table -- and never its bytes, as `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 has it: §8.1.2's raster extent "does not limit the number of pixels a device may image" (its p. 9, PDF p. 920), §2 asks that "The protocol should support any size rasters, although not all devices will be capable of displaying them" (p. 2, PDF p. 913), and no section read limits a body's bytes. The raster's `Ph`, "a summary to avoid a complete scanning of the data" (§8.1.2), lays the rows out; data past it is drawn, and a stride wider than the image is given back at the next band. At its terminator an image moves to the lowest gap that holds it, where a measured decode would have stored it, and over generated programs of Sixels, with frees between them and between two reads of a body, the store is the buffered decoder's of e46ee3a7 (but for a band refused before such a free), image for image, offset for offset, refusal for refusal (`a_program_of_sixels_leaves_the_store_the_buffered_decoder_left`, `a_free_inside_a_body_leaves_the_store_the_buffered_decoder_left`; a resize inside a body, `a_resize_inside_a_body_frees_room_the_rest_of_it_can_use`), as are the committed yazi walk's thirteen previews (`every_preview_in_a_yazi_walk_is_the_buffered_decoders_image`); a terminal draws the same at every read boundary (`a_sixel_program_draws_alike_at_every_read_boundary`, `every_read_size_and_every_split_of_an_abandoning_program_draw_alike`), a body cut off by `CAN`, an `ESC` that is not `ST` or a C1 is placed from what it received and the byte then runs, as if `ST` had come first, since 2026-10-19, where it left nothing, and a `SUB` in one is a blank sixel, `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §9.1 and §9.3 (`a_body_cut_off_is_placed_from_what_it_received`, `a_sixel_cut_off_is_placed_from_what_it_received`, `a_sub_inside_a_sixel_is_a_blank_sixel`, `a_sixel_cut_off_keeps_its_claim_and_ris_gives_it_back`), and a body of any length draws (`a_body_past_any_old_scratch_draws_its_image`, `a_body_of_any_length_stores_at_the_default_budget`). Until then a body was held whole in a scratch and one past it refused: 256 KiB, at which 19 of the 41 Sixels in nineteen recorded yazi runs were refused, and from 2026-10-14 a 3 MiB interim. **A refused image is counted by the limit it hit**: `ImageStore::refusals` splits `images_refused` into the pixel arena (with the image's whole size, an unprovisioned terminal's empty arena included), the slot table, an image with no pixels and, since 2026-10-11, one wider or taller than the 65535 pixels an image coordinate holds (`an_image_past_65535_pixels_either_way_is_refused_whole`; it tripped a debug assertion and placed a cut image before), and the app logs one `warn` line a pump naming the limit and the `[images]` key that raises it -- `an_image_past_the_arena_is_counted_against_the_arena_with_its_size`, `an_image_that_outgrows_its_claim_is_refused_at_its_whole_size`, `a_full_slot_table_is_counted_against_the_slots`, `a_sixel_with_no_pixels_is_counted_as_empty`; `every_yazi_preview_of_the_walk_stores_at_the_default_budget` (note S). Gates: `baud-core/tests/sixel.rs` (decode and place, chunk invariance, refuse-and-count by cause, text over an image), `baud-core/tests/sixel_stream.rs` (the decode as the body arrives, against the buffered decoder), `baud-core/tests/image_anchor.rs` (every resize and scroll case, asserted by the text beside the image, including foot's blank-line trap and WezTerm's text-on-the-row trap), `baud-render/tests/image.rs` (a pinned frame digest, the one-row scroll, a view scrolled back after a shrink, the per-screen rule) and the pixel oracle. **It follows its row inside the screen too**: a scrolling region, `IL` and `DL` move rows without moving the window, so each moves the images on the rows it moves, and one whose row the operation deletes -- off the top of a region that copies, which is a middle region, or a top-anchored one on the alternate screen with at least as many rows under it as in it, or past a region's bottom -- goes and is counted; a top-anchored region that rotates on the primary, at full width, retires its top line, image and all, to the history; on the alternate screen, which keeps none, the image goes and is counted. **Under side margins narrower than the screen every scroll is a rectangle's** (since 2026-10-12): nothing retires, and an image moves only when its anchor's column is inside the rectangle, and goes and is counted when the scroll takes its row off. A status line under a region keeps its image while the region scrolls, on either screen. **Open**: a Sixel that a margin scroll, `IL` or `DL` moves still goes whole when its anchor leaves the band, its lower rows with it; a kitty placement there is clipped and kept until its last shown row leaves. **An image on a wrapped continuation row** -- placed after text that wrapped onto its row -- follows the cell its row began with, not whichever cell a narrower re-cut put first on the row it landed on, so a narrow-then-widen round trip brings it back to its row and every path of widths ends where one resize from the width it was placed at ends (`an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_comes_back_on_its_row_after_a_round_trip`, `an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_lands_by_the_width_not_by_the_path`, `an_anchor_on_a_wrapped_row_of_wide_characters_round_trips`). Until 2026-10-11 that round trip brought it back a row higher. A column a resize at a prompt cut off is asked as its row's first cell (`an_anchor_whose_column_a_prompt_resize_cut_off_asks_by_its_rows_first_cell`). **Erasing clears its pixels**, since 2026-10-21: DEC STD 070's B.8.11 leaves the interaction of text erasing and graphics to the device ("application software should not depend on interactions between graphics and text"), and the VT330 and VT340 keep text and graphics in one bitmap, so ED, EL, ECH and DECERA clear a Sixel's and the ReGIS page's pixels in exactly the positions they erase, back to clear, and a Sixel left with no pixel is freed, as text over it frees it, over xterm's whole-rows ED with no EL or ECH and over freeing the whole image; DECSED, DECSEL and DECSERA clear the positions they erase, the occupied unprotected ones, and keep a protected or unoccupied one's ("Unoccupied character positions are not affected"; where xterm's DECSERA clears the whole rectangle and its DECSED and DECSEL nothing, `[[source: xterm-screen]]`); ICH, DCH and IRM's gap clear the span they shift, from the cursor to the right margin, as a VT340 was seen to ("Colors are removed from the line when text is shifted left by Esc [ P", `[[source: vt340test-faketextcolor]]`, where xterm leaves them); IL, DL and a scroll inside a region move the ReGIS page's rows with their lines, as a Sixel moves by its anchor; DECIC and DECDC leave an image where it is (IMAGES_STAY, 2026-10-19); a kitty image is untouched, its text's rule (`each_erase_clears_the_sixel_in_its_positions`, `a_shift_of_characters_clears_the_sixel_from_the_cursor`, `a_selective_erase_clears_only_the_positions_it_erases`, `each_erase_clears_the_page_in_its_positions`, `a_selective_erase_spares_the_page_over_a_protected_position`, `the_lines_a_band_moves_take_the_page_with_them`, `irm_s_gap_clears_the_page_from_the_cursor`, `erm_spares_the_page_over_a_guarded_position`). Under side margins a rectangle scroll leaves the ReGIS page where it is. |
| `CSI ? 80 h/l` | DECSDM, Sixel display mode | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). **Set turns Sixel scrolling off**, `[[source: ctlseqs]]`'s numbering and the VT382 manuals' it quotes, against `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`'s "When sixel display mode is set, the Sixel Scrolling feature is enabled", a conflict between the sources; `[[source: vt3xx-tp]]`'s Table 12-3 lists DECSDM as mode 80 and gives no direction. Set, a Sixel starts "at the upper-left corner of the active graphics page" -- the window's first cell, whatever DECOM and the margins say, as xterm -- and "the text cursor does not change" (`display_mode_draws_at_the_windows_corner_and_leaves_the_cursor`); "The terminal ignores any commands that attempt to advance the active position below the bottom margin of the graphics page": a `-` whose band would start at or past the screen's bottom edge moves nothing, and rows past the edge are not kept (`display_mode_ignores_a_new_line_past_the_bottom_edge`) -- the literal reading, over a `-` that still returns left or clipping, which stays open; with no cell height known nothing limits it. **And nothing at or past the screen's right edge is drawn**: `[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-9 §11.2.2, "Sixels defined to be printed past the right margin are not printed", so the bands of a tall image that the ignored `-` lays side by side stop at the screen's width, and the image is stored at most the screen's size rather than refused for the arena (`display_mode_draws_nothing_past_the_right_edge`, `display_mode_keeps_a_tall_image_at_the_screens_size`, `display_mode_lays_no_row_out_past_the_right_edge`); with no cell width known nothing limits it. **Recorded only while DA lists Sixel**, the claim and a provisioned store, the condition ctlseqs' Sixel section puts DA's `4`, these three modes and `DCS q` under ("If xterm is configured as VT240 ... it supports Sixel Graphics controls"); otherwise counted in `unsupported_param` and answered `0`, and withdrawing the claim returns all three to power-on (`while_da_does_not_list_sixel_the_modes_are_counted_and_not_kept`, `withdrawing_the_claim_returns_the_modes_to_power_on`). xterm answers DECRQM from its flags whatever its configuration; which of the two is right stays open. Reset at power-on and by `RIS`, kept by `DECSTR`, as xterm's full reset alone restores it; DECRQM answers `1` or `2`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it (`decrqm_answers_each_sixel_mode_from_its_state`, `ris_restores_the_sixel_modes_and_decstr_keeps_them`). `CSI 80 h`, ANSI mode 80, which timg sends, is not it (`ansi_mode_80_is_not_decsdm`). |
| `CSI ? 1070 h/l` | private Sixel colour registers | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "use private color registers for each graphic". **Set at power-on**, xterm's resource default, so each Sixel starts with every register undefined, which is also 070-9 §10's "No Sixel state is preserved from one entering of Sixel mode to the next". Reset, a register a Sixel defines starts the next shared Sixel with that definition, and **since 2026-11-01 a picture drawn under shared registers changes colour when a later shared Sixel redefines a register it drew with**, a palette with no data included, as a VT340's one colour map does -- "You can draw an image, then change its appearance by changing the associated output map location" (`[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`, "Output Mapping") -- and xterm's shared set (`[[source: xterm-graphics-c]]`). Such a picture keeps the register of each pixel, a byte after its four, for its life, so it takes 25% more image memory: 95 MiB holds three 3840x2160 pictures drawn with private registers and two with shared ones (`the_default_budget_holds_three_private_screen_sixels_and_two_shared`); where the arena holds its pixels and not that byte as well, it is refused at its size and counted, as any picture without room is (decided 2026-11-01 by the lean; `a_shared_picture_without_room_for_its_numbers_is_refused`). An erased pixel stays erased and a background fill stays the fill, and a picture split across a side margin, or whose pixels a margin scroll moves within it, keeps each pixel's register with it (`an_erased_pixel_and_the_fill_stay_as_they_are`, `both_parts_of_a_split_shared_sixel_are_recoloured`, `a_shared_sixels_registers_move_with_its_pixels`). Open departure: the pictures change when the redefining body ends, at `ST` or a cut, where a VT340 and xterm change them as each definition arrives. Open departure, too: with no foreground colour known, a pixel an undefined register drew is clear, and a later definition does not recolour it. A body that only sets colours rescans every shared picture: with two 3840x2160 shared pictures stored, one 17-byte body costs 7.6 ms. **Under either setting a redefinition inside a body recolours the pixels that body drew before it**, since 2026-11-01: the decode keeps each pixel's colour while that colour names one register, as before, and its register's number from the first data that would not -- a register painting in a colour clear or another's, or a painted one redefined -- and the body's end writes each register's last colour; nothing draws a Sixel before then (`two_registers_of_one_colour_keep_their_own_pixels_when_one_changes`, `colliding_colours_leave_the_store_the_buffered_decoder_left`). A picture drawn with private registers keeps nothing past its end and is never recoloured (`a_later_shared_sixel_recolours_the_pictures_drawn_with_its_register`, `a_redefinition_inside_a_body_recolours_the_pixels_drawn_before_it`, `a_private_sixel_neither_recolours_nor_is_recoloured`, `a_palette_alone_recolours_and_moves_the_epoch`). All three draw paths show the new colours on the next frame (`sixel_recolour_gate` in `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs`, `a_recoloured_sixel_is_drawn_on_an_incremental_frame`). The VT340's map also colours its text, which baud's palette does not share. A private Sixel neither feeds nor clears the shared set, and `RIS` forgets it, with every picture (`shared_registers_carry_into_the_next_sixel_and_private_ones_do_not`, `a_private_sixel_neither_feeds_nor_clears_the_shared_set`). Recorded under the claim, reset and saved as `?80` is. |
| `CSI ? 8452 h/l` | Sixel cursor to the right of the image | done — since 2026-10-15 (note V, V18). `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Sixel scrolling leaves cursor to right of graphic, RLogin, xterm" -- the column `ceil(w / cell width)` past where the image began, and past the right margin the left margin of the next row, scrolling at the bottom, as xterm does; with no cell width known the column stays (`cursor_right_mode_leaves_the_cursor_past_the_image`). The row is the image's last row, where xterm's `finished_parsing` puts it (`[[source: xterm-graphics-sixel]]`, `new_row = charrow - 1 + ceil(h / FontHeight)`, the column alone changing under the mode); on the right margin itself the cursor stays. Until then it was the row below the image, below and right of the graphic rather than to its right. Recorded under the claim, reset and saved as `?80` is. Note S credited this mode to `[[source: vt3xx-sixel]]`, which has no 8452. |
| `CSI ? 1 ; 1 ; 0 S` | XTSMGRAPHICS, `CSI ? Pi ; Pa ; Pv S`, Sixel colour registers and geometry | done — since 2026-10-15. Answered `CSI ? Pi ; Ps ; Pv S` in xterm's shape (`CASE_GRAPHICS_ATTRIBUTES`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): **256 registers**, 070-9 §8.1.3's "Devices must implement color numbers 0 to 255", to a read, a reset and a read of the maximum, and a set succeeds only to 256; **the Sixel geometry** reads as the text area in pixels, a failure (`3`) while no cell size is known, and its maximum as `0 ; 0`, ctlseqs' shape for "implementations which do not use the maximum graphics dimensions" (baud limits an image by its arena and a coordinate's range, not a width and height); a geometry set or reset fails; **ReGIS's geometry** (`Pi` 3), since 2026-10-20, reads as the page ReGIS draws on, the text area in pixels or the VT300's 800 by 480 while no cell size is known, its maximum as `0 ; 0` as the Sixel maximum reads, and a set or reset fails, the page being the window's; any other item is an error in `Pi` (`1`), an unknown action an error in `Pa` (`2`) (`xtsmgraphics_answers_the_registers_and_the_geometry`). While DA does not list Sixel every success is a failure, ctlseqs' "not configured (runtime) for graphics", but ReGIS's: ReGIS is always on, so baud is configured for it though DA lists no attribute 3. **Only the three-parameter form**, "xterm accepts a three-parameter control sequence": another arity is counted in `unsupported` and unanswered (`xtsmgraphics_of_another_arity_is_counted_and_unanswered`). The Sixel maximum geometry is the plain reading of the text, which stays open. **It does not scroll**, though `CSI Ps S` is SU on the same final byte (`xtsmgraphics_does_not_scroll_and_is_counted`). Counted and unanswered until then. |
| `DCS Pn p ... ST` | ReGIS graphics (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-8) | **partial** — since 2026-10-20 (note AW, section 7). A program's vectors, circles, curves and filled areas draw on a page over the text area. **Built**: the envelope, where the low bit of `Pn` resynchronises and any other entry resumes where ReGIS was left (070-8 § 8.9.1; `[[source: vt3xx-regis-intro]]`'s `DCS p` to `DCS 3p`); § 8.4's general grammar, keyletters, bracketed extents, quoted strings with their doubled quotes, numbers kept to 1/65536 until whole ("1.02e2 must resolve to 102"), options to six levels and the synchronising `;`; macrographs (§ 8.4.4), 10,000 characters, sixteen levels, a macrograph playing itself refused and counted, a full store failing soft; the position stack of sixteen; **the page**, an image of the text area's own pixels placed at the window's top line, the VT300's 800 by 480 device fitted into it at its upper-left with its shape kept, drawn from a plane of output-map indices through the VT340's sixteen-entry map, a pixel never written clear; `P` and `V` with pixel vectors and the stack, `C`'s circles, arcs either way, circumferential arcs and interpolated open and closed curves (a uniform Catmull-Rom spline, which meets § 8.5.5.4.2.1's four constraints, streamed for any number of points), `F` by the even-odd rule with `F(F ...)` sub-polygons and a 4,096-vertex list, `W`'s overlay, replace, complement and erase writing, negate, the plane mask, the VT300's 8-bit pattern memory and its multiplier, and shading to a line, a column or a point; `S(A)`, `S(E)` filling the page with the background, opaque, `S(I)` and `S(M)` recolouring what is drawn; text, `T`'s sizes, spacing, direction, italics and the cell's own rotation, in a loaded alphabet (`L`, `L[h,w]` height first) or the built-in one, which is baud's embedded face rasterised by `baud-font` into each unit cell (§ 8.5.6, "must have a built in character definition"), a quoted string's UTF-8 decoded and its character taken as the alphabet's code, one past 15/15 drawn as the error character; command display, `Pn`'s second bit, the body drawn in a line of its own along the page's foot ("not mandatory", and built); text the shell writes over the page clearing the page in its cells, the VT340's one bitmap; the page scrolling with its lines and the next drawing after a scroll beginning a page at the window's top; and the reports, `R(P)`, `R(P(I))` as a non-interactive device answers it (§ 8.8.5.3), `R(M(=))`, `R(L)` as `(A'<name>')` (§ 8.7.4.1), `R(E)`, and `R(M(x))` answered empty, a carriage return alone (§ 8.5.7.4.1, "The preferred fallback is the empty report"). `S(T)`'s delay, interpretation "suspended" for its ticks, a sixtieth of a second each (§ 8.7.1.4.3): the read stops after the delay, the macrograph it was in stops where it is, and the app feeds the rest and every read behind it on the display link's tick or the `poll` timeout its deadline falls on, nothing sleeping, a delay with more than four mebibytes behind it ended early and counted (an embedder that holds nothing passes each over and counts it, `Term::set_regis_hold`); XTSMGRAPHICS's `Pi` 3 (that row). **Graphics input and the cursors**, since 2026-10-21: a program can ask the user to pick a point on its drawing. `R(P(I))` in one-shot mode (`R(I0)`, the default) shows a crosshair the pointer and the arrow keys move -- a device pixel a key, Shift ten, wrapping at the screen's edge (`[[source: vt3xx-report]]`) -- and holds the program's output (070-13 § 5.1) until a key or a button ends it: any key that sends something sends its own bytes, a button `CSI 241 ~`, `243`, `245` or `247` (`[[source: vt3xx-locator]]` Table 15-1) or what DECLKD loaded (that row), then the position in user coordinates to a tenth, `[102.5,200]`, and a carriage return. Multiple mode (`R(I1)`) answers `R(P(I))` at once behind the null button `CSI 240 ~` and reports each button press, while the keys stay the program's, arrows included; it works inside an envelope only and resumes at the next `DCS p` where it was left (070-13 § 5.2, where the VT340 ends it at `ST`); `R(I0)` and `S(A)` end it, and `DECSTR`, whose list names no graphics state, keeps it. A press is the input's, not the selection's, Shift still selecting, and DEC's locator reports after it (070-13 § 5.5). An embedder that holds no read answers one-shot as a non-interactive device, counted; one that holds more than four mebibytes behind a one-shot input gives it up with the empty report, a carriage return alone (§ 8.8.5.3). `S(C ...)`: the input cursor a crosshair by default, as 070-13 § 5.1 has it, "a cross hair cursor appears", numbered as the VT330/VT340 manual numbers it (`[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`): `I0`, `I2` and an omitted number the crosshair, `I1` the diamond, `I3` and `I4` rubber bands anchored at the drawing position, a valid `S(A)` selecting the crosshair, as the VT340 does; the output cursor, the diamond or `S(C(H2))` the crosshair at the drawing position, shown whenever a read ends inside an envelope and gone at `ST`, or at the end of the read that cancels the envelope with `CAN`, `SUB` or a stray `ESC`, `S(C0)` turning it off; a user-defined pair of characters from the drawing alphabet with its offset, `S(C(I[+x,+y]"XO"))`. A standard cursor is drawn in map entry 0 with a one-pixel border in entry 15, a pair in entries 14 and 1, as the VT340 colours them, into the page shown and never into its plane, so nothing drawn under a cursor is changed by it. The indicator line shows the input position while input runs (070-14 p. 14-6). **Open departure**: the input cursor's numbers are the VT340's, against 070-8 § 8.8.1.4.2 b, whose selection syntax "has the force of a standard": "A value of zero or 1 for <n> selects a the diamond cursor ... This too is the default cursor, and is selected after a valid S(A) has been executed", so `I0`, an omitted number and `S(A)` select the crosshair where 070-8 selects the diamond, and `S(A)` differs from its written equivalent in § 8.8.1.4.1 g, "S(C(H1)(I1)1)", which selects the diamond. **Open departure**: DA lists no attribute `3` though ReGIS is built, against 070-4's DA, "The rest of the parameters indicate the architectural extensions supported at the current operating level" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]`, PDF 221), its Table 1 registering "3 1-4 ReGIS Display" (PDF 223), kept out while DA1 answers `?6c` (the `CSI c` row). **The clip rectangle**, since 2026-10-21: a program can fence its drawing in, or out. `S(R<n>[][])` limits every drawing to the rectangle's interior, its boundary included, for `n` above zero, and to its exterior for `n` below zero, "shielding", where "the boundary is NOT part of the exterior"; `S(R0)` turns it off and keeps the extent, and an extent alone keeps the switch; one corner leaves the extent and more than two use the last two, a relative part measured from the corner before and the first corner's from the drawing position, "multiple specifiers have cumulative action" (§ 8.4.5.1); `S(E)` erases the whole page whatever the clip and erase writing stays inside it; a valid `S(A)` sets the extent to its own and turns clipping off; the command display's line is the terminal's and is not clipped (070-8 § 8.8.1.4.6, § 8.8.1.4.1 d; `a_clip_rectangle_limits_drawing_to_its_inside_and_boundary`, `shielding_draws_outside_and_not_on_the_boundary`, `the_extent_and_the_switch_are_set_apart`, `relative_corners_accumulate_from_the_drawing_position`, `screen_erase_ignores_the_clip_and_erase_writing_keeps_it`, `a_valid_s_a_restores_the_default_clip`, `the_clip_is_not_counted_and_does_not_clip_the_command_display`). **Line width**, since 2026-10-21: `W(L<n>)` draws lines and curves `n` times the nominal width, today's one page pixel, as a round pen swept along the line, a fraction to the nearest pixel as a coordinate rounds, `W(L0)` the minimum, a pixel, and no width too wide, "W(L3) three times, and so on": the width field's 65,535 pixels is past twice the diagonal of any page up to 23,170 pixels a side, and the pen costs a step its rows on the page and a line at most one whole pen held to the page; each pixel of a stroke, a polyline or a curve, is written once, so complement writing turns it once; a valid `S(A)` sets it back to nominal (070-8 § 8.8.3.2, § 8.8.1.4.1 b; `a_wide_line_is_a_round_pen_swept_along_it`, `nominal_minimum_and_fractions`, `a_width_has_no_limit_short_of_the_page`, `a_width_changed_inside_a_stroke_draws_from_the_next_step`, `a_patterned_wide_line_is_the_pen_at_each_step`, `a_wide_stroke_complements_each_pixel_once`, `a_new_stroke_writes_over_the_last`, `the_width_resets_at_s_a_and_a_temporary_one_ends_with_its_command`, `moved_rows_keep_no_marks`). **The screen scroll**, since 2026-10-21: a program can slide its picture, as a strip chart does. Under the scrolling model the VT340 has (070-8 § 8.8.1 b), `S[x,y]` makes the address it names the screen's upper-left and `S<pv>` moves it a pixel vector step at the `W(M)` or `S(W(M))` multiplier, each digit a scroll (§ 8.8.1.2, § 8.8.1.4.8): the page's pixels and the indices they are drawn from move together, every plane, the clip holding nothing back (§ 8.8.1.4.6 e), the lit count recounted, and the drawing position and the coordinates stay ("Only the data is offset, not the coordinate system", `[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`); a relative or omitted part is measured from the origin, `S(A)`'s first corner, not the drawing position, and what the move reveals is the background, opaque, "moved off and erased from the display medium". **A departure**: the text does not move with it, baud's text being cells beneath the page where the VT340 keeps one bitmap; and the revealed band is opaque over the text (`a_scroll_moves_the_page_and_reveals_the_background`, `a_scroll_is_measured_from_the_origin`, `a_pixel_vector_scrolls_a_step_at_the_multiplier`, `a_scroll_moves_the_indices_keeps_the_count_and_ignores_the_clip`). **The display's scale**, since 2026-10-21: a program can magnify its picture. `S(S<n>)` shows each pixel `n` by `n`, `S(S(X<n>)(Y<n>))` each way apart, from the screen's upper-left, whole factors to sixteen, the limit a larger one is mapped to, zero and negatives changing nothing; the bitmap is kept, so what is drawn while magnified is drawn on it, a scroll moves it through the window, "S(S) and S[] then combine to move a small window through a large display space", and `S(S1)` or a valid `S(A)` shows it again (070-8 § 8.8.1.4.7, § 8.8.1.4.1 h). Text over a magnified page and the erases clear the bitmap's pixels shown in their cells, a whole block each; a line's move and the window's move take the bitmap's rows nearest the line's height; the pointer names the bitmap's pixel under it; the cursors keep their size; the command display stays on the screen's bottom line, its characters the size they are unmagnified, "Other ReGIS-settable states have no effect", drawn over the page shown as the cursors are and recoloured with the map, and a line feed erases the bitmap's pixels shown on the line, a whole block each, so at a scale that does not divide the line's rows it erases up to a block row above it (`magnified_the_command_display_stays_on_the_bottom_line`, `magnified_a_line_feed_erases_what_the_bottom_line_shows`, `magnified_a_line_feed_clears_the_line_s_characters`, `the_magnified_line_stays_over_later_drawing_and_goes_with_the_scale`, `scaling_magnifies_from_the_upper_left_and_s_s1_shows_the_bitmap_again`, `drawing_and_scrolling_while_magnified_work_on_the_bitmap`, `a_drawing_past_the_magnified_window_stops_at_the_edge`, `the_limit_the_refusals_and_s_a`, `a_magnified_page_stays_one_with_its_bitmap`, `a_magnified_page_moves_with_its_lines`, `the_input_position_is_the_bitmap_s`). **Hard copy**, since 2026-10-21: `S(H)` prints the visible image to the printer as a sixel dump (§ 8.8.1.4.4, "Sixels are the implementation choice in video terminals"; 070-7 § 7.8), the whole screen, the rectangle between two positions or between one and the drawing position, `S(H(P[x,y]))`'s offset kept until the next or a valid `S(A)`, `[0,0]` at power-up (§ 8.8.1.4.4; the VT330/VT340's `[50,0]` is that device's). Magnified by `S(S)`, the visible image is the page as it is shown, and a region's positions name the plane pixels it shows as blocks (`a_magnified_page_prints_as_it_is_shown`). **The texts disagree on a signed corner**: § 8.4.5.2 item 6 says H's brackets "ARE positional ... and may be expressed in terms of the current position", and § 8.4.5.1 makes multiple specifiers cumulative, which is built, the second corner relative to the first; the VT330/VT340 manual says relative values are "relative to the cursor position, not to any other position specified" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` p. 29), and § 8.8.1.4.4 calls `S(H[][])` "a non-positional use of bracketed extents ... always interpreted absolutely". Both are departures. The offset is held to 17 inches of the dump's pixels each way, 2040 at grid 6 and 1360 at grid 9, the text giving it no range and its relationship to the coordinate space "implementation- and retransmission protocol- dependent" (§ 8.4.5.2 item 6; `a_large_offset_is_held_to_a_sheet`). **The visible image is text and pictures**, as the VT340's one bit map is and its own dump shows (`[[source: vt340test-background-select-2-png]]`): each cell's background, its character drawn by the face the built-in alphabet uses at the cell's size, an underline along its last pixel row, then the Sixel images and the page in drawing order; reverse video swaps, an invisible character is not drawn. **Level 2** (070-7 § 7.8.2.4): SSU `ESC [ 2 SP I`, `DCS 0 ; Ps2 ; Pn3 q`, raster attributes at 1:1, their width and height, "number of pixels in image horizontally" and "vertically", counting the offset's blank pixels as well as the picture's, so a printer reading the extent takes the offset as part of the image, no `CR` first, `ST`, an `FF` after under DECPFF; black and white by default, every pixel with a bit set in any plane inked, a ReGIS pixel written with index 0 printing nothing and covering the text under it as it does on the screen (070-7 § 7.8.2.1, "a logical OR of all pixels which are selected for any color"; `index_zero_prints_no_ink_and_covers_the_text`), or the registers named in HLS or RGB with the background as register 0 when it is printed, past 255 colours a fixed 6 by 7 by 6 cube; grid 6 or 9 decipoints by DECGEPM, turned counter-clockwise when rotated (the `?43` row). With no printer it is counted, and one the print area has no working room for prints nothing and is counted (`Term::print_refusals`). The app's printer reads the dump into a picture on its own line at the grid's size, shrunk to fit the sheet; a job's pictures hold at most 2^25 pixels together, a larger one reduced to fit and one with none left left out, each counted and named in the panel's title (`a_picture_past_the_budget_is_reduced_and_the_title_says_so`) (`s_h_prints_the_screen_as_a_level_two_dump`, `a_region_prints_between_its_corners`, `pictures_print_over_the_text`, `offset_size_rotation_syntax_and_background`, `no_printer_form_feed_and_no_room`, `reverse_and_invisible`, `any_picture_round_trips`, `a_hard_copy_reaches_the_panel_as_a_picture`). **Microcoded writing**, since 2026-10-21: a program can say what each colour becomes where it draws. `W(W<i>(P<j>N<k>))` writes a pixel holding index `i` to `j` where the pattern, the area pattern or the character cell has a one and to `k` where it has a zero; only the entries loaded take effect, the rest writing as the writing mode does, and any writing mode, `V`, `R`, `C`, `E` or `N`, clears them, as a valid `S(A)` does (070-8 § 8.8.3.4). The bit is the pattern's own, before `W(N1)` inverts it, and the plane mask still limits the write (`w_w_writes_its_loaded_entries_and_the_mode_the_rest`, `a_writing_mode_command_clears_the_entries`, `the_bit_is_the_pattern_s_own_before_negate`, `the_plane_mask_limits_a_microcoded_write`, `a_valid_s_a_resets_the_entries_and_a_temporary_one_ends_with_its_command`, `an_entry_over_index_zero_writes_a_clear_pixel_too`). **Flood**, since 2026-10-21: a program can colour in an area already drawn, whatever drew its edges. `F[x,y]` and `F<pv>` flood from a seed, each seed in turn, a relative one measured from the seed before it and the first from the drawing position, which no flood moves (070-8 § 8.8.6.1; § 8.4.5.1's "cumulative action"); the flood spreads side by side and never corner to corner, "A boundary need only be connected by diagonally adjacent pixels", leaves the boundary as it is, and is written in the writing mode and foreground in force with the last shading snapshot, "even if shading has been subsequently disabled" (§ 8.8.6.4), each pixel once. `F(B(F1))`, the default and restored by a valid `S(A)`, spreads over the seed's index, § 8.8.6.4's second way, "any change in attribute with respect to the seed location attribute value"; **an open departure**: § 8.8.6.3.1.1 bounds it "by any foreground value different from the seed value, or any foreground if the seed location has the background attribute", which leaves the background open from a foreground seed, and SEED_ONLY follows § 8.8.6.4 against that sentence; `F(B(F0))` over the seed's state, foreground or background, § 8.8.6.4's "any change between the foreground and the background state"; `F(B(I<n>))` and `F(B(I(<colour>)))` up to that index; a pixel never written holds index 0, as complement reads it. The clip limits what a flood writes, not where it spreads (`a_flood_fills_the_closed_area_and_not_its_boundary`, `a_diagonal_boundary_closes_the_flood`, `f1_floods_the_seed_s_index_and_f0_the_seed_s_state`, `f_b_i_floods_up_to_the_named_index`, `a_flood_writes_each_pixel_once`, `the_texture_is_the_last_shading_snapshot`, `the_clip_limits_what_a_flood_writes_not_where_it_spreads`, `flood_seeds_accumulate_and_the_position_stays`, `a_stack_of_one_floods_what_a_full_stack_floods`). **Blink**, since 2026-10-21: a program can make what it draws blink. `W(A1)` sets the blink attribute on the foreground data drawn after it, a one of the pattern, cell or texture under `V`, `R` and `C`, every pixel under `E` with `N1`, a microcoded one, while a background write and any write without blink clear a pixel's; `W(A0)`, `S(E)` and a valid `S(A)` end it and what is drawn keeps blinking (070-8 § 8.8.3.1). A blinking pixel shows its own colour and then the background index's, opaque over the text as `S(E)` is, at SGR 5's rate on the display link, held shown under Reduce Motion, never a sleep; the embedder flips it (`Term::set_regis_blink`), both apps with text blink's slow phase, the Linux app waking at its flips while the page shown keeps blinking pixels (`Term::regis_blink`; `a_blinking_regis_page_wakes_at_the_slow_flips`). Under macOS's Dim Flashing Lights the off phase is faint as blinking text's is, each pixel half way to the background index's colour, at the same flips (`a_blinking_drawing_is_faint_in_the_faint_phase`, `a_regis_page_mixes_faint_as_text_does`). A page the next drawing leaves behind keeps its pixels in their own colours, and a hard copy prints them so in every phase (`a_blinking_drawing_shows_the_background_in_the_hidden_phase`, `only_foreground_data_blinks_and_any_other_write_clears_it`, `w_a0_s_e_and_a_valid_s_a_end_the_attribute_and_drawn_pixels_keep_it`, `a_page_with_no_blinking_pixel_left_stops_repainting`, `a_page_left_behind_keeps_its_own_colours`, `a_blinking_flood_and_a_blinking_character_blink`, `a_blinking_pixel_prints_its_own_colour_when_hidden`, `the_tick_flips_regis_blink_with_the_slow_phase`). **Drawing surfaces**, since 2026-10-21: a program can draw a second picture out of sight and show it at once. `P(P1)` draws on the VT340's second page of graphics memory and `P(P0)` on the first, `S(P1)` displays the second and `S(P0)` the first, other values ignored, "All drawing surfaces share the same state store" (070-8 § 8.8.2.1; `[[source: vt3xx-position]]`, `[[source: vt3xx-screen-control]]`); the surface drawn on is kept from one envelope to the next and is the first after a reset, `S(E)` erases it, and the cursors show only while it is displayed. The second is the screen's: the shell's text, its erases and its scrolling act on the first, the text page it is coupled to, and never reach it. Each envelope's entry and its `ST` display the first again, and a cancelled envelope's end does at the read's end, the VT340's "When you exit ReGIS, the displayed text page becomes the same as the active text page (if the Page Coupling feature ... is enabled)", coupling the VT420's default (`[[source: vt420-uu-ch9]]`). A hard copy prints the surface displayed, a page a resize leaves behind shows the first, and text or an erase after an envelope left on the second, in the same read, clears only its own cells of the first. **A departure**: the second surface is shown over the shell's text, baud's text having one page where the VT340 shows the second page's own (`the_second_surface_shows_only_while_it_is_displayed`, `each_surface_keeps_its_own_picture`, `the_drawing_surface_persists_and_each_entry_displays_the_first`, `s_e_erases_the_drawing_surface`, `the_second_surface_is_the_screen_s_and_the_text_scrolls_the_first`, `the_cursors_show_on_the_displayed_surface_only`, `a_reset_forgets_the_second_surface`, `a_hard_copy_prints_the_surface_displayed`, `a_resize_while_the_second_is_displayed_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_st_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_a_cancel_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `an_erase_after_st_on_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `text_after_an_escape_that_leaves_the_second_keeps_the_first`, `an_erase_of_all_of_the_first_frees_the_page_once_the_first_shows`). **Data movement** is the scrolling model, the VT340's (070-8 § 8.8.1 b, above), and baud has no other: "Neither model is required", and "For devices supporting both data movement models, the D option switches between them" (§ 8.8.1.4.3), so `S(D1)` names the model in force and `S(D0)`, the windowing model, is parsed and counted, an open gap against building both (`s_d1_names_the_scrolling_model_and_s_d0_is_counted`). **Not built yet**: kitty's images in a hard copy, which take three layers against the text, above it, under its characters and under its backgrounds, and blend by their alpha, where the dump reads one picture's pixel over the text; the windowing model; and `S(N)`, which no pinned DEC text defines, each parsed and counted (`Term::regis_counts`) (`a_bare_p_is_regis_and_answers_its_reports`, `a_dollar_or_plus_p_is_not_regis`, `the_interpreter_resumes_in_the_next_envelope`, `a_body_split_anywhere_answers_the_same`, `a_macrograph_report_is_empty_whatever_it_holds`, `a_vector_draws_its_pixels_in_the_foreground_and_nothing_else`, `complement_twice_is_nothing_and_a_joint_is_complemented_once`, `text_written_over_the_page_clears_its_cells_and_no_more`, `a_built_in_character_is_drawn_by_the_font_at_its_unit_cell`, `without_a_font_a_built_in_character_is_counted`, `command_display_shows_the_commands_on_the_bottom_line`, `a_delay_holds_the_rest_of_the_read_until_it_is_fed`, `a_delay_in_a_macrograph_stops_it_and_the_next_byte_plays_on`, `a_delay_holds_the_reads_behind_it_until_its_deadline`, `a_one_shot_report_waits_for_a_key_and_holds_the_read`, `arrow_keys_step_the_cursor_and_wrap_at_the_edge`, `a_button_ends_one_shot_with_its_string`, `multiple_mode_works_inside_the_envelope_and_resumes_on_reentry`, `the_default_input_cursor_is_a_two_tone_crosshair`, `s_c_i1_selects_the_diamond_and_i0_i2_and_omitted_the_crosshair`, `a_valid_s_a_selects_the_crosshair_and_s_c_i1_the_diamond`, `the_cursor_is_not_drawn_into_the_plane`, `the_output_cursor_shows_while_the_envelope_waits`, `a_cancelled_envelope_takes_its_cursors_away`, `a_one_shot_input_holds_the_reads_until_a_key_ends_it`, `a_regis_input_cursor_is_drawn_and_taken_away`) Erasing, shifting characters and moving lines clear or move the page's pixels as the Sixel row says, since 2026-10-21. **A page with nothing left lit is freed**, its plane and memory with it, since 2026-10-21, as a Sixel with no pixel left is; the store keeps the page's lit count through every write, so no erase reads the page again (`a_page_with_nothing_lit_is_freed`, `the_page_s_lit_count_is_kept_exactly`). **Complement writing reads what a scroll left on the screen**, since 2026-10-21: "complement the contents of the pixels present in the image" (070-8 § 8.7.2.4.1.1), so when the window has moved down the next drawing's page takes the old page's lines still on the screen and the indices they are drawn from, and the old page keeps only its lines in the history (`complement_reads_the_page_that_scrolled_under_it`, `a_page_scrolled_wholly_away_carries_nothing`) |
| `CSI ? 2004 h/l` | bracketed paste | done — recorded and exposed as `Term::bracketed_paste()` |
| `CSI ? 1004 h/l` | focus reporting | done — `Term::focus` queues `CSI I` on gain and `CSI O` on loss only while the mode is set, and nothing otherwise, because an application that did not ask reads `CSI I` as a keypress. The macOS app polls the window's key status on the tick and before a mouse event and forwards a change (`Driver::poll_focus`), because the window crate's hook reports the *view's* first-responder status, which fired once at `show()` before the driver was installed and never on an app switch -- so **no transition was ever reported on macOS until 2026-09-20**, found by the OSC 8 live check, and a live check reads `CSI I`, `CSI O`, `CSI I` around an app switch now. The Linux app calls it from its keyboard hook. **Dropped uncounted until 2026-09-20**: `1004` was named nowhere in the code and had no row here, while `vim-unicode.raw` sends it on its first line; `set_private_mode`'s wildcard counts now, as the ANSI table's always did. `gain_sends_csi_i_and_loss_sends_csi_o`, `nothing_is_sent_unless_the_application_asked`, `the_vim_recording_enables_it` |
| `CSI ? 9 h/l` | X10 compatibility mouse, a tracking level | done — since 2026-10-09. A press of button 1, 2 or 3 and nothing else: no release, no motion, no wheel and no modifiers (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, "X10 compatibility mode": "only on button press ... Cb is button-1, where button is 1, 2 or 3"). The encoding in force still applies, as xterm's `EmitButtonCode` writes it (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), so `?9` with `?1006` is `CSI < 0 ; x ; y M`. A level beside `?1000`-`?1003`, and a reset of any of the five turns tracking off (xterm's `really_set_mousemode`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); `RIS` clears it and `DECSTR` keeps it, as for the others. It was counted in `unsupported_param` and dropped before. The Linux app sends it too, since 2026-10-20, from `wl_pointer` (`seat.rs`). `x10_compatibility_reports_a_press_and_the_button_alone`, `x10_compatibility_sends_no_release_no_motion_and_no_wheel`, `x10_compatibility_takes_the_encoding_in_force`, `any_tracking_reset_turns_x10_compatibility_off_and_on_is_a_level`, `ris_clears_x10_compatibility_and_the_encoding_and_decstr_keeps_them` |
| `CSI ? 1000/1002/1003 h/l` | mouse tracking | done — a level; any reset turns it off. No level reports a wheel release, "Release events for the wheel buttons are not reported" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, `wheel_releases_are_not_reported`). **Since 2026-10-21 the wheel across is reported too**, a tilted wheel or a sideways swipe: button 6 toward the left and 7 toward the right, "encoded like the wheel mice, by adding 64 (for buttons 6 and 7)", 66 and 67 in every encoding, a press a column of the cell width and no release (where xterm reports their release and neovim reads one as a bare `<Release>`); `?9` reports neither. Shift with a mouse wheel, which macOS gives as a sideways scroll, takes Shift's override as the vertical wheel does (the `CSI > 1 s` row), so it reaches the program only under XTSHIFTESCAPE 1. ctlseqs' "button 7 (left) and button 6 (right) with a Logitech mouse" is that mouse's own numbering; the X convention and foot send 6 for the left (`[[source: foot-input]]`). Each event scrolls along its larger delta alone, as AppKit's scroll views do by default (`[[source: apple-nsscrollview-usespredominantaxisscrolling]]`), so a vertical swipe sends no stray 6 or 7 (`buttons_6_and_7_are_encoded_like_the_wheel`, `the_wheel_across_is_buttons_6_and_7_and_nothing_untracked`, `the_wheel_across_reports_buttons_6_and_7_under_tracking`, `each_event_scrolls_along_its_larger_delta`). **Since 2026-10-29 a mouse's side and extra buttons reach the program as buttons 8 to 11**, "by adding 128 (for buttons 8 through 11)": 128 to 131 in every encoding, their releases reported as xterm's `EditorButton` reports any release past button 5 (SGR keeps the button, the X10 family names none), a drag with one held 160 and up, and `?9` reports none of them. They are X's numbers: the Wayland seat's `BTN_SIDE` to `BTN_BACK`, "8 + b - BTN_SIDE" as X's input driver numbers them (`[[source: xf86-input-libinput-btn]]`), and AppKit's `buttonNumber` 3 to 6, as XQuartz gives "additional mouse buttons (4 and higher in AppKit) to buttons 8 and higher in X11" (`[[source: xquartz-x11application]]`). A button past the eleventh, where "the encoding is ambiguous", sends nothing and is counted in `mouse_unencodable`, as a position past X10's 223 is; xterm sends 12 to 15 as 192 to 195 under SGR, codes ctlseqs does not define. Until then each was a press of "3=release", `CSI < 3 ; x ; y M` (`buttons_8_to_11_add_128`, `a_button_past_the_eleventh_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_side_button_is_button_8_to_a_tracking_child`, `appkits_buttons_past_the_three_are_xs_8_to_11`). **Motion is reported per cell**, as ctlseqs' button-event text has it: "Motion events are reported only if the mouse pointer has moved to a different character cell". `Term::mouse_report` keeps the cell of the last report that went out -- a press or release as well as a move, as xterm's `EditorButton` sets `mouse_row`/`mouse_col` on every report (`[[source: xterm-button]]`) -- and drops a move into it, comparing the position and not the button or modifiers; a refused or unqueued report does not move it, and a tracking or encoding change and both resets forget it, where xterm forgets only at `VTInitialize` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file; practice). Presses and releases are never held back. Until 2026-10-09 it kept no last position and the macOS app's `Driver::mouse` hands it every AppKit move and drag, so a pointer inside one cell sent one identical report per event. The Linux app reports at every level since 2026-10-20, from `wl_pointer`'s frames (`a_tracking_child_gets_the_buttons_and_the_wheel`). `motion_inside_one_cell_is_reported_once`, `a_drag_inside_one_cell_is_reported_once`, `a_modifier_change_inside_the_cell_is_not_a_move`, `a_press_or_release_is_always_sent_and_moves_the_cell`, `a_refused_motion_does_not_move_the_cell`, `a_mode_change_or_a_reset_forgets_the_cell` |
| `CSI ? 1005 h/l` | UTF-8 mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. X10's bytes, each UTF-8 encoded past 127: one byte below one-based 96, two from 96 to 2015 (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "expanding the maximum encodable position from 223 to 2015"), and past 2015 refused and counted in `mouse_unencodable`, where xterm clamps and sends a `0` byte (`EmitMousePosition`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`). **The same bytes under `ESC % @`**: the mode is the encoding, ctlseqs names the cost ("will not pass through luit(1) unchanged") rather than a fallback, and xterm's emit path reads no encoding. Counted and dropped before. The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `utf8_encoding_is_x10_below_ninety_six_and_two_bytes_from_it`, `utf8_encoding_reaches_2015_and_refuses_past_it`, `utf8_encoding_ignores_the_input_encoding`, `utf8_mouse_reports_are_x10_until_127_and_utf8_after` |
| `CSI ? 1006 h/l` | SGR mouse encoding | done — no ordinate limit. **A set replaces the encoding in force and a reset undoes only its own**, xterm's rule for all four ("a reset is only effective against the matching mode", `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file); ctlseqs makes them "mutually exclusive" and says no more. `?1006 l` returned to X10 whatever was set until 2026-10-09. **Since 2026-11-01 a program that saves any of the four with XTSAVE and restores any of them gets back the encoding it had**, X10 included: one value, as xterm saves it (the `CSI ? s` row). The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `setting_an_encoding_replaces_the_one_in_force`, `resetting_an_encoding_undoes_only_that_encoding`, `a_set_selects_an_encoding_and_a_reset_undoes_only_its_own` |
| `CSI ? 1007 h/l` | alternate scroll | done — since 2026-10-09, `Term::alternate_scroll()`, read by the macOS app's wheel (`wheel::route`): on the alternate screen with no tracking, set sends the cursor keys through `Term::key`, so in DECCKM's form, and reset sends nothing (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`, "Wheel mice": "if Alternate Scroll mode is set, then cursor up/down controls are sent when the terminal is displaying the Alternate Screen Buffer"). **Set at power-on**, which is what baud did before it read the mode; xterm's `alternateScroll` resource defaults to `False` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), and ctlseqs leaves the initial state to the resource -- baud's default differs from xterm's. **Set again by `RIS`** since 2026-10-24, the initial state ECMA-48 8.3.105 returns to, and kept by `DECSTR`, whose list does not name it; xterm keeps it on both, as no `ReallyReset` path names it, a practice not followed for `RIS`. Tracking takes the wheel whatever it says. **The wheel across sends no keys**: ctlseqs gives Alternate Scroll "cursor up/down controls" alone, and with no tracking a sideways step does nothing on either screen, as in xterm and foot, where alacritty sends cursor left and right (`[[source: alacritty-0151-input-mod]]`). The Linux app's wheel reads it too since 2026-10-20 (`the_alternate_screens_wheel_is_the_cursor_keys`). `after_1007l_the_wheel_on_the_alternate_screen_sends_nothing`, `the_alternate_screen_sends_arrows_until_an_application_says_otherwise`, `tracking_and_the_primary_screen_ignore_1007`, `the_wheels_keys_are_the_cursor_keys_as_decckm_has_them`, `a_full_reset_sets_alternate_scroll_and_a_soft_one_keeps_it` |
| `CSI ? 1015 h/l` | urxvt mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. `CSI Cb ; Cx ; Cy M`: X10's button value, its `+ 32` and its release code kept, in decimal, and `M` for a release too (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "the same button encoding as X10, but printing it as a decimal integer rather than as a single byte"). herdr and zellij send it before `?1006`, which replaces it. Counted and dropped before. The Linux app reports in it too since 2026-10-20. `urxvt_is_the_x10_button_value_in_decimal`, `urxvt_mouse_reports_have_the_documented_shape`, `herdrs_start_ends_in_sgr_any_event_and_nothing_is_counted` |
| `CSI ? 1016 h/l` | SGR-Pixels mouse encoding | done — since 2026-10-09. `?1006`'s shape with the position in pixels (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "report position in pixels rather than character cells"), one-based on the wire as xterm writes it (`value + 1`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`). The pixel is the event's `PointerPx`: zero-based from the text area's first pixel, in the device pixels `CSI 16 t` and `?2048` answer in; the macOS app derives it and the cell in one function and clamps it to the text area, where xterm reports the border as `0` or less. An event with no pixel, or at `u16::MAX` where `ImagePx` saturates, is refused and counted, but for `?1001`'s short release report, which writes the region's one cell in cells and no mouse position (the `?1001` row). **Motion is gated by the cell, not the pixel**: button-event tracking's "moved to a different character cell" names no encoding and `?1016` changes only the format; xterm reports a move per pixel here, its row and column holding pixels in `SET_PIXEL_POSITION_MOUSE` (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), a practice baud departs from. Counted and dropped before. The core takes the pixel from whichever embedder puts one on `MouseEvent::px`: the macOS app, and since 2026-10-20 the Linux app, whose surface coordinates are its pixels with no buffer scale set and whose grid starts at the frame's first pixel. `sgr_pixels_motion_is_gated_by_the_cell_as_the_text_says`, `sgr_pixels_reports_the_pointer_in_pixels`, `sgr_pixels_refuses_an_event_with_no_pixel_position`, `sgr_pixel_mouse_reports_carry_the_pixel_one_based`, `a_point_names_its_pixel_and_the_cell_that_holds_it`, `a_wheel_under_1016_reports_the_pointer_in_pixels` |
| X10 mouse encoding (`ESC [ M Cb Cx Cy`) | the default, what tracking gives with no encoding mode set | done — positions past 223 refused and counted, not truncated. **A release is code 3** since 2026-10-09, ctlseqs' "3=release": it carried the released button, so under plain `?1000` a left release went out byte for byte as a left press. `an_x10_release_says_release_not_which_button`, `x10_family_releases_are_code_three_and_sgr_keeps_the_button` |
| `CSI ? 1016 $ p` — DECRQM on the mouse modes, `?9`, `?1000`-`?1003`, `?1005`-`?1007`, `?1015`, `?1016` | DECRQM | done — every tracking level and every encoding answers `1` only while it is the one in force, and `?1007` its flag, as xterm's `do_dec_rqm` answers them (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); `?9`, `?1005`, `?1007`, `?1015` and `?1016` answered `0`, not recognised, before. The answer is the core's state, so the Linux app gives the same answers, and since 2026-10-20 sends the reports they name. `decrqm_answers_every_mouse_mode`, `the_mouse_modes_land_the_same_at_every_read_split` |
| `CSI ? 1001 h/l` | highlight tracking | done — a tracking level, the handshake, the drawn region and the release reports; see the mode table. The "xterm will hang" warning describes an event loop, and `baud-core` has none: an event before the program's answer is normal tracking's. `under_sgr_the_reports_are_decimal`, `a_release_before_the_answer_is_normal_and_a_late_answer_is_ignored` |
| `CSI ? 1042 h/l` | bell urgency, xterm's `bellIsUrgent` | done — since 2026-10-14. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Enable Urgency window manager hint when Control-G is received, xterm.
