This enables the bellIsUrgent resource", and the reset disables it. `Term::bell_is_urgent()`, read by the macOS app's bell: a `BEL` while the window is not focused asks for the user's attention, `requestUserAttention` of the informational kind: "The dock icon will bounce for one second" (`[[source: apple-nsapplication-informationalrequest]]`), and to an application already active it "has no effect" (`[[source: apple-nsapplication-requestuserattention]]`) (`BEL` above). xterm likewise sets the hint only while unfocused (`setXUrgency`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`). **Reset at power-on**, the resource's default (`Bres(XtNbellIsUrgent, ..., False)`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file; `[[source: xterm-man]]`); **reset by `RIS`**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "set the modes into the reset state" (`[[source: ecma48]]`), where xterm keeps it, no `ReallyReset` path naming it -- a departure not followed; kept by `DECSTR`, whose list does not name it. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->bellIsUrgent)`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. The config's `bell = "none"` does not silence it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app asks the compositor to activate the window through `xdg-activation-v1`, with a token of its own, as foot marks a window urgent; the compositor decides whether it comes forward or is marked for attention (`BEL` above; `the_window_is_activated_with_a_token_it_asks_for_or_is_given`). `each_mode_sets_and_resets_alone`, `ris_resets_both_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_carry_both`, `the_bell_modes_land_the_same_at_every_read_split`, `an_unfocused_window_does_what_the_modes_say` |
| `CSI ? 1043 h/l` | raise on bell, xterm's `popOnBell` | done — since 2026-10-14. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Enable raising of the window when Control-G is received, xterm. This enables the popOnBell resource", and the reset disables it. `Term::pop_on_bell()`, read by the macOS app's bell: a `BEL` while the window is not focused orders it front with `orderFrontRegardless`, "without changing either the key window or the main window" (`[[source: apple-nswindow-orderfrontregardless]]`), as xterm's `XRaiseWindow` restacks without focus (`Bell`, `[[source: xterm-misc]]`); a minimized window is left alone, "If the window is iconified, this has no effect" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). A focused window is not raised, only an unfocused one; xterm raises whatever the focus. **Reset at power-on** (`Bres(XtNpopOnBell, ..., False)`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file), reset by `RIS` and kept by `DECSTR`, as `?1042` is. DECRQM answers `1` or `2`, as xterm's `MdBool(screen->poponbell)`; XTSAVE and XTRESTORE carry it. Before, it was counted as unsupported and DECRQM answered `0`. Since 2026-10-21 the Linux app sends the same activation `?1042` does: Wayland has no raise without focus, and the compositor decides whether the window comes forward (`BEL` above). `both_are_reset_at_power_on`, `each_mode_sets_and_resets_alone`, `ris_resets_both_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `a_minimized_window_is_not_raised_and_still_asks` |
| `CSI ? 2026 h/l` | synchronised output | done — exposed as `Term::synchronized_output()` |
| `CSI 3 J` | ED 3 erase saved lines | done — since 2026-10-09. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 3 -> Erase Saved Lines, xterm". The history goes and the screen, the cursor and the rendition stay (`erase_saved_lines_empties_the_history_and_keeps_the_screen_and_the_cursor`); **the primary's from either screen**, as xterm's `do_erase_display` zeroes its one `savedlines` count whichever buffer is shown (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`; `erase_saved_lines_on_the_alternate_screen_empties_the_primarys_history`); every ring row past the screen and the cut rows is erased, not only the count, for `RIS`'s reason (`erase_saved_lines_leaves_nothing_of_them_in_the_ring`). **The rows a shorter window cut below the cursor stay**: they are the bottom of the screen a growth brings back, not saved lines (`erase_saved_lines_keeps_the_rows_a_shrink_cut_below_the_window`). An image on an erased line goes. `Term::history_erasures` moves on it and on `RIS`, and the macOS app puts a window over the history on the live screen and settles a slide when it moves, however the read falls (`an_erased_history_puts_the_window_on_the_live_screen_however_the_read_falls`); since 2026-10-14 the terminal keeps that window and sends it live at the erasure's byte, from either screen (note V, V2; `an_erasure_sends_the_window_live_from_either_screen`); a selection naming an erased line is dropped or clipped to the screen by the clip every read applies. EL has no `3`: `CSI 3 K` stays counted |
| `CSI ? 3 J` | DECSED 3 selective erase saved lines | done — since 2026-10-09. "Selective Erase Saved Lines, xterm": DECSED over the history, so what DECSCA protected stays, the rest become spaces in their own rendition as DECSED's do, and the lines stay lines (`selective_erase_saved_lines_spares_what_decsca_protected`, `selective_erase_saved_lines_leaves_spaces_in_their_rendition`), from either screen. **xterm erases them whole** -- its `case 3` is one branch for both forms and ignores protection (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`) -- where DECSCA's text says DECSED cannot erase a protected character (`[[source: vt510-decsca]]`); baud follows the text, since 2026-10-10. Since 2026-10-21 each is counted, `Term::history_wipes`, so an embedder that keeps what it read of the history reads it again: the find field drops a match it wiped (`the_wipe_count_moves_on_each_selective_erase_of_the_saved_lines`, `the_list_kept_across_reads_is_the_list_read_afresh`) |
| `CSI ? s` | XTSAVE, `CSI ? Pm s`, save DEC private modes | done — since 2026-10-09. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps values are the same as for DECSET ... this uses a one-level cache ... Only those modes listed as parameters are saved." **Every mode DECSET acts on keeps the value `DECRQM` answers**, one slot each (`PrivateMode::slot`), but for two families, each saved as one value whichever member is listed, as xterm saves them: XTSAVE of any of `?1050`-`?1053`, `?1060` and `?1061` saves the keyboard in force, as xterm's `DP_KEYBOARD_TYPE` does (`xtsave_keeps_the_keyboard_family_as_one_value`), and since 2026-11-01 XTSAVE of any of `?1005`, `?1006`, `?1015` and `?1016` saves the mouse encoding in force, X10 included, as xterm's `DP_X_EXT_MOUSE` does (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 8226-8233): ctlseqs' "protocol encoding", the second of its "two sets of mutually exclusive modes" (`xtsave_keeps_the_mouse_encodings_as_one_value`); and one saved twice keeps the second (`the_cache_is_one_level_and_a_restore_keeps_it`); a mode DECSET does not act on -- unnamed, the read-only `?1020` to `?1023`, the permanent `?2027` -- is counted in `unsupported_param`, a parameter at a time, as DECSET counts it (`a_mode_decset_does_not_act_on_is_counted_by_both`). Held mode by mode against DECSET's own behaviour over `?0`-`?3000` and `?9999` (`every_mode_decset_changes_is_saved_and_restored_and_no_other_is`). The `?` keeps it from SCOSC and DECSLRM (`the_question_mark_keeps_them_apart_from_scosc_decstbm_and_decslrm`). **`RIS` empties the cache**, ECMA-48 § 8.3.105's "initial state" (`[[source: ecma48]]`), and `DECSTR` keeps it (`ris_empties_the_cache_and_decstr_keeps_it`). xterm saves a different set (`savemodes`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`): not `?2` or `?8`, and `?3` only under the `c132` resource `?40` sets, where baud saves `?3` whatever `?40` says and restores it through DECSET, so a restore acts only under `?40` -- following the text; one slot for `?1000`-`?1003` (`DP_X_MOUSE`, charproc.c 8212-8220) and one for `?9` (`DP_X_X10MSE`, 8091-8093), each holding the tracking level, where baud keeps one for each mode (XT-4); and it keeps the cache across `RIS`, where baud empties it for ECMA-48's initial state |
| `CSI ? r` | XTRESTORE, `CSI ? Pm r`, restore DEC private modes | done — since 2026-10-09. "The value of Ps previously saved is restored ... Only those modes listed as parameters are restored." Only those, except the two families XTSAVE keeps as one value: a restore of any keyboard brings back the keyboard saved, and since 2026-11-01 a restore of any mouse encoding brings back the encoding saved, X10 included, through DECSET of the one saved or DECRST of the one in force, so either can set or reset a mode that was not listed (`xtsave_keeps_the_keyboard_family_as_one_value`, `xtsave_keeps_the_mouse_encodings_as_one_value`). "Only those modes listed" read mode by mode would leave the others alone; baud reads the keyboards and the mouse encodings each as one of the text's "specific settings", as xterm restores them (`[[source: xterm-reset]]` 8656-8663 for the encodings), and keeps the tracking levels mode by mode: a choice the project made. **Through DECSET's own path**, so a restore has the effects setting or resetting the mode has -- `?1049` saves the cursor and clears, or leaves and restores it; `?6` homes; `?3` clears (`xtrestore_has_the_side_effects_of_setting_the_mode`) -- and `DECRQM` answers from the state it left (`xtrestore_brings_back_what_xtsave_saved`). **Only a mode whose value differs from the saved one is set or reset**, so restoring a tracking family puts back the level saved in either order, a restore at the encoding in force keeps the cell a report was last sent from, which a DECSET of it forgets, and `?1049` restored set on the alternate screen does not clear it (`a_restore_changes_only_what_differs_from_the_saved_value`). The text is silent on a value already in force; xterm re-applies `?6` whatever its value, and `?3` too under its `c132` resource (`restoremodes`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`), and baud changes nothing. **A mode never saved restores nothing** (`only_what_was_saved_and_listed_is_restored`): a bare `CSI ? 2 r` stays in ANSI. xterm restores `?1049` as the bare switch of `?47`, where baud follows the text; it restores the tracking level saved under any of the four mouse modes (XT-4) and a never-saved mode from a zeroed array -- `?25 r` hides the cursor (XT-1) (`restoremodes`, `VTInitialize`'s `memset`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`): each a choice the project made. The `?` keeps it from DECSTBM. Generated programs reach both through `?25`'s atoms (`properties.rs`) |
| `CSI ? 25 h/l` | DECTCEM cursor visibility | done — state exposed via `cursor_visible()`. A hide that is exactly `ESC [ ? 2 5 l` with a complete `CUP` or `HVP` straight before it also records where the cursor is as `placed_cursor()` -- helix hides its cursor for good and moves it to the selection first on every frame, and an embedder's trail follows the placement; `?25h`, a screen switch, either reset and a change of geometry forget it (`baud-core/src/placed.rs`, `tests/placed_cursor.rs`) |
| `CSI > 4 ; 2 m` | XTMODKEYS, `CSI > Pp ; Pv m`, every key-modifier resource | done — since 2026-10-19 an application can set how the cursor, function, keypad, modifier and special keys carry their modifiers, not only the ordinary keys: `Pp` 0 `modifyKeyboard`, 1 `modifyCursorKeys`, 2 `modifyFunctionKeys`, 3 `modifyKeypadKeys`, 4 `modifyOtherKeys` (levels 0-3, 3 sending unmodified keys too, ctlseqs' "space sends CSI 2 7 ; 1 ; 3 2 ~"), 6 `modifyModifierKeys`, 7 `modifySpecialKeys` (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), each read by `Term::key` as xterm's `Input` reads it (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`): modifyCursorKeys and modifyFunctionKeys -1 to 4 as `modifyCursorKey` gives them, the extended form at 4 for any resource -- `CSI 27 ; mod ; code ~` or its `CSI u`, the code the key's keysym through `keysym2ucs` (`[[source: xterm-keysym2ucs-c]]`). `modifyKeyboard` is stored and reported and changes no key, as on xterm's default keyboard, which never reads it. An omitted `Pv` resets one resource and no parameter all seven, as the text says, where xterm's loop takes 1-5; `CSI > 4 ; Pv : mask m` sets modifyOtherKeys' mask, as xterm parses it. The mask is the parameter's bits, Shift 1, Alt 2, Control 4, Meta 8, as ctlseqs' "a mask of modifier bits to factor out of the parameter encoding" reads; xterm applies ignore_now to the X event state (Shift 1, Lock 2, Control 4, Mod1 8), so the two agree on Shift and Control only. A key whose one modifier is masked goes to the base layer at levels 1 and 2 and, at 3, is still a sequence with the modifier factored out: Shift-a under `CSI > 4 ; 3 : 1 m` is `CSI 27 ; 1 ; 97 ~`, as xterm's min_mod 0 there gives it. **A value no text documents is refused and counted**, not rounded. Open departure: ctlseqs' own example of the mask, `CSI > 4 : 1 m`, which puts it on `Pp`, is refused and counted; xterm's `CASE_SET_MOD_FKEYS` reads the mask only as `Pv`'s sub-parameter, and baud reads it as xterm's code does. Open departure: a vendor keysym, such as a media key, sends nothing under modifySpecialKeys 4, where ctlseqs maps those keysyms to the private use area from U+F0000; a u16 parameter cannot carry the number, and no driver sends those keys today. Open departure: at modifyOtherKeys 3 Shift plus a letter sends the unshifted code (`CSI 27 ; 2 ; 97 ~`) where xterm sends the keysym's (65). Open departure: under `CSI > 4 ; 3 : 1 m` Shift-Tab sends `CSI 27 ; 1 ; 9 ~`, an unmodified Tab's bytes, where xterm's Shift-Tab is the keysym ISO_Left_Tab, which is not an ordinary key, so the mask never applies to it and its function-key branch sends `CSI 27 ; 2 ; 9 ~` (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]` 1354-1362); for a plain Tab keysym xterm sends what baud sends. `modify_other_keys_3_sends_every_ordinary_key`, `modify_cursor_keys_each_value`, `modify_function_keys_each_value`, `the_keypad_modifier_and_special_resources_act_at_4`, `the_ignore_mask_factors_a_modifier_out`, `an_undocumented_value_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_texts_mask_example_is_refused_and_counted`, `the_worked_examples_are_what_baud_sends` |
| `CSI > 1 n` | disable key modifier options, `CSI > Ps n` | done — since 2026-10-19. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "This control sequence corresponds to a resource value of "-1", which cannot be set with the other sequence ... If the parameter is omitted, modifyFunctionKeys is disabled. When modifyFunctionKeys is disabled, xterm uses the modifier keys to make an extended sequence of function keys rather than adding a parameter": Shift-F1 is F11, Control-F1 F21, `CSI 42 ~` as xterm numbers keys past F20; modifyCursorKeys -1 drops the modifier. The sources conflict on which modifier adds what: `[[source: xterm-man]]` says that at modifyFunctionKeys 0 "Control adds the value given by the ctrlFKeys resource. Shift adds twice", and xterm's input.c does the reverse at -1 (`mfkNone`), Shift adding ctrl_fkeys and Control twice it (`[[source: xterm-input-c]]`); baud follows input.c, so Shift-F1 is F11. `the_controls_reset_set_and_disable`, `modify_function_keys_each_value` |
| `CSI > 4 ; 1 f` | XTFMTKEYS, `CSI > Pp ; Pv f`, every resource's format | done — `Pv` picks `CSI 27 ; mod ; code ~` or `[[source: fixterms]]`'s `CSI code ; mod u`, for each of the seven resources since 2026-10-19 (`formatCursorKeys` and the rest, read at 4, `[[source: xterm-man]]`). **This is the only route to the `CSI u` form**, which defines no negotiation of its own |
| `CSI > 1 s` | XTSHIFTESCAPE, `CSI > Ps s`, Shift over mouse tracking | done — since 2026-10-19. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 0 -> allow shift-key to override mouse protocol. Ps = 1 -> conditionally allow shift-key as modifier in mouse protocol", 2 and 3 "disallowed in the control sequence" and counted, an omitted `Ps` 0. At 0, the default, Shift takes every pointer event from a program tracking the mouse, the wheel's included, to the selection or the screen's wheel (the history, or the cursor keys under `?1007`), as xterm's `ShiftOverride` does (`[[source: xterm-button-c]]`); at 1 only the left button, the one the selection binds, and a Shift middle or right press or wheel goes to the program with its Shift bit. An explicit 0 is 0, as the text gives it, where xterm's `one_if_default` reads it as 1. Kept by DECSTR; **since 2026-10-25 RIS returns it to 0**, the state at power-on (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105), where xterm writes `shift_escape` nowhere else, a practice not followed; the `>` keeps it from SCOSC and DECSLRM. `Term::shift_escape`; both drivers' pointers read it. `xtshiftescape_is_the_state_the_pointer_reads`, `xtshiftescape_1_hands_shift_only_the_selection_button`, `shift_takes_the_wheel_from_tracking_only_under_xtshiftescape_0` |
| `CSI > 2 p` | XTSMPOINTER, `CSI > Ps p`, when the pointer hides as the user types | done — since 2026-10-20, **typing hides the mouse pointer until the mouse moves, unless a program is using the mouse**, and a program can choose otherwise. `[[source: ctlseqs]]`: "Ps = 0 -> never hide the pointer. Ps = 1 -> hide if the mouse tracking mode is not enabled. Ps = 2 -> always hide the pointer, except when leaving the window. Ps = 3 -> always hide the pointer, even if leaving/entering the window. If no parameter is given, xterm uses the default, which is 1"; built since 2026-10-20, with xterm's default. A value past 3 is refused and counted, where xterm's `CASE_HIDE_POINTER` stores any value; the first parameter alone. The core keeps the mode (`Term::pointer_mode`) and answers whether a key that sends bytes hides the pointer (`Term::hides_pointer_on_key`): "tracking" is any of `?9`, `?1000`-`?1003` and DEC's locator, xterm's `send_mouse_pos`. When it comes back is xterm's, but for a press (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`, `xtermShowPointer`, `unselectwindow`, `xevents`; `[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): a move, at every mode; crossing the window's edge or losing the keyboard, below 3; a program turning tracking on or changing it, a new `OSC 22` shape and `RIS`; a key under a mode that no longer hides; a button press, at every mode, as xterm(1) documents the resource: "It will be redisplayed if the user moves the mouse, or clicks one of its buttons" (`[[source: xterm-man]]`, `pointerMode`), where xterm's `xevents` passes `ButtonPress` by, its departure from that text, which baud does not follow. Power-on and `RIS` are `[pointer] hide_while_typing`, `"on"` (1) by default or `"off"` (0), xterm's `pointerMode` resource; `DECSTR` keeps the mode, as xterm's man page lists it under the full reset only (`[[source: xterm-man]]`). **macOS** hides it with `NSCursor.setHiddenUntilMouseMoves(_:)` (`[[source: apple-nscursor-sethiddenuntilmousemoves]]`), which shows it on a move by itself; **Linux** with `wl_pointer.set_cursor` and no surface, "If surface is NULL, the pointer image is hidden" (`[[source: wayland]]`), and at mode 3 again at each enter. xterm hides at every key press its `Input` handles, a modifier's included; baud at a key that sends bytes, "as the user types". Counted as unsupported before (`xtsmpointer_is_acted_on_not_counted`, `each_value_sets_when_a_key_hides_the_pointer`, `a_value_past_three_is_refused_and_counted`, `ris_returns_the_embedders_pointer_mode_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `xtsmpointer_is_the_same_at_every_split`, `a_key_hides_it_and_motion_shows_it`, `a_press_shows_it_at_every_mode`, `crossing_shows_it_but_at_mode_3`, `tracking_turned_on_shows_it`, `the_pointer_hides_with_no_surface_and_a_shape_shows_it`) |
| `CSI = 1 ; 1 u`, `CSI ? u`, `CSI > 1 u`, `CSI < 1 u` | kitty progressive enhancement | **done -- all five flags, and the query is answered.** `[[source: kitty-keyboard]]` detects support by whether the query is *answered*, so a reply is the claim: `CSI ? u` now answers `CSI ? flags u`, made only after `Term::key` honours every enhancement (disambiguate, event types, alternate keys, all-keys, associated text) for text, functional, keypad and the Private Use Area keys. `CSI = / > / <` maintain the per-screen flag stack. Its own words -- "It does not make sense to implement only a subset" -- is why this was all-or-nothing and the query stayed silent through every partial increment. `the_query_is_answered_with_the_current_flags`. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset empties both screens' stacks**, so a program that crashed with the protocol on leaves the shell's keys as at power-on once the user types `reset`, the case the spec keeps Enter, Tab and Backspace legacy for; `DECSTR` keeps them (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105; `ris_empties_both_kitty_keyboard_stacks_and_decstr_keeps_them`) |
| `CSI ? 4 m` / `CSI > 4 g` | XTQMODKEYS / XTQFMTKEYS | done — since 2026-10-19 an application can ask which key-modifier format is in force and send the answer back to restore it, except for a disabled resource (the open departure below). Each answers in xterm's shape, `report_mod_fkeys` and `report_fmt_fkeys` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`'s file): `CSI > Pp ; Pv m` and `CSI > Pp ; Pv f`, the value included, so the reply is the XTMODKEYS or XTFMTKEYS that sets it, one reply a parameter as xterm loops over them (`xtqmodkeys_answers_modify_other_keys_with_its_value`, `xtqfmtkeys_answers_format_other_keys_with_its_value`). `[[source: ctlseqs]]` gives the reply's shape without the value at lines 1355-1371 for XTQMODKEYS (the shape `CSI > Pp m` is at 1371) and at lines 883-899 for XTQFMTKEYS (`CSI > Pp f` at 899); xterm's code sends it. **Every resource is answered** since 2026-10-19; a `Pp` naming none with the value xterm writes for its `DEFAULT`, -1 printed unsigned, 65535, and counted; a disabled one as xterm writes it, `CSI > Pp ; 65535 m`, the form xterm's code writes, since ctlseqs gives no other. Open departure: that answer cannot be fed back to restore the state, because baud refuses 65535 as a value (`each_queried_resource_is_answered_or_counted`, `every_resource_answers_as_xterm_reports_it`). **The XTQFMTKEYS query is `CSI > Pp g`**, the form xterm 411's parse table reads (`[[source: xterm-vtprstbl-c]]`, `dec2_table`); ctlseqs line 883 documents `CSI ? Pp g`, which xterm ignores, and baud counts it |
| `APC G a=t` / `a=T` / `a=q` | kitty graphics: transmit, transmit and display, query | done — since 2026-10-16 (note V, V19 steps 5-6; `[[source: kitty-graphics]]`). An `APC` whose body begins `G` is routed to `baud_domain::KittyStream` by its first byte, the graph unchanged. `f=24` and `f=32` are base64 decoded straight into the image's reserved arena span, `o=z` inflated into it (or into the PNG scratch) as its chunks arrive, `f=100` read whole from the scratch; chunks of any length, a continuation's keys but `m` and `q` ignored, a delete aborting an upload in flight, a cancelled chunk aborting and counted. Every declared size -- `s` x `v`, `S`, a PNG's `IHDR` -- is checked against the arena before anything is reserved, refused, counted and answered. A PNG and inflated pixels are placed, and the cursor moved, at the last chunk and decoded in steps by `Term::decode_step`, shown and answered when whole; through a file medium, decoded whole at the terminator and only then placed (see `APC G t=f`); a query is decoded at its terminator, before the next byte, into the probe below, and stores nothing. Each image is marked by its own pixels. Replies `ESC _ G i=..[,I=..][,p=..];OK` or the error, only for a command naming `i` or `I`, `q=1` silencing `OK` and `q=2` all, always the 7-bit `ESC _ G ... ESC \` the text writes, whatever S8C1T says (`replies_keep_the_seven_bit_shape_under_s8c1t`; following S8C1T is an open question); `i` with `I` is EINVAL, an `I` alone gets a fresh id. Re-sending an id deletes the old image and its placements; a nameless image goes with its last placement and at the next transmission, as kitty does. **The quota is the text's** ("Image persistence and storage quotas" and "Deleting images"): a new image that finds no slot or no arena gap deletes the oldest kitty image with no placement, on screen or in the history, and once none is left, since 2026-11-01, the oldest with one, its placements going with it, a placeholder's virtual placement and the relative placements under them included, and the rows it covered drawn again, until it fits, each counted in `images_evicted`; none it needs goes: the parent a put names with `P`, the image a frame is added to, and every image their placements hang under, whose delete would take theirs with it (`the_quota_keeps_the_parent_a_new_image_is_put_against`, `the_quota_keeps_the_image_a_frame_is_added_to_and_its_parent`), and where more than 32 would be kept none goes; when deleting every other kitty image would not make space none goes and it is refused ENOSPC and counted once (`a_new_image_deletes_the_oldest_unplaced_image_to_make_space`, `once_no_unplaced_image_is_left_the_oldest_placed_image_goes`, `with_nothing_to_delete_the_new_image_is_refused_and_counted_once`, `the_quota_takes_a_held_image_last_and_its_virtual_placement_with_it`, `a_picture_the_quota_deletes_is_taken_off_an_incremental_frame`). Oldest is by when an image was stored, where kitty orders the placed ones by when each was last added or put (`apply_storage_quota()`, `[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`), and baud deletes one at a time until the new image fits, where kitty first deletes every image with no placement. A Sixel or an iTerm2 picture is never deleted for space, refused and counted instead when no room is left (settled 2026-11-01), so a session that fills the arena with them can push kitty pictures off the screen. **A query stores nothing** -- it "will not replace an existing image with the same id, nor will it store the image" -- so since 2026-10-16 it decodes in a 64x64 probe outside the arena and its quota, answered on a full arena, deleting and trimming nothing and moving no epoch; a larger query decodes in an arena gap, deleting nothing, else ENOSPC (`a_query_on_a_full_arena_is_answered_and_deletes_nothing` and the five after it). **The app's default arena holds "at least a few full screen images"**: 95 MiB, three 3840x2160 screens, since 2026-10-16, at no idle cost, the arena coming from zeroed memory (`host::IMAGE_BUDGET`, `the_default_budget_holds_three_3840x2160_screens`). A reply the stream's outbox cannot hold is dropped whole and counted in `KittyCounts::replies_dropped`. Open departure: a `t=d` PNG is reserved, and placed, at its last chunk, before its stepped decode, so one whose header parses and whose later data does not decode has already deleted older images for its space (image 7 unplaced at 3614 pixels, a 21x23 PNG, a 4096-pixel arena: `a=T,i=1,f=100` answers EBADPNG, then `a=p,i=7` answers ENOENT). `f=24` and `f=32` are reserved before their data arrives, so short data, a broken `o=z` stream or a missing raw file deletes for space as well. The text deletes older images only "to make space for the new one", and kitty, which applies its quota only after an add, deletes nothing for one that fails (`grman_handle_command`, `[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`). Chunk-invariant at every read size and two-read split across the body, base64 quanta, zlib blocks and `m=1` chunks (`tests/kitty_graphics.rs`) |
| `APC G a=p` | kitty graphics: put a stored image | **partial** — since 2026-10-16. By `i` or the newest `I`, ENOENT when none; `(i, p)` replaces the placement with that pair; the cursor moves right by the columns and down by the rows the placement is drawn in, `C=1` not at all; a count not given is the cells the drawn image reaches, where kitty adds `X` to the box (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]` `update_dest_rect`). Kitty moves down by the rows less one, its practice and not followed. **Drawn as the text lays it out** since 2026-10-16 ("Controlling displayed image layout"), on the CPU and both Metal paths from one description (`KittyPlace::draw`) and one set of nearest-neighbour tables (`Renderer::image_map`): the source rectangle `x y w h` intersected with the image; `c` alone or `r` alone scaled with the other from the aspect, to the nearest pixel, the `X`/`Y` offset not added to the box; both letterboxed or pillarboxed and centred, where kitty stretches; `z` negative under the text and over the backgrounds, and the order by `z`, then the lower client id under, then placement order, where kitty orders by its internal id. A margin scroll, a `DECSLRM` rectangle's included, moves a kitty placement only when its shown rows, and under side margins its columns, are all between the margins and clips the rows it takes past them, "Interaction with other terminal actions"; `IL` and `DL` the same, baud's choice; under a top-anchored region, which moves the window, the rows under it are put back with their images and one across its bottom margin stays (`a_top_anchored_region_leaves_the_images_it_does_not_scroll`). Each pixel is the source texel under its centre (nearest, an interim and open choice; kitty and ghostty sample linearly). `baud-render/tests/kitty_draw.rs`, the `kitty_draw_gate` of `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` holding all three paths to the text's pixels, `a_margin_scroll_moves_only_an_image_inside_the_margins_and_clips_it` and `a_rectangle_scroll_moves_only_an_image_inside_its_margins_and_clips_it`. A `z` strictly below `INT32_MIN/2` is drawn under the text and under every cell whose background is not the default one, whose background is composited over it at its own alpha with nothing but the window under the image there, so on the glass the image shows through a dimmed coloured cell as the desktop does and an opaque one hides it (since 2026-10-16, kitty's blend, `[[source: kitty-cell-slang]]`); a cell is default when the colour its background is drawn in, after `SGR 7` and `?5` exchange the pair, equals the default background's colour as stored, the theme's or `OSC 11`'s, so an explicit colour of that value is default and the comparison follows `OSC 11`, a default cell under one of `SGR 7` and `?5` is not and under both is, and a selected cell or one under a block cursor is not (the value, since 2026-10-16, as kitty's `cell_has_default_bg`, kitty's `is_reversed` keeping an `SGR 7` cell special where baud decides it by the value, `[[source: kitty-background-slang]]`); a placeholder's image never goes below its own cells' backgrounds ("visible if the image is transparent"). `a_z_below_half_int32_min_draws_under_non_default_backgrounds`, `a_z_of_half_int32_min_exactly_stays_over_every_background`, `an_explicit_background_of_the_default_colour_shows_a_deep_image`, `on_the_glass_a_coloured_cell_is_blended_over_a_deep_image` and the `kitty_below_gate` and `kitty_picks_gate` of `cells.rs`. A placement with `P` is relative since 2026-10-23 (the row after `a=d`). **A picture placed one cell at a time is drawn whole**, since 2026-10-18: the table holds a placement for every cell of the largest screen the storage displays (64 at least, `NPLACEMENT`), where it held 64 and yazi 26.9.1's preview, an `a=p` per cell, showed one row and part of the next; the text sets no limit on placements and its quota is on image data; allocated zeroed and walked only to its highest live slot, so an idle terminal pays nothing for it (`a_picture_placed_one_cell_at_a_time_is_placed_whole` and the three after it, the `kitty_per_cell_gate` of `cells.rs`). The placement a put replaces, and the one a delete naming `i` and `p` takes, is found by the pair through an index beside the table, a step a lookup where it was a walk of every placement before it, since 2026-10-18 (`a_put_and_a_delete_find_their_placement_by_its_key`, `puts_and_deletes_in_any_order_leave_each_pair_found`); a placement goes and frees its slot when its last shown row leaves the retained history, or the alternate screen's top, its image kept while an id or number can name it (`a_preview_scrolled_out_of_the_history_frees_its_slots_and_keeps_its_image`); so the lower rows of a tall picture stay over the text the history still holds, "images must be scrolled along with text", kitty's `scroll_filter_func` (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`), since 2026-10-18: it went with its anchor row before (`a_tall_picture_goes_when_its_last_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_tall_picture_on_the_alternate_screen_goes_with_its_last_row`, `a_cut_picture_goes_when_its_last_shown_row_leaves_the_history`, `a_reflow_keeps_a_picture_past_the_far_end_and_drops_it_with_its_rows`, `erasing_the_history_keeps_a_picture_with_rows_on_screen`, and `kitty_history_gate` in `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` on all three paths); a Sixel goes with its last row too (the Sixel row). **A reverse scroll moves a picture down with its text**, since 2026-10-25: a full-screen `RI` or `SD` takes the history's newest line back as the blank top row, and a picture anchored there is drawn on that row, a tall one's lower rows one row down over the text that moved, where it went with the line before; and a line feed that brings back the row a reverse scroll pushed under the primary's window brings its picture back over the blank row. Both are kitty's: its `INDEX_DOWN` moves every image down a row and drops none, and its `INDEX_UP` moves one back (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`; `a_reverse_scroll_moves_a_picture_on_the_line_it_takes_back_down`, `a_picture_pushed_below_the_screen_comes_back_with_its_row`, `a_reverse_scroll_moves_a_picture_on_the_top_row_one_row_down`, `a_reverse_scroll_that_blanks_an_image_row_drops_the_image`). **Open**: the alternate screen keeps no row under its window, so a kitty picture pushed off its bottom goes, where kitty keeps it off the screen and a line feed brings it back. **Open**: a Sixel on the line a reverse scroll takes back still goes with it, a tall one whose lower rows are on screen included, where xterm drops no graphic (the doc of `ImageStore::expire`). **Open beside it**: baud's `ED 3` keeps a picture with rows on screen, as the text's "other commands to erase text must have no effect on graphics" asks, where kitty clears every image (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`). **A full placement table** places nothing, moves no cursor and, since 2026-10-16, answers `ENOSPC:No room for another placement` where it answered `OK`, the text naming no code for a full table and ENOSPC the code it gives for storage run out; through a file medium, the reply is the single EBADF (see `APC G t=f`); an `a=T` answers for the placement it asked for in the same way, at its terminator or, deferred, when its decode completes, and through a file medium the reply is the single EBADF (see `APC G t=f`), where kitty builds the `a=T` reply before it places (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`) and so answers `OK` (`a_placement_a_full_table_refuses_is_answered_enospc`, `a_transmission_whose_placement_is_refused_is_answered_enospc`, at every read size and two-read split, `q` absent, 1 and 2). `U=1` is the row below |
| `APC G a=p,U=1` / `a=T,U=1`, `U+10EEEE` | kitty graphics: Unicode placeholders | done — since 2026-10-16 (note V, V19; `[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "Unicode placeholders"). **A virtual placement** is kept in the kitty stream's own table of 256 (`KITTY_VIRTUALS`), apart from the placements on screen, holds a reference on its image so the quota deletes it only once no image without a placement is left, and then with the image, moves no cursor whatever `C` says, is answered as a put is, and is replaced by the same image and non-zero placement id; a full table refuses it, counts it (`KittyCounts::virtuals_refused`) and, since 2026-10-16, answers `ENOSPC:No room for another virtual placement`, never `OK` (`a_virtual_placement_a_full_table_refuses_is_answered_enospc`; the text names no code for it, kitty having no such table, and ENOSPC is the code it gives for storage run out); an image no id names -- `a=T,U=1` with neither `i` nor `I` -- is refused and counted and answered nothing, the text replying only to a command that named an image. **A U+10EEEE cell** is one cell wide (private use, narrow whatever the ambiguous width); a listed diacritic after it, up to three, goes into the cell's own content word, not the cluster store (`Content::placeholder`, since 2026-10-16: the store dropped 2366 of an 80x24 kitten picture's 5760 marks), any other mark sends it to the store, and a copy reads back what was written. **Resolved as drawn** (`Term::placeholder_runs`, `PlaceholderRuns`): the image id from the foreground (24-bit value or palette index) with the third diacritic's top byte, the placement id from the underline colour, the row and column from the diacritics in `[[source: kitty-rowcolumn-diacritics]]` (a table generated by `scripts/gen-placeholder-table.py`, `placeholder_table_matches_the_pinned_list`); a missing value inherited from the cell immediately to the left when the two ids match (where the text's words compare "colors") and the rest follows, else row 0, column 0, byte 0, kitty's practice (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`). **Drawn** on the software compositor and both Metal paths as one `ImagePlacement` a run: the virtual placement's whole image fitted into its `c` x `r` cells, aspect kept and centred, a count not given the image's size in cells, `x y w h X Y` unused, each run its strip, clipped to its cells; the placeholder and its diacritics are never drawn as glyphs, and with no image to show the cell is its background (since 2026-10-16; TUS 5.3's `.notdef` the other option). `tests/kitty_placeholder.rs` in baud-core and baud-render, the `kitty_placeholder_gate` of `baud-present-macos/tests/cells.rs` holding the three arms to a placement of the same box, and `kittens_placeholders_place_the_picture`, kitten 0.49.2's own output replayed. Chunk-invariant at every read size to 8 and every two-read split. **In a smooth scroll's leaving row** too, since 2026-10-16: the line above the window resolves its runs from its own cells, `view(back + 1).row(0)`, the row the cell path slides in, so a slide shows the strip that row showed on screen, and a settled frame clips it above the top; with no line above the window, an empty history or the alternate screen, nothing is placed there (`the_leaving_row_places_the_strip_it_placed_on_screen` in baud-render; `kitty_placeholder_leaving_gate`, the cell arm over the ring and over the window's copy at a whole and a half row's slide, and all four arms settled, at `z` 0, -1 and below `INT32_MIN/2`). A placeholder image's `z` is the virtual placement's, never below its own cells' backgrounds. Horizontal scrolling and overlapping images are the text's "will not work" |
| `APC G a=d` | kitty graphics: delete | done — since 2026-10-16: every specifier of the "Deleting images" table, `a i n c p q r x y z`, and since 2026-10-23 the frames, `f` (the row for `a=f`), lowercase removing placements and keeping named images, uppercase freeing what it leaves unplaced, `I`, `N` and `R` freeing an image only stored, as kitty's `handle_delete_command` does (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`); no reply. Text does not erase a kitty image and `ED 2` and `RIS` clear them ("Interaction with other terminal actions"); a Sixel keeps the text-erases rule. **A virtual placement** goes only by `d=i`, `I`, `n`, `N`, `r` and `R`, uppercase freeing an image it alone held; "a, c, p, q, x, y, z and their capital variants never affect virtual placements", nor do `ED 2` and the alternate screen; re-sending its image's id takes it, `RIS` everything (`only_the_id_number_and_range_deletes_reach_a_virtual_placement`, `a_virtual_placement_outlives_the_screen_and_not_its_image`) |
| `APC G a=p,P=...,Q=...`, `H`, `V` | kitty graphics: relative placements | done — since 2026-10-23, **a picture can be pinned to another picture, and moves and goes with it** (`[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "Relative placements"). A placement made with `P` and `Q` sits at its parent's top-left cell plus `H` columns and `V` rows, "Negative values move left and up", hanging over the screen's edge where they put it there; with no `Q`, against the parent image's oldest placement (kitty takes the first of its map, `[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]` `handle_put_command`), a re-put keeping its age as kitty updates the one ref in place. It moves with its parent -- a re-put of the parent's pair, a margin scroll, a reflow -- and goes when its parent goes, with the placements under it and, when it was its image's last placement, the image: "If its parent is deleted, it is deleted as well. If the image that the relative placement is a placement of, has no more placements, the image is deleted as well." Under a virtual parent it is drawn at the least row and the least column of the placeholders showing it, and not while none is on screen. The cursor never moves, whatever `C` says. Refused in kitty's codes and shape, its texts less the ids kitty prints, nothing placed: ENOPARENT for a parent image that does not exist, one with no placement on this screen or virtual (asked before `Q`, as kitty asks), or a `Q` none of its placements has; EINVAL for a virtual placement with a parent and for a placement named as its own parent; ECYCLE; ETOODEEP past eight parents, kitty's limit where the text sets "at least 8". A delete naming a cell, a row, a column, the cursor's cell or the visible screen finds it where it is drawn, the text's "Delete all placements that intersect a specific cell": under a virtual parent the delete reads the placeholders on the screen as the drawing does, and one whose placeholders are all off it is drawn nowhere and intersects nothing. kitty tests the cursor cell it was put at, which need not be where it is drawn, a departure from "intersect", so the text's reading is built. Held at every read size (`a_relative_placement_sits_at_its_parent_plus_h_and_v`, `a_re_put_parent_is_still_the_oldest`, `every_refusal_is_answered_in_kittys_shape`, `under_a_virtual_parent_a_delete_finds_it_where_it_is_drawn`, `under_a_virtual_parent_shown_nowhere_it_is_not_visible`, `eight_parents_are_allowed_and_a_ninth_is_too_deep`, `a_parents_deletion_takes_its_children_and_their_images`), through a margin scroll and a reflow (`a_parent_that_moves_takes_its_children`) and on all three draw paths (`kitty_relative_gate` in `cells.rs`) |
| `APC G a=f` / `a=a` / `a=c`, `d=f` / `d=F` | kitty graphics: animation | done — since 2026-10-23, **animated pictures play in the terminal, and hold still under Reduce Motion or, on macOS, while Animated Images is off, unless `[images] animation` says `"on"` or `"off"`** (`[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "Animation"). `a=f` frames arrive as an image's data does -- `f=24`, `f=32`, `f=100`, `o=z`, chunked, the file mediums (`an_animation_frame_reads_through_a_file_medium`) -- and are answered with the frame's number, `i=..,r=N;OK`, as kitty's `finish_command_response` adds it; a new frame is made on its canvas, the base frame `c` or the background `Y` "a 32-bit RGBA color", the data composed at `x`, `y`, alpha blended unless `X=1` or the data is RGB, kitty's blend to the bit (`[[source: kitty-simd-string-h]]`); a frame covering the whole image with no base is the data as sent, `Y` colouring only "pixels not specified in the frame data"; `r` edits a frame in place; the gap is `z`, 40 ms when not given, a negative `z` gapless, the root frame's zero until set. Every frame is stored whole after its image in the image's own span, so an animation takes one slot however many frames it has and is bounded by the arena, older images deleted for room as the quota deletes them, those with no placement first, and never the animation's own nor an image it hangs under. A span grows by moving to a new gap while the old one is still live, and the incoming frame's data needs room too, so an animation holds fewer than half the arena's frames: measured, a 32x32 animation in an arena of 12 frames and 64 words takes 5 frames and the sixth is ENOSPC, and a 1920x1080 one in the app's 95 MiB, twelve frames' worth, takes 5 too. A frame is a snapshot coalesced when it is made; kitty keeps one as its data over its base frame and coalesces when it shows it, so after the base is edited, composed into or deleted kitty's dependent frame changes and baud's does not, practice not followed (the text says only that frames are "created by composing the transmitted data onto a background canvas"); kitty keeps frames on disk under a quota five times its image quota, practice not followed. `a=c` composes a rectangle from frame `r` onto frame `c`, ENOENT for a frame neither names and EINVAL for a rectangle out of bounds or one frame's rectangles overlapping, answered `OK`; `d=f` deletes frame `r`, the root when none is named, frame 2 taking its place, and `d=F` an image of one frame; deleting the frame shown shows the one after it, where kitty's `handle_delete_frame_command()` moves its index back and shows the one before, the text silent, practice not followed. `a=a` sets frame `r`'s gap, makes frame `c` current, stops, loads (waiting at the last frame for more) or runs (`s`), and loops `v` (1 for ever, `n` `n - 1` times), answered only with an error, as kitty answers it. **Frames change on the display link's clock** on macOS and on the loop's deadline on Linux, never on a timer of their own (`Term::animate_images`, `Term::next_image_frame`): one frame a tick once its gap has passed, past gapless frames, a loop counted at the wrap, only while the image is placed somewhere, kitty's `scan_active_animations`. **Under reduced motion, and on macOS while Animated Images is off, the frames hold where they are** and a program's own frame changes still show, the protocol state kept so the animation goes on when the setting allows it again (the text is silent; Apple's "reducing automatic and repetitive animations" and "Pause animations in animated images in your app when people turn off the Animated Images setting", `[[source: apple-hig-accessibility]]`). Since 2026-10-23 Animated Images is read through `AXAnimatedImagesEnabled()` and followed when its notification arrives (`[[source: apple-accessibility-animated-images]]`), and `[images] animation` decides: `"os"` by default as above, `"on"` always playing, `"off"` always holding (`an_animation_plays_as_the_setting_motion_and_animated_images_say`, `the_animation_setting_plays_or_holds_whatever_the_motion`). Held at every read size (`a_frame_is_made_and_answered_with_its_number`, `every_frame_refusal_is_answered_in_kittys_shape`, `animation_commands_are_the_same_at_every_read_size`), by the clock (`the_clock_runs_a_placed_animation`, `loading_waits_at_the_last_frame`, `a_held_clock_moves_nothing_and_the_client_still_does`, `a_kitty_animation_wakes_at_each_gap_and_holds_under_reduced_motion`) and on all three draw paths (`kitty_animation_gate` in `cells.rs`) |
| `APC G t=f` / `t=t` / `t=s` | kitty graphics: file, temporary file and shared-memory mediums | done — since 2026-10-16 through an embedder hook (`[[source: kitty-graphics]]`, "The transmission medium"). The core never opens a file: at the command's terminator, inside the read, it hands `baud_domain::KittyFiles` the decoded path or shared-memory name, `O`, `S` and the most bytes the transmission can use, and decodes what comes back as a `t=d` payload, so a query is answered before the next byte and a placement moves the cursor before the text after it (`a_served_query_per_medium_is_answered_before_the_primary_da`). Raw pixels are read to `s * v` and no further, a PNG bounded by the scratch and zlib data by its bound, refused before reading past them; an object "smaller than the client claimed" by `S` is the one error, the text's plain reading, where kitty reads `S` as a bound and kitten's detection sends `S` as the path's length (so kitten's detection, refused its `t=t` probe, sends `t=d`); a path past 2048 bytes is EINVAL, kitty's; a file medium is one chunk. Once the reader is asked, every refusal -- a failed read, and a PNG that does not decode, a size past the store or a placement refused after it, which would each tell the client the file was there -- is `EBADF:Failed to read image file` (`every_file_failure_answers_one_identical_reply`, `every_refusal_after_the_read_is_the_one_reply`; kitty answers a decode failure `EBADPNG`, not followed), and keeps nothing, as a missing file keeps nothing: the image freed, so an `a=p` after it answers ENOENT, and the cursor unmoved, the image being decoded whole at the terminator and placed only then, so the reply comes before the next byte (`a_refusal_after_the_read_keeps_nothing`, raw, raw through zlib and PNG, a relative placement and either full table; `a_file_png_that_fails_late_moves_nothing_and_answers_in_order`, at every read size and two-read split). **A PNG that does not decode deletes no older image for space**, as a missing PNG file deletes none (a raw image reserves, and deletes, before its file is read): everything in its decode that can refuse -- the inflate, every scanline's reconstruction, each palette index against `PLTE` -- runs in the scratch it was read into, before the reserve that deletes, so an image never added makes no space for itself (`[[source: kitty-graphics-rst]]`, "Image persistence and storage quotas"), as kitty decodes before it adds and applies its quota after (`[[source: kitty-graphics-c]]`: `handle_add_command` decodes, and `grman_handle_command` applies the quota once it returns; `a_file_png_that_does_not_decode_deletes_nothing_for_space`, seven ways a PNG whose header parses fails, each medium, `a=t` and `a=T`, direct and through zlib; `reconstructing_then_writing_is_the_one_shot_decode`). A refusal decided before, from the control data and the path alone, keeps its code and reads nothing (`a_refusal_before_the_read_keeps_its_code_and_reads_nothing`). baud-app's `LocalFiles` resolves the path, following links, refuses `/proc`, `/sys` and `/dev` (the text's "may refuse") but a file under `/dev/shm`, which the text names a temporary directory, and a relative path (interim and open), refuses anything not regular before opening, walks the resolved path from `/` a directory at a time with `openat`, following no link, requires the last directory's device and inode to be the one resolved, and only then opens the file in it, following no link, with `O_NONBLOCK`, the same order on Darwin and Linux (`an_ancestor_swapped_for_a_link_before_the_checks_is_refused`, `a_directory_renamed_in_after_resolving_is_refused`, `a_directory_swapped_for_a_link_after_resolving_is_refused`), and requires the descriptor's `fstat` to be the regular file checked, device and inode (the swap test); deletes a `t=t` file only in a resolved temporary directory, by component, with `tty-graphics-protocol` in its path, from the directory it was read from (`unlinkat` on the walk's descriptor, `a_directory_swapped_before_the_unlink_does_not_move_it`); reads `t=s` through `shm_open` (mapped on Darwin), then unlinks it (`baud-app/src/kitty_files.rs`'s tests). With no reader lent the answer is the error, nothing reserved (`with_no_reader_a_file_medium_reserves_nothing`). `kitten icat` 0.49.2's `file` (`t=f`), `memory` (`t=s`) and `detect` (`t=d`, above) decode through it live (`kitten_icat_reads_through_the_reader_live`, ignored, `BAUD_KITTEN`) |

---

## Out of scope, with reasons

Recorded so that "not implemented" is a decision rather than an omission.

| seq | name | why not |
|---|---|---|
| `OSC 9 ; 1` to `OSC 9 ; 12`, but `9 ; 4` | ConEmu's other `OSC 9` forms: sleep, message box, tab title, wait for a key, GuiMacro, run a process, an environment variable, the directory, xterm emulation, a comment, the prompt | **out** — refused and counted, never posted as a notification (`conemus_other_forms_post_nothing`). Five are unsafe from a remote program: `9;7` "Run some process", `9;6` "Execute GuiMacro", `9;8` "Output value of environment variable", `9;2` a modal box and `9;1`/`9;5` block the console (`[[source: conemu-ansi-escape-codes]]`); `9;3` and `9;9` are the title and the directory, which `OSC 0`-`2` and `OSC 7` carry; `9;10` switches ConEmu's own emulation; `9;11` is "Just a 'comment', skip it"; `9;12` marks a prompt, which `OSC 133` does |
| `OSC 777 ; Pt` but `notify` | rxvt-unicode's perl extensions | **out** — "Call the perl extension with the given string, which should be of the form extension;parameters" (`[[source: rxvt-7-pod]]`): a call into urxvt's perl, which baud does not have. Counted |
| `OSC 1337 ; StealFocus` | iTerm2: bring the terminal to the foreground | **out** — refused and counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`, among its refusals): a program bringing the window in front of whatever the user is in is focus stealing, which iTerm2 itself puts behind a prompt ("A control sequence attempted to activate a session. Allow it?", `[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetProfile=name` | iTerm2: switch to another profile | **out** — refused and counted under its key, among its refusals: a program switching the user's settings for another set of them rewrites what the user chose (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetProfileProperty=key=base64-JSON` | iTerm2: change one profile setting | **out** — refused and counted under its key, among its refusals: a program changing the user's settings one at a time rewrites what the user chose (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; CopyToClipboard=name` | iTerm2: with `EndCopy`, copy the text between them | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; AddAnnotation=..., AddHiddenAnnotation=...` | iTerm2: attach a note to a range of text | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetBackgroundImageFile=base64` | iTerm2: a background image, after the user confirms | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; RequestUpload=format=tgz` | iTerm2: ask the user for files to upload | **out** — since 2026-10-22, refused with silence, so **nothing a program asks for leaves your machine and nothing reaches your shell as a typed command**. The page has iTerm2 "respond with a status of `ok` or `abort` followed by a newline" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), and iTerm2's own source names the risk in that, "The protocol calls to send \"abort\\n\" but this introduces a security risk because reports must not contain newlines" (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-ptysession]]`): a newline in a reply reaches a shell as an entered command when the request came from a file someone printed. iTerm2 sends Control-C instead, which is no status the page names. baud sends neither: an unsafe answer is not sent and an answer of another shape never is, as `OSC 52`'s clipboard read is refused. iTerm2's `it2ul` then waits for its status line until it is interrupted (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-it2ul]]`). Any format alike: the page says it "must always be `tgz`". The refusal: an upload hands files from your machine to the program that asked, a remote one included, and baud opens no file chooser for a program, as it writes no file a program sends. Counted under its key, among the refusals (`Term::iterm_counts`). `request_upload_is_refused_with_silence` |
| `OSC 633 ; A`/`B`/`C`/`D`/`E`/`P` | VS Code's shell integration | **out** — counted, and nothing changes. The page that defines it says "These sequences should be ignored by other terminals" and asks senders to check that `$TERM_PROGRAM` is `vscode` first (`[[source: vscode-shell-integration]]`), and its own shell scripts send them only there; a shell elsewhere marks its prompts with `OSC 133` and names its folder with `OSC 7`, both done. kitty ignores it by name (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-vt-parser]]` 650) and Windows Terminal routes the number to one other action, `Completions`, and acts on no A to P (`[[source: wt-c91793f-output-state-machine-engine]]` 902-904, `[[source: wt-c91793f-adaptdispatch]]`). Whether to build A-D on `OSC 133`'s marks is unresolved, and counting stays (note BF). `the_refused_strings_are_counted_and_answered_with_silence` |
| `OSC 5522 ; type=read` | kitty's clipboard: a read | **out** — counted, and answered with silence, as an `OSC 52` read is: the page has "Terminals should ask the user for permission before allowing a read request", and "a terminal that rejects a read request must simply ignore it", there being "no error status for read requests" (`[[source: kitty-clipboard]]`), so silence is the text's own refusal. `DECRQM ? 5522` answers not recognized, since its paste events offer data a read then fetches. Writes, `type=write` and `wdata`, are not built yet (note BF's next work): the text has a terminal "accept at least 64MB of data" across MIME types |
| `OSC 5113 ; ...` | kitty's file transfer | **out** — counted, and nothing is read or written. "Since untrusted software should not be able to read/write to another machines filesystem, a session must be approved by the user in the terminal emulator before any actual data is transmitted, unless a pre-shared password is provided" (`[[source: kitty-file-transfer]]`): a send writes the program's files to your disk and a receive sends yours to the program, the safety question `OSC 1337 ; RequestUpload` is refused on, and baud has no prompt to approve one |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetKeyLabel=key=value, PushKeyLabels, PopKeyLabels` | iTerm2: Touch Bar labels | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; Custom=id=secret:pattern` | iTerm2: a custom control sequence for a script | **out** — no counterpart: a sequence handed to a script through iTerm2's Python API, which baud does not have. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; ShellIntegrationVersion=n` | iTerm2: the shell integration version | **out** — no counterpart: "if it sees a lower number the user will be prompted to upgrade" (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`), iTerm2's own shell-integration scripts, which baud does not ship. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; ClearCapturedOutput` | iTerm2: clear the captured output | **out** — no counterpart: the output iTerm2's triggers capture into its toolbelt, which baud does not have. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; OpenURL=:url` | iTerm2: open a URL | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; Disinter` | iTerm2: the inverse of burying a session | **out** — no counterpart: iTerm2 buries sessions, baud does not. Counted under its key |
| `OSC 1337 ; Block=id=...;attr=start|end, UpdateBlock, Button` | iTerm2: foldable blocks of output and their buttons | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (`RequestAttention` was built with notifications, each other subcommand its own work). Recognised and counted in `strings_ignored` and under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen (`[[source: iterm2-escape-codes]]`) |
| `OSC 1337 ; SetWindowFrame`, `ReportWindowFrame`, `ReportScreenFrames`, `AddNote`, `AddHiddenNote` | iTerm2: keys its source compares and its pages do not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built.** Recognised and counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`), so a program sees nothing happen. `SetWindowFrame` and the two frame reports have a meaning in baud; `AddNote` and `AddHiddenNote` are the old names of the annotations, unbuilt with them. `every_key_not_acted_on_is_counted` |
| `OSC 1337 ; BeginFile`, `EndFile`, `Env`, `it2ssh`, `SendConductor`, `EndSSH`, `wrap`, `ExecFailed`, `Debug` | iTerm2: keys its source compares and its pages do not name (`[[source: iterm2-964a75d-vt100terminal]]`) | **out** — each counted under its key (`Term::iterm_counts`). `BeginFile` and `EndFile` iTerm2 itself ignores ("Deprecated and unsupported"); the rest drive iTerm2's ssh integration and its debug build, which baud has no counterpart of. `every_key_not_acted_on_is_counted`, and `every_iterm2_key_has_its_row` holds the table to the pinned pages' keys and the source's |
| `OSC 21 ; transparent_background_color1` to `7` | kitty: cells whose background is that colour drawn at an opacity of its own | **In scope and unbuilt; it stays in this table until it is built** (per-colour background opacity is its own work, on every path). "A background color that is rendered with the specified opacity in cells that have the specified background color" (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-color-stack]]`). Until then each is a key baud does not know: a query answers `?`, as kitty answers transparent_background_color9, and is not counted, as DECRQM's "not recognized" is not; a set or a reset is counted (`a_transparent_background_colour_is_an_unknown_key`) |
| `DCS Ps $ t D...D ST` | DECRSPS restore presentation state | done — since 2026-10-19. A DECCIR's data (`Ps` 1) or a DECTABSR's (`Ps` 2) restores what it reports (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-205; `[[source: vt510-decrsps]]`), so a report fed back puts the state back (`a_report_fed_back_restores_what_it_reported`). The cursor goes to the position, confined to the margins under a restored origin mode as DECRC confines it; the four renditions are set or cleared and the rest of the rendition kept; a pending wrap comes back pending on the column restored, as `DECRC` restores one, since 2026-11-01 (before, only on the line's last column or right margin); a single-shift bit leaves the shift pending after the string; `Scss` says which series each final of `Sdesig` is from, so `A` is the UK set or ISO Latin-1 Supplemental. The tab stops become the listed columns at the terminator, each kept as a bit as it arrives, so a body of any length restores (`decrsps_restores_a_stop_at_every_column`) and one a `CAN`, an `ESC` or a C1 cancels changes nothing (`a_cancelled_tab_restore_leaves_the_stops`). **An invalid value ends the restore there**, the fields before it kept: `[[source: vt510-decrsps]]`, "the terminal ignores the rest of the sequence", where STD 070 has the terminal "ignore the error, if possible" first (`decrsps_stops_at_an_invalid_value`); a tab stop past the screen is one too, and `Pgr` 0, G0 in GR, which vt510's DECCIR lists and ECMA-35 8.3.3 rules out, is accepted. The position is the screen's, under DECOM too. `Ps` 0 and other values are consumed and dropped |
| `DCS Ps $ p D...D ST` | DECRSTS restore terminal state | done — since 2026-10-22. `Ps` 1 restores a DECTSR (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-208): the body is kept whole and checksummed at the terminator, and a byte outside `4/0..4/15`, a failed checksum, a version not baud's or a cut body restore nothing, "If the checksum fails, the entire sequence is ignored" (`a_report_that_does_not_check_restores_nothing`). **Each field goes through the control that sets it, and only where it differs from the state now**, XTRESTORE's rule: the conformance level first, since DECSCL soft-resets, and the C1 transmission alone through `S7C1T`/`S8C1T` when the level is the same (`the_c1_transmission_is_restored_without_a_soft_reset`); the input encoding as `ESC % @` and `ESC % G` set it, the bytes after the string decoded under it (`the_input_encoding_is_restored`); each mode through SM or DECSET, so `?3` resizes and clears as it does, and the alternate screen is switched to or from through `?47`, the first of its three modes in slot order, so `?1049`'s cursor save is not replayed, with `?40` and `?1046` first, since `?3` moves only while `?40` is set and the alternate screen is reached only while `?1046` is (`a_gated_mode_is_restored_after_its_gate`); the status line's type and display (`the_status_line_type_and_display_are_restored`); DECSTBM and DECSLRM; DECSCUSR, DECSWBV, DECSMBV, DECSACE, DECSCPP and DECSLPP, the last two as window requests under `[window] operations` (`the_page_size_is_restored_as_a_window_request`); XTSMTITLE and the key-modifier controls; the rendition as its SGR; the cursor information through DECRSPS's restore; then the tab stops and the shown screen's `DECSC` save, written directly since no control restores either, the save into the slot of the screen the modes left shown, or emptied when the report had none (`the_saved_cursor_is_restored`, `decrsts_restores_what_every_tsi_control_reports`, `the_level_is_restored_before_what_it_resets`). A field with a value baud cannot take is stepped over and the rest restored, STD 070's "the terminal will ignore the error if possible", where the VT340, VT420 and VT510 manuals stop the restore (`[[source: vt3xx-tp-002]]` p. 209, `[[source: vt420-rm]]` p. 247, `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-113; `an_invalid_field_is_stepped_over`). `Ps` 2 restores a DECCTR: each group is set as its `/` arrives, as `OSC 4` sets a colour, "only the index entries specified are changed" (p. 5-109), and an invalid group -- `Pc` past 255, `Pu` not 1 or 2, a value past its range, a parameter missing -- stops the restore there, the manuals' "ignores the rest of the sequence" (`an_invalid_colour_group_stops_the_restore`); a `CAN` keeps the groups set before it. 0 and any other `Ps` are ignored. `DCS 1 $ p` and `DCS 2 $ p` are not ReGIS, whose `p` is bare (`a_dollar_p_string_is_no_regis`), and the `$` kept to tell them apart does not reach an escape that cancels the string (`a_cancelled_dollar_string_leaves_no_intermediate`). **A restore that asks for a page width holds the read** for an embedder that resizes, as DECSCPP and `?3` do, and a single shift pending in the report's DECCIR flags waits for the held rest, whose first character it takes at the width asked for, as SS2 "causes the next character in the received data stream" to be G2's (070-3 p. 3-80; `a_restore_that_asks_a_width_holds_with_a_single_shift_pending`); until 2026-10-22 the shift replaced the hold and the rest was parsed at the old width. baud's own report never carries a pending shift; a report a host made can. **Open**: the hold takes effect only when the string ends, so every field of the restore lands at the width before the request, and a 132-column report restored on an 80-column page loses its cursor information, whose restore stops at a column past the screen's last, and its side margins, even where the embedder then takes 132 |
| `DCS ... | DECUDK` | user-defined keys | **The stated reason expired and the row survives on a new one.** It read "Input-side; baud has no input path", and baud has one -- `encode_key` and `Term::key`. What keeps DECUDK out is that it redefines what a *key* sends, which needs a table of user-programmable strings and a policy for who may set them; the resource above it needs neither. Out on scope, not on absence |
| `DCS ! { D...D ST` | DECSTUI set terminal unit ID | **out** — counted, and nothing is kept. A VT420 control (`[[source: vt420-rm]]` Table 4-1), whose one reader is DA3: DECRPTUI reports "its unique terminal unit ID", "factory set to a unique value" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-4 p. 4-31). DA3 is out, whether to answer DA2 and DA3 an open question, and a unit ID kept that nothing reports would be a setting recorded and never read |
| `DCS " x D...D ST` / `DCS " y D...D ST` | DECPFK program function key / DECPAK program alphanumeric key | **out** — counted. Both are the VT510's: they program "one or more keys (and their modifier combinations)" by key station number to "a local terminal function" or a user-defined sequence, out of the 804 bytes DECUDK shares (`[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` pp. 5-77, 5-83), and neither is in `[[source: vt420-rm]]` or `[[source: dec-std-070]]` (whose DECSTUI and DECDLD are found by the same search), so both are above the VT420 class, as DECAC is; and they carry DECUDK's open question. Both, and DECSTUI, are held counted and answering nothing by `the_refused_strings_are_counted_and_answered_with_silence` |
| `CSI 20 t`, `CSI 21 t` | XTWINOPS icon label and title reports | **Refused because answering is unsafe**: the reply, `OSC L label ST` or `OSC l label ST`, puts text a program set back into the terminal's input, so a remote program that can set the title can make the terminal type a command for it. Nothing is written, and each is counted in `Term::title_reports_refused`, not in `unsupported_param`, so an embedder can see the requests arrive (`the_title_reports_are_refused_and_counted`). xterm refuses both by default too, its `disallowedWindowOps` (`[[source: xterm-main-h]]`). |
| `OSC 7 ; Pt` | the shell's working directory, a `file:` URI | done — since 2026-10-21, **a shell can tell baud which folder it is in**, as Apple's own shell files do at every prompt, "using a "file:" scheme URL, including the host name to disambiguate local vs. remote paths" (`[[source: macos-zshrc-apple-terminal]]` 17-20). No standard defines the sequence ("to my knowledge there is no documentation on the behavior of this escape", `[[source: terminal-wg-issue-20]]`); the payload is read on RFC 8089's grammar (`[[source: rfc8089]]` § 2) with RFC 3986's `host` and `path-absolute` (`[[source: rfc3986]]`): `file://host/path` and the authority-less `file:/path`, the scheme and `localhost` in any case, the path percent-decoded (RFC 3986 § 2.1), the bytes classed by a table built at compile time. The core keeps the host it named and the decoded path (`Term::working_directory`): no host and `localhost` are this machine, as RFC 8089 § 2 says of both, and any other name is the embedder's to compare with its own, since § 3's "resolves to the machine from which the URI is being interpreted" needs the machine. **Every report replaces the last**, and one the core cannot read leaves none, counted in `Term::directories_refused` and as unsupported: off the grammar (a raw space or UTF-8 byte, a query, a fragment, a broken escape, no path), of another scheme (`kitty-shell-cwd://`, which kitty's and ghostty's shell integrations send unencoded), decoding to a NUL, or cut at `OSC_MAX`, whose surviving path names another directory. **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset forgets it** and `DECSTR` keeps it: a terminal made operational has none reported (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.105), and a shell reports it again at its next prompt. **The app shows it in the window's title** when a program sets none (the `OSC 0/1/2` row), and only when the host is this machine: no host, `localhost`, or one of its names without case -- the name `gethostname` gives, which shells send (zsh's `$HOST`, bash's `$HOSTNAME`), and on macOS also `NSProcessInfo`'s `hostName`, which there can be a different name, such as `<name>.local`; on a Mac with `HostName` set the two can be the same, and baud accepts either (`an_osc_7_naming_the_hostname_is_this_machine`); § 3's "resolves to the machine" is narrowed to those names, since a lookup would wait. A report naming another host is refused there and counted, once a report however often the title is built, and the program's own folder shown (§ 5: "Treating a non-local file URI as local ... can result in security problems"; `a_local_osc_7_names_the_folder_and_a_remote_one_does_not`, `a_remote_osc_7_is_counted_once_a_report`). Under `ESC % @` a GR byte is its 7-bit byte, as in every `OSC` body (`apples_report_keeps_its_host_and_decodes_its_path`, `no_host_and_localhost_are_local`, `a_refused_report_leaves_no_directory_and_is_counted`, `a_report_cut_at_osc_max_is_refused`, `a_report_is_chunk_invariant`, `ris_forgets_the_directory_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `under_latin1_a_gr_byte_is_its_seven_bit_byte`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 3 ; prop=value` | set an X property on the window | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can name the window and its icon through their X properties** (note AV): "Set X property on top-level window. Pt should be in the form \"prop=value\", or just \"prop\" to delete the property" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). baud has no X window, so the properties with a counterpart are mapped and every other one is counted in `Term::properties_dropped` and as unsupported: ICCCM's `WM_NAME` and EWMH's `_NET_WM_NAME`, "the title of the window", set the title, as `OSC 2` does, and `WM_ICON_NAME` and `_NET_WM_ICON_NAME` the icon name, as `OSC 1` does, the last write winning (`[[source: icccm-2-0]]` chapter 4, "WM_NAME Property"; `[[source: ewmh-1-5-app]]` 5.1 and 5.3). Pt is split at the first `=` and a name is matched exactly, as xterm's `ChangeXprop` interns an atom (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`); a bare name deletes the property and leaves the window or its icon unnamed, which the app shows as its own name. The bytes are read by the property's type: ICCCM's two as ISO 8859-1, the `STRING` xterm writes them as ("STRING as a type ... specifies the ISO Latin-1 character set", ICCCM chapter 2), and EWMH's two as UTF-8, their `UTF8_STRING`, a value that is not UTF-8 dropped and counted; never in hexadecimal, as `ChangeXprop` goes past `ChangeGroup`, whose XTSMTITLE modes it does not read. xterm gates it under `allowWindowOps`, subcategory `SetXprop`, so `[window] operations = "off"` refuses it and counts it in `Term::window_ops_refused`, and `OSC 61 ; allowWindowOps` names `SetXprop` only then (`wm_name_names_the_window_in_iso_8859_1`, `net_wm_name_names_the_window_in_utf8`, `the_icon_name_properties_set_the_icon_name`, `a_bare_name_deletes_the_name`, `every_other_property_is_counted_and_dropped`, `the_hexadecimal_title_mode_does_not_apply`, `the_window_operations_setting_refuses_it`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 46 ; Pt` | change the log file | done — since 2026-10-20, **the file a program's log goes to** (the `?46` row): "Change Log File to Pt. The parameter Pt sets the logFile resource" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). As xterm's: it only renames, so a log already started keeps its file until the next start; `?`, at which xterm rings, and an empty `Pt`, which its `do_osc` drops for want of data, change nothing and are counted, as is a name `OSC_MAX` may have cut, which would be another file (`[[source: xterm-misc]]`). A GR byte under ISO 8859-1 is its 7-bit byte here as in every `OSC` (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 3.5.4.5), as xterm's parser stores it too, so a Latin-1 `é` in a path is `i`; under UTF-8 the path's characters are kept whole, over reading the path as ISO 8859-1 (`an_osc_46_path_in_gr_is_its_seven_bit_path_under_latin1`). Refused and counted while logging is off (`osc_46_names_the_file_the_next_start_opens`, `off_both_are_refused_and_counted`). Counted as unsupported before |
| `OSC 50 ; Pt` | set or query the font | done — since 2026-10-20, **a program can ask which font is in use, switch to another font baud has loaded, or make the text bigger or smaller**: set and query, on by default, and a setting turns changes off. "Set Font to Pt ... If Pt begins with a \"#\", index in the font menu, relative (if the next character is a plus or minus sign) or absolute. A number is expected but not required after the sign (the default is the current entry for relative, zero for absolute indexing). The same rule ... is used when querying the font" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`); the text's defaults are followed where xterm's `ParseShiftedFont` steps one for a bare sign and keeps the current entry for a bare `#` (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 3759-3796). **The query** answers `OSC 50 ; name` in xterm's `QueryFontRequest` shape (3798-3846), with the query's terminator: since 2026-10-22 the name a program last set for the face that leads, as it sent it, the text's own reading, since under renderFont the control "sets/queries the faceName resource" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), and as xterm answers the face name its `setFaceName` stored (`getFaceName`, `[[source: xterm-fontutils]]` 3453-3489) and mintty the name `win_change_font` stored (`[[source: mintty-wintext-0aea388]]` 1120-1154, answered by `[[source: mintty-termout-0aea388]]` 4984-5001), so a program's name sent back finds the same face (`the_query_answers_the_name_a_program_set`); the answer is only ever a name baud took, one that matches a loaded face's own name by full case folding and nothing else, 64 bytes at most, so it carries no text a program chose beyond a spelling of that name; for a face no program has named, the family of the face that leads the chain, from its `name` table (nameID 16, else 1, `[[source: ms-opentype-name]]`), so the default stack answers "Proto Hebrew Mono C", the built font's name while its family name is open; `#0 ` before it when anything follows the `?` or the sign (xterm's `*buf != 0`), and `OSC 50` with no `;` for an entry baud has not. A core no embedder told is silent. **What the index means**, baud having no font menu: entry 0 is the configured size, `#+n` and `#-n` step the size `n` points within 6 to 72, a step past them refused where the View menu's Bigger and Smaller clamp, and on Linux, which has no size keys, `n` of the configured pixel size, and entries 1-7 are none (xterm's TrueType path never resizes through `OSC 50`); a query of a relative step names no numbered entry and is answered empty. **A name resolves to a face baud has loaded**, the embedded ones and the configured ones (the system faces the default stack names too), through the lookup a config's `fonts` names take, since 2026-10-22: a face's PostScript name (nameID 6), else its full name (nameID 4) where that is none of its own family names, else a family name of a face in the fallback chain, any of its nameID 16 and 1 records, each by the default caseless match CSS Fonts gives a family name (`[[source: css-fonts-3]]` 5.1), so `OSC 50 ; ProtoHebrewMonoC-Bold` finds the embedded Bold (`a_program_names_a_face_by_its_postscript_or_full_name`); until then only a family name did. The face named leads the chain as the regular face, the rest follow in order, and its family's other styled faces come with it, a styled face named alone having no style beside itself. Entries 1-7, an empty `Pt`, the current entry with no name and a name past 64 bytes change nothing and are counted in the core (`Term::fonts_refused`); a family baud has not loaded and a step past the bounds change nothing and are counted too, by the app through `Term::refuse_font_request`, and said at info (mintty and xterm hand the name to the system's lookup, `[[source: mintty-termout-0aea388]]`). Both apps restack the renderer (`Renderer::restack`) and re-lay the grid as a zoom does. `[font] changes = "off"` refuses every change and keeps the query answered, where xterm's `allowFontOps` off refuses both by default; `OSC 60` names `allowFontOps` and `OSC 61` says `SetFont` while changes are off. **Open departure**: a query after a change in the same read names the font from before it, since the embedder performs the change after the read (`a_query_after_a_change_in_one_read_names_the_font_before_it`).
