Counted as unsupported before (`osc_50_is_acted_on_not_counted`, `the_query_names_the_font_in_use_in_xterms_shape`, `a_stepped_font_has_no_numbered_entry`, `a_change_is_queued_for_the_embedder`, `a_change_that_cannot_be_made_is_refused_and_counted`, `a_change_the_embedder_refuses_is_counted`, `a_change_the_app_cannot_make_is_counted_in_the_core`, `naming_the_face_in_force_replaces_a_waiting_one`, `with_changes_off_a_change_is_refused_and_the_query_answered`, `the_feature_queries_say_what_osc_50_does`, `osc_50_is_the_same_at_every_split`, `each_form_parses_as_the_text_gives_it`, `a_change_is_planned_whole`, `a_restack_takes_the_new_faces_metrics_and_shapes_afresh`, `jetbrains_mono_names_its_family`). **Since 2026-10-25 a full reset puts back the configured font** after a program changed it: `RIS` asks for the configured family at the configured size in place of whatever was still queued, as "RIS replaces all set-up features with their saved settings" (`[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-184), and asks nothing when no program changed it; `DECSTR` asks nothing. xterm keeps a font a program set, a practice not followed (`ris_asks_for_the_configured_font_after_a_program_changed_it`, `a_reset_plans_the_configured_font`) |
| `OSC 13`, `OSC 14`, `OSC 113`, `OSC 114` | pointer foreground and background colours | **out** — the system draws the pointer: macOS's cursors take no colour, and neither do the Linux driver's, named shapes the compositor draws (`cursor-shape-v1`), so a colour set would be drawn nowhere and a query would name a colour nobody sees. A pointer baud draws itself is open. Counted, and an `OSC 12 ; c ; c` walks past them (`a_colour_baud_does_not_keep_is_counted_and_the_walk_goes_on`), over drawing baud's own pointer and storing without drawing |
| `OSC 15`, `OSC 16`, `OSC 18`, `OSC 115`, `OSC 116`, `OSC 118` | Tektronix foreground, background and cursor colours | **out** — they colour the Tektronix window, which baud does not have (Tektronix 4014, below, out). Counted, and walked past in an `OSC 10`-family list |
| `OSC 21 ; Pt` | DECSWT, set window title (VT520) | **out** — DECSWT is the VT520's ("Ps = 2 1 -> Change Window Title to Pt (DECSWT), VT520", `[[source: ctlseqs]]`; williams: "present on the multisession VT520 and VT525 terminals. Earlier terminals treat OSC in the same way as PM and APC"), baud identifies as a VT102, and xterm ignores it below a VT520 (`[[source: xterm-misc]]` 4172-4177). **Since 2026-10-22 the number is kitty's colour control** (the `OSC 21 ; key=value` row), as kitty takes it whatever the body (`[[source: kitty-6d5d0c4-vt-parser]]` 609-611) and ghostty does (`[[source: ghostty-f54340a-osc-kitty-color]]`), so a title sent as DECSWT is read as a record of a key baud does not know, sets no title and is counted, as it was counted before (`a_title_under_osc_21_sets_no_title_and_is_counted`). Note AK, section 1.4 |
| `OSC 51 ; Pt` | reserved for Emacs | **out** — "reserved for Emacs shell" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`), with no function defined; xterm's `do_osc` does nothing with it. Counted |
| `CSI 2 i`, `CSI ? 2 i`, `CSI ? 8 i` / `CSI ? 9 i`, `CSI 10 i` / `CSI 11 i` | MC's screen to host, graphics to host, printer-to-host session, xterm's HTML and SVG dumps | **Refused because answering is unsafe**: `CSI 2 i`, "Sends screen data through host port" (`[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-178), would type the screen back into the program's input, the reason the title reports are refused; `CSI ? 2 i`, "Graphics to Host" (`[[source: vt3xx-gp-002]]` ch. 16), would send a ReGIS hard copy's image of the screen to the program instead of the printer, refused for the same reason since 2026-10-21; a printer-to-host session relays a printer's input to the host and baud's print panel sends nothing back; the dumps write files a program names (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). Each counted in `Term::print_refusals`, apart from the unknown (`the_refused_kinds_are_counted_apart_from_the_unknown`). **Out and counted as unsupported**, since 2026-10-20: `CSI ? 18 i` and `CSI ? 19 i`, which assign and release the printer between two sessions, and baud has one; 070-7 § 7.8's graphics printing was out with them until 2026-10-21, and is built now (the ReGIS row); the VT510's printer-type and printed-data-type selections (DECSPRTT, DECSDPT and the ProPrinter forms, `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` Table 4-9), which configure a serial printer's protocol where baud's printer takes UTF-8; `CSI 6 i` and `CSI 7 i`, the locator row's (070-13's locator controller mode); the VT510's MC page reads them as printer-to-host, "functionally equivalent to CSI ? 8 i and CSI ? 9 i", and under either reading they stay refused and counted, in `unsupported`. |
| Tektronix 4014 | vector graphics in xterm's second terminal personality | **out** — since 2026-10-20 (note AW, section 7): no DEC text defines it, and xterm runs it as "standard Tektronix 4014 control sequences" in a second window with its own emulator and graphics-input mode (`[[source: ctlseqs]]` 3245-3300; `[[source: xterm-man]]`, `tekInhibit`), a second terminal rather than a protocol of this one. ReGIS, the DEC vector language, is in, in Tier 3 |
| `DCS Pc $ w ... ST` | DECLKD locator key definitions | done — since 2026-10-21 a program can choose what each pointer button sends in ReGIS graphics input: `Ky/Std/Stu` hex pairs for a key's press and release, six bytes each, `Pc` 0 emptying every key first ("All button definition strings are empty (not the default)") and 1 only the keys redefined (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13 § 5.4; `[[source: vt3xx-locator]]`'s DECLBD). The press comes first, as 070-13 and a real VT340's script load it (`[[source: vt340test-locatorprog]]`); the VT330/VT340 manual's own example and its separator sentence put the release first, a contradiction inside that manual, not followed. An empty string reports nothing and does not end a one-shot input (DECLKD note 2); the null button's `CSI 240 ~` is no key and stays (a real VT340's test leaves the question open). Read as DECUDK's loading reads one (070-11 `FILL_KEYS`): a key past 4 skips its definition, a broken hex pair stops the rest, a string past six bytes is not stored and stops the rest, a `Pc` past 1 refuses the string, each counted (`Term::locator_keys_refused`). `RIS` restores the defaults and `DECSTR` keeps the keys, as the text says (`a_declkd_programs_a_keys_press_and_release`, `pc_0_empties_every_key_and_an_empty_key_does_not_end_one_shot`, `pc_1_clears_only_the_keys_redefined`, `ris_restores_the_keys_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `a_bad_declkd_is_refused_as_decudk_refuses_one`). Until then it was counted and ignored, "waiting on the ReGIS row" |
| `CSI Ps1 ; Ps2 SP k` | SCP select character path | done — since 2026-10-25, **a program can make its lines run right to left, or keep them left to right**: with `?2501` reset, SCP 2 draws an English line against the right edge and SCP 1 keeps a Hebrew line left to right, "for the active line ... and subsequent lines ... This takes effect immediately" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.111). With BDSM set and autodetection reset (the `?2501` row) the path is the paragraph's level, set outright (`[[source: uax9-bidi]]` HL1); **with autodetection set, as at power-on since 2026-10-25, it is the fallback** of a paragraph with no strong letter, so SCP 2 alone leaves an English line left to right and puts a line of digits right to left (terminal-wg's model); with BDSM reset a right-to-left line is drawn in reverse, its mirrored characters mirrored and a bar cursor on its cell's right, terminal-wg's explicit RTL (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-basic-modes]]`). It turns the paragraph holding the cursor's line at once, wherever the cursor is in it, and becomes the path in force, which the draft's rules carry (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`): a line feed into a new line, a line a scroll brings in that a wrap did not, the lines ED erases whole, SU, SD, IL and DL bring in and a clear leaves take it, a Sixel image's scroll included; SU, SD, IL and DL end the lines either side of what they insert or remove, so each new line is one of its own, terminal-wg's rule for SD and SU ("wherever lines are removed or inserted, we ensure that the boundaries are all hard newlines"), which baud applies to IL and DL as well; a wrap joins its row to the paragraph above, whose path it takes; EL, ECH and DCH keep a line's; a cursor move takes nothing; ED 1 and ED 2 end the history's newest line. Each row keeps its paragraph's path beside its mark, so scrolling, scrollback, IL, DL and a reflow carry it (`baud_domain::RowExtent`). On a table row each cell takes the path. `Ps1` 0 or omitted is refused and counted: SCP has "No parameter default values" and defines only 1 and 2, where terminal-wg adds 0 as the terminal's default. `Ps2` 0 or omitted applies at once with the cursor where it is; 1 also moves the cursor to the line's first character position, as `CHA 1` addresses it; 2, which updates the data from the presentation, is refused and counted. `DECSTR` returns the path in force to none and leaves the lines', as DEC's table returns DECRLM to left to right (`[[source: vt510-decstr]]`); `RIS` returns every line and the path in force to none. `DECRQSS` answers it as invalid, as no DEC or xterm text lists it. A prefixed form is counted (`scp_2_draws_an_english_line_right_to_left`, `scp_1_keeps_a_hebrew_line_left_to_right`, `the_direction_turns_the_active_line_and_carries_to_later_lines`, `a_line_the_cursor_moves_to_keeps_its_direction`, `ps1_zero_is_refused_and_decstr_returns_to_first_strong`, `ps2_two_is_refused_and_counted`, `ps2_one_moves_the_cursor_to_the_line_start`, `a_wrapped_line_keeps_its_direction_on_every_row`, `a_paragraph_whose_first_row_scrolled_off_keeps_its_direction`, `the_direction_survives_scrollback`, `the_direction_survives_reflow`, `ris_returns_every_line_to_first_strong`, `ed_spreads_the_direction_over_the_erased_lines`, `the_lines_a_scroll_brings_in_take_the_direction`, `a_table_row_under_scp_keeps_its_columns_and_turns_each_cell`, `with_bdsm_reset_scp_2_draws_the_line_in_reverse`, `a_direction_change_alone_redraws_the_line`, `il_and_dl_move_a_line_with_its_path`, `an_erase_inside_a_line_keeps_its_path`, `scp_on_a_continued_line_reaches_its_history_rows`, `ind_brings_in_a_line_with_the_path_and_a_move_takes_none`, `the_alternate_screen_takes_the_path_and_the_primary_keeps_its_own`, `ris_clears_every_path_on_both_screens`, `ed_1_and_2_end_the_history_line_a_wrap_left_continuing`, `decrqss_answers_scp_as_invalid`, `a_line_a_sixel_image_scrolls_in_takes_the_path_in_force`, `su_sd_il_and_dl_end_the_lines_either_side_of_what_they_move`, `a_line_sd_or_il_brings_in_under_a_wrapped_row_is_its_own_paragraph`; the three paths' pixels, implicit and explicit: `a_line_scp_turns_draws_alike_on_every_arm`) |
| `CSI Ps1 ; Ps2 SP S` | SPD select presentation directions | done — since 2026-10-25, **a program can turn every line of the screen at once**: SPD 3 sets every line on the screen right to left and SPD 0, its default, left to right, "in the presentation component ... This takes effect immediately" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.130), the history aside, and makes it the path in force, as SCP does; **like SCP's, the path is the level only with `?2501` reset, and with it set, as at power-on since 2026-10-25, the fallback** of a line with no strong letter (the SCP and `?2501` rows), and SPD sets only the path, not autodetection or box mirroring ("It would be ugly for SPD to spread the current BDSM, autodetection etc. modes too across all the lines", `[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`). `Ps2` 0 or omitted leaves the cursor; 1 moves it to "the first character position in the first line", as `CUP 1 ; 1` addresses it. The vertical and bottom-to-top values (1, 2, 4 to 7) and `Ps2` 2 are refused and counted (`spd_turns_every_line_of_the_screen`, `spd_with_ps2_one_homes_the_cursor`). A prefixed form is counted |
| `CSI Ps [` / `CSI Ps ]` | SRS start reversed string / SDS start directed string | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can mark a run of text to be drawn left to right, right to left, or reversed**: SDS 1 and 2 begin a string "establish[ing] the direction left-to-right" or "right-to-left", SRS 1 one whose "direction is opposite to that currently established", and SDS 0 and SRS 0 end one, "Every beginning of such a string invokes the next deeper level of nesting" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.114, 8.3.137). Each character written carries its string in its rendition, as SAPV's variants are carried, so strings move with their cells through ICH, DCH, scrolling, the history and reflow, a string at a line's start needs no character before it, and a copy gives the text as written. **With BDSM reset** a line is laid out by TR/53's rule, each string in its own direction with nothing implicit inside it and kept apart from its neighbour, reversed inside its line's path: TR/53's worked example 1 line for line (`[[source: ecma-tr53]]`), mirrored characters of a right-to-left string mirrored. **With BDSM set**, as at power-on, each string is given to UAX #9 as an isolate of its direction (HL3), so a Hebrew string keeps its number's order and a string of Latin letters reads as written. A reversed string at a line's top reverses the line's character path, left to right where none is set. An end ends only a string of its kind; a mismatched end, an end with nothing open, a string past UAX #9's depth of 125 and one the table of 255 has no room for once the strings no cell names are freed are refused and counted (`Term::strings_refused`), a refused string's end matched to it. An erased position stays in its string, and ICH's blanks join the string of the character they push on (TR/53 note A.1). `SGR 0`, `DECSC`/`DECRC` and `XTPUSHSGR`/`XTPOPSGR` keep the string open; `DECSTR` ends every string, and `RIS` empties the table. SDS `Ps` past 2 and SRS `Ps` past 1 are unsupported, counted. The standard leaves the active position inside a nested string undefined; it is the cell column, drawn where its cell is drawn (`each_character_takes_the_string_it_is_written_in`, `adjacent_strings_are_apart`, `a_mismatched_or_stray_end_ends_nothing_and_is_counted`, `sgr_0_and_decrc_keep_the_string_and_decstr_ends_it`, `an_erased_or_inserted_position_stays_in_the_string`, `a_string_past_the_depth_is_refused_and_its_end_matched`, `the_table_frees_strings_no_cell_names`, `ris_ends_every_string`, `tr53_example_1_left_to_right`, `tr53_example_1_right_to_left`, `a_wide_character_in_a_reversed_string_stays_whole`, `with_bdsm_set_each_string_is_an_isolate`; the three arms' pixels: `a_reversed_string_draws_alike_on_every_arm`) |
| `CSI Ps... SP ]` | SAPV select alternative presentation variants | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can choose how the text it writes after is drawn**: "SAPV is used to specify one or more variants for the presentation of subsequent text" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.108), each value carried in the rendition of the characters written after it, as `SGR` is, so text written before keeps its own; `SGR 0` leaves them, and so does DECALN, which sets the rendition normal as `SGR 0` does (`decaln_keeps_the_variants`), and `DECSTR` and `RIS` return the default presentation. 2 draws the digits as the Arabic script's, U+0660-U+0669, and 1 and 20 as stored (`value_2_draws_arabic_indic_digits`); 10 a FULL STOP between two digits as a COMMA, 9 as stored (`value_10_draws_a_decimal_mark_between_digits_as_a_comma`); 5 to 8 the following graphic character in the form asked, the nearest form a letter has where it has not that one, spent unstored on a character that takes none (`a_form_is_spent_on_a_character_that_takes_none`), 21 holding forms for every character after it and 22 letting them go (`a_form_asked_draws_the_following_letter_in_it`, `a_form_is_the_following_characters_alone`, `value_21_holds_forms_and_22_lets_them_go`, `a_form_waits_across_reads`); 13 joins Arabic with the lam-alef ligature (the default), 14 without it, 18 and 19 not at all (`value_14_draws_a_lam_and_an_alef_apart`, `values_18_and_19_draw_arabic_unjoined`); 16 hides the vowels, Arabic tanwin and harakat and the Hebrew vowel points (`value_16_paints_a_letter_without_its_vowel`), and 11 shows them; 15 leaves a right-to-left path's mirrored characters as stored with BDSM reset, where the device does no bidi, 3 the paired ones and 4 the mathematical mirrored again, while with BDSM set UAX #9 L4 mirrors them whatever SAPV says ("mirroring must be done according to rule L4", `[[source: uax9-bidi]]` section 7; `value_15_unmirrors_a_reversed_line_with_bdsm_reset`); 17 draws italics on a right-to-left run, "when the string direction is right-to-left, the italicized characters are slanted to the left", as the upright glyph sheared left by the italic face's angle, on every path, with BDSM set at the level UAX #9 resolves and with it reset on a right-to-left path, while left-to-right italics lean right as the italic face draws them (`value_17_slants_right_to_left_italics_to_the_left`, `presentation_variants_draw_alike_on_every_arm`). 12, vowels after their letter, is refused and counted (`Term::variants_refused`): a vowel is stored on its letter's cell and the grid has no column for it to follow in. A value 8.3.108 does not define is counted in `unsupported_param` (`value_12_is_refused_and_an_undefined_value_counted`); DECRQSS answers it `DCS 0 $ r ST`, as it answers SCP |
| `CSI Ps Q` | SEE select editing extent | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can make an insertion or a deletion push text on across line ends, or keep it inside a field or a guarded area**: "SEE is used to establish the editing extent for subsequent character or line insertion or deletion ... 0 the shifted part is limited to the active page ... 1 ... the active line ... 2 ... the active field ... 3 ... the active qualified area 4 the shifted part consists of the relevant part of the entire presentation component" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.115). **Before any SEE the extent is DEC's**: `ICH`, `DCH` and a write under IRM shift within the line between the margins, SEE 1's, and `IL` and `DL` within the scrolling region, as DEC STD 070's ICH note 1 and xterm do and as terminfo's xterm entries, which carry no `in`, tell curses; SEE's "Ps = 0" is the value of an omitted parameter, so `CSI Q` selects the page. Under SEE 0 what `ICH` pushes off a line's end goes on at the next line's start and the page's last positions leave, and `DCH` pulls the following lines back, through the rectangle DECSTBM and DECSLRM bound, from a line's right margin to the next line's left margin, the edit ignored outside it; SEE 4 is the page, the history not being on the display; SEE 2's field is `EF`'s (the `CSI Ps N` row) and SEE 3's qualified area `EA`'s run of positions sharing the cursor's guard (the `CSI Ps O` row), each within the margins; HEM's PRECEDING shifts from the extent's start to the cursor (the `CSI 10 h` row). `IL` and `DL` under SEE 1 and 2 erase the active line, the shifted part being that line alone, and under SEE 3 shift the lines from the cursor's to the area's last, within the region. **A wide character stays whole, as under DEC's `ICH` and `DCH`**: an edit at either half takes the whole character, the shift starting at whichever half it reaches first, the lead under FOLLOWING and the tail under PRECEDING, and a delete taking the character and the n-1 positions after it in the shift's direction, as `Grid::delete_cells` does, under FOLLOWING and PRECEDING, and one the shift would part, across a line end, off the shifted part's end or with a half outside it, is erased, both halves. A character written under IRM and PRECEDING on a wide character's lead goes in after its tail, since 2026-10-27; it was written over the tail, the lead erased. A value past 4 is counted; `RIS` returns to DEC's extent and `DECSTR` and a resize keep it; DECRQSS answers it as it answers SCP, and DECTSR does not carry it. Under DCSM's PRESENTATION, on a line holding an SDS or SRS string, the positions are the ones drawn, the content moving between the cells drawn there, each keeping its string; a wide character there moves whole where its halves land side by side in their order and is erased where they would not (`before_any_see_ich_shifts_within_the_line`, `see_page_shifts_across_line_ends`, `see_four_is_the_page`, `see_field_shifts_within_the_field`, `see_qualified_area_shifts_within_the_area`, `a_shift_across_lines_keeps_within_the_margins`, `preceding_shifts_from_the_extent_start`, `a_write_under_irm_shifts_along_the_extent`, `il_and_dl_under_a_line_or_field_extent_erase_the_active_line`, `il_and_dl_under_a_qualified_area_shift_its_lines`, `a_wide_character_a_line_end_cuts_is_erased`, `a_wide_character_pulled_across_a_line_end_is_erased`, `a_wide_character_the_field_end_cuts_is_erased`, `an_edit_on_a_wide_tail_takes_the_whole_character`, `dch_on_a_wide_lead_removes_both_halves`, `preceding_on_a_wide_character_takes_the_whole_character`, `a_shift_by_the_positions_drawn_keeps_a_wide_character_whole`, `see_with_an_undefined_value_is_counted`, `ris_returns_to_decs_extent_and_decstr_keeps_it`, `see_shifts_the_positions_drawn_under_presentation`, `decrqss_answers_see_as_invalid`) |
| `CSI Pn SP U` / `CSI Pn SP V` | SLH / SLL set line home and line limit | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can choose where a carriage return goes and where a line wraps, without margins**: SLH sets "the position to which the active presentation position will be moved by subsequent occurrences of CARRIAGE RETURN (CR), DELETE LINE (DL), INSERT LINE (IL) or NEXT LINE (NEL)", and under DATA "the position ahead of which no implicit movement of the active data position shall occur"; SLL, under DATA, "the position beyond which no implicit movement of the active data position shall occur" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.122, 8.3.123). Positions of their own, apart from DECSLRM: `CR`, `NEL`, `LF` under LNM, `IL` and `DL` go to the line home, in both DCSM states, and with BDSM set, which reads DCSM as DATA (7.3.4 c), or DCSM at DATA a character written at the line limit wraps to the next line's home under DECAWM and stays on the limit with it reset, as at a right margin; a wrap at the right edge goes to the home too. Under PRESENTATION those clauses are a "device without data component"'s, which baud is not, so the limit does nothing there and a wrap goes to the left margin. SLL's `CR` and `NEL` clause needs SIMD 1, which never holds (the `CSI Ps ^` row). DECSLRM still bounds `ICH`, `DCH`, scrolling and DECOM, and where both are set the inner bound holds. `CNL` and `CPL` keep "the first character position" (8.3.12, 8.3.13) and `ICH` the cursor (the `CSI @` row); under DECRLM `CR` keeps the right margin. A position past the line is its last column; an omitted or zero `Pn`, which has no default, is counted. A resize keeps both, each the line's last column while the line is narrower; `RIS` forgets them and `DECSTR` keeps them. DECRQSS answers neither, as it answers SCP, and DECTSR carries neither. While either is set and bounds implicit movement the text is written a character at a time. **A wrap waiting at the line limit is reported on the limit**, since 2026-10-27: the character written there leaves the active position on it, "the position beyond which no implicit movement of the active data position shall occur", as DEC's Last Column Flag leaves it on the last column, "the Active Position cannot be advanced" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` D.6.1), and `CPR` reports the active position (8.3.14; 070's CPR, p. 5-54), so `CPR`, DECXCPR, `Term::cursor`, a status line's cursor, the indicator and DECCIR name the limit, DECCIR with Sflag's bit 4, "auto-wrap pending (1 if pending)", `Term::wrap_pending` answers yes, HPB and DECBI move back from the limit, DECFI on from it, SPA and EPA guard it, and DECSWL, which resets the Last Column Flag (D.6.1), ends the wait on it; a cursor a move put past the limit is reported where it is. It was reported as the column after the limit. **A move from the wait counts from the limit**, since 2026-10-27: 8.3.123 bounds only implicit movement, so after `ESC[5 SP V abcde` `CUF`, `HPR`, `HT`, `CHT`, `CBT`, `HTS`, `TBC 0` and HTJ start from position 5, `ESC[C` reaching 6, as DEC STD 070's CUF and HT add to the active column and xterm's `CursorForward()` and `TabNext()` count from the column the wrap waits on; they counted from 6, `ESC[C` reaching 7 and `HT` passing a stop at 6. `CUB` and `BS` count from the limit too, as from a right margin's pending wrap, since 2026-11-01 (the wait was their first step before). **An SLL that moves the limit ends a wait there on the position it waited on**, and so does BDSM or DCSM making the limit bound nothing, as DECSWL ends one; an SLL naming the same position keeps it. The wait stayed marked and came back with the limit, the report meanwhile naming the column after it. **On a double-width line a cursor past the limit stays inside the line's half**, the limit bounding only implicit movement: DECDWL, a move onto the line and a read beginning on the status line put it back on the limit, and text written from past it ran on to the screen's last column, which the property suite found once its alphabet had a second limit (`past_the_limit_on_a_double_line_the_half_bounds_the_cursor`). **With `DECAWM` reset such a write stops on the half's last column**, since 2026-10-27, "the last column of the Active Line" (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-139), as xterm's last column is the line's, `LineMaxCol` (`[[source: xterm-reset]]`, charproc.c 7049-7058): it stopped on the screen's, the cursor past the half's sentinel, so `CUB 2` after text written to the half's end landed a column right of where it belongs and `ICH` there made room nowhere, until a read beginning on the status line pulled it back (`without_autowrap_past_the_limit_a_double_line_s_half_pins_the_cursor`, `a_write_pinned_past_the_limit_on_a_double_line_agrees_atom_by_atom`, `a_wide_run_past_the_limit_on_a_double_status_line_agrees_atom_by_atom`). **A line home past a double line's half is its last column**, since 2026-10-27, as a home past the screen is the screen's: `CR`, `IL`, `DL` and a wrap went into the half past the line's end and text was written there (`a_line_home_past_a_double_line_s_half_is_its_last_column`); `NEL` and `LF` under LNM take the home on the line they arrive at, "the line home position of the following line" (8.3.86), so onto a single line below a double one they reach a home past its half (`nel_and_lf_under_lnm_take_the_home_on_the_line_they_arrive_at`). With `DECAWM` reset a wide character at the limit writes its lead alone there, on a double line too (the `?7` row). `DECSC` saves a wrap waiting at the limit and `DECRC` brings it back waiting there, since 2026-11-01, as a right margin's (`a_wrap_waiting_at_the_line_limit_is_saved_and_restored`; `a_move_from_a_wrap_waiting_at_the_line_limit_counts_from_the_limit`, `an_sll_that_moves_the_limit_ends_a_wrap_waiting_there`, `a_wrap_pending_at_the_line_limit_is_reported_on_the_limit`, `a_cursor_moved_past_the_line_limit_is_reported_where_it_is`, `slh_takes_cr_nel_il_and_dl_to_the_line_home`, `cnl_and_cpl_keep_the_first_position`, `sll_wraps_at_the_line_limit_to_the_home`, `sll_with_autowrap_reset_stays_at_the_limit`, `slh_alone_takes_the_wrap_at_the_edge_to_the_home`, `under_presentation_only_cr_reads_the_home`, `the_inner_of_the_margins_and_the_bounds_holds`, `a_missing_position_is_counted_and_one_past_the_line_is_its_last`, `ris_forgets_the_bounds_and_decstr_keeps_them`, `a_resize_keeps_the_bounds`, `under_decrlm_cr_keeps_the_right_margin`, `decrqss_answers_slh_and_sll_as_invalid`, `a_wide_character_too_wide_for_the_limit_wraps`, `slh_with_a_private_marker_is_no_control`, `ich_leaves_the_cursor_where_it_is`) |
| `CSI Pn U` / `CSI Pn V` | NP / PP next page and preceding page | done — since 2026-10-27, **a program that turns to another page finds the cursor at the top left of its page, as DEC STD 070 has it on a terminal with one page**. baud's page memory is one page, its screen, as xterm's "single page (which corresponds to its visible window)" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`). `[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s algorithms (pp. 5-77 to 5-80) clamp the page, "the highest numbered page is used", "the first page is used", and then "NP moves the Active Position to the home position in the new page obeying Origin Mode", so on the one page NP and PP take the cursor home, to the top and left margins under DECOM, a count of 0 or 1 meaning one page (`np_and_pp_home_the_cursor_on_the_one_page`, `np_and_pp_home_to_the_margins_under_decom`, `np_ends_a_pending_wrap`). **A source conflict**: `[[source: vt510-np]]` and `[[source: vt510-pp]]` have "If there is only one page, then the terminal ignores NP", and `[[source: vt420-rm]]` ch. 6 "If there is only one page, the terminal ignores NP", the products' departure, not followed; `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.87 and 8.3.95 leave the active position "not defined". Each is counted in `Term::pages_missing`, as every count names another page (`a_page_baud_lacks_is_counted`). From Level 3, "Levels: 3x (Windowing), 4" (`below_level_three_the_page_functions_do_nothing`). Not built: several pages, the VT420's 144 lines in six pages of 24 down to one of 144, with the display showing one while the host writes another; DECPCCM is the `?64` row |
| `CSI Pn SP P` / `CSI Pn SP Q` / `CSI Pn SP R` | PPA / PPR / PPB page position absolute, forward and backward | done — since 2026-10-27, **a program that moves to the same place on another page stays where it is**: each moves "to the corresponding line and column" of the page it names (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` pp. 5-81 to 5-83; `[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.96 to 8.3.98, "the corresponding character position"), clamped to baud's one page (the NP row), so the cursor keeps its line and column (`ppa_ppr_and_ppb_keep_the_line_and_column`). ECMA-48 names `SP Q` PAGE POSITION FORWARD, `[[source: vt510-ppr]]` Page Position Relative. PPA 1, and PPA's 0 or omitted `Pn`, name the one page; any other PPA, and every PPR and PPB, is counted in `Term::pages_missing`. `[[source: vt510-ppa]]` and `[[source: vt510-ppb]]` have the terminal ignore each on one page, which leaves the cursor where the clamp does. From Level 3, as NP |
| `CSI ? 64 h` / `CSI ? 64 l` | DECPCCM page cursor-coupling mode | **out** — not recognized: DECRQM answers `0`, and a set or a reset is counted as an unsupported parameter (`decpccm_is_not_recognized_on_one_page`). baud has one page and DECPCCM "is only useful with a multiple-page format" (`[[source: vt510-decpccm]]`); `[[source: dec-std-070]]` has "DEC Private modes that cannot be set or reset from the host will report as unknown" (p. 5-193), so it answers not recognised rather than `3`, permanently set. It comes with several pages (the NP row) |
| `CSI Pn SP i` / `CSI Pn SP j` | SPH / SPL set page home and page limit | **out** — counted, and nothing moves. SPH sets "the position to which the active presentation position will be moved by subsequent occurrences of FORM FEED (FF)" and SPL "the position beyond which the active presentation position can normally not be moved" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.131, 8.3.133), each also bounding implicit movement "in the case of a device without data component", which baud's DCSM gives one. FF is `LF` here (the `0x0C` row), so a page home would be read by nothing, and the line a line feed scrolls at is DECSTBM's bottom margin; implicit movement runs along a line (6.1.6), and its wrap past the last line is the implementation's (6.1.6's note). So both are counted and move nothing (`sph_and_spl_are_counted_and_move_nothing`) |
| `CSI Ps1 ; Ps2 SP D` | FNT font selection | **out** — counted: no `Ps2` names a font. FNT identifies "the character font to be selected as primary or alternative font by subsequent occurrences of SELECT GRAPHIC RENDITION (SGR)", and "Ps2 identifies the character font according to a register which is to be established" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.53); no such register exists, so there is no number to put in a slot. The slots themselves are SGR 10-19's row. Each of the `SP` functions with a row here is held counted and writing nothing by `the_typesetting_functions_are_counted_and_write_nothing`; the twelve that set a character's size or spacing, and JFY, QUAD, IDCS, GCC and SCO, have none, being still to build (note BF, section 8) |
| `CSI Ps SP J` | PFS page format selection | **out** — counted: it sets "the available area for the imaging of pages of text based on paper size" for the pages "introduced by the subsequent occurrence of FORM FEED (FF)" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.91), and baud has one page, whose FF is `LF` (the `0x0C` row), so no later page is introduced for the area to take effect on, as no page home is read (the SPH and SPL row). DECSLPP, by contrast, "sets the number of display lines per page" now (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` p. 5-75), and is done. Annex E's Table E.1 gives each format's lines and characters at a line and character spacing that SVS and SHS select, neither built, and leaves formats 10 to 15 without a width at 10 characters per 25,4 mm, so it is refused and counted |
| `CSI Ps SP M` | IGS identify graphic subrepertoire | **out** — counted: it indicates "that a repertoire of the graphic characters of ISO/IEC 10367 is used in the subsequent text", identified as "registered in accordance with ISO/IEC 7350" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.66). It identifies and selects nothing: what a byte draws is the encoding's and the designations' (`ESC %`, SCS), so there is nothing for it to change |
| `CSI Pn1 ; Pn2 SP T` | DTA dimension text area | **out** — counted: it sets "the dimensions of the text area for subsequent pages" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.36), and on baud's one page, whose FF is `LF`, no subsequent page begins, as for PFS. Its unit is SSU's, which baud does not act on, so its established unit is CHARACTER, the default. Built now, it would be the window request `CSI 8 ; Pn1 ; Pn2 t` makes; it is refused and counted, as DECSLPP "sets the number of display lines per page" where DTA's are "subsequent pages" |
| `CSI Pn SP W` | FNK function key | **out** — counted: "a control function in which the parameter value identifies the function key which has been operated" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.52), a device's report to its host of a key operated, and receiving one operates no key. baud's own function keys send what `docs/input-coverage.md` lists |
| `CSI Ps SP X` | SPQR select print quality and rapidity | **out** — counted: "the relative print quality and the print speed for devices the output quality and speed of which are inversely related" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.134). baud is no printing device: what it prints goes to the system's print panel (the MC row), which chooses the quality |
| `CSI Ps1 ; Ps2 SP Y` | SEF sheet eject and feed | **out** — counted: it "causes a sheet of paper to be ejected from a printing device into a specified output stacker and another sheet to be loaded into the printing device from a specified paper bin" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.116), bins and stackers the print panel chooses, as for SPQR |
| `CSI Ps SP ^` | STAB selective tabulation | **out** — counted: it aligns text by "a tabulation stop which is selected from a list", and "means of specifying a list of tabulation stops to be referenced by the control function are specified in other standards, for example ISO 8613-6" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 8.3.144); neither ECMA-48 nor the DEC texts make such a list, so no `Ps` names a stop. TAC, TALE, TATE and TCC set aligned stops and are done |
| `CSI 9 h` / `CSI 9 l` | DCSM device component select mode | **partial** — since 2026-10-26, **a program that turns right-to-left drawing off can edit a line as it is drawn**: "PRESENTATION: Certain control functions are performed in the presentation component ... DATA: ... in the data component" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.3). RM 9 selects PRESENTATION and SM 9 DATA, as 7.1 ("the reset state is shown first"), 7.2 ("the first state is caused by RM") and Table 6 have it, where 7.3.4 c) calls DATA "the reset state"; PRESENTATION at power-on and after `RIS`, 7.1's recommendation; `DECSTR` keeps it. With BDSM reset it acts on a line holding an SDS or SRS string, the one place a presentation position holds another data position, and the functions 7.2.3 lists that baud implements take the cursor's presentation position: `CR` goes to the cell drawn at the line's home, `LF`, `NEL` and `RI` keep the presentation position on the line they reach, `DCH` deletes the cells drawn at the positions it covers, `ECH`, `EL` and `ED` erase them where they stand, `ICH` opens its blanks at the presentation position, joining the string there (TR/53 note A.1), `EA` and `EF` erase the positions as drawn (their rows), and `CPR` reports the presentation column; `DL` and `IL` act on lines, the same in both. SLH and SLL, the rest of 7.2.3's list, set the line home and limit (their row), and SPH and SPL are counted (theirs). With BDSM set it "has no effect; it is considered to be set to DATA" (7.3.4 c), which `DECRQM` answers then, the state set once BDSM is reset; DECTSR carries the state set. **The cursor moves as the line is drawn, in both states**: `CUP`, `CHA`, `CUF`, `CUB`, `CUU`, `CUD`, `CNL`, `CPL`, `HT`, `CHT`, `CBT`, `CVT` and `VT`, each of which moves "the active presentation position" "in the presentation component" (8.3.7-8.3.23, 8.3.60, 8.3.161), with no paragraph for either DCSM state, put the cursor on the cell drawn where the move ends, a move to another line keeping the position as `LF` does, and `HTS`, `CTC` and `TBC` set and clear the stop at the cursor's drawn column; 7.2.3's NOTE leaves them out of the functions DCSM affects, and Annex F F.7, informative, calls them "independent of the setting of" DCSM. **Since 2026-10-26 under DATA too**, where keeping them by cell under DATA is TR/53 worked example 2's reading: until then, on a line `SCP 2` set right to left, `CUF` under DATA moved left on the screen. TR/53 is a technical report, its worked example "based on the arbitrary implementation assumptions" (note A.1), and its DATA lines reach data columns with `CUP` and `CHA` (`[[source: ecma-tr53]]`), which `HVP` and `HPA` reach here. On a right-to-left line `CUF` moves right on the screen and `CUB` left, "rightwards in the presentation component" (8.3.20, 8.3.18); terminal-wg's draft reads them along the cells, as baud did. `HVP`, `HPA`, `HPR`, `HPB`, `VPA`, `VPR` and `VPB` move "the active data position" (8.3.63, 8.3.57-8.3.59, 8.3.158-8.3.160) and address the cells in both states. A character written under IRM goes in at the presentation position under PRESENTATION, and HEM's PRECEDING shifts the positions before it (the `CSI 10 h` row). A string drawn to the line's edge shifts on the line as one in its middle does, under `ICH` and IRM: the cells drawn at the far end of the shifted part leave the line, "the contents of n character positions at the other end of the shifted part are removed" (8.3.64), and a wide character too wide for the last position wraps, or with autowrap reset goes in at the last two, where a line with no string writes its lead at the last column. Open departure: under DCSM's PRESENTATION and HEM's FOLLOWING, after a character is inserted under IRM the cursor moves on by the cells, where 7.3.3 b moves the active presentation position; two characters typed into a reversed string are drawn apart, and at the line's edge the second goes to the next line, or with autowrap reset in front of the first. | `CSI 10 h` / `CSI 10 l` | HEM character editing mode | done — since 2026-10-26, **a program can make an insertion or a deletion push the text before the cursor instead of after it**: "FOLLOWING ... a character insertion causes the contents of the active presentation position and of the following character positions ... to be shifted in the direction of the character path", "PRECEDING ... in the direction opposite" (`[[source: ecma48]]` 7.2.9), "Control functions affected are: DCH, ICH". RM 10 selects FOLLOWING, the state at power-on and after `RIS`, as 7.1 recommends, and what baud always did; SM 10 PRECEDING. `DECSTR` keeps it, as its table names no HEM and as it keeps DCSM; `DECRQM` answers it, `0` until now, and DECTSR carries it (version 8). Under PRECEDING `DCH` removes the active position and the `n - 1` before it and closes the gap from the line's start (8.3.26), `ICH` pushes the active position and the ones before it toward the line's start, the first `n` leaving the line (8.3.64), and a character written under IRM goes in at the cursor, the ones before it moving toward the line's start, and "the active position does not move" (7.3.3 b); a wide character takes the cursor's column and the one before it, and at the shifted part's start the cursor's and the one after it. The shifted part starts at the left margin under DECLRMM for a cursor inside it, as FOLLOWING's ends at the right margin and as DECRLM's insertion stops at the left margin, DEC's extent before any SEE; an extent SEE chooses shifts from its start (the `CSI Ps Q` row). With BDSM reset and DCSM at PRESENTATION, on a line holding a string, the positions shifted are the ones drawn, and a character written under IRM is inserted at the presentation position, "the graphic symbol ... is inserted at the active presentation position" (7.2.10), what was drawn there moving on along the path, where it was inserted at the cell (the DCSM row); TR/53's worked example 2, line 7, inserts before the cell in the data instead, the cell keeping its drawn position. 7.2.9's PRECEDING paragraph for PRESENTATION says "following" twice where its DATA paragraph says "preceding", which read word for word makes an insertion overwrite the position before the cursor; it is read as the DATA paragraph, an erratum. Under DECRLM a write under IRM shifts toward the left margin as DECRLM's own text says, in both HEM states, and `DCH` and `ICH`, which DECRLM does not affect, follow HEM. **And a run of wide or of narrow multi-byte characters opens its gap under IRM**, since 2026-10-26: one width that fit the row wrote over the line, so text after the cursor was lost or kept depending on where the reads fell; under IRM the text is read a character at a time, each opening its gap. **A wide character stays whole under PRECEDING**, since 2026-10-27, before any SEE and under SEE 1 as under SEE's other extents: an edit on either half takes the whole character, the shifted part running on through the tail where the cursor is on the lead, so a character written under IRM there goes in after it; a delete takes the character and the n-1 positions in the shift's direction; and one the shift would part, at the line's start, at the left margin, at the fence or pushed off the line, is erased, both halves. Until then the line's shift cut it at the cursor: `a中cy` with the cursor on the lead read `中`, a blank and a lone tail after `ICH`, `a` and a lone tail after `DCH`, and `中X` and a lone tail after a write under IRM (`preceding_on_a_wide_character_takes_the_whole_character`, `preceding_on_the_line_is_the_page_on_its_first_line`). Open departure: under DCSM's PRESENTATION and HEM's FOLLOWING, after a character is inserted under IRM the cursor moves on by the cells, where 7.3.3 b moves the active presentation position, so two characters typed into a reversed string are drawn apart. (`dch_under_preceding_closes_the_gap_from_the_line_start`, `ich_under_preceding_pushes_toward_the_line_start`, `irm_under_preceding_inserts_at_the_cursor_which_stays`, `a_mark_under_preceding_joins_the_character_at_the_cursor`, `preceding_shifts_from_the_left_margin`, `decrqm_answers_hem`, `ris_returns_insertion_to_following`, `irm_under_presentation_inserts_at_the_presentation_position`, `irm_under_presentation_and_preceding_pushes_toward_the_home`, `dch_and_ich_under_presentation_and_preceding`, `irm_opens_a_gap_before_a_run_of_multibyte_characters`, `decrsts_restores_hem_as_set`, `the_hem_atoms_reach_the_editing_functions`) |
| `CSI ? 2501 h` / `CSI ? 2501 l` | terminal-wg's direction autodetection | done — since 2026-10-25, **a program can choose whether a line's direction comes from its first letter or only from what it set**: set, as at power-on, a paragraph runs the way its first strong letter gives it, UAX #9 P2-P3, and the direction SCP or SPD chose is only the fallback where it has none, a line of digits or an empty one; reset, the chosen direction is the paragraph's level outright and a line nobody set a direction for runs left to right ("When in explicit mode OR autodetection disabled, the model's corresponding flag is used directly. When in implicit mode AND autodetection enabled, it's autodetected ... still possibly falling back to the model's corresponding flag", `[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-basic-modes]]`; SCP "affects the direction of both implicit and explicit paragraphs (including of course the fallback direction of implicit paragraphs with autodetection)", `[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`). **Set at power-on**, so a line nobody set a direction for takes its first strong letter's, since 2026-10-24; the draft leans to reset on "a few weak arguments" and leaves the choice open ("terminal emulators might pick their favorite one"), mintty starts with it set and VTE reset, and both take SCP as the fallback while it is set (`[[source: mintty-termline-0aea388]]`, `[[source: vte-bidi-cc-56bad78]]`). A paragraph property, kept on every row of a paragraph beside SCP's path (`baud_domain::LineBidi`) and carried from line to line by the SCP row's rules; it applies at once only to the paragraph whose first position the cursor is on, "if the cursor stands at the first logical position of a paragraph then the received BiDi property value is immediately applied on that paragraph", and else becomes the value the next new line takes. SPD spreads only the path ("It would be ugly for SPD to spread the current BDSM, autodetection etc. modes too across all the lines"). Ignored with BDSM reset ("The autodetection flag is ignored in explicit mode"). `DECRQM` answers the value in force, XTSAVE and XTRESTORE keep it ("there's also a slot for saving and restoring the value"), DECTSR carries it (the report's version 3); `DECSTR` returns the value in force to set and leaves the lines', as mintty's soft reset does (`[[source: mintty-term-0aea388]]`); `RIS` returns every line (`a_path_is_the_fallback_while_autodetection_is_on`, `decrqm_answers_the_three_modes`, `xtsave_and_xtrestore_keep_the_three_modes`, `a_mode_applies_to_the_paragraph_whose_start_the_cursor_is_on`, `decstr_returns_the_modes_in_force_and_ris_every_line`, `decrsts_restores_the_three_bidi_modes`, `the_paragraph_mode_atoms_reach_the_rows`, `a_right_to_left_default_takes_a_text_with_no_strong_letter`) |
| `CSI ? 2500 h` / `CSI ? 2500 l` | terminal-wg's box mirroring | done — since 2026-10-25, **a box a program draws on a right-to-left line closes**: set, a Box Drawing character drawn right to left, U+2500-U+257F, is drawn as its left-right counterpart, `┌` as `┐`, `├` as `┤`, `╱` as `╲` ("to mirror the U+2500 .. U+257F glyphs in RTL", `[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-misc]]`; UAX #9 HL6, "Certain characters that do not have the Bidi_Mirrored property can also be depicted by a mirrored glyph in specialized contexts"), the counterpart the character whose UnicodeData.txt name says the same with left and right exchanged, compiled into `bidi_data.rs` (`[[source: unicode-data]]`; compared with VTE's table and equal to it entry for entry, `[[source: vte-bidi-cc-56bad78]]`). **Only a character drawn right to left**, L4's resolved R with HL6 widening which characters: every one on a line BDSM reset draws in reverse, and none on a table row, whose rules stay where they were written at the left-to-right level (the BDSM row). So with BDSM set it reaches only a box character carrying a mark: any unmarked Box Drawing character makes its paragraph a table row, drawn left to right, and `?2500` acts on the lines BDSM reset draws in reverse. U+2500-U+257F only, as the draft and VTE define the mode, not mintty's Block Elements as well. **Since 2026-10-26 a box line two cells wide closes too**: under `ambiguous_width = "wide"` its whole two-cell mask is mirrored, the counterpart with its verticals in its last cell -- the first the line is read in, where a one-cell piece in that cell has its own -- and its horizontals run to the far edge, so `│` and `┼` move their vertical there as well (`a_mirrored_wide_line_meets_a_one_cell_piece_across_and_down`, `a_mirrored_wide_frame_is_the_frame_mirrored`, `a_mirrored_two_cell_mask_is_the_mask_flipped`, `a_wide_line_mirrored_and_as_written_in_one_frame`; the three paths' pixels: `the_cell_arm_draws_what_the_instance_arm_draws`); without `?2500` it is drawn as written (`a_wide_line_right_to_left_without_box_mirroring_is_drawn_as_written`), and an input method's composition on such a line is mirrored as the same text written (`a_composition_on_a_mirrored_line_is_mirrored_as_its_text_written`). A paragraph property as `?2501` is, carried and applied by the same rules; reset at power-on, as VTE has it. `DECRQM`, XTSAVE, DECTSR, `DECSTR` and `RIS` as `?2501` (`box_mirroring_closes_a_box_drawn_right_to_left`, `box_mirroring_leaves_a_table_row_as_written`, `every_box_drawing_counterpart_is_its_counterparts_counterpart`; the three paths' pixels: `a_line_scp_turns_draws_alike_on_every_arm`) |
| `CSI ? 1243 h` / `CSI ? 1243 l` | terminal-wg's arrow-key swapping | done — since 2026-10-25, **Left and Right move the way a right-to-left line reads**: set, "the sequences generated by the Left and Right arrows are swapped whenever the cursor is inside a RTL paragraph. (In case of autodetection, it's the autodetected direction that matters and not the specified fallback.)" (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-arrow-key-swapping]]`), the keypad's Left and Right too, in every encoding, before the key is encoded; "Home and End should not be swapped". **Decided at the press**, "conditionally to the cursor being inside an RTL paragraph at the time the key is pressed" (`[[source: terminal-wg-bidi-escape-sequences]]`): a key's repeats and release take its press's decision and name the key it named, though the cursor has moved or `?1243` changed since, and one with no press recorded goes as pressed; `DECSTR` and `RIS` forget every held key's. The direction is the one the paragraph is drawn in (`Term::paragraph_direction`): its first strong letter, the fallback, or its path, over the rows a soft wrap joins and the history's lines above them; on a table row, the cell the cursor is in, each a paragraph of its own, a rule in the cell before it, and past the row's text in none; with BDSM reset, its path. **Set at power-on and by `RIS`**: "this new arrow key swapping mode needs to be the default", as VTE has it; the draft's argument that this changes nothing by default assumed autodetection reset, which baud's is not, so a line whose first strong letter is Hebrew or Arabic swaps from the start, a shell prompt that starts in Latin letters does not. Global, "Unlike the output modes, the keyboard mode is global". The cursor keys the wheel and a `?2001` click send count logical columns and are not swapped. `DECRQM`, XTSAVE and DECTSR carry it; `DECSTR` returns it to set, as DEC's table returns the cursor keys and VTE's soft reset every mode (`the_arrow_keys_swap_inside_a_right_to_left_paragraph`, `a_repeat_and_a_release_take_the_decision_of_the_press`, `the_swap_follows_the_direction_drawn`, `on_a_table_row_the_cell_holds_the_direction`, `a_paragraph_begun_in_the_history_is_read_from_its_start`, `the_cursor_keys_the_wheel_and_a_click_send_do_not_swap`, `the_direction_the_core_reads_is_the_one_drawn`, `the_one_pass_direction_is_the_algorithms_on_every_character_test_text`) |
| `CSI 6 i` / `CSI 7 i` | locator controller mode | **out** — the Locator Port's other control (`[[source: dec-std-070]]` 070-13 § 1.2, § 7). Controller mode passes the host's bytes "directly to the locator port", a "guideline-documented exception" for a serial mouse or tablet on a terminal's own port; baud's pointer is the window system's, with no port to talk to. Counted (`locator_controller_mode_is_counted`) |
| `CSI ? 1040 h/l` | keepSelection, "Keep selection even if not highlighted" (`[[source: ctlseqs]]`) | done — since 2026-10-22 **a selection's text survives output that writes over it**. Set at power-on, xterm's default ("The default is \"true\"", `[[source: xterm-man]]`): output that writes or erases on a selected row takes the highlight away, as before, and the text the selection held stays for Cmd-C and Ctrl+Shift+C until another selection is made, as xterm's `ScrnDisownSelection` keeps it while the resource is set (`[[source: xterm-screen]]`); the text is taken before the first read after the selection is made, where xterm's `SaltTextAway` takes it as the selection is made (`[[source: xterm-button]]`), and a selection made between two reads replaces what was kept (`output_over_a_selection_leaves_its_text_while_1040_is_set`, `the_kept_text_is_the_last_selections`, `a_selection_dismissed_between_reads_replaces_the_kept_text`). **Since 2026-10-22 the text kept is the one the selection was made with**: a scroll that carries or trims the selection, or the end of the history that clips it, moves only its ends, as xterm's truncating `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll` move only `startH` and `endH` (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 460-525), and Cmd-C and Ctrl+Shift+C copy that text while the selection is up (`a_trimmed_selection_copies_the_text_it_was_made_with`); a selection the end of the history clips away leaves its text to copy, as xterm disowns a selection scrolled off its margins (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 482-490 and 502-506) and its disowning under keepSelection takes the highlight alone (`a_selection_the_history_clips_away_copies_the_text_it_was_made_with`); and a click that makes no selection takes the highlight and leaves the text to copy, as xterm's press takes the highlight (`StartSelect`) and its release disowns nothing (`SelectSet` on a null selection, whose `ScrnDisownSelection` finds no highlight). Until then a trimmed selection's text was taken again, trimmed, and a selection clipped away or a click left nothing to copy. Reset, the text goes with the highlight, and a scroll that would trim the selection drops it, as xterm's reset disowns it where its set truncates it (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`, `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll`): across a region's edge, pushed out of a region or under the screen, across a top region's bottom, off the alternate screen's top, and off the primary's top into the history; `IL` and `DL` trim in either mode, as `InsertLine` and `DeleteLine` read no keepSelection (`while_keep_selection_is_reset_a_trimming_scroll_drops_the_selection`, `while_1040_is_reset_a_selection_scrolled_off_the_top_goes`). DECRQM answers it, XTSAVE keeps it in slot 60 and DECTSR carries it, the report's version 2, `DECSTR` keeps it and `RIS` sets it (`keep_selection_is_set_at_power_on_and_answers_decrqm`, `each_switch_sets_resets_saves_and_restores`). Reset, output that takes the selection gives up what SELECT offered on Linux, the primary selection or under `?1041` the clipboard, as xterm's `DisownSelection` gives up each selection it owns (`XtDisownSelection`, `[[source: xterm-button]]`), and only while the source the selection's copy set still offers it (`output_that_takes_the_selection_while_1040_is_reset_gives_up_select`, `a_selection_is_given_up_only_while_it_is_offered`); since 2026-10-22, where it kept offering the text. Open: the macOS pasteboard a copy-on-select wrote keeps the text, since a pasteboard holds a copy where X offers one and emptying it would lose what was copied; and a switch of screens, `RIS` or a resize dropping the selection gives nothing up, xterm's handling of those not read; and a selection the history's end clips away gives nothing up, where xterm's disowning there, `ScrnDisownSelection` with the resource reset, gives up SELECT (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 482-490 and 502-506). One departure is a choice the project made: set, `ICH`, `DCH`, `DECIC` and `DECDC` on a selected row take the highlight away where xterm's `InsertChar`, `DeleteChar` and `xtermColScroll` shift it -- one column for `ICH` and `DCH` whatever their count, and by the count for every end whose row is in the top and bottom margins for `DECIC` and `DECDC`. Since 2026-10-22 the highlight goes (`a_write_or_an_erase_on_a_selected_row_drops_it`, all four). One departure, open: reset, xterm disowns the selection on any scroll that is not full-line (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]` 451-456 and 553-559), so a scroll under side margins drops it wherever it lies, where baud drops it only when the rectangle's rows hold it, in either mode. Until 2026-10-22 `?1040` was not recognised and no text was kept. The highlight goes on a write or an erase on a row of the selection, since 2026-10-17 (`every_writer_reaches_the_row`, `a_write_or_an_erase_on_a_selected_row_drops_it`). For text, `EL`, `ED`, `ECH`, `ICH`, `DCH`, `DECIC` and `DECDC` that is what xterm does with the resource reset: `WriteText`, and `ClearInLine` under `ClearLeft`, `ClearRight`, `ClearLine`, `ClearAbove` and `ClearBelow`, disown always, and `InsertChar`, `DeleteChar` and `xtermColScroll` only while it is reset, shifting the selection while it is set (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`). A joined mark, `DECALN`, `DECSEL` and `DECSED` drop it as xterm disowns it, by the rows they touch: `ScrnUpdate` on the mark's row and over the rows `ScrnFillRectangle` fills (`[[source: xterm-screen]]`, `[[source: xterm-reset]]`), `ClearInLine` row by row (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`). xterm's `ClearScreen` -- `ED 2`, `DECSED 2` with nothing protected, `ED 0` at home, `ED 1` at the last cell, and the clear for `?3` -- disowns with no row test, a selection wholly in the history too; baud keeps that one, whose text the clear leaves, as kitty does (`[[source: kitty-screen-c]]`, its erase in display), and alacritty. A redraw mark's erase and `CSI ? 3 J` on the saved lines drop a selection on the lines they erase (`a_redraw_mark_reaches_the_rows_of_a_block_on_the_screen`, `a_watch_on_saved_lines_is_reached_where_their_text_goes`). **A scroll carries the selection with its text**, since 2026-10-20, as xterm's `adjustHiliteOnFwdScroll` and `adjustHiliteOnBakScroll` do with the resource set (`[[source: xterm-scroll]]`): a full-width region's scroll moves it and trims the lines it takes out of the region, keeping the part inside of one across the region's edge; a scroll down trims what it pushes under the screen; the alternate screen's top trims what leaves it at that scroll; the rows under a top region's scroll keep theirs; `IL` and `DL` move it below the cursor and drop it where the cursor's row or a deleted row is in it, as xterm's `InsertLine` and `DeleteLine` disown it (`a_scroll_carries_a_selection_with_its_text_at_every_read_size`, `a_copying_scroll_up_carries_the_regions_rows_and_only_those`, `il_carries_the_rows_from_the_cursor_down`). A scroll under side margins still drops it, as xterm disowns it there. Until then each of these dropped it. |

---

## A source conflict, recorded

`[[source: vt510-decawm]]` gives DECAWM's default as **"No autowrap"**.
`[[source: ctlseqs]]` documents xterm, where autowrap is on, and every modern
terminal wraps. baud follows xterm: an application emitting a long line expects
it to wrap, and the DEC default would break essentially all of them. The older
and stricter reading is the one baud does **not** implement, and that is a
choice rather than an oversight.

**DECPEX's default** (`?19`) is "Scrolling region" in `[[source: vt420-rm]]`
p. 210 and `[[source: vt510-rm-b]]` p. 5-81, and xterm's `printerExtent` is
false; the VT420's own Table 11-5 marks the page "(D)", and
`[[source: dec-std-070]]`'s `host_state_init` sets `PRINT_DISPLAY`, from NVM.
baud resets it, printing the scrolling region. And **the underline sent to a
printer** is SGR 4 in STD 070 § 7.4.2 and SGR 2 in the VT420's p. 215, which
ECMA-48 makes faint; baud sends 4.

## Why these decisions went the way they did

The reasoning — the refusals, the source conflicts, the things that look like
gaps and are not — is in [protocol-decisions.md](protocol-decisions.md). It was
in this file, 180 lines below the last table, where nobody scanning for an
operation's status was ever going to find it.

---

## How this file stays true

`baud-core/tests/coverage.rs` reads this file and fails if a row marked **done**
names an operation no test mentions. Run by `devenv test`, so it gates CI.

Be precise about what that proves, because this paragraph was itself the
failure it warns about for most of the project's life: it named
`tests/coverage.rs` while **no such file existed**, so the guarantee was
enforced by nothing.

* The test **does** prove every `done` row is mentioned by some test. Marking a
  row done with no test at all fails.
* It **cannot** prove the test would fail if the operation regressed. Nothing
  mechanical can. That stays a review obligation, and it is stated here as one
  rather than dressed up as a guarantee.

A weaker check that is real beats a stronger claim that is not. Documentation
claiming coverage it does not have is the failure mode this file exists to
prevent — the README claimed "~22 operations" while three of them were parsed
and dropped.

### An operation is done on *both screens*, or it is not done

`EL` and `ED` sat at **done**, with tests, and did **nothing at all** on the
alternate screen in `baud-core2`'s `OpMode::Runs`. That core's `erase_line`
had a span-truncation fast path that read the `promoted` flag raw; that flag belonged to
the primary screen, so on
the alternate one it was false for every row, the erase took the span path, and
it returned without touching a cell. `HELLO WORLD` survived an erase-to-end
from column 6.

Every erase test in `baud-core2` ran on the primary. So did every scrolling
test — full-screen scrolling in that core's alternate screen was copying rows
for the same reason, at 26x the cost.

**The enumeration was complete in the wrong dimension.** This file lists the
operations and `coverage.rs` checks each is mentioned; neither asks *in which
state* it was exercised. The alternate screen is not an operation and so has no
row here, but it changes the behaviour of the ones that do — in `baud-core2`
it was where the run table stopped applying — and every full-screen
application spends its whole life there.

Two gates came from that in `baud-core2`, and neither survives as it was
written: the run table and the op modes went with that core (2538310), and
this one has neither. Here each screen is a surface of its own -- cells,
extents, ring and rotation -- and `set_alt_screen` swaps the view onto the
other (`View::swap_surface`), so an operation reaches whichever screen is up
through the one view. What stands now:

* `erases_on_the_alternate_screen_are_not_swallowed_by_the_span_path`
  (`baud-core/tests/vt100/scrolling.rs`) runs six erases on the alternate
  screen, once each: there is one mode, and what it pins is that the surface
  erased is the surface shown. core2 ran each in `Computed` as well as `Runs`.
* core2's `screen_isolation.rs` required every function touching its run
  table to be screen-aware, structurally, because a wrongly zeroed run count
  showed through no public accessor. It was deleted with that core, and this
  file then named `baud-core/tests/xterm/alt_screen.rs` in its place, which
  holds no structural check. A write through to the parked primary, or a
  primary back under a wrong rotation, shows in its rows, so the checks read
  rows. A wrong extent does not, until something trusts it: with
  `View::swap_surface` swapping no extents, every row of
  `a_scrolled_primary_comes_back_in_the_order_it_left`'s trip compared
  equal, and the test caught it through what trusts the extents -- the
  history read back, the erase, and an exit that indexed past the screen.
  `a_round_trip_through_the_alternate_screen_preserves_the_primary` and
  `index_on_the_alternate_screen_leaves_the_primary_intact` scroll the
  alternate screen over a primary that never scrolled, so they fail when the
  primary comes back under the alternate's rotation and pass when it comes
  back reset to zero, the rotation it started from.
  `a_scrolled_primary_comes_back_in_the_order_it_left` scrolls the primary
  first, to rotations of 24, 33, 11, 111 and 57 (the last three over
  history), then crosses `?1049` and `?47` and holds every row, the history
  and the cursor to a twin that never switched, at six ways of cutting the
  reads; then it erases rows 42-50 on both and compares again, for the
  extents.

The reviewer's question for a new operation is therefore not only "is there a
test" but "does it run where applications will run it": on the alternate
screen as well as the primary, and over a primary that has scrolled as well
as one that has not.
